# Completion Log

*The note each landed item recorded at its gate, moved out of [the implementation checklist](implementation-checklist.md) so that the checklist carries the open work and one line per landed item. The checklist owns every item, its estimate cell and its order; this file owns the evidence a landing wrote, verbatim, and is the artifact the findings register indexes.*

## How to read this

Each entry sits under a heading spelling the item's label as the checklist writes it, and the checklist's one-line cell for that item links here. The notes are gate-time measurements: a figure in one is what the item's gate measured at the revision it names, and it stays as written when the tree moves on, which is the exception the working rules make for completion evidence. A findings block here is counted by `tools/check.py` exactly as it was in the checklist, so the findings register's `Raised:` lines still name the item and every counted finding still has one entry. A `Landed:` line is read here too. Nothing in this file is an open obligation: an act a note reports as owed is indexed by the findings register, which is where its disposition lives.

The sections follow the checklist's own order, and an entry's depth follows the item's: a child item of a split milestone sits one heading level below its parent.

## The summary's state before S10b landed (2026-09-05)

The checklist's current summary carried the paragraphs below on the day the notes moved, and they are kept here as the reading they were rather than deleted: what each lane held, what gated it, and the thesis the plan's shape rested on. The checklist's summary now carries the completed roster, the figures the checker recomputes and the critical chain, and nothing else.

**In one paragraph.** Take the owed register acts and the two licence reads first, because they are the only work that cannot be delegated and they gate the most. Re-base the compiler item on stock CompCert, where the delta is a back end rather than a memory model. Let the Gallina front run on the Wasm oracle it was designed for, and let the assembler this repository already built be the assembler. Join the RVFI-DII ecosystem, build the three generators, and stop hand-writing what a model can emit. Generate the derived artifacts rather than policing their transcriptions, which retires much of the tool surface and the rules bolted on to hold those transcriptions equal. Split the gate that welded two independent tracks, strike the emulator fork whose two jobs other items now do, and make the lane portable so the host is a per-run choice rather than a migration. **The stated bill barely moves. What halves is the open work before a booting system, and what falls by nearly two thirds is the serial chain through it**, because the plan's problem was never the cost: it was that the schedule was serial where it need not be, hand-validated where it could generate, and pointed at start-froms that do not carry what they were priced to carry.

* **The serial constraint was lane 1 and it is discharged.** S1's owed register acts were the author's own, they gated the whole Gallina front and both RTL lanes, and nothing else could take them; thirteen of the fourteen are taken and the fourteenth is conditioned on a measurement rather than owed to an author. What is left in lane 1 is the profile amendments a later act creates, which arrive one at a time rather than as a block. **So the opening is no longer spent almost entirely there**, and the Gallina lanes open on the register as it now stands rather than waiting on it.
* **The Gallina front is not blocked and was sequenced as though it were.** §0's own discipline is that each component runs host-side on a stock Wasm engine *before it is lowered at all*, needing no toolchain, no image and no machine model, and M1.5 is landed and green. **All ten of the items that carried the label have landed under it** and M3.3 landed against the same discipline: M3.2, M3.4a, M4.2b, M4.3, M5.1, M5.2, M6.1a, M6.2a, M6.3a and M6.4 are complete, nine of them agent-parallel, and M6.4's own gate on M6.0b was met by that item landing first. What still carries the label is M3.4c alone, which is not of that ten: M3.4a's landing created that cell out of its own residue, and it is class X at the crypto upstreams M3.4a was class X at. Only the lowering-and-integration items genuinely want the backend: M1.7, M3.5, M4.4, M5.3 and M7.1. **M6.5a was read onto that list and did not belong on it**: the copy-based service's SPSC, notification and capacity obligations are statements over an index algebra and an ordered protocol, so it landed at the prover level like the ten above and never carried the label that would have said so.
* M0's serial path: **closed**. The C-class freeze is taken: M0.6 closed the decode surface, M0.14 and M0.16 put both latency classes and their maintenance in the model without adding an instruction, M0.8a fixed the per-class parameterization at the C class, M0.9 put the timing annotations over it, and M0.10 generated the emulator and gave it the revision every downstream artifact records. What is left in M0 is M0.8c. M0.8c is not a datapath awaiting its turn: its scratchpad landed, and its instruction surface is a second-act item conditioned on R-15-116's margin (R-15-014a (ix)), so what closes it is a profile act rather than a model one and it gates no milestone here. The next serial work is M1's toolchain spine, and it is ungated. Behind it the pin builds whole on this toolchain through to a linked `ccomp`, which is what M1.1b weighed; the pretty-printer census that build took no longer locates M1.2's cost, that item having struck the `-dcapasm` work outright and moved its start-from to stock CompCert's RISC-V backend, against which the delta is unmeasured.
* Available parallel work, in nine lanes rather than three, and the list is the open residue rather than the roster: **lane 1** the author's own, whose named content is discharged and which now carries the profile amendments a later act creates, one at a time; **lane 2** the spine, M1.2 then M1.7; **lanes 3–5** the Gallina front's residue, which M6.0b, M6.3a and M6.4 landing has reduced to M3.4c alone, M3.4c being what M3.4a's own landing left; **lanes 6–7** the RTL, R1b and R1c's devices; **lane 8** the instruments, whose open content is S10b alone; and **lane 9** the rules, because if each lane writes its own rule every lane edits `counts.py` and a conflict per landing is guaranteed, where one lane fed by items and by S8's register removes the collision and concentrates the one skill that most benefits from repetition. Four gating facts sit behind that list, and two shared surfaces sit under it.
  **Four documents every lane writes, and only two of them have an item.** `counts.py` is S10a's and is sharded; the per-item notes are S10b's and are not. The other two are held by no item at all: [the findings register](findings-register.md) takes an entry per counted finding from every landing, with K-82 holding it total in both directions and two figures `--fix` recomputes, so two lanes landing at once conflict on the text and on the arithmetic alike; and [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) takes a row and a gitlink from M1.4 and R1c-ii against one table K-81 holds total against the index. The discipline lane 9 uses for K-numbers is what both want, a block of ids pre-assigned per lane. A fifth surface is narrower and worth naming: R1c-i is filed in an RTL lane and writes only [the composition](../model/config/verifiedos.json), the attested devicetree and [the validator](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail), which are the three files M0.8c also writes.
  **M3 is entered ahead of M1 and M2 by an ordering call rather than by accident**: M3.1 needed nothing from either, reading the frozen profile and writing devices, and the build order put it behind them only because later items of its milestone need a lowered image. M3.2 and M3.3 gate on M3.1, and M3.2's seal, unseal and quote halves on M3.4's crypto and M3.5's chain.
  **R1 gates on the M0 freeze rather than on M1**, R-18-003b putting the absence contract among the day-one deliverables, so the RTL track runs beside the toolchain spine and closes at its own gate rather than rendezvousing with the software one. R1a is taken; S3 initialized five of the imported cores R1b and R1c reach through, and `opentitan` stays uninitialized, which is what the elaboration refuses by name on.
  **M1.8's second gate is M1.4′ rather than M1.2**: [the measurement contract](freeze-measurement-contract.md) joins three inputs into the analyzer's one table, and two of them, the link map and the encoded image, are the composer's rather than the backend's. The analyzer needed neither milestone, the contract fixing every enumeration and every declared value, so what M1.8b waits on is a transport against a declared schema, and it waits behind the M8a gate with the freeze it serves.
  **Ungated is not unblocked, and M4.2 is where the two part**: no fork holds it, and its second half is held by the register acts M4.2b enumerates, each an obligation with no mechanism under it, which is a gap a milestone cannot close and which S1 is the act that closes. I1 was open the same way, its two standing clauses being settings on the human's box rather than in this tree, which is why it is struck rather than scheduled; S9 no longer closes them, the lane staying on WSL by choice, so they stand as an accepted cost rather than as an item.

## S · Serial-path decisions and program instruments

### S1 · Discharge the owed register acts

  * **Thirteen of the fourteen are taken and the fourteenth is not owed yet.** Each was a register or profile act the plan could not take for itself, and each blocked work that was otherwise ungated. Six kernel-semantics acts land as entries: R-07-031b closes the invocation enumeration at five and R-07-027a the object inventory at three classes plus two tables, with no reply object surviving (f1, f7); R-07-029a fixes the blocking discipline as rendezvous-or-refusal, collected by the new seam R-17-030x (f2); R-07-037c and R-07-037d answer the rotation in opposite directions, swapping the pending bits and admitting the in-domain residue (f3, f4); and R-15-220a puts the restore and the swap in a context term beside R-15-220's three platform terms rather than amending it to four (f6). Four more land where they belong: R-15-007a states `setCapBounds`'s containment domain (M2.1, R1a), R-09-034 carves the Debug Module out of the permanent test-exit closure (M3.1), M6.0b gives the IDL profile an item of its own (M6.4), and the K-64 hole is answered by a struck registry row (f8). The SoC definition is split at its own seam, the address map to R-15-002b's attested devicetree and the peripherals to route (c) (R1c). And the two rulings with priced consequences are taken: M4.4 is single-instance bring-up, and measured boot covers a statically composed image, which splits M6.2.
  * **What stays open is the matrix instruction surface, and it is not this item's to close.** R-15-014a (ix) admits it against R-15-116's sustained dense-GEMM margin, which no backend and no composed image can supply before M1, and the re-pin target it would otherwise be a version pin of has not ratified: `Zvvm` stands at v0.9.0 ARC review candidate, its own plan's v1.0 date of 2026-08-27 already missed. So the act is conditioned on a measurement rather than owed to an author, which is a different disposition from the thirteen above and is M0.8c's.
  * **What the discharge cost that the estimate did not carry**: six new entries each needed the prose side they are extracted from, which is the co-read discipline read forward rather than a surprise, and sixteen pairs were re-read and blessed. Three prose sides carried the reading the act narrows and were repaired with it rather than left to disagree: R-11-023's *widths and offsets*, R-15-247h's die-wide reset hold, and R-09-034's unqualified permanent closure.
  * Landed: Tier A.

### S2 · Adopt the two-tier landing rule and hold it by rule

  * **A landing declares its tier, and a Tier-B landing names what holds it.** Three of the four conditions Tier B is admitted on are gates that already run; the fourth is a claim about the item and had nothing under it, so a Tier-B landing was admissible on a promise. It becomes a declaration, and **K-84** in the [meta](../tools/vos/checks/meta.py) group holds it: every bolded rule id in the corpus names a rule [the registry](../tools/check-rules.md) carries, and every Tier-B declaration names one.
  * **The form is the one the landed items already write**, a rule id in bold beside what it holds, where a rule merely discussed stays plain. That is what makes the reading live at thirteen citations rather than beginning empty, and it is why the gap at K-64 stays with the register act that owns the numbering instead of being reported against the document that reports it.
  * **What the arms not taken forfeit.** Holding every figure a completed item states was the stronger arm and is declined on the tree's own evidence: the completed items state gate measurements, `664/664 tests pass` and a 1,442,433-byte cache among them, which no rule should hold, so the arm needs the measurement-against-created-fact distinction it was meant to supply. It also collides with the claim rows, which already own every derived figure, giving one fact two owners. Inferring the tier from the item's own diff was the third arm: the checker's corpus is the git index at one revision, it has no diff, and nothing binds an item to a commit, which is S8's work. What the taken arm forfeits is a tier nobody can mis-declare.
  * **What it cannot decide, stated rather than closed.** Whether the named rule holds *that* fact is a reading, which is the residue every conferral here declares. And a declaration is read where it is made and never demanded where it is absent, forty-four landings predating the tiers and none assignable retroactively, so that half is forward-looking and the first declaration is this item's own. A holder written plain rather than bold is invisible to it, and the loss shows as a falling citation count floored at zero.
  * Net change: 143 lines added and 2 removed across four files.
  * Exit evidence: the checker green on all **82 rules** with all **92 mutants** killed and the registry covered both ways; `gate.py --fix` rewriting nothing, so no derived figure moved; the co-read ledger unmoved at 1,377 pairs; the tools' own suite green and both type checkers clean at their pins; and the six figures this item rests on each re-verified rather than restated, the proof gate at 104 axiom-free constants, the differential corpus at 26 of 26 with 245 checks, and the two type checkers at ty 0.0.74 and ruff 0.16.4. No register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit, so the review gate is not re-entered.
  * Landed: Tier A.

### S3 · Delete the dormant backends and inert vendored surface; initialize the gitlinks

  * The Lean target and its handwritten support are the row that mattered: a dormant target's support goes stale where no typechecker can see it, which c2 found and c4 found again, and deleting the target removes the class permanently. Rocq and SMT stay. With it went `handwritten_support/hgen/` M0.7 booked rather than deleted, `model/.github/` I8 measured as never having run, `model/os-boot/` and `model/doc/`, and `model/test/.gitignore`.
  * **A deletion is not an edit**, which is why it does not meet I4's and I8's refusal of permanent vendored divergence: it leaves nothing to reconcile at the next pin, M0.18's own ground for deleting `encoding.h` rather than curating it.
  * **The Lem, Isabelle and HOL4 targets go with Lean rather than beside it**, which is the one place the item's own list was wrong: `riscv_extras.lem`, `Holmakefile.in` and `ROOT.in` were live inputs to those three blocks of [model/model/CMakeLists.txt](../model/model/CMakeLists.txt), so *unreferenced* was true of `riscv_extras_fdext.lem` alone. Rocq keeps `riscv_extras.v` and the `build_rocq_common` target [rocq-sail-riscv.opam.in](../model/rocq-sail-riscv.opam.in) builds, and that file drops `sail_lean_backend` and `sail_lem_backend` with the two it no longer needs.
  * **`model/test/` beyond `unit_tests/` is refused, and the refusal is measured.** That tree's `ENABLE_RISCV_TESTS` block is the only thing that downloads the suite [tools/run.py model](../tools/vos/cli/model.py) configures with `-DENABLE_RISCV_TESTS=TRUE` and that `sweep`, `trace-diff` and a new lane's seeding then glob out of `test/*/riscv-tests`, so deleting it takes the 199-program profile sweep with it. What went is `test/.gitignore`, whose one entry named a JUnit file only `ci.yml` ever wrote. `model/dependencies/` is refused on the item's own ground, verified per member: CLI11 in `cli_options.cpp`, elfio in `elf_loader.cpp`, jsoncons in `config_utils.cpp`, softfloat in `riscv_softfloat.cpp` and the surviving vector floating point, and asio in the gdbserver and in S11's socket layer.
  * **One reference out of the deleted tree runs at configure rather than at build**, which is the reason a deletion still needs the reference sweep: [model/cmake/packaging.cmake](../model/cmake/packaging.cmake) gzipped `doc/ChangeLog.md` through `file(ARCHIVE_CREATE)`, which cmake evaluates while configuring, so `model/doc/` could not go without that rule going in the same act. The failure would have been every lane's `configure` rather than one dormant target.
  * **What the deletion costs the checker is half a percent, where M0.18's cost a sixth, and the difference is what the window is sized for.** `corpus.is_model_citation_path` admits by kind, and the kinds a prune deletes are almost all outside it, so the window falls from **205 files and 1,696,424 bytes to 203 and 1,687,676**, only `Holmakefile.in` and `ROOT.in` being members, while K-63's own figure is **unchanged at 1,614 citations across 91 files**. `MODEL_FACTS` does not move, none of its ten members being a candidate. A window admitting by kind is sized for what a curation edits and not for what a prune deletes.
  * **The residue is named rather than swept**, because reaching it is an edit and not a deletion: six `val` declarations in [prelude/prelude.sail](../model/model/prelude/prelude.sail) carry `lean:` extern names, [postlude/step.sail](../model/model/postlude/step.sail) and [riscv.sail_project](../model/model/riscv.sail_project) each explain a construct by a Lean-backend limitation, and [sys/platform.sail](../model/model/sys/platform.sail) carries nine `FIXME: Redundant zero_extend currently required by Lem backend`. The last is not only a comment, the C++ backend compiling the `zero_extend` calls it annotates.
  * **The gitlinks are the cheap half and the one that was blocking most**, and what they needed was one command: five of the imported cores R1b and R1c reach through, `cva6-cheri`, `ibex`, `axi-cheri-tagcontroller`, `mocha` and `cheriot-ibex`, are initialized. Three stay uninitialized because nothing open reaches them: `cheri-compressed-cap` and `qemu` belong to the struck M2, and `llvm-project` to M1.4 behind the M8a gate. `opentitan` stays uninitialized and the elaboration reaches it: [`tools/run.py rtl elaborate`](../tools/vos/cli/rtl.py) puts its primitive packages at the head of the imported core's file list, and refuses by name while that gitlink is absent.
  * Net change: 6,930 lines and 899,359 bytes removed across 61 files. Fifty-three are deleted outright and carry 6,692 of the removals; the other eight are the files that named a deleted target, tree or link.
  * Exit evidence: build green; **ctest unmoved at 2 of 2**; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **96 properties**; the profile sweep unmoved at **199 refusals of 199**; the differential corpus green at **26 of 26** with 245 checks over 8,830 records at manifest version 9; `model.py devicetree` at 5,717 bytes; `model.py reference` green; the proof gate closing **104 axiom-free constants**; the checker green on all **81 rules** with every mutant killed and the registry covered; and both type checkers clean.
  * Three claims elsewhere this item falsifies, repaired at their own sites rather than restated here: M0.18's `model/.github/` finding and I8's CI-workflow finding both rested on a tree this item deletes, and M0.8b's reading of the type-level configuration named four prover lanes where two now exist.

### S4 · Measure Sail emulator throughput against the M2 gate

  * **The gate demanded a measurement and stated nothing to measure against, so the criterion is authored here and said to be authored.** M2's entry condition asks whether Sail is *sufficient through M6* and its trigger sentence asks whether S4 reads it *insufficient through M6*, and no register entry, no profile line and no contract states a kIPS floor or a boot-time ceiling for the development vehicle. R-18-004c is the nearest thing and it points the other way, its acceptance putting the emulator outside timed obligations by deciding completeness and footprint and nothing timed. No throughput figure for any emulator existed in this tree before this item; the ones below are the first.
  * **The criterion, in two conjuncts, stated before the figures are read against it.** *Through M6* the emulator is sufficient if no loop this plan runs is emulator-bound, which is decidable today because every such loop is already in the tree. *Past M6* the question is the composed boot, and the ceiling taken is upstream's own rather than a preference: `sail-riscv`'s README prices its Linux boot at about four minutes at 300 KIPS with tracing disabled, and its `os-boot/linux` target prices reaching userspace at order 2 × 10<sup>8</sup> instructions and about ten minutes. Those are the boot times this interpreter's own community already lives with, so 240 s untraced and 600 s traced are the ceilings, and at a measured rate they are an instruction budget rather than a rate floor, which is the form the question actually has: what is unknown is the image, not the machine.
  * **Nothing in the tree can carry the figure, so two programs were authored and neither is tracked.** The 199 `riscv-tests` are refused 199 of 199 on the frozen profile, every one addressing memory through an integer base register; the 26 differential-corpus members run 120 to 1,996 records and are startup-dominated; and the composed image does not exist until M7.1. So a twelve-instruction integer loop and a twelve-instruction loop with four capability memory operations in its body were written in the frozen dialect and assembled by [run.py model asm](../tools/vos/asm.py), retiring 24,000,017 and 24,000,020 instructions respectively to a clean HTIF exit. Neither joins [the differential corpus](differential-corpus.md), which is a membership K-50 and K-51 hold: a benchmark decides nothing about the model's answers and would be a corpus member that no check reads.
  * **The measurement, three runs an arm, on the emulator this repository's own tools build.** `sail_riscv_sim --show-times` reports initialization, execution, retired instructions and kIPS ([riscv_sim.cpp](../model/c_emulator/riscv_sim.cpp)); initialization reported **0 ms in all twelve runs** against executions of 60 to 335 seconds, so every figure below is execution and the startup that made a small program's rate meaningless is out of the measurement entirely. Untraced the integer loop runs **97, 107 and 95 kIPS** and the capability loop **71, 79 and 78**; under `--trace-commit` at 6,000,000 instructions the integer loop runs **76, 73 and 100** and the capability loop **68, 67 and 66**. Model revision `d359483-dirty` ([run.py model reference](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)), Sail 0.20.2, upstream release 0.13.1, clang 21.1.8 at `RelWithDebInfo`, on a twelve-core Snapdragon X Elite X1E78100 under WSL. The spread is the host's: the tightest arm's three runs span 3% and the loosest 37%, and the ruling below is taken on the slowest run of each arm rather than on a median.
  * **The rate is a third of upstream's and neither reason is a defect.** Upstream's 300 KIPS is the plain `sail-riscv` C emulator; this one is capability-widened, so every fetch goes through PCC's bounds and permissions and every load and store through a capability check and the tag plane, and the repository builds it `RelWithDebInfo` at `-O2` rather than `Release` at `-O3`, the `--fast` profile differing from the canonical one only by dropping `-g`. So no build here is an `-O3` build, and the figure is the emulator the tools actually produce rather than the fastest one they could.
  * **The ruling: the strike stands, and the first conjunct decides it outright.** No loop before M7.1 is emulator-bound. The RVFI-DII rig is socket-bound at 1,022 instructions in 0.66 s and 500,000 generated words in 73.8 s (S11), which is 1.5 and 6.8 kIPS against the emulator's own floor of 66, so the rig waits on the socket by between ten and forty times; the whole differential corpus is 8,830 records, a seventh of a second of emulation against a process launch; and the profile sweep refuses 199 of 199 and emulates almost nothing. Reinstating M2.1 through M2.4 would spend 64.5 h of class-X work to accelerate a loop the emulator is not the bottleneck of.
  * **The second conjunct is a projection and this states it as one.** At the slowest untraced arm, 71 kIPS, the 240 s ceiling is a budget of **1.7 × 10<sup>7</sup> retired instructions** for the composed image's boot, about a twelfth of Linux's 2 × 10<sup>8</sup> to userspace; the traced ceiling at 66 kIPS is 3.96 × 10<sup>7</sup> and does not bind. Nothing in this tree sizes that boot, and the four things that dominate a Linux boot are the four the composed image does not do: no device probe over a general driver model, no dynamic relocation or linking, no initial ramdisk to unpack, and no page cache to warm. **M7.1 is what replaces this projection with a measurement**, and it is the one act that can reopen M2.
  * **What binds the traced arm is bytes rather than time.** Tracing costs about a fifth of the rate and not an order of magnitude, but the commit trace runs **57.6 bytes a retired instruction** on the integer loop and **67.0** on the capability one, 345,888,911 and 401,888,795 bytes per 6,000,000 instructions. A boot at the budget above therefore writes on the order of **1.1 GB** of trace per run, which is the cost a CI arm pays and the daily driver does not, and it is a storage figure rather than a throughput one.
  * Seven findings.
    * **The M2 gate carries no threshold at all.** Its entry condition and its trigger sentence both turn on *sufficient through M6* and neither states a number, and no artifact in the repository states one for it to mean. Closed at the gate by this item stating the criterion it rules against; the register half is the next finding.
    * **No register entry owns a throughput floor or a boot-time ceiling for the development vehicle.** Every `throughput` obligation in the register is on shipped hardware, and R-18-004c puts the emulator outside timed obligations outright, so the gap is a gap rather than an oversight. Reported and not closed: a register act reruns the review gate.
    * **No workload short of the composed boot can carry the figure, and none can before M7.1.** The refusal is total on `riscv-tests`, the corpus members are startup-dominated at 120 to 1,996 records, and the rig is socket-bound. Any later item wanting a throughput figure authors its own program or waits for M7.1.
    * **The commit trace's cost is storage rather than speed.** Twenty percent of the rate against 57.6 to 67.0 bytes a retired instruction, which is what makes the CI arm's constraint disk and the daily driver's constraint neither.
    * **A lane's emulator is stamped `-dirty` whatever the lane's state is.** [The configure line](../tools/vos/cli/model.py) exports `GIT_DIR` to the child so that `git describe` finds the lane's commit, which is I7's finding answered, but it exports no `GIT_WORK_TREE`, so git takes the child's working directory as the tree and reads every tracked file as deleted. The commit is right and the flag is noise that would mask a genuinely edited lane. Reported and not closed.
    * **Three commands the table declares host-capable could not run on the host.** `config-keys`, `validate-config` and `asm` were in the `model` command's `host_ok` set, and `model`'s own `main` called `env.load()` before dispatch, which refuses on `win32`, so the declaration was unreachable and an assembly on the host was a hop into the guest. Reported and not closed here; M1.4′ has since answered it, the environment reader taking a host-lane arm and the dispatch asking the entry point's own table which subcommands need the guest.
    * **The merged register file makes an ABI integer name an authority destroyer, and the failure surfaces millions of instructions late.** `s0` is `x8` and `c8` is where every program here parks the root data capability, so a loop counter named `s0` clears that authority and the program runs correctly to its exit store and then traps there. The corpus header warns about `c1` for the same reason and nothing warns about `c8`.
  * Exit evidence: the twelve runs above, taken against model revision `d359483-dirty`; build green; **ctest at 2 of 2**; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **96 properties**; the profile sweep unmoved at **199 refusals of 199**; the differential corpus green at **26 of 26** with 245 checks over 8,830 records at manifest version 9; `model reference` green and `model devicetree` at 5,717 bytes; the proof gate closing **180 constants**; the checker green on all **84 rules** with every one of **94 mutants** killed, the repair path holding and the registry covered; both type checkers clean; and the co-read ledger unmoved at **1,384 pairs**, this item touching no register entry and no prose span.
  * Landed: Tier A.

### S7 · Reconnaissance passes on R1b, M4.4, M6.2 and M2.3

  * Four passes in M1.2's shape, each grounded in a measurement or a quoted licence file rather than a reading, and each refuted from two lenses afterwards, on grounding and on completeness. The refutations bit: one scout claim truncated the requirement it declared satisfied, one cited an entry it never located, and one presented an address-map gap as uncovered where R1c's cell already books it. What survives is below; what did not is struck from it.
  * **The kernel-C read is the result that re-prices three cells, and it forecloses the start-from.** `CHERI-Alliance/CHERI-seL4`'s tree is multi-licence by SPDX tag and the kernel half is **GPL-2.0-only**: 733 of 1,045 tracked files carry the tag, `src/` at 313 of 324 and `include/` at 297 of 311. The CHERI work is contributed **under** that licence rather than beside it, the RISC-V boot, trap and vspace paths each carrying a Capabilities Limited copyright line above a GPL-2.0-only tag. seL4's syscall note exempts user-level code that *calls* the kernel and not code taken *from* it, so it does not reach here, and §6 of the GPL text is what refuses the composition with this repository's Apache-2.0 content rather than taste. **M4.1b is now a decision with its read done rather than a read**, and M4.4, M6.3a and M6.5a are priced as class I against an arm that is not available.
  * **The CVA6 question has an answer with operands, and the operands point three ways.** The caches are **60.1% of the curated netlist by cell instance** and 44.3% of it is RAM macro, so deleting A-09 and A-10 removes most of what elaborates and takes the AXI shim with it; what survives is **108 cell instances over 37 module kinds**, and **67 of those 108 are the bit-manipulation extension's population-count forest**, leaving roughly **41 structural instances** of actual pipeline. Against that, the register has already made the call: R-15-092's authored list is the DMA fabric, the TDM NoC and the fixed-function sequencers and the scalar core is not on it, R-15-090 puts CHERI-CVA6 on the imported side with an evidence path rather than a proof, and the absence contract splits discharge on that same line. The two alternatives that are not *author it* are unavailable, both pinned Ibex trees being RV32 with R-15-005 forbidding CHERIoT's format by name and the CTSRD Bluespec cores not pinned at all; and what Kami and Kôika ship as example cores is RV32, in-order and cache-free, which is the artifact an authored arm would start from.
  * **What the pass found against the delta and the provenance record, each with a consequence.** The **static-only prediction rule is already what the curated configuration does**, which three artifacts state the opposite of and which retires one of the four items §4 books as authoring work that no parameter reaches. **There are two store-holding structures in the curated netlist and the flush-set completeness claim names one**, the second being a fully-associative write buffer that forwards to loads, so that claim is false of today's netlist and becomes true exactly when A-10 goes. **The store buffer's depths are literals in a package rather than fields of the configuration record**, and the `fence.t` drain bound depends on them, which puts the delta's own second kind on the structure the flush set has one member of. And **the delta enumerates zero sites for the instruction cache and zero for any front-end file**, which is where two of R1b's four named items live, so R1b's midpoint is priced against three of the delta's sections and its own cell says which obligations that leaves out.
  * **The two hardware-DSL licences are re-read at their own files and confirm the record exactly.** Kami is MIT with no reciprocity; Kôika's file is the bare LGPL-2.1 text whose only *any later version* language is the licence's own §13, so **the only-versus-or-later question is undecided at the file**, which is the finding rather than an answer and which is what the recorded preference already rests on. Nothing is incorporated here, so no obligation attaches and the reading is a re-take rather than the milestone read.
  * Seven things the pass could not measure are named rather than estimated, the load-bearing one being **the cell count of a cache-free elaboration**: no parameter removes A-09 or A-10, so the only way to take that figure is to author the flat-SRAM replacement and elaborate against it, which is R1b's own work.
  * Landed: Tier A.

### S8 · Extract and index the findings register

  * [The findings register](findings-register.md) indexes every finding this plan records: an id, one of four types, the item that raised it, and one of three dispositions, with `· Restates:` where a fact is met twice. **The note owns the finding and the register owns the index over it**, so an entry names what was found as far as a reader recognizes it and carries no figure the note carries.
  * **The extraction is exhaustive and the stated figure was short both ways.** The notes count their findings and state more in prose besides, and the 158 this item quoted is exactly the counted findings *outside* the build-loop instruments section while the 31 blocks is one short of the 32 that sum to it. Both figures are now the register's own, recomputed under `--fix` rather than maintained by care.
  * **K-82** in a new [findings](../tools/vos/checks/findings.py) group, over the new [vos/findings.py](../tools/vos/findings.py), holds the index over the notes in both directions: every counted finding has one entry, every entry names an item the plan carries, and a block's size is counted from its bullets with its own count word held against them, because a note that came to lie about itself would otherwise carry a register agreeing with the lie. The prose half is a reading, marked `in prose`, held only at its item existing, and declared in the register rather than left to be met.
  * **The S1 feed is measured rather than asserted.** The counted half independently rediscovers nine of S1's fourteen owed acts; the other five are stated outside the shape a note declares, three in open items' cells and one in M3.1's *four register questions* bullet, which never uses the word. The repair for that is on the plan's side, to state a reported gap in the note's declared shape.
  * **Two open owed acts S1 does not carry are reported and not closed**: R-11-023's criterion enumerating the admission check's inputs more narrowly than R-11-006 and R-11-009 state it, and the sentence [cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) states about its own exponent range, which the wrap domain the `setCapBounds` finding names falsifies.
  * **The rule bit on its first run against a moved tree**, M0.19's and M3.6a's notes having landed after the register was built, and both are indexed where the plan puts them. Its repairable seed computes the register's own size rather than pinning it: a literal there is a derived fact hand-maintained in the selftest, stale on the next item that lands a finding rather than on any change to the rule.
  * Net change: 1,463 lines added and 1 removed across eight files, four of them new.
  * Exit evidence: the checker green with K-82 new, every mutant killed and the registry covered; the selftest's repair path at nine seeded figures against eight; the floors group's uncounted enumerations at 51 against 49; `check.py --fix` rewriting nothing on the landed tree; the tools' own suite green at 30 modules and 269 cases against 29 and 259; the co-read ledger unmoved at 1,377 pairs, no register entry or prose span having been touched; and both type checkers clean. No WSL run is in this item's evidence: nothing here reads `model/`, the RTL, or a toolchain.
  * Landed: Tier A.

### S9 · Make the lane host-portable, and retire S14's first blocker

  * **The posture call is portability rather than a move: WSL stays the daily driver, and a dedicated box or a hosted Action instantiates the same lane whenever one is run there.** What decides it is not the environment findings, which are an irritation rather than an argument, but that S14 is unbuildable without *something*: I8 declined push CI on two grounds and the first is that no stock image carries the opam switches this lane declares, the prepended z3, or the interpreter floor. A host that *is* this lane carries all three by construction, and so does any host a provisioner stands up, which is the weaker premise that buys the same thing without a migration.
  * **The deliverable is [tools/run.py provision](../tools/vos/cli/provision.py), the lane stated as an executable fact list.** Nineteen rows, one per switch, pin, checker, distribution package and layout invariant, each naming the loop that wants the fact, the artifact that owns it, a probe that reports what is actually there, and, where this tree states one, the command that would put it there. The default reports and changes nothing, `--apply` runs exactly the plan and re-probes, and `--only gate|toolchain` narrows and names the rows it did not decide about, which is S13b's rule for a narrowed run read one instrument over. It is declared `lane="guest"`, so a host invocation hops exactly as `model` and `rtl` do. Native rather than containerized is what makes it architecture-agnostic, and the measurement below is what makes that concrete rather than asserted.
  * **Every version, switch name and pin in the table is imported from the module that fixes it**, so the file is rows and not a second copy of the tree's pins, and the constants moved to their owners in the same act: `SAIL_SWITCH`, `SAIL_VERSION`, `SAIL_INSTALL`, `Z3_VERSION`, `Z3_PREFIX`, `Z3_DISTRIBUTION`, `ROCQ_VERSION`, `ROCQ_INSTALL`, `BUILD_ROOT`, `TYPECHECK_CACHE` and a public `build_root()` in [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py), with `ROCQ_SWITCH` now composed from its own pin, and `quickchick.py`'s install recipe made public. **A recipe is argv and the sentence a person pastes is composed from it** by `env.install_line`, so the refusal that prints a recipe and the tool that runs it cannot come to disagree; written the other way round they are two copies and only one is ever run.
  * **The one figure written as a literal is the interpreter floor, and K-75's window holds it.** A TOML setting is not importable, so this tool either restates `ty.toml`'s figure or cannot probe the running interpreter at all. That is one appended row in `_FLOOR_SITES` and one registry row, with no new mutant: the existing `py314` seed still applies and the new site is fail-closed by K-75's own reading, both a moved pattern and a wrong figure being findings there. What stands in for a mutant at the new site is a behavioural case rather than prose, [test_checks.py](../tools/tests/test_checks.py) building a sandbox whose provisioned floor disagrees with `ty.toml`'s and asserting K-75 names that site, with its agreeing twin beside it. **K-67's window does not widen**: the provisioner composes `ty==` and `ruff==` from `typecheck.py`'s own constants, and a pin composed from the constant that fixes it is not a restatement of it. So the pins and the floor cost two edits rather than three plus a mutant, and the rule id S14 was priced against is unspent.
  * **A row installs only what an artifact here states as a command.** Three routes have owners this tree cannot hand over as an argument vector: uv's own installation, the creation of an opam root, and the CertiRocq oracle switch, whose recipe is prose in [wasm-oracle/README.md](../tools/wasm-oracle/README.md) for a person to read. Inventing a command for one of them would be the unowned derived fact the working rules refuse, and it is the same discipline that keeps the distribution-package rows to packages with a named consumer already in this tree. **The three routes are not three rows**, which the findings block below states as the measurement it is: two of them are rows, the third is a route nothing here probes, and two rows carry no command for reasons that are neither.
  * **It is the substrate every other arm needs anyway**, which is what selects it. A flake, an image and a workflow each need the same fact list, so the provisioner is what each of them would have to contain. The arms not taken: a Nix flake is hermetic and adds a fourth toolchain to a repository that holds two languages and one Python tools lane, and risks the pinned Sail, Rocq and z3 points not all existing at one nixpkgs revision, which is the fidelity those pins exist for; a multi-arch image published from here retires the arm64 exclusion outright and wants a container runtime on WSL, which I8 measured as absent and M1.5 already declined on this box.
  * **Two decisions inside the deliverable, each with what it forfeits.** The lane is *guest*, which forfeits a one-command answer to whether the Windows host is still the lane: the host's own interpreter, uv and two checkers stay `run.py typecheck`'s to decide, and the answer is split across two commands rather than one. Declared host instead, it could not reach the switches that are the whole reason the item exists. And the fact list is *one tier with a narrowing* rather than two lists, which forfeits a pre-decided split between a quick arm and a canonical one; `--only` makes the question askable and nothing answers it, S14 having declined the workflow that would have, so the narrowing is what stands in for a decided split.
  * **What it does not retire is stated rather than absorbed.** I1's two standing clauses are a setting in `%USERPROFILE%\.wslconfig`, global to every distribution, and a working posture on a person's editor; no provisioner reaches either, and a run prints both as not reached and counts neither into its verdict. The per-configure tax and the cross-lane ccache miss are properties of the `/mnt/c` layout I1 measured and are untouched. Those stay open on WSL **by choice** rather than by oversight, which is what the posture costs and what a move would have bought.
  * Ten findings, and the first is the one that turns the architecture-agnostic claim from an argument into a measurement.
    * **The pinned solver's acquisition route was stated in no artifact and is now measured.** `/root/z3-5.1.0` is an unpacked `z3-solver` wheel, tagged `manylinux_2_38_aarch64`, and `uv pip install --target` of that distribution reproduces its `bin/z3` byte for byte at 20,515,824 bytes on one sha256. Wheels for that distribution are published for both architectures, so the pinned solver needs neither a source build nor an amd64-only image, which is precisely what I8's exclusion was about. The provisioner is the route's owner now, and the wheel version is composed from `env.Z3_VERSION` rather than typed beside it.
    * **No register entry owns the development lane's toolchain at all.** The register books reproducibility of the shipped placement map and of the certifying toolchain, and nothing about the environment the golden model is built in. So the provisioner discharges no requirement and creates none, and this item stays a build-loop instrument like I0 through I12. Reported and not closed: a register act reruns the R-05-150 review gate and is the author's.
    * **Verilator's pin was restated in three places and no rule held it, and I15 has since answered it**, [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md)'s development-tools row, its own Verilator paragraph, and `rtl.py`'s `VERILATOR_PIN`. K-81 holds commit pins alone and K-67 the two checker pins alone, so a version pin restated in a document and in code was unheld. The provisioner added no fourth copy, importing the constant, so the gap was recorded as a candidate rule rather than closed on an id that act did not hold. **K-97** is that rule and it reads the constant as the *owner* rather than as a third restatement, which puts the plan's own RTL-track cell in the window beside the record's two sites.
    * **No artifact declared the distribution-package set the model build needs**, and the provisioner becomes its owner, which was a fact nothing held. What stood in for the rule was the discipline the rows are written under: every row names a consumer already in this tree, `find_package(GMP)` for libgmp-dev, `_compiler_args` for clang, `_ccache_args` for ccache, cmake's `git describe` for git, and a row that cannot name one is not written. Recorded as a candidate rule, and **I15 has since written half of it**: **K-100** holds that discipline row by row, every path against the git index and every symbol and `run.py` command against the file or the entry point that would carry it. Whether the set is *complete* is the other half and stays a fact nothing holds, no artifact here declaring one.
    * **Three provisioning routes have owners this tree cannot state as argv, and they are not the rows that plan nothing.** The routes are uv's own installation, the creation of an opam root, and the CertiRocq switch; each is reachable prose and none is a command a tool can run. Two of them are rows, `uv` and the oracle switch, and the third is a route no row probes at all, the `opam` row installing the binary by `apt-get install -y opam` while nothing here asks whether a root exists. So four of the nineteen rows report and plan nothing rather than three: those two, the interpreter floor, which is the interpreter taking the probe and so has nothing a command of its own could replace, and the memo-cache invariant, whose repair is to give a lane a copy and never to delete a warm cache. The difference between an empty plan because there is nothing to do and one because there is nothing this tool may do is carried by the verdict rather than by the plan.
    * **The install arm is unexercised on this box, every fact being satisfied already**, so what the tests hold is the plan and never the execution: an injected table drives the probe-to-command mapping, and idempotence is held as the property that a satisfied probe yields an empty plan. Recorded rather than papered over, because a claim of idempotence over an arm that never ran would be exactly the check that decides nothing.
    * **I7's lane defect reaches a second site, and it is the corpus parse rather than cmake.** [vos/corpus.py](../tools/vos/corpus.py) reads the git index without the `GIT_DIR` translation `env.git_dir` already provides, so inside the guest on a *linked worktree* `python3 tools/check.py` ends in a traceback naming `not a git repository` rather than in a verdict, and three `test_figures` cases fail the same way; the primary worktree is green on both lanes. Reported and not closed: that parse is the one every rule reads, and this item's own guest case reaches `env.git_dir` rather than repeating it.
    * **I8's ground names two opam switches and this lane now carries four**, M1.5's CertiRocq switch and S13a's QuickChick switch having landed after that sentence was written. Its argument is unaffected and stronger rather than weaker, no stock image carrying any of the four, so I8's cell is left standing rather than repaired at its own site.
    * **Every figure any document states about the fact table was a hand-copy, and three of them were wrong on the day they landed.** The row count, the rows carrying a command, the rows carrying none and the opam switches among them are each read off `FACTS`, restated across this cell, S14's and [tools/README.md](../tools/README.md), and owned by nothing: K-75 holds one literal in that file and no rule computed a count over the table. Three of the stated figures disagreed with it, in three sentences across two documents, with every gate green. Closed rather than reported, and by the cheapest stated act rather than by a rule: the four counts are quantities in [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py) and eight rows in K-24's claim table, computed off `FACTS` by import rather than by a second parse of the file, so `--fix` rewrites them and the rule id this item was priced against stays unspent. Registering a quantity is not free of the rest of the group, and the price is stated rather than discovered later: K-26's alternation is built over every quantity's *value*, so registering the rows that carry a command would have made an unrelated fifteen elsewhere in the plan a loose figure, and the documents state the complement instead, one figure rather than two. The same reading found one more hand-copy, [test_checks.py](../tools/tests/test_checks.py)'s docstring pricing a fixture against sixty-seven rules where the registry now carries far more; that one is made non-numeric rather than given an owner, an aside in a docstring being no place for a figure a rule would then have to hold.
    * **A lane can block its own merge from outside its worktree, and no gate it runs can see that it has.** A zero-byte untracked `tools/vos/cli/provision.py` stood at this tool's own path in the primary worktree, where `git merge` refuses to overwrite an untracked file with a tracked one, and where the selftest's sandbox, which reads untracked files against `check.py`'s index, would take it as an empty source under `tools/`. The path is clear at this landing, the primary's `git status --porcelain` answering empty, so nothing is owed; what stands is that every gate in this tree decides about the worktree it is run in and none of them about the one a branch merges into.
  * Net change: 1,189 lines added and 80 removed across fourteen files, two of them new, and none under `model/` or `rtl/`.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run, exiting 0, and `check --fix` over the landed tree rewriting nothing and moving no byte; the checker green on all **86** registered rules, with K-24 at **107** asserted counts against 99, K-26 finding no unheld restatement of one, and K-75 reading **eight** floor sites against seven; the selftest killing all **97** mutants with the repair path at ten seeded figures and the registry covered both ways; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; the tools' own suite green at **34 modules** against 33 and **309 cases**, the provisioner's own at 9 on the host and the checks module at 10 against 7; and the co-read ledger unmoved at **1,384 pairs**, no register entry or prose span having been touched. K-75 is shown to bite at the new site by a case that reruns rather than by a seed taken once: a sandbox whose provisioned floor reads 3.13 against a `ty.toml` fixing 3.14 reports `tools/vos/cli/provision.py's provisioned floor states 3.13, tools/ty.toml fixes 3.14`, its agreeing twin names that site in nothing, and a provisioner with no floor at all reports the site missing rather than dropping it. The wall time the landing recorded is not restated: this box is on battery, where its clock is a fraction of the mains figure and a second reading would compare two machines. The guest half is not re-measured either and is not carried as though it were: WSL's own service is down here, `wsl -e echo` exiting 255 with `Wsl/Service/E_UNEXPECTED`, and restarting it would kill the builds the sibling lanes are holding. What makes that a gap rather than a hole is that no executable line of the provisioner moved: the diff is its docstring and one comment, so no row, probe, plan or verdict this repair touched is one the guest decides. No model build and no evidence sweep is in this evidence and none is owed: nothing here reads `model/`, the RTL sources or a proof.
  * Landed: Tier A. Tier B's fourth condition is met in more than part and still not in whole: K-75 holds the one figure this act restated and K-24 the counts any document states about its table, while the two facts the act *created*, the distribution-package set and the z3 acquisition route, are held by nothing. So rules hold part of what the landing created and a full read is what the rest is owed. Inventing a rule to hold a package list would be worse than saying so, which is I12's and S13b's precedent exactly.

### S10 · Shard the hot files for ten-lane operation

  * Both halves are landed and each carries its own note.

#### S10a · Shard `counts.py` by rule family

  * Fifteen rules over ten modules, all still group `counts` and every id unmoved. [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py) holds the claim table, the quantity table every claim is held against, and the run; the nine `counts_*.py` families beside it are one per artifact their rules read, the tag plane (K-54), the owned figures (K-69), the capability format (K-79), the welded block (K-57), the freeze delta (K-70), the core classes (K-60), the model window (K-63, K-66), the shipped configurations (K-65, K-78), and the two register fields the first and fourth are each arithmetic over.
  * **The family split is the set of rules reading one artifact, taken from the registry rows rather than from line count**, which is what makes the two deliberate pairings legible: K-63 and K-66 share the model-citation window and splitting them would open it twice, and K-65 and K-78 share the shipped-configuration parse while each states in its own docstring what the other structurally cannot see. No helper is duplicated, a fact having one owner, and `counts_fields.py` exists because the two field patterns are read by two families and the package root cannot hold them, `counts.py` importing the families so a family importing back would be a cycle.
  * **The run order is preserved and said to be load-bearing rather than alphabetical**, at the docstring and at the call site: the claims resolve against the quantity table first, the window is opened once, and the floors group prices what all of them recorded. That is the finding this tree has already made twice, that a validator group placed after another inherits what that one already refuses.
  * **The prescribed package shape is refused on a measurement rather than a preference, and the refusal is the item's sharpest result.** `check-selftest.py`'s K-46 mutant opens `tools/vos/checks/counts.py` **as a file** and seeds a defect into the `CLAIMS` table inside it, so a directory at that path makes the sandbox read raise rather than return false. The path therefore stays a file and keeps the claim table, and the families are siblings rather than children, which is the shape this item's own text asks for. It also costs less: `meta.carried_rules()` globs `checks/*.py` and finds every id unmoved, where a subdirectory would have forced a `glob` to `rglob` edit inside K-00's own reach.
  * **Invisibility is held by measurement rather than by claim.** Fifty-one constants and twenty-two function bodies compared against the pre-shard module agree byte for byte but for two intended comment lines and one continuation indent; a run's output moves at **two lines**, the marks group's two file censuses, which the nine new modules join, with every finding identical on a seeded run; and `tools/check-rules.md` and `tools/run.py selftest` are **byte-identical across the change**, which is what keeps a rule registered by its id and its group rather than by the file carrying it.
  * One observation recorded and deliberately not acted on: under `--fix`, K-26's held spans are recomputed over every claim for a rewritten file including claims whose quantity is dead, where the unrepaired path takes only the spans the resolve loop populated, so a loose-figure finding at a dead claim's site is suppressed under `--fix` and reported without it. Pre-existing, reachable only on a run already red from K-24, and untouched here.
  * Net change: 577 lines in the root against 1,792, 2,006 across ten modules, the 12% growth being nine licence headers, nine docstrings and nine import blocks.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run and green again under `--fix`, which rewrote nothing; the selftest killing all **90 mutants** with the registry covered both directions; the tools' own suite green at 24 modules; and both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.74 and ruff 0.16.4.
  * Landed: Tier B, held by **K-00**, which carries every one of the fifteen ids across the shard in both directions.

#### S10b · Move the per-item completion notes to the completion log

  * **The notes are moved whole and the checklist keeps one line per landed item.** Every landed or struck item's completion note now sits in this file under a heading spelling its label, verbatim and at the indentation it had, and the item in the checklist keeps its header line, its estimate cell, one summary line stating no figure, and a link to the entry here. The current summary's lane-by-lane narrative moved with them, into the section at the head of this file, and the summary keeps the completed roster, the figures the checker recomputes and the critical chain.
  * **One file rather than the `docs/items/` directory the item named, and the ground is what reads the notes.** Three rules read a note's content, K-82 for its findings blocks, K-84 for its landing declaration and K-24 for the claims a note states, and each reads a document by name, so a directory of per-item files would have put a corpus walk inside each of them where one file puts one name. The conflict the item existed to remove is removed either way: a landing writes its note into a file whose only other edits are notes, and the checklist takes one line.
  * **What holds the move is a reading K-82 gained rather than a new rule.** The log's entries are held total against the plan's landed and struck items in both directions, so a landing whose note was never written is a finding and so is an entry whose item was struck or renamed under it; the checklist's link is what K-12 resolves; the mutant that proves the reading bites deletes an entry's heading and the cell's link together, so the case decides on K-82's own report and not on a dead fragment. `Landed:` lines are read in both documents, the claim sentences the notes state are read here, and the floors group carries the log's entry count.
  * **Two holds could not follow their sentences and were dropped rather than pointed at this file.** K-96 read the class-I outturn out of this item's own open cell and the pooled class-X fit out of S19's note, both as live arithmetic; a note is gate-time evidence, so a live figure inside one would be the one sentence in this file the tree rewrites, and both sentences stay as their gates measured them, with the two judged lines removed from the estimates group.
  * Three findings.
    * **A figure a checker rule holds live inside an item's cell loses its hold when the item lands**, the cell becoming a note this file keeps frozen, so an item whose cell carries a K-96 judged sentence or a K-24 claim is one whose landing has to move or drop the hold in the same act; this item's own cell and S19's note were the two.
    * **No landed item carried prose after its child items**, so every note was one contiguous span between its header line and the next item line, and the move was one span per item with nothing to attribute by hand.
    * **The fragment links inside the moved notes named two headings of the checklist**, the build-loop instruments section, which this file carries and the links now resolve inside, and the calibration record, which it does not and whose link was rewritten to point back at the checklist.
  * Net change: the checklist falls from 2,716 lines to 1,417 and this file is new; the tools' side is the findings parse reading a second document and holding the entry set, the landing reading and nine claim rows re-pointed at this file, two judged lines removed, one mutant added and the registry's two rows and its closing section saying so; the findings register's preamble names this file, and the working rules carry the move as a row and a bullet.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run with the tools' own suite beside them; the checker green on all **94** registered rules, K-82 reading an entry for each of the plan's **118** landed items beside the **562** findings the register indexes across 90 items, K-84 reading **52** landing declarations across the plan and this file, this one among them, and K-12 resolving every link; the selftest killing all **114** mutants against 113, the new one the deleted entry, with the registry covered both ways; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; `check.py --fix` run once for the arithmetic this landing moved and a second run moving no byte. No guest run is in this evidence and none is owed: nothing here reads `model/`, the RTL or a proof.
  * Landed: Tier A. It rewrites the checklist's conventions and its summary, retitles this item, and edits the findings register's preamble, so it is read whole; **K-82** holds the entry set this landing created and **K-12** the links, which is more of Tier B's fourth condition than most landings meet, and the read is still owed for the conventions.

### S11 · Join the TestRIG ecosystem over RVFI-DII

  * The rig is [tools/run.py testrig](../tools/vos/cli/testrig.py) over two new modules and one old one: [vos/rvfi.py](../tools/vos/rvfi.py) is the wire format and the projection from one of its packets onto the commit trace's records, [vos/vengine.py](../tools/vos/vengine.py) is the stream generator over the frozen dialect, the socket, four seeded defects and delta debugging, and adjudication stays [vos/trace.py](../tools/vos/trace.py)'s, so the rig and the corpus decide what a divergence is through one function. Four commands: `protocol` reads the wire off the codec on the host, `handshake`, `run` and `bridge` drive the emulator in the guest. [differential-corpus.md](differential-corpus.md) §9 is the contract, and §8 carries the four predicates CI holds.
  * **The protocol is read at its own source rather than guessed.** `CTSRD-CHERI/TestRIG` is pinned and read at its own licence file, which is BSD-2-Clause and permissive like every other row of the submodule table; the version-2 packet is **not** in that repository's own protocol document, which describes version 1 alone, and is read from the model's own [core/rvfi_dii_v2.sail](../model/model/core/rvfi_dii_v2.sail). **The socket needed nothing added**, which is what M0.12 preserved the plumbing for: the emulator negotiates version 2 on a port and retires an injected instruction, and no line of `model/` changed.
  * **This repository carries a dialect of the standard packet rather than an extension of it.** The M0.12 widening is *inside* the packet, the destination's tag being one bit of the Integer extension's padding and the access's tag one bit above the CHERI-widened masks. What does not fit is structural: a variable-length effect list for a block write, a fifth register file for the four capability registers, and a cause field where the packet's trap is a boolean. That is a different grammar and not a longer packet, and extending would fork the wire away from every other TestRIG implementation to buy fields the record stream already has. **Version 1 cannot carry the widening at all**, truncating the masks to their byte halves and so deleting the tag bit rather than clearing it.
  * Net change: 2,092 lines added and none removed across nine files, four of them new, and none of them under `model/`.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite at **30 modules**, the new one at **21 cases**; the model typechecking and building green with its bundled suite; the differential corpus at **26 of 26** with every digest matching; the legacy rig at **prefix 67 on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members**; and the rig itself over **1,307,963 generated instructions**, of which 1,215,700 in the no-defect arm across five templates agreed over 1,995,965 records, 500,000 uniformly random words agreed in 73.8 s, and **all four seeded defects were reported on 20 of 20 seeds each**, the counterexamples shrinking from 1,027 instructions to **1, 1, 5 and 7**. The packet-against-record bridge agrees on every template; on the random one it measures the format gap at **10,560 of 12,769 records the packet has no field for**.
  * **The second executor still does not exist and the rig says which one it drove.** M2's fork is struck and the RTL is R2's, so today it adjudicates the emulator against itself under a seeded defect, each written to need a witness most instructions do not carry, which is what makes the shrink a measurement rather than a demonstration. Its other arm needs no second executor at all: one run emits both dialects, the packets over the socket and the records into the trace log, and that is the only place §9's field-by-field meeting is measured rather than read. When R2 supplies the RTL's `rvfi` port it is this socket it connects to, and nothing on this side changes.
  * Seven findings, and the first two are why the rig works at all.
    * **A torn record reads as a divergence about a run that agreed.** The commit trace's default destination is `stdout`, block-buffered into a pipe, while the RVFI diagnostics go to an unbuffered `stderr`, and the end-of-trace acknowledgement prints verbosely whatever the trace flag says, its call site passing a comparison where every other passes the flag. On one descriptor a diagnostic lands inside a half-written record. The rig gives the trace a file of its own.
    * **Forty-seven milliseconds an instruction, and it is Nagle's algorithm on the implementation's socket.** A version-2 reply is two or three separate writes, the packet and each announced extension, and the second waits on an acknowledgement this side has nothing to piggyback on. Disabling it here does not reach the held write, which is the emulator's; quick acknowledgement, re-armed after every read, took 1,022 instructions from **49.1 s to 0.66 s**.
    * **The block operations stop the emulator rather than reporting something narrower.** The packet's write and read raise an internal error above sixteen bytes, so `cbo.zero`, `cbo.scrub`, `cloadtags` and `creclaim` abort a run under injection. A generated stream excludes them, which is §5's *the packet is not the transport* seen from the side that has to generate around it.
    * **The two emitters disagreed about a word whose low two bits are not `11`.** The profile excludes `C` and fixes ILEN at 32, but the fetch path still took upstream's compressed branch: the commit trace reported the sixteen-bit halfword the model decoded and the packet reported the whole injected word. Which was right was the model's question, and **M0.20 took it against the fetch**, deleting the length decision so that both emitters report the word whole. The generator spends no stream on encodings the profile does not have either way.
    * **The source half of the Integer extension is declared and never written**, both source addresses and their data always zero, which is vacuously legal under the packet's own rule and is what a comparison over the full field set would find first.
    * **The physical-address width is 64 and not 36**, so a commit memory record's address is sixteen hexadecimal digits: the 36-bit space is a property of the composition and not of the model's address type. The bridge measures the width off the records it is comparing rather than restating it.
    * **The drift this item would otherwise leave unheld is held by a rule**, K-85 in the differential group: every record kind §4's schema table declares appears in exactly one of §9's meeting table and its elision table, and that membership is the record set the projection and the view actually produce. Without it a schema-version bump drifts §9's tables away from the code in silence, which is the stale-claim shape every other rule here exists to catch.
  * Landed: Tier A.

### S12 · Read, pin and dispose the six unnamed start-froms

  * **Six pins, three declines, and every reading permissive.** Each was read at the files that state its terms (R-18-001a) and [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries the rows and the decompositions. Pinned: `mit-plv/rupicola` (MIT, carrying Bedrock2 as its own submodule, also MIT), `katamaran-project/katamaran` (BSD-2-Clause), `katamaran-project/sail-backend` (BSD-2-Clause, stated in its packaging rather than in a licence file), `logsem/cerise` and `logsem/cerisier` (BSD-3-Clause over an `extra/` carve-out whose own file is BSD-2-Clause), and `CTSRD-CHERI/sail-cheri-riscv-verif` (BSD-2-Clause). No fork of the M1.1a, M4.1a or M6.0a class arises on any of the six.
  * **The sixth states its terms in its packaging and in no licence file.** The Sail-to-µSail translator R-13-016's owed translation would ride carries `BSD-2-Clause` in `default.nix`, `nanosail.nix` and `monads.nix`, each over a derivation whose `src` is the tree itself and whose three `pname` values are exactly the three packages it ships, so the grant covers the whole of it. It carries no `LICENSE`, `LICENCE`, `COPYING` or `COPYRIGHT`, no forge-detected licence, and no `license:` field in any of its three generated opam files, that last being one omission rather than three: `dune-project` sets `(generate_opam_files true)` and carries no `(license ...)` stanza, which is the stanza dune propagates into that field. It is pinned so the edition is fixed, it may be copied and vendored on those terms, and a permissive grant reaches no further than the tool, so what it emits is this repository's. What the tree lacks is the notice a redistribution retains, which `dune-project`'s own authors field supplies.
  * **The call is taken: use the automated translation, and keep the hand translation as the fallback it always was.** A permissive grant stated first-party leaves nothing to wait on, so the licensor ask is a courtesy rather than a precondition and no schedule turns on it; it is still worth sending, a licence file with a copyright line being what a redistribution retains and what this tree otherwise composes for itself. **Containment is not an arm here**, containment answering a *restrictive* term by keeping an artifact out of what is conveyed and BSD-2-Clause restricting nothing that needs keeping out. **The reading rule is the durable half of this act** and it is [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md)'s: terms are read wherever an upstream states them, a search over licence filenames and the forge answering whether a licence file exists and never what terms the authors stated.
  * **What Rupicola covers against what §8 assigns it.** The covered subset is machine words, arrays and buffers, let-bound straight-line code, conditionals, bounded loops with hand-supplied invariants, inline lookup tables, stack allocation, and monadic nondeterminism and I/O, its authors stating outright that it is not a universal compiler and accepts a restricted input. §8's Lustre node is out of reach by language. **What M6.1a's Gallina state machine costs against that subset is M1.6's measurement and not this reading's**, and the two disagree: read from the paper the blockers are inductive datatypes and general recursion at a hand-written compilation lemma per constructor, and read construct by construct over the artifact they are neither, general recursion being at zero sites and the three inductives being nullary enumerations, while what does not cross is higher-order code at 73 of the file's 100 definitional commands. M1.6's cell carries the figures. **The target gap is not closed and this cell records it**: Bedrock2's verified compiler emits bare-metal position-independent RISC-V over the basic I extension with no purecap backend, so M1.6's *its target exists* is true of plain RV64 and false against R-18-002. The difference from the Rust route is size rather than kind, that backend being small and Coq-verified, and the CompCert-C/VST route stays the default.
  * **Two of the three declines are the plan's own claims repaired.** The CHERI Alliance line is external evidence [the version matrix](cheri-version-matrix.md) already places, its standalone repository archived and its CHERI work a branch of a `sail-riscv` fork; the Rocq build target this item attributed to it is documented in the README of `CTSRD-CHERI/sail-cheri-riscv` **at the commit this tree already pins**, which also names the properties repository, so both halves were reachable at editions already on the page and the pin the item implied is not owed. StkTokens is a discipline with no mechanization published under any repository, so R-13-017's citation stands as an idea that carries nothing, on M4.1a's own distinction.
  * **What the pinned properties buy over the model's own harness is a quantifier.** The `$[test]` functions assert at chosen concrete values and say so in their own header, where `sail-cheri-riscv-verif`'s `smt` target emits one SMT-LIB file per property and asks whether its negation is satisfiable, with `check_properties` putting the same questions through Isla. The Rocq target is the other axis, a prover-level object where `$[test]` is an emulator-level one.
  * **M3.4's NTT survey is half falsified and its conclusion is unmoved.** The artifact is a Zenodo deposit that is neither a fork nor a branch but a patch and a source directory applied over one pinned commit of `mit-plv/fiat-crypto`, and no licence file was found in it, so it is read and never copied, vendored or extracted from. **That standing rests on a licence-file search and is owed a re-reading against [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md)'s rule**, which is that terms are read wherever an upstream states them and that a deposit stating them in its packaging is one this reading would otherwise refuse on an absence it has not established. A repository does exist, contrary to the survey: the author's personal fork carries two NTT branches under that project's own election, both predating the published artifact by more than a year, and upstream carries no NTT branch, so *unmerged* stands. The disposition holds on a replaced ground.
  * **A register gap is reported and not closed.** R-13-016 names the Sail-to-µSail seam's faithfulness obligation and books no terms obligation beside it, so what a translation carries across that seam is read at the upstream rather than owed by an entry.
  * Exit evidence: six gitlinks staged and held by **K-81** in both directions, which now reads **18 upstream pins against the index and 29 restating sites**, against 12 and 23 before; the three host gates green in one run; the selftest killing every mutant over a covered registry; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.74 and ruff 0.16.4; and the tools' own suite green at 29 modules.
  * Landed: Tier A.

#### S13a · The three instruments

  * **The technique is an instrument rather than a method.** [tools/run.py oracle](../tools/vos/cli/oracle.py) is M2.1's and R1a's rig with the question taken out of it: a spec under [tools/oracle-specs/](../tools/oracle-specs/) names the model sources and, per line kind, the parameters, the domain that walks them, the Sail that calls the model and what to print, and the harness is emitted from it. A parameter's domain is `all`, `draw` or `table`, which is the exhaustive sweep, the sampled one, and the corners a sweep off entropy alone does not reach, and a probe may mix all three, R1a's decode sweep being `all, all, all, draw`. The vectors cross as **text** and the format is R1a's unchanged, so `rtl.py`'s census and comparison read it already. Two specs land: the capability format at **19,472 vectors over eight probes** and the frozen Keccak permutation at **1,000 over two**.
  * **[tools/run.py seed](../tools/vos/cli/seed.py) generates the defects rather than carrying a list of them.** [vos/mutate.py](../tools/vos/mutate.py) walks a Sail or Gallina source with 25 named operators, cutting out comments (a scanner, Rocq's nesting) and string literals, and defaulting the Coq lane's region to the definitional commands, a mutation inside a proof script killing itself and deciding nothing. **Three verdicts and never two**: a stillborn mutant did not compile and decided nothing about the oracle, a killed one moved its answer, and a survivor is the finding. Four oracles are wired: the generated vectors, the prover, the model's own `$[test]` harness and QuickChick.
  * **[tools/run.py quickchick](../tools/vos/cli/quickchick.py) is the Gallina front's input side, which the Wasm oracle has never had.** The enumerative half prints **1,152 vectors** over the admission algebra in the CertiRocq oracle's own switch; the randomized half runs **four property sets at 10,000 draws apiece** under QuickChick, with the counterexample shrinking no enumeration has.
  * **Each instrument is held to seeded defects it kills, and 61 distinct ones are.** The capability format's algebra: nine live mutants of `cap_common.sail` killed on between **1 and 5,499** of 19,472 vectors, and five of `cap_format.sail`'s packing on between **5,638 and 9,170**. The Keccak permutation: seven killed on **1,000 of 1,000**, every one in the rotation and modulus tables that item's own transcription defects lived in. The admission algebra: **27 mutants, the whole population over its definitions, all killed**, ten of them by the generated vectors alone after the shipped theorems had accepted them, on between 28 and 1,152 lines. Stillborn counts are reported apart and never as kills.
  * **Those ten are a result about the statements rather than about the definitions**, and R-11-023's widening and R-15-220a's monotonicity clause are both written against them: the shipped theorems constrain the invariance without pinning the width sum, the slot count, the disjointness test, either half of `slot_index_at`'s half-open interval, or the join of the switch cost with the table-load cost. Every one of those definitions is the one M4.2a intended; what the run measures is that a reader could change it and the proofs would still go green.
