  * **M0.8d's finding is reproduced live rather than cited, and it corrects this item's own premise.** The same five packing mutants run against the `$[test]` harness are **all killed**, and all five report the same property, the alphabetically first to fire; the vectors name which of six abutting fields moved and on how many lines. So the argument for generation at that site is **resolution and not reach**, and M0.12's structural claim is about the *decode* surface a `$[test]` never sees rather than about a packing a property happens to exercise.
  * **Two survivors were closed the way R1a closed its own, by extending the sweep until it reached them**, the capability spec gaining both address setters and the representability and bounds checks over eight exhaustive edge-relative targets, and the Gallina grid gaining a background slot ending exactly where a focus slot begins, which turned `disjoint`'s second disjunct from a clause no vector could tell from its absence into one killed on 28 lines.
  * **The two Gallina generators are complementary and the measurement says so.** QuickChick killed 2 of the 10 the enumerative vectors killed; the other 8 are sites its four named relations do not constrain. The union is 27 of 27. That is reported rather than engineered away.
  * **QuickChick is installed in a switch of its own and the two alternatives are priced.** Into the CertiCoq oracle's switch the solver downgrades dune and **recompiles 59 packages** including `rocq-certirocq` itself; holding dune fixed removes `rocq-core` and twenty packages above it; and the proof gate's switch carries no library at all on purpose. Terms read at its own licence file and recorded in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md). A finding on the way: asking for the package by name in a clean switch resolves **Coq 8.16.1** rather than the Rocq 9.1.1 this tree pins, so the prover is asked for by name.
  * Net change: 4,014 lines added and 191 removed across 23 files, thirteen of them new, plus the lock below. `rtl.py` loses its copy of the rig to [vos/sailrig.py](../tools/vos/sailrig.py) and `proof-gate.py` its copy of the Require-order parse to [vos/proofs.py](../tools/vos/proofs.py), a parse being written once.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite green at **33 modules**, four of them new; both type checkers clean; `model.py typecheck` clean and the build green through configure, build and ctest; the proof gate closing **104 constants**; the `$[test]` harness at **96 properties**; and `rtl.py crosscheck` unmoved at **658,659 vectors with no disagreement, line for line over 658,662 lines**, which is what makes the rig refactor behaviour-preserving rather than asserted so.
  * Three findings.
    * **The `$[test]` harness reaches the packing after all, and the difference is resolution rather than reach.** All five live packing mutants are killed on both sides; the harness names one property for every one of them, being the first to fire and the point at which it stops, and the vectors name which field moved and on how many lines.
    * **A mutation loop that writes into the checkout is a loop nothing else may read beside.** `seed.py properties` mutates `model/` because cmake is pointed there and a copy is a cold build per run. The write is byte-for-byte reversible, refused over an edit, verified before the next mutant, and the lane's build tree is rebuilt from the restored source before the run reports. What none of that covers is the window: for the length of one mutant the tree on disk is wrong, so a `git add` stages a defect and the selftest copies a mutated tree into its template. The loop now holds the lane's build lock, which is `model.py build`'s own rather than a second mechanism and which refuses a second run of this loop and a build beside it; **an advisory lock reaches no reader that does not ask for it**, so a `git add`, a `check.py` and a selftest are unreached and the printed warning stands beside the lock rather than being replaced by it.
    * **The model lane's opam environment leaks into the prover lane, and the failure names neither.** Every model loop needs the Sail switch's variables exported, a prover child inherits them, and where that child shells out to `ocamlfind`, as QuickChick's extraction does, findlib sees two definitions of one library and picks the wrong one. What it prints is an unbound-module error inside a generated OCaml file under `/tmp`. A prover child is now given its own switch's variables laid over the inherited ones.
  * Landed: Tier A.

#### S13b · Absorb the checker's selftest into the mutation engine

  * **One vocabulary, four oracles.** [vos/seeded.py](../tools/vos/seeded.py) holds the verdicts, the report over them and the exit code they imply, and every loop that seeds a defect reads it: `Verdict` is about a `Seeded` protocol exposing one line naming the defect, which `mutate.Mutant` already satisfies from a site and a rewrite and an authored case satisfies from its rule and its own words, and `chosen` is generic in the member. [run.py seed](../tools/vos/cli/seed.py) loses 75 lines and [run.py selftest](../tools/vos/cli/selftest.py) loses its hand-rolled tallies and its print block.
  * **The fourth verdict is the decision this item takes, and it is not a merge.** The engine's third verdict is `stillborn` and does not fail a run; this oracle's is `unseeded` and does. A stillborn mutant is a fact about the subject, the seed having applied and the result not built, and it decides nothing about the oracle. An unseeded one is a fact about the *case*, whose seed no longer applies to a document that moved under it, and it is a finding, because a case that has stopped applying reports its rule live for as long as nobody looks. A generated population regenerates itself and cannot go unseeded; an authored one can, which is why the population that can drift is the one whose third verdict fails. `summarize` prints the unseeded count only where there is one, so the three generated lanes report exactly what they reported before.
  * **`CASES` does not move file, and the reason is a rule.** K-83 holds that nothing in the landing loop names the quarantine and exempts [selftest.py](../tools/vos/cli/selftest.py) by name so that the two cases seeding that rule can spell the coupling they seed; moving the table would move that exemption into [meta.py](../tools/vos/checks/meta.py). It also keeps the table beside the run that reads it, one of the three edits a rule costs being a row of it, and keeps every rule-adding lane an append rather than a delete-and-re-add.
  * **Behaviour is preserved by measurement rather than asserted.** The `CASES` region is byte-identical across the change, **570 lines and 34,969 bytes at sha256 `4ccbd2c8`**, moved from lines 789-1358 to 823-1392, and [check-rules.md](../tools/check-rules.md) is byte-identical at 118 lines and 66,640 bytes. The run is the same on both sides: **96 cases over 85 registered rules, every mutant killed**, the repair path at its nine seeded figures with `--fix` rewriting nine and the tree then passing, both fixpoint proofs moving no byte, and the registry covered in both directions.
  * **A narrowed run says what it did not decide.** `--limit` and `--sample` are deliberately not wired into this oracle, and `--rule` now closes by naming the cases that did not run: the coverage check reads `CASES` rather than the run and reports the registry covered however few cases ran, so a gate exiting 0 over a sample while leaving rules unkilled would be a gate that lies.
  * Net change: 445 lines added and 197 removed across seven files, two of them new.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run; the selftest killing **96 of 96** mutants over **85** registered rules with the repair path at nine seeded figures and the registry covered both ways; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.74 and ruff 0.16.4; and the tools' own suite green at **33 modules**, one of them new. No WSL run is in this evidence and none is owed: nothing here reads the model, the RTL or a toolchain.
  * Two findings.
    * **A fifth mutation loop was outside this absorption, and I14 has since absorbed it.** [tools/quarantine/gate.py](../tools/quarantine/gate.py) carried its own `MUTANTS` table, its own in-memory seeding and its own accounting. It may read `vos.seeded`, K-83 forbidding only the landing loop reaching into the quarantine and not the reverse, and it already imported `vos.report` and `vos.corpus`; absorbing it was scope this item did not carry. Its own report was the narrower defect: a mutation changing nothing was reported as **unseeded**, and its loop merged that case with a survivor into one findings list under one label, so both failed the run and neither was named. It reads the shared verdicts now and names the two apart.
    * **This item's own stated blocker does not survive inspection.** An appended case sits at the end of a 570-line data region and the run loop is 170 lines below its close, which is far outside the three lines of context a merge reads; the region came through this act byte-identical, so a concurrent append had nothing to conflict with. What is real is a last mile and an ordering preference.
  * Landed: Tier A. Tier B's fourth condition is a rule holding whatever fact the landing created, and this act moves no rule id and creates no fact any K- rule reads, so K-84 has nothing to hold; inventing a rule to hold a module listing would be worse than saying so.

### S14 · Unattended CI on whichever host runs it

  * **Declined, and the measurement removes the premise by finding that S9 retired the half of I8's first ground a workflow does not start from.** I8 declined push CI on two grounds. The first is that no stock image carries the opam switches this lane declares, the prepended solver or the interpreter floor, and [run.py provision](../tools/vos/cli/provision.py) answers it: the lane is nineteen stated facts and all but four of them carry a command. The second is that a workflow installing the two checkers restates both pins and the floor outside K-67's and K-75's windows and names a runner image no rule owns. **S9 removed the pins and the floor from that sentence and nothing else.** A workflow calling `run.py provision` restates neither, because the provisioner reads them from the modules that fix them; what is left of I8's second ground is the whole of what a workflow must still state on its own, and every member of it is a fact nothing here owns.
  * **The measurement, taken on a host stripped to the distribution's own PATH rather than argued from the table.** With `PATH` at `/usr/bin:/bin` and the uv tool directory pointed at nothing, `provision --check --only gate` reports **3 of 5** rows absent, and `--apply --only gate` forms a plan of exactly **two** commands, `uv tool install ty==` and `uv tool install ruff==`, **both of which invoke an installer the host does not have and this tree states no route for**. That is the arm a push workflow runs, and it cannot be stood up from zero by anything in this repository. The three routes S9 recorded as ownerless are uv's own installation, the creation of an opam root, and the CertiRocq switch, and the first of them is what both commands of that plan invoke. It does not sit under the whole group: the gate group's other three rows are the interpreter itself, uv, and a `jsonschema` the distribution's own package manager installs, and none of the three is reached through uv.
  * **So the rule this item was priced for cannot be written, and the id it was priced against stays unspent.** No artifact here reserves that id and none is asked to: [check-rules.md](../tools/check-rules.md) registers a rule when a check carries it, so an id named in a document and registered nowhere would be a citation no reader can resolve and no gate can catch, K-84 reading a holder citation and not a mention. What S14 owed was one rule holding the workflow's restatements inside K-67's and K-75's windows. There are no such restatements left to hold: the workflow's residue is a runner label, the action references it pins, and a bootstrap route, none of which any artifact here fixes, so a rule over them would have nothing to compare a site against. A rule with nothing to hold is a rule that decides nothing, which is the defect this checker exists to catch, and inventing owners for three facts in order to have something to hold them against would be the same act one layer down.
  * **And the workflow's own green was unobservable at this decline.** No runner was registered against either remote and nothing in the session exercised a hosted image, so a landed workflow would have been a gate whose passing had never once been seen, which is precisely the promise this item exists to replace with a gate; **Tier B without CI is a promise and Tier B with an unrun workflow is the same promise in another file.** [host-gates.yml](../.github/workflows/host-gates.yml) has since answered it, running the host gate wave and `run.py test` on an Ubuntu runner at every push and pull request to `main`.
  * **What un-declines it is one act and it is already booked**: give the bootstrap route an owner, the way S9 gave the pinned solver's route one, by reproducing the artifact and holding a digest against it rather than by transcribing a command from somebody's page. With uv installable from a route this tree states, `provision --apply --only gate` completes from zero, a workflow becomes a trigger and a host over two `run.py` lines, and the rule that holds those two lines against the entry point's own table is worth writing. That is F-197d, open, and it is measurement work rather than authoring.
  * **The arms not taken at this decline, and what each forfeits.** *Land the workflow anyway*, unrun: it would go green or red on somebody else's machine before anyone here had seen it do either, and its red would be about the three ownerless routes rather than about the tree, which is a gate reporting on the wrong subject. *Widen K-67 and K-75 to reach `.github/`*: it forfeits nothing, and against the workflow this decline had in view, one calling `run.py provision` rather than installing the pins itself, it would have had no restatement to hold. *A self-hosted runner on this box*: it is the lane by construction, `provision --check` being clean here, and it forfeits the second opinion on a third machine that was the whole of what push CI added, leaving a trigger over the machine that already runs the gates by hand. **Two of the three have since been taken together**: [host-gates.yml](../.github/workflows/host-gates.yml) landed and the widening went with it, its two `uv tool install` lines and its `python-version` joining K-67's and K-75's own site tables, which is the reading [I8](#build-loop-instruments)'s cell states.
  * Five findings.
    * **The gate group cannot be stood up from zero and the figure is two commands and one missing installer**, measured on a stripped host: `provision --check --only gate` at 3 of 5 absent and `--apply --only gate` planning two `uv tool install` lines with no uv to run them. It is I8's second ground arriving one layer below where I8 found it, on the bootstrap rather than on the pins.
    * **A workflow's green was unobservable at this decline**, no runner being registered against either remote and nothing here exercising a hosted image, so an authored workflow could not be held to anything before it landed. Recorded as the ground of the decline rather than as a defect: it is what makes an unrun gate a promise. The push workflow has since landed and runs the host gate wave and `run.py test` at every push and pull request to `main`.
    * **Three classes of fact a workflow must state have no owner in this tree**, the runner label, the action references it pins, and the bootstrap route. Reported and not closed, and the rule id this item was priced against is left unspent and unreserved: what a rule would hold does not exist until an owner does.
    * **`--apply` ended in a traceback rather than a verdict where an installer was absent**, which is S9's own artifact pointed at a host that is not the lane and is the one defect this measurement found in it. Repaired here: an absent installer is reported per step and the run goes on to re-probe, so the row it belongs to fails on its own terms and the tool keeps the exit convention.
    * **The decline falsifies eight present-tense sentences elsewhere and each is repaired to what is true**: lane 9's open content, the two sequencing rows, the Tier-B risk row, M7's lead and M7.1's cell, S9's own forfeit clause naming this item as what answers the group split, and F-181's disposition in [the findings register](findings-register.md). Six are repaired at the decline and two, the Opening sequencing row and S9's clause, stand one commit longer, which is this lane's own named defect class arriving inside the item that names it. The rerun returns to M7.1 as a clause of its own and that cell is **not** re-priced here, the rerun being a command rather than a build, which is a reading M7's entry owns rather than this decline.
  * Net change: this item adds no workflow file; the repair to the provisioner is 12 lines added and 1 replaced, five of them the guard and seven the docstring that states why it is a verdict, and the rest is this note, its register entries, and the eight sentences the decline falsifies.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run and green again under `--fix`, which rewrote only the arithmetic this landing moved; the selftest killing all **97** mutants over **86** registered rules with the registry covered both ways; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; the tools' own suite green at **34 modules** and **306 cases**; and the co-read ledger unmoved. The decline's own figures are the two stripped-host runs above, both exiting 1 and both naming the missing installer rather than raising on it. No workflow was run and none could be: that is the finding rather than a gap in the evidence.
  * Landed: Tier A.

### S15 · Generate the derived artifacts from their owners

  * **The model's own view of itself is tracked, and one command writes it.** [tools/generated/sail_riscv_model.json](../tools/generated/sail_riscv_model.json) is what `sail --doc` emits over the curated module set, and [run.py model bundle](../tools/vos/cli/model.py) writes it in about 35 seconds, byte-identical across runs. It is under `tools/` and not under `model/`, that tree being `-text` and vendored byte-identically from its pin, so a generated artifact there breaks both properties; the path adds no top-level directory, so [the map](../CLAUDE.md) is unmoved. The cmake target is not the way to it: `generated_sail_riscv_docs` chains a second full Sail run and a tar for nothing this wants.
  * **The premise the cell was written on was false twice, and the measurement is what found it.** The bundle is not *built on every configure*: the target carries no `ALL` keyword, nothing in the tree names it, and the file existed in none of the thirty build trees on this host, so the emitter had never once run here. And there were **four** live Sail parsers rather than five, [banks.py](../tools/quarantine/banks.py) having moved to the quarantine at S16 and reading a configuration rather than Sail. Only *read by nothing* was accurate.
  * **What generation buys at the decode surface is resolution rather than reach, and the figure is large.** The hand-written skeleton read 218 assembly clauses and yielded a non-empty answer for 136 of them, so **82, or 38%, were dropped in silence** for assembling their mnemonic inside a mapping and leaving no literal to match. Resolving each application against its own mapping's literal arms enumerates **217 of the 218 into 946 distinct mnemonics**, the single residue being `FENCE`, whose `forwards ... when` form arrives as body text with no structured right-hand side. The converse is the soundness check and it holds: 352 of the encoder table's 353 rows appear in the 946, the one miss being `fence` again. Twelve junk names leave the decode surface with the backwards scan that produced them.
  * **K-66 gains that reach and loses a direction, which is a different sentence rather than a stronger one.** A skeleton under-approximated, a form assembled entirely inside mappings going unseen. An enumeration over-approximates: `LOAD` spells `"l" ^ width_mnemonic(width) ^ maybe_u(is_unsigned)`, whose cross product admits `ldu`, and the joint constraint forbidding it lives in the `encdec` clause rather than in the assembly mapping. For that rule the over-approximation is fail-safe, an over-report never being a false green, and any rule wanting a census of the surface would be reading a ceiling.
  * **K-88 splits by what each lane can decide, and neither half is silent.** The host decides three things without Sail: the artifact against the git index, which is the hand edit and the half `--fix` repairs; the artifact against its own owners, the bundle recording the md5 of every file it was emitted from, so a curated source edited without a regeneration is caught at the gate that edit lands at; and the library precondition, a recorded path outside the layout [env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) fixes failing closed and naming itself. The guest decides the residue, *would the emitter write these bytes now*, as `run.py model bundle --check`, and the rule's own passing line names that command so a green host run says which half it took. Running the generator inside the gate was refused twice over: it would be red on the machine the repository is edited from, which is a rule that gets switched off rather than one that bites, and it would put a 35 second toolchain run inside a wave that answers in seconds. **The repair is guarded**, the index's own copy held against the sources it records, so restoring a stale blob over a fresh regeneration is reported rather than written.
  * **The retirement half does not follow, and none of the seven retires.** K-54, K-57, K-60, K-69, K-79 and the restatement halves of K-24 and K-26 each hold a figure sitting inside a hand-authored prose sentence in [spec.md](spec.md), [the profile](isa-profile.md), [the version matrix](cheri-version-matrix.md) or [the delta](rtl-reparameterization-delta.md), or inside authored SystemVerilog at [vos_cheri_pkg.sv](../rtl/vos_cheri_pkg.sv). The bundle is a machine-readable view of the Sail model and is not a generator for prose or for a package, and K-79's own row already argues against generating its sites. That is a separate item at a separate estimate, and a different kind of work rather than a residue of this one. All seven ran green throughout.
  * Net change: 1,413 lines added and 453 removed across twenty-two files, three of them new, plus the generated artifact. Two test modules move with the parsers they hold, a fixture that built a synthetic Sail tree now building a bundle.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run, and the four including the tools' own suite; the checker green on all **86** registered rules against 85, with K-47 at **41** uncounted enumerations against 40; the selftest killing every one of **97** mutants against 96, the repair path holding at ten seeded figures with a second `--fix` rewriting nothing and moving no byte, and the registry covered in both directions; both type checkers clean; and the tools' own suite green over 33 modules. Guest evidence is one command: `run.py model bundle --check` reporting the tracked artifact byte-identical to what the emitter writes from this checkout, with all 158 recorded owner digests verifying. The cost is measured rather than asserted: the bundle adds about 210 ms to a `check.py` run, 129 ms of it reading the blob, and about nine seconds to a selftest.
  * Six findings.
    * **The emitter writes the checkout into the artifact, and a generated file holding a commit could never be held byte-for-byte.** `sail --doc` asks git where it is standing and, where that answers, writes the commit and a dirty flag into the bundle. Two things follow and either is fatal: the bytes move on every commit, so a tracked bundle is stale the moment it lands and the rule over it reports on the checkout rather than on the model; and whether the object appears at all depends on whether `GIT_DIR` reached the stage, which a lane's configure exports and a bare emission does not, so two lanes emit different bytes for one model. Found by running the guest half on a lane other than the one that produced the artifact, the first emission having answered *byte-identical* inside its own worktree and *73 bytes short* outside it. Closed by pointing `GIT_DIR` at a path that is not there, so the question fails rather than answers and what is left is a function of the Sail sources alone. **It is the second site of the same defect S4 found**, a lane's git environment reaching a child that should not read it.
    * **A generated artifact broke a total-class rule on contact, and the rule was silent about why.** The bundle is one line of four megabytes in which every Sail bit literal reads as a short object id on a line that has already named an upstream, which K-81 read as **2,809 pin restatements**. Repaired by taking the generated artifacts out of that rule's window on the rule's own subject, a machine-written file restating nothing. **It generalizes**: every total-class rule over the tracked corpus now has a four-megabyte machine-written member, and K-40's em-dash prohibition is reachable from an anchored Sail comment, which is a coupling nobody chose.
    * **Repointing a parse moved a selftest case's subject out from under it.** K-78's mutant seeded a model source that the core-class reading no longer opens, so the edit reported as a stale artifact and the rule it was meant to prove survived. Re-anchored on the bundle's own clause body. The hazard is general: a case seeding a Sail *definition* must now seed the bundle rather than the file.
    * **Two artifacts cited constructs this tree does not carry.** The decode parser's docstring and K-66's registry row named an instruction shape taken from upstream under a name the curated model does not spell, the vector segment form and a widened atomic having each been curated out. Both repaired to cite the atomic clause that is really there.
    * **The encoder table was a subset of the surface and nothing held the gap, and M1.4′ has since closed it by generating the table.** 593 of the 946 mnemonics the model spells had no row in the dialect's encoder table when this item measured it, subject to the over-approximation above. K-66 asks only about the names the profile excludes, so no rule read it. Recorded as a candidate rule; none was added here, and I15 declined the id on the measurement the generated table now supports, the residue standing at 14 and each of the 14 named by the artifact itself.
    * **A count went stale where its own list shrank.** The tools' README stood a sentence naming five readers above a list of four, S16's move having taken one out. Repaired there and in the two parser docstrings that named the departed module as a sibling.
  * Landed: Tier A. The generated artifact and its owner record are held by **K-88**, but K-66's direction is a changed claim about what a registered rule decides rather than a repair to it, and a changed normative claim is a full read.

### S16 · Quarantine the instruments whose decisions are deferred

  * Both instruments move whole to [tools/quarantine/](../tools/quarantine/), with their two rules, their four selftest cases, their three test modules and their registry rows. **Neither rule is struck**: a rule struck ahead of the emitter that replaces it is a fact nothing holds, and both hold a fact that is still true, so they stop running in the landing loop and go on running under [the quarantine's own gate](../tools/quarantine/gate.py), which carries the two rules over the live tree, the twelve floors their enumerations owe, that registry against the checks reporting under it in both directions, their four mutants seeded in memory rather than in a sandbox, and the three test modules. **The two contracts stay in `docs/`**: what was quarantined is the machinery and never the obligation, K-70 and K-55 still reading both documents from the landing loop.
  * **K-83** in the meta group is what separates a quarantine from a folder. The directory is an ordinary package on `tools/`, so `import quarantine.freeze` resolves exactly as `import vos.freeze` did and the move alone decouples nothing. Two ways in are held, an import naming the package and a path naming the directory, both quantified over the git index rather than a transcription, so a module added here is protected the day it is staged and a tool added there is inside the window the same day. Fail-closed on an empty quarantine, an empty window and an indexed file not on disk; no floor is owed, its own reading being the floor, on K-67's and K-75's ground. One rule gets two cases, because a rule narrowed to the import half still kills a case seeded at an import.
  * **What un-quarantines each is stated as a condition rather than a judgment**, in [the quarantine's README](../tools/quarantine/README.md): the freeze analyzer at **M1.8b**, when `freeze-report.py` reports fewer than three of §4's three inputs absent; the bank-count instrument at **R4** and **R5**, when the droop envelope carries no pending coefficient and the composition's `qualified` flag reads true. The reverse act is four moves and no design, and K-83 being green is what makes that list exhaustive.
  * **Two couplings only the merge could see are repaired with it, and both are cross-lane rather than either lane's.** K-84 resolves a holder citation against **either** registry, a quarantined rule still holding what the landing created and quarantine having moved only which loop runs it, so two landings held by the departed rules read as naming nothing until it did. And `carried_rules()` reads every mention under `checks/`, comments included, which is the fail-closed direction and left one prose citation of a departed rule reading as a registration.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green with the checker on all **82 registered rules**, one new and two departed, every mutant killed and the registry covered in both directions; the floors group's uncounted enumerations falling by the twelve that moved; `check.py --fix` rewriting nothing and moving no byte; the tools' own suite green over 24 modules; both type checkers clean, with two `ruff` carve-outs each stating why a hyphenated command name is not a module name to be renamed. The quarantine's own gate exits 0 over **2 rules, 12 floors, 4 mutants and 51 test cases**, `freeze-report.py` over the same record of **36 variants and 612 required columns** with 4 of 12 predicates deciding and 8 deferring on a named symbol, and `bank-dse.py` over the same **11 candidates, 8 clearing the shape constraints and none admitted**. No WSL run is in this item's evidence and nothing under `model/` is read or written.
  * Landed: Tier A.

### S17 · Re-read the standing adoptions against the ratified extension list

  * **None of the five has ratified, none is abandoned, and nothing is struck.** Read two ways against RISC-V International's own material: the ratified-extensions list's most recent row is `Ziccid` of June 2026, and `src/unpriv/zv.adoc` on `riscv-isa-manual`'s `main` at release `riscv-isa-release-07531fd-2026-08-24` includes `zvfbfmin`, `zvfbfwma`, `zvbb` and `zvbc` and nothing else. [The profile](isa-profile.md) §2.1 now records each track's state; §11's standing obligation is unmoved, what it watches being unchanged.
  * **The three are one task group's one programme rather than three tracks**, the Vector SIG naming video processing its first specification priority and the dot product, the zip and unzip, and the absolute difference as its three components, so their schedules are correlated and a re-pin act reads them together. The integer vector absolute difference has a name to record, **`Zvabd`**, and is the furthest along: internal review from 2026-01-20 to 2026-02-03, ARC feedback on 2026-08-18, ARC approval on 2026-08-25, with public review, TSC approval and board ratification still ahead. Its shape is wider than the profile's row claimed, the widening accumulating `vwabdacc.vv` and `vwabdaccu.vv` putting the sum-of-absolute-differences reduction inside the extension and not only the per-element difference (R-15-067n).
  * **`Zvldot`/`Zvbdot` is the Unprivileged ISA Committee's fast track rather than the SIG's and stands at freeze**, its own plan's TSC and board approvals of 2026-02-10 and 2026-02-25 six months past and neither taken. A second and narrower dot product, `Zvdot4a8i` over four 8-bit integer elements, has a plan approved on 2026-04-15 and has not begun internal review, so the lane carries two proposals and the batched form's encoding is not settled. Neither is a supersession of the other and neither track is abandoned (R-15-067l).
  * **`Zvzip` is the one whose shape moved, and it is the least advanced of the three despite being the oldest pull request.** Its v0.1 never entered internal review, and v0.2 replaces it, holding all five forms to `vl` elements where v0.1 let the count-changing ones read and write against twice that; the chair expects encoding changes out of the ARC before freeze. R-15-067m's ground stands under the move, the forms remaining a permutation of a register group at a latency the element width and the group fix, and what changed is upstream semantics inherited at ratification rather than an argument to retake. The v0.1 anomaly it removes, a `vstart` above `vl`, had no expression here at all (R-15-039a).
  * **The one arm that bit before an external body acted is the denominator, and it resolved against the dot product.** R-15-116a admits dot products into the matrix extension's normative RVV denominator only where R-15-067l's adoption has ratified by the time the measurement is taken; it has not, so **the denominator M0.8c measures against carries no dot product**, and the margin taken now is the larger of the two the clause admits. `Zvbdot` collects the per-output-element reduction tree and produces eight outputs at once, so against an 8 to 10 times bar it is not a rounding term. What M0.8c therefore owes beyond R-15-116a's extension set is the set's **date and the track's stage**: a freeze-stage term is a denominator with a scheduled expiry, one public review and two votes from moving, and a ratification before the second act admits the surface at R-15-014a (ix) makes the measurement stale rather than arguable.
  * **The two recorded candidates keep their dispositions and one of them names a new price.** `Zibi` is alive and four months behind its own plan, its freeze request having reached the ARC on 2026-04-27 where the plan put board ratification at 2026-03-25; nothing there reaches R-15-067o's arithmetic, which is this platform's own. `Zvfbfa` and `Zvfofp8min` stand at freeze with the same missed ratification dates, and their frozen text takes its opcode space in an **additional field in `vtype`** selecting bf16 against fp16 and one OFP8 format against the other, which is a globally scoped mode changing a later instruction's result, the class this profile deletes the dynamic rounding mode as (R-15-039c, R-15-083).
  * **Two register amendments are owed and neither is taken here, a register act rerunning the review gate (R-05-150).** The frozen bf16 and OFP8 text states that scalar variants are not provided, which falsifies the premise R-15-067p prices the pair on; the disposition survives and its ground does not, and the replacement ground is larger than the one it replaces. And R-15-067k names ratification and abandonment as a standing adoption's two exit events, where `Zvzip`'s reshaping is neither: an unratified extension can today be re-shaped out of its own admission argument with no gate firing, and whether the disposition should name that is a ruling this item reports rather than takes.
  * Net change: 5 lines added and 1 removed in one file, with no register act.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run; the checker green on all 81 rules with 1,377 co-read pairs read as they now stand, 22 co-stated facts at 63 sites and 5 owned figures at 12 sites unmoved; the selftest killing every one of 90 mutants with the repair path holding and the registry covered; and both type checkers clean. The co-read ledger is unmoved by construction, its pairs running between the register and [spec.md](spec.md) while this item's edit lands in [the profile](isa-profile.md), which is not a side of one.

### S18 · Author the acceptance predicates five open items owe

  * **Five cells, one per item the conventions above name, and each says what measurement closes its item rather than what the item is.** M0.11 and M1.8a are the precedent and M1.8a states the reason: an instrument arriving with its own acceptance criteria has graded its own homework. Two close whole, M1.7 and M4.4. Three carry a predicate over the half that is writable and name the owner of the half that is not, in M3.6b's shape: M5.3, M6.3b and M6.5b. **No estimate cell moves**, which is the one outcome the convention invites and this act does not take: every predicate is stated over artifacts that already exist, and none of the five turned out to be priced against a different scope.
  * **Every predicate is stated over an instrument this tree already runs**, which is what keeps it a precondition for a lane rather than a second deliverable ahead of one: the corpus's two questions of one run with its manifest digest beside them (`run.py model corpus`), the host checker's own rules over that corpus and this plan (`tools/check.py`), the proof gate the conformance campaign compiles under (`run.py proofs`), and the ring contract's generator held byte-identical to its declaration (`run.py ring check`). Where a measurement would want a *new* reader it is refused rather than written, and [the corpus document's §4](differential-corpus.md#4-the-commit-trace-schema-version-1) supplies the ground in its own words: the rig does not re-derive base and top, a second implementation of the bounds algorithm inside a trace reader being a second place for it to be wrong. So M4.4's containment check sits in the member and reports through HTIF.
  * **What stops the other three is a register decision or a machine mechanism, never an instrument, and each cell names which.** M5.3's crash half wants a recovery discipline no entry chooses and a block device the model does not answer at; M6.3b's intent and translation-cache halves want a closed intent vocabulary and a settled ambiguity discipline, both already open at their own entries; M6.5b's on-machine half wants a second bus initiator this model does not have. Naming the owner is what the convention asks where a predicate is not yet writable, and it is the whole of what separates a deferred measurement from an invented one.
  * **Three of the five are on the critical chain, which is where the convention's precondition is worth the most.** M1.7, M4.4 and M5.3 sit inside M1.2 → M1.7 → M3.5 → M4.4 → M5.3 → M6.5a → M7.1 → M8a, so a lane opening on any of them can now self-check against a criterion it did not author, which is Tier B's fourth condition read one step earlier than the landing. What this act does **not** do is make any of them Tier-B admissible: no rule reads a predicate cell, and the cells say so.
  * Net change: 81 lines added and 2 removed across two files, and both removals are rewrites rather than deletions: the convention's own sentence naming the five open items, which this landing makes false of the plan, and the summary's `Completed:` line, which takes S18.
  * Nine findings, three of them the review gate's own reading of these cells and all three repaired here.
    * **Three device windows every shipped composition declares are claimed by no accessor, so an access answers zero instead of refusing.** `platform.blkdev`, `platform.uart` and `platform.boot_rom` are declared in all three configurations, held to `IOMemory` placement by [the validator](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail), and emitted into the attested devicetree as `verifiedos,block-device`, `verifiedos,uart` and `verifiedos,boot-rom`; none of the three appears in `within_mmio_readable`, `within_mmio_writable`, `mmio_read` or `mmio_write` in [sys/platform.sail](../model/model/sys/platform.sail), so an access falls through to the RAM path. That is M0.16's own distinction met from the other side: it claimed the sequencer's window precisely so that a program could run into a refusal, and these three are the windows a program runs into nothing at. Reported and not closed, being a model act; R1c-i's reading that no entry obliges the three devices to exist stands beside it and is a different gap.
    * **The emulator has no second bus initiator, so the DMA obligations have no mechanism to be measured over.** R-12-100's acceptance clause has the engine present the capability to the fabric for every access and a non-capability DMA write clear the tags of every granule it covers, and R-15-206 fixes the two admitted shapes; *DMA engine* occurs once in [sys/platform.sail](../model/model/sys/platform.sail), in a comment naming what the reset hold covers, and every access this model makes is an instruction's. Reported and not closed: it is what M6.5b's on-machine half waits on, and the artifact half is written without it.
    * **The recovery discipline M5.3's crash half would be stated over is still unchosen**, which is [JournalIndex.v](../proofs/JournalIndex.v)'s reported gap met at the item that would measure it: both arms are exhibited, each keeps every obligation the other keeps, and from one crashed journal they reach different stores, so a predicate naming a post-crash store would take the decision rather than measure it.
    * **The intent vocabulary a deterministic translation cache would be keyed on is still unenumerated**, which is [HandlerGraph.v](../proofs/HandlerGraph.v)'s reported gap met at the item that owns the cache: the entry calling an intent a closed variant closes no variant set anywhere, so the key has nothing to quantify over.
    * **A predicate that would have a reader compute what the machine already decides is refused, and the corpus document says why in its own words.** M4.4's containment half was first written as a condition on the trace, that no record carry a tagged capability outside the instance's root; deciding that means decoding the bounds triple, which §4 declines to do inside the reader on the ground that a second implementation of the bounds algorithm is a second place for it to be wrong. Rewritten as the member's own attempted derivation reported through HTIF. The general form is that a predicate belongs on the side that already holds the algorithm, and the two other places this act reached for a reader, M6.3b's tag-in-a-key check and M5.3's one-index-body check, are answered by a store's own tag bit and by the link map for the same reason.
    * **M4.4's name claims a plurality its own first bullet has ruled out, and no rule reads a cell's name against its bullets.** The item is *bring up one isolated C instance per emulated core* and its opening bullet takes the single-instance ruling, so *per emulated core* is one instance and the duplication the name promises is R2's. That is why the predicate is stated on the authority axis and says so. Reported rather than taken: the ruling is S1's and the wording is the milestone's, and the name is now the one place the plan still reads the other way.
    * **A predicate authored from a register entry alone re-asks what a landed artifact already answers.** R-18-037 names five test families and R-12-100 three clauses, and M6.4's generated [RingContract.v](../proofs/RingContract.v) already attains the batch and segment maxima, states the payload as exactly the declared segments, spends a cancellation interval with the DMA quiescence inside it, and refuses a stale generation, over [a declaration](../interfaces/ring-reference.json) fixing `max_segments` at 4 and `segment_max_bytes` at 1024. What an item owes is the entry's obligation set minus what the artifact carries, so M6.5b's cell names the two R-12-100 adds and says of each of the rest which theorem holds it. The general form is that an entry states what must hold and a generated artifact states what already does, and only the difference is work.
    * **A measurement is stated in the unit its instrument emits.** An in-slot bound is cycles: R-11-015 derives it as a static max-path sum and [CyclicExecutive.v](../proofs/CyclicExecutive.v) puts `slot_bound` in `slot_width`'s and `switch_cost`'s unit. The commit trace's `I` record carries order, `pc` and the decoded word, and R-15-077 deletes `mcycle`, `minstret` and both counter banks in every lifecycle state, so nothing on this machine reads a cycle at all. A retire count held against that bound would restate in an absent unit an obligation R-11-006's acceptance clause already discharges as a Coq artifact, so M4.4's frame question is stated over the slot order the trace does carry.
    * **A condition a host rule already decides for every member adds nothing at the item that restates it.** **K-51** assembles every corpus program on every host run, so once M1.7's image is a corpus member its assembling is decided upstream of any condition that cell could write, and a condition that cannot fail where the bullet above it passes discriminates against nothing. What survives is the pair a green K-51 does not imply: a tagged capability put through memory and read back tagged, and reproduction on a lane other than the one that built it.
  * Exit evidence, red where a lane may not repair it, and stated as the tree carries it rather than as a state no commit holds. `python tools/run.py` exits 1 with **13 findings under exactly K-36, K-37 and K-82**: the S subtotal and one open item's percentage, nine totals the summary and the grand-total basis restate, and the findings register's own two figures. Every one is a sum `--fix` recomputes, this landing's `3 h actual` being the only input that moved, and none is a judgment, so the repair is the integrator's rather than a lane's. **A red baseline stops the selftest deciding anything** and it reports exactly that here, so **no mutant verdict is claimed for this landing** and the third gate is owed at the merge that brings those figures current. What does decide on this tree is the rest of the checker and both type checkers: the other **88** of the registry's **91** rules report ok, K-34 among them at **143 items**, K-84 at **35 holder citations and 36 landing declarations**, and K-61 at **1,394 pairs** read as they now stand; ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5 are clean. The co-read ledger is unmoved by construction, no register entry or prose span having been touched. No WSL run is in this item's evidence and none is owed: nothing here builds `model/`, reads the RTL, or compiles a proof, and the model, proof and interface facts the cells quote are read out of tracked sources rather than measured.

Every cell this item writes is a claim about what a later gate must measure and no rule reads a predicate cell, so nothing holds what the landing created and a full read is what it is owed. The owed acts it reports are the register's, the model's and the plan's in turn, and none of them is a rule this act could have written instead.

### S19 · Re-fit the calibration across both execution modes

  * **The ruling is stated before the figures are read against it: the two modes are not pooled, and the ground is prior to the arithmetic.** An attended actual is an elapsed attended session interval and an agent-parallel actual is the summed agent-session wall-clock of the passes an item took, which are two quantities and not one quantity measured twice. **No item has been measured on both clocks**, so the conversion between them is not in this tree and cannot be fitted from the two records; a pooled ratio would be a quotient whose numerator and denominator are in different units, and its value would be an artefact of how many items happened to land in each mode. That is why the reading below is two fits stated beside each other rather than one fit over the union of the two series.
  * **The arithmetic corroborates the ruling, and the corroboration is thinner than the ruling it corroborates.** Both fits are stated at [the basis](implementation-checklist.md#estimate-and-schedule-basis), each over its own record, and what decides is the sign rather than the magnitude: the class-X-authored pool sits above one attended and below one under fan-out. **That straddle is not an overrun pattern and it rests on one item.** Both attended members overran, and so did one of the two parallel members, M6.3a at 1.25 against the estimate its record row states. What puts the parallel pool below one is **M6.0b**, at 7.5 h against a recorded 16 h, and without that one item the pool reads 1.25 on the record's own basis and 0.83 on the taken prices below. **Fold the two together and the finding disappears**: the pool is then five items at 42 h actual against 47 h estimated, a ratio of **0.89**, so pooling would not widen the class-X prior, it would retire it, and it would retire it on a unit conversion nobody has taken.
  * **The series is priced a second way, which the attended record does not need and this one does.** Walking every revision of this document, **six** of the ten fitted items were re-priced downward at one act before any of them ran, the same act that first gave every open item an authority class, and it took 23.5 h out of the series: M1.6 18.5 to 8, M3.2 22.5 to 19, M4.2b 11.5 to 8.5, M4.3 9 to 7.5, M5.1 21.5 to 18.5 and M5.2 14 to 12. One went the other way, M6.3a by 3 h at a later act, and **three were never re-priced at all**, M6.0b, M6.2a and M6.4, so for those three the earliest recorded figure is the figure the work was taken at. Against the taken prices the same ten actuals run at **0.63** over 115.5 h estimated, with class I at **0.62**, the external read at **0.88** and the authored pair at **0.60**. The two bases are 20.5 h apart, 136 h earliest against 115.5 h taken, and the ruling is the same at either, which is what makes it robust to the basis rather than an artefact of one. Those prices are read off the plan's own history at each item's last open pricing, which is a corpus no later edit moves; the records carry the earliest figure so that both series are fitted on one convention.
  * **The overrun pattern was expected to separate the two modes and it does not, which is this item's own negative result; the ruling above does not rest on it.** Re-derived row by row over both records, an overrun being an actual above the estimate the row states: attended, **eleven of the fifty-nine items overran and nine of the eleven are class I**, S4 at 3.50, R1c-i at 3.00, M1.4′ at 2.17, S9 at 1.83, c4 and M0.7 at 1.17, M0.9 at 1.11, M1.9 at 1.08 and S3 at 1.05, with the class-X-authored pair inside that eleven at 1.69 and 1.67 rather than exhausting it. Under fan-out **two of the ten overran, M6.2a at 1.55 and M6.3a at 1.25, and M6.3a is class X-authored**. So by how often a pool overruns, the two modes order the three pools the same way rather than differently: X-authored runs over in 2 of 2 attended and 1 of 2 under fan-out, class I in 9 of 45 and 1 of 7, and X-read in 0 of 12 and 0 of 1. **X-authored is the most overrun-prone pool in both**, which is the opposite of an asymmetry.
  * **The asymmetry that reading was expected to show is an artefact of the basis, and naming the basis is what removes it.** Against the taken prices the parallel overruns are M4.2b at 1.12 and M6.2a at 1.55 and the X-authored pool overruns in 0 of 2, which is where the mode difference appeared to sit; on the record's own basis, which is the basis both ratios are fitted on, it is M4.2b and M6.3a that change places and only M6.2a overran on both. M6.3a is the whole of the difference, and the reason is in its history rather than in its work: its 6 h was recorded while the cell read class I, and the act that raised it to 9 h is the act that re-classed it X, so the earliest convention joins the X-authored pool to a price set under another class. **What separates the modes is therefore the pool ratio alone**, 1.68 against 0.68, and that straddle is M6.0b's. A pool of two is not a population to conclude from in either mode, which is why all of this is recorded as a measurement and deliberately not as a second prior.
  * **n = 2 stays 2, and the execution mode is what holds it there for two of the four candidates rather than for all four.** Four items have landed class X since the fit was taken, M1.6, M3.4a, M6.0b and M6.3a, and all four are agent-parallel. Only **M6.0b and M6.3a** authored against an external authority, so those two are the ones the ruling keeps out of the pool that decides. M1.6 read rather than authored, so an attended landing would have joined the attended read pool and left n = 2 where it is, and M3.4a carries `n/a` on the scope-move ground stated below and is outside the fit whichever mode it ran in. The pool is not waiting on class-X work to exist, the basis counting that residue before the M8a gate beside the gate figures. What moves the count is a lane landing an open class-X-authored cell at one attention, which is a choice about how a lane is run rather than a fact about the schedule.
  * **M3.4a is outside the fit and this is where F-205l's owed convention is stated.** Its estimate was taken over four deliverables it shed into M3.4c at exit, so the pair measures two scopes and its ratio is a number about the split rather than about the work. The convention: **an item whose scope moved between its pricing and its landing is outside the fit and carries `n/a`**, stated at the parallel record where the case arose rather than widened into the attended record, whose `n/a` goes on meaning an item that never carried an estimate.
  * **Nothing is re-priced and no midpoint moves**, which is the convention read forward rather than an omission. The ratio is a prior on range width, the calibrated total is stated separately, and the parallel ratio prices no cell at all: which mode an item will land in is not known when the item is priced, so a prior conditioned on the mode is not available at the moment a prior is wanted.
  * **The record's shape is kept and the mode reading sits beside it, because changing the shape would have made the rule silent rather than wrong.** K-96 reads the calibration record by a row pattern three cells wide and holds it total over the completed attended items in both directions; a fourth column matches that pattern in no row at all, so a mode column would have emptied the record and left the rule reporting every completed attended item unrecorded, with the ratios it recomputes undefined and the calibrated total K-37 holds undecided. The second record is therefore a table of the same three columns under a heading of its own, which is where the record's reading already stops, and **K-96** is extended over it rather than a new rule being written: the same totality in both directions, the same three pools, and every ratio and count the basis states over it recomputed from it.
  * Net change: 248 lines added and 60 removed across seven files, none of them new. Two of the seven are this document and the findings register; the other five are the rule, its registry row, its floor, its mutant and its test, which is the three-edit discipline plus the two a reading of an enumeration owes.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run, and the four including the tools' own suite; the checker green on all **91 registered rules** with the registry covered in both directions; the selftest killing every one of **108 mutants** against 107, the one added being K-96's fourth and seeded in the record this item creates, with the repair path holding at **twelve** seeded figures and a second `--fix` moving no byte; the floors group's uncounted enumerations at **46** against 45, the one added being this record's rows; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; and the tools' own suite green over **38 modules**, one of whose cases grew a second half rather than gaining a case, the crossed fixture holding each record total against its own series. The co-read ledger is unmoved at **1,394 pairs** by construction, its pairs running between the register and [spec.md](spec.md) and this item touching neither. No WSL run is in this item's evidence: nothing here reads `model/`, the RTL or a toolchain.
  * Six findings.
    * **The two execution modes cannot be pooled, and the disqualifying fact is the unit rather than the spread.** The attended figure is elapsed attended time and the parallel figure is summed agent-session wall-clock, no item has been measured on both, and no artifact here states a conversion. The arithmetic agrees from the other side, the pool the whole calibration rests on falling on opposite sides of one in the two modes, which is the sign of the effect and not its size; that straddle rests on M6.0b alone, which is why the unit and not the arithmetic is what the ruling is taken on.
    * **The overrun pattern does not separate the modes, and the asymmetry this item set out to record was an artefact of reading it on one basis and the ratios on another.** On the record's own basis the class-X-authored pool is the most overrun-prone pool in both modes, 2 of 2 attended and 1 of 2 under fan-out against 9 of 45 and 1 of 7 for class I; the appearance that no class-X item overran under fan-out holds only against the taken prices, where M6.3a reads 0.83 and on the record 1.25. What separates the modes is the pool ratio alone, and that straddle of one is M6.0b's. A negative result: the class is still the axis the calibration is cut on, but the ground for reading a prior apart by mode is the unit above and never an overrun pattern.
    * **The n = 2 pool is closed to new members by execution mode rather than by scarcity.** Both class-X-authored items landed since the fit, M6.0b and M6.3a, ran under fan-out, while the class-X residue before the M8a gate is not empty. The other two class-X landings sit outside the pool for reasons that are not the mode: M1.6 read rather than authored, and M3.4a's scope moved. Reported and not closed: what would move the count is a lane landing one of those cells attended, which is not this item's to schedule.
    * **The record's earliest-estimate convention measures a different thing in each series.** Six of the ten fitted agent-parallel items were re-priced downward at one act before any of them ran, one was re-priced upward at another, and three were never re-priced at all, so the earliest figure and the price the work was taken at differ by 20.5 h over the series and by nothing at all for those three. Both bases are recorded here so the gap is visible rather than latent, and the ruling holds at either.
    * **M3.4a's pair is unusable for calibration, and the convention F-205l asked for is now stated**: an item whose scope moved between its pricing and its landing is outside the fit and carries `n/a`. Stated at the parallel record, the attended record's `n/a` keeping its narrower meaning.
    * **The parallel actual is a sum over passes and nothing records how many.** The convention defines the figure as the summed wall-clock of an item's authoring, verification and repair passes, and no cell states its pass count, so two items reading 7.5 h may be one pass or four and no reader can tell. Reported and not closed: recording it is a change to what every future agent-parallel cell carries, which is the plan's to state and not this item's to impose retroactively on the cells already landed.
  * Landed: Tier A. The ruling is a decision item and the `n/a` convention is a new normative claim about the record, so the mechanical conditions do not reach it however green the gates are.

### S20 · Repair the claims a later act falsified, across the plan and the findings register

  * **This is a sweep for a defect class rather than the repair of one site, and the class is the one this tree names as its own recurring silent one.** A claim that was true when written and that some later act elsewhere falsified, with every gate green: [check.py](../tools/check.py) holds citations, counts, conferral sets and the derived arithmetic, and decides nothing about whether a sentence is true. The corpus is this document and [the findings register](findings-register.md). Every candidate was re-measured against the tree as it now stands rather than taken from the reading that proposed it, and every survivor repaired where it stands, in the past tense with the act that answered it named, which is the shape S3's prune and S14's decline already set.
  * **Four acts did the falsifying and each leaves a different repair.** A **register act**: all five of M3.6a's reported gaps are taken at their own entries, so those bullets name the answer instead of owing it, and the register's own entries had already moved while the cell had not. A **tools act**: the Coq mutation loop compiles the mutated source and its `Require` closure rather than the whole staged directory, and the model dispatch asks the entry point's own table which subcommands need the guest, which is what S4's, M1.9's and R1c-i's bullets each reported as owed. A **landing**: [host-gates.yml](../.github/workflows/host-gates.yml) runs the host gate wave and `run.py test` on an Ubuntu runner at every push and pull request to `main`, which is the ground I8 declined push CI on and S14 declined the workflow on. And the **ordinary passage of landings**, which leaves rosters that stopped enumerating, denominators the next pin moves, and figures an artifact computes about itself.
  * **A completed item's exit evidence is not touched, and that distinction is what makes a sweep of completed cells safe at all.** An exit-evidence line is a measurement recorded at its gate and stays frozen however far the tree has moved since: M0.12's corpus line still reads 11 of 11, S10a's net-change line still reads 577 lines against 1,792, M3.6a's still counts thirty-two generated constructions, and M6.4's proof gate still reads the constants it closed. What moves is the present-tense sentence beside it saying what an artifact **is**, which is the split M0.12's cell was read on once already and which this item applies at twenty-nine sites.
  * **A figure a live site already carries is deleted rather than re-measured, and only a figure carrying its own sentence's point is re-taken.** Deleted: the bundle's byte size, which R1c-i's exit line states live; `counts.py`'s line count, which S10a's own net-change line states as the gate measurement; the gitlink denominator, which K-81's passing line states; the declared-parameter total, which M6.0b's own cell states above it; and the ring campaign's obligation count, which the artifact will move again at the next statement. Re-taken: the differential corpus at twenty-six programs, 245 checks and 8,830 records, and the generated discharge family at fifty-one members and seven early release schedules. **The deletion is also what a rule asked for once**: the ring count was first re-measured, and K-26 reported the word form as a loose restatement of an owned quantity, which is the checker naming a hand-held figure without being able to say the old one was false.
  * Net change: 82 lines added and 32 removed across two files, neither of them new and neither outside `docs/`. Thirty-one of the removals are the claims repaired where they stand, twenty-nine here and two dispositions in the register; the rest is this note, its eight register entries, the roster line and the record row.
  * Exit evidence, and it is stated as it was taken rather than as a landing usually reads: the checker green on every one of the **94** registered rules but the four whose arithmetic this landing's own entry moves, **K-36**, **K-37**, **K-96** and **K-82**, which report **20** figures between them, each of them a share, a total, a series count or a register total that `--fix` rewrites and none of them a claim this item authored; the selftest's baseline unmutated sandbox therefore fails on those same four, so no mutant decided anything here and the population is owed at the repair run; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; and the co-read ledger unmoved at **1,394 pairs**, this item touching no register entry and no prose span. No `run.py test` is owed and no guest run is: the whole change is two documents under `docs/`, and `git status --porcelain` names those two and nothing else. **The arithmetic is left for the repair run by design** rather than hand-written here, because a derived figure this document states is computed and not copied, which is the first rule its own preamble states.
  * Eight findings.
    * **A register act closes a gap the plan reported and nothing carries the answer back to the cell that reported it.** M3.6a reported five register gaps; all five have been taken at their own entries and read `closed` in the findings register, while the cell went on saying *is unstated* and *Owed at* at four of the five bullets and naming no ruling at the fifth. K-61 pairs a register entry with the prose span that cites it and reaches no checklist finding bullet; K-82 holds the register total over the plan's counted findings and never their agreement. So the two halves of one fact are held apart by construction, and what closed it here is a reading rather than a rule.
    * **A disposition moved to `closed` while the plan bullet it indexes still read *reported rather than closed*.** F-142i and F-204 both record acts that have been taken, and the bullets at M1.9 and R1c-i that those entries index still owed them. The direction matters: the register owns disposition and the note owns content, so a disposition moving alone is the expected half-repair rather than a slip, and the note is what a later reader has open.
    * **A landing falsifies the decline that priced it, which is [F-198d](findings-register.md)'s class running the other way.** S14 declined the workflow on a measurement, and the workflow has since landed; four present-tense sentences in S14's and I8's cells and two dispositions in the findings register asserted there is no continuous integration here. Nothing reads them: the workflow is inside K-67's and K-75's site tables, which is agreement between the workflow and the pins and says nothing about a sentence describing the repository.
    * **A figure an artifact computes about itself was transcribed into the plan and drifted by twenty-three members.** [DischargeSequence.v](../proofs/DischargeSequence.v) computes its own generated-family size, and two later passes over that file took the family from twenty-eight to fifty-one and the early-release count from four to seven while M3.6a's cell stood. A figure with a computing owner is the case where a citation costs least and a transcription is worth least.
    * **A count of a proof file's obligations moves when a later item adds to that file.** M6.4's cell counted the ring campaign's obligations and the repair pass that rewrote the lost-wakeup statement added three theorems to the same file, so the cell understated its own artifact while the exit-evidence figure beside it stayed frozen and correct. The count was deleted rather than re-taken, because the next statement moves it again and no rule reads it.
    * **A denominator nothing owns goes stale at the next landing and the numerator does not.** Three sites carried one: six of nineteen gitlinks against twenty-two today, three of twenty declared parameters against twenty-one, and one of fourteen proof sources against sixteen, that last already wrong on the day it landed. In each the numerator is the sentence's point and the denominator is decoration a sibling landing moves, so the repair is to drop it where a live site states it.

The roster shape is what fails rather than any one edit: an enumeration under a count is a second holder of a fact the checked cells already carry, so it is replaced by the rule it was enumerating wherever the rule is statable.
    * **The truth of a prose sentence is the one thing no rule in the registry is about, and this sweep is the measurement of what that costs.** Every one of the thirty-one repairs here landed on a tree where `check.py` exits 0, and ten of them are figures, so the gap is not confined to judgment sentences. Reported rather than closed: what would hold them is an owner per figure, which is K-24's device and is one edit per figure rather than a rule.
  * Landed: Tier A. Nothing holds what this item created: what it repaired is the truth of prose sentences, which is what every rule in [the registry](../tools/check-rules.md) is explicitly not about, so Tier B's fourth condition has nothing to name and a full read is what the rest is owed.

Dropping the two closing words would have made the map true and left it naming some of a tool, so the five are written into its own column form instead.
    * **A lane split written when the table held one row survived the row that falsified it.** The docstring's *cannot run at this gate at all* and its refusal to run the generator were both true of the guest row and both false of the host row, whose generator needs no toolchain and whose bytes are compared outright. The same landing carried the registry row for the rule and left the module's own prose, which is the shape of the defect one level in: the reviewable account moved and the reasoning beside the code did not.
    * **A docstring counted by hand what an enumeration in the same file states.** Seven restating sites were described where `_FLOOR_SITES` holds nine, the two it misses being a comment in a checker settings file and the workflow's interpreter line. The neighbouring rule's paragraph in the same module already declines to count and names the enumeration instead, so the repair is the module's own convention applied one paragraph down.
    * **Three wall clocks for the wave stood where the tool's own page refuses to quote one.** They were taken over a population that has since grown by half, and F-128n is standing on the ground that this host does not reproduce a wave figure within a factor of two. Re-measuring them would have re-created that finding rather than closed it, so they are deleted and the ground is stated in their place.
    * **A decline's present-tense ground outlived the act that took it.** The wave's docstring said nothing runs the gates but a person, by hand, before anything lands; the push workflow runs the wave and the tools' tests at every push and pull request to `main`. This is F-198d's class running forward rather than backward, a landed act falsifying a sentence written before it that no gate reads, and the repair is the sentence rewritten where it stands with the workflow named beside it.
  * Landed: Tier A. The mechanical conditions do not reach it. The first fails on the tree, the checker standing red on twenty sums a lane may not repair and no mutant verdict being claimable over a red baseline. The fourth wants a rule holding whatever fact the landing created, and every fact this one created is the truth of a sentence: rules do read several of the artifacts this item edited, [the rule registry](../tools/check-rules.md) at the rule ids K-00 pairs against the checks modules, [the tools' page](../tools/README.md) at the claim figures and the pin and floor sites its own rules enumerate, and this document and the findings register at citations and sums, which is where the twenty findings above come from; not one of those readings decides whether a sentence is true, which is the ground the item opens on. A full read is what the item is owed, and its own subject is the argument for it.

What all six share is the reason none of them was reported rather than the shape of the defect.

Repaired at all three sites in one act.
  * **The register convention is stated at three sites and one names a different register.** [The corpus document](differential-corpus.md) §2 owns it, *capabilities live in `c8` upward*, and all **26** members keep it. `cap-inspect`'s header said `c10` upward *here and in every corpus program*, eight lines above its own `cmove c8, c1` and the comment naming `c8` thereafter. The charitable reading, that *capabilities* means the per-check derived authorities rather than the root, fails at `cap-trap`'s `la c9, handler`. The header is repaired to the figure its owner states rather than deleted, because a program's header is read by somebody holding that program open and not the document beside it.
  * **The two model-tree headers were falsified by the corpus they name, six and a half hours after they were written.** Both sentences landed in one commit at 16:45 on 2026-08-20, [test_cheri_insts.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_cheri_insts.sail)'s calling its properties *the only programs exercising it until M0.12's purecap corpus exists* and [test_capability.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_capability.sail)'s calling its own *the whole of the machine's own regression corpus*. M0.12 landed at 23:16 the same day with **eleven** purecap programs, and it is that commit which makes both false. **It also edited `test_capability.sail`**, so the falsifying act had the sentence open and left it, and `test_cheri_insts.sail` was edited three further times over the two days after it without the sentence being re-read.
