#### Q2a · Demonstrate the lowering route host-only on a wire parser

  * **The loop closes host-side on one authored component, and the component is authored because the tree has none to lower.** The item lets measurement decide which parser and there is no candidate to measure: [the crown-jewel inventory](crown-jewels.md)'s wire-format row reads `not authored`, this plan carries no item that authors one, and a definitional-command search over [proofs/](../proofs/) finds no decoder over bytes. The component is therefore authored inside the experiment, and against a register-fixed layout rather than an invented one. `descriptor_check bs len` reads a ring descriptor from a byte buffer of declared length and answers R-12-093's `status_ok` where the header is well formed under the encoding rows of [the typed IDL profile](idl-profile.md)'s section 4.2 and `status_invalid` otherwise: a tag byte present and below the operation count, the operation's packed descriptor with no interior padding, each buffer reference's direction and content-type discriminants, the optional deadline's presence byte with the class index it admits, and the flag byte with every bit above the declared count zero. It has the error path and the explicit buffer bounds the bullet asks for, and no byte past the declared length is read on any path. Every width, size and case count is evaluated at compile time from [the generated interface artifact](../proofs/RingContract.v), which K-89 holds byte-identical to its generator, so no declared figure is typed a second time. **Three things the file states rather than reads**, and the recipe lists all three: the case indices of `op` and of `status`, each a total match so that a constructor added to the owner fails this file rather than drifting past it, and the descriptor's field order, which the owner carries only as the summation order of `descriptor_bytes` and of `buffer_ref_bytes` and as the profile's section 4.2 prose. The third is held by no lemma and cannot be, there being no object in the owner an order could be checked against: `enc_direction_bytes` and `enc_content_type_bytes` are both `1`, so a profile that swapped those two fields leaves every numeral, both reflexivity lemmas and the emitted C byte-identical while each read takes the other field's byte. That is the sixth finding below.
  * **What is checked is the first of four boundaries, and nothing here claims more than that.** Gallina to bedrock2 is the `Derive`, closing at `Qed` with `Print Assumptions` reporting `Closed under the global context`. bedrock2 to C is `ToCString`, whose two editions carry zero `Theorem`, `Lemma`, `Proof` and `Qed` tokens over 217 and 324 lines. C to RV64 is CompCert's theorem about a target R-18-002 forbids anywhere. RV64 to this machine does not exist, and no theorem here reaches the Sail model, constant-time behaviour, resource bounds or the hardware refinement. The Done-when's *checked relation at every claimed boundary* is satisfied by claiming one boundary and naming the three it does not reach.
  * **The output is regenerated rather than edited, and the digest that says so is checked from a second checkout.** [regenerate.py](../tools/bedrock2-lowering/regenerate.py) stages the source and its owner outside every checkout, compiles them in the switch, cuts the C out of the prover's `Redirect` output between its two quotes, and prints its size, line count and SHA-256 beside the derivation's own figures; [DIGESTS.md](../tools/bedrock2-lowering/DIGESTS.md) records the three, and `--check` exits 1 on any drift and on any emitted file with no row. A digest recorded and re-checked in the tree that produced it tests the driver rather than the loop, so the reading that decides is a fresh clone at `46ee801`, the commit these sources stand at and the one every later commit of this landing leaves untouched, run into a stage of its own, and it emits a byte-identical file. Both red paths are reachable: one seed stops the derivation before anything is emitted, and one moves the digest with the derivation closing untouched.
  * **The maintenance cost is asymmetric, and a single probe would have reported only the cheap half.** A constant the owner moves is free: the declaration gains a flag, two owner lines and no component line, the derivation closes as it stands with no new hint and no new lemma, and the emitted C moves at its flag tests and nowhere else. A hypothesis the component states is a proof-engineering act: tightening the precondition from the machine-word bound to the owner's own slot size is one component line and no owner line, and the derivation stops, reporting an incomplete compilation at every reduced site and refusing at `Qed` with nothing emitted. Regeneration absorbs a moved constant and does not absorb a moved specification, which is the answer to *expose the maintenance cost* and is what a route widening past one component would pay.
  * **The boundary audit is a table over the emitted text, and the bullet's own last sentence comes out confirmed rather than argued.** Pointer representation, provenance, bounds, calling convention, external calls and failure behaviour each take a row quoting the emitted C or its assembly, and each row says what an unchecked printer edit would and would not repair. Every address is integer arithmetic on the buffer parameter, which on this machine yields an untagged capability (R-05-136); the only bound is the specification's precondition, no runtime check reaching the C; the sole failure kind is the returned status word, bedrock2 having no trap. So an integer address is not turned into a capability by an unchecked change to the printer, because every property lives either below the printer, in a relation stated over integer-addressed memory, or above it, in a backend that does not exist yet.
  * **The two device arms are compared on this program, the recommendation is Arm A, and the choice stays where the item puts it.** Arm A is measured to the token: the emitted C reaches the contained purecap `ccomp` at exit 0 and comes out as plain RV64 with no capability instruction, and the struck capability printer emits its stubbed arms, every one of them an arm this program needs. Arm B is measured by running the verified backend over the same component, which lands on the plain RISC-V R-18-002 forbids at either width, with `riscv-coq`'s instruction type closed at sixteen constructors and no arm a capability set could be added at. Arm A is recommended because it is the only arm aimed at a target the register admits and its remaining work is already priced and sequenced as M1.2b through M1.2f, and what it does not buy is stated beside it. Choosing the device path, displacing the CompCert-C/VST default, re-pointing the route list and any position on the non-commercial compiler chain are decisions this item reserves, and none is taken here.
  * **The tracked set follows M1.5's precedent and stops short of the emitted C.** [tools/bedrock2-lowering/](../tools/bedrock2-lowering/) carries the Gallina sources, the recipe a later reader re-runs, the driver and the digest record, which is what makes the output reproducible rather than merely present; no `run.py` command reaches it, on the ground the Wasm oracle already states, and nothing is added under `proofs/`, whose gate compiles in a switch carrying none of these five packages. The emitted C stays out of the index for two reasons and both are recorded: bedrock2's printer prepends its own load-and-store preamble, which is bedrock2's text on bedrock2's terms, and no marks ruling exists for the `.c` kind. **Three sentences in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) that this landing falsifies are repaired at their own site**, each narrowed to what is now true rather than deleted: the claim that no command invokes the lowering, the claim that nothing the switch emits is tracked, and the claim that no tool here invokes the compiler, that last one narrowed to this switch's compiler because the driver runs a contained `ccomp` only when it is handed its path.   * Ten findings.
    * **The route's checked relation covers the first of four boundaries and the other three are not near.** The derivation closes Gallina to bedrock2 at `Qed` under no axioms; the printer that turns bedrock2 into C carries no theorem at all; the C reaches machine code by a compiler whose theorem is about a target the register forbids; and nothing relates any of it to the Sail model. A note claiming a checked relation at every boundary would be claiming three it does not have.
    * **The maintenance cost of relational compilation is asymmetric, and the expensive direction is the specification.** A constant moved in the owner costs zero component lines and one regeneration; a precondition moved in the component costs the derivation, which stops at seventeen sites and emits nothing. The route absorbs a declaration change and does not absorb a specification change, which is the term a widening of the technique pays.
    * **The reaching exit's gap on this program is its whole stubbed-arm set, every arm one the program needs.** This is the same measurement the lowering item took, now on a second and larger program, at 16 arms against its 19, which is what makes the census a size of the gap rather than a property of one program.
    * **The verified backend's operation compiler takes no instruction-set argument, so an instance declared for the base integer set emits multiply instructions from the M extension.** `compile_op_register` maps `mul`, `mulhuu`, `divu` and `remu` to their own instructions unconditionally, and this component's array index scalings come out as that many such instructions under an `RV32I` instance. The alphabet a caller instantiates is not the alphabet it gets.
    * **The generated C is 5.9 times a hand-written C of the same check by bytes and 2.4 times by instruction, and the proof side of that comparison is not taken.** The route re-materializes each bound boolean and gives every refusing path its own status assignment, and the printer's preamble is a seventh of the difference. No refinement of this check exists in the tree, so the comparison is size and alphabet alone and decides nothing about proof cost on the manual route.
    * **The generated owner fixes every width and count and fixes no field order, so a consumer's offset arithmetic restates the profile's prose and nothing holds it there.** The component reads every width, size and case index from the artifact K-89 holds to its generator, and computes each offset itself in the order section 4.2 states; the owner carries that order only as the summation order of two definitions. Because the direction and content-type widths are both one byte, a profile that swapped those two fields would move no numeral, fail neither reflexivity lemma and change no emitted byte while each read took the other field's byte.
    * **The item names a wire parser as the component and the tree carries none to lower.** The wire-format inventory row reads `not authored`, no item on the plan authors one, and no decoder over bytes exists under the proofs tree, so the demonstration authors its own component under the tools tree and the inventory row is unmoved by this landing.
    * **Two register entries assume a single lowering path carrying both proof transport and an admissible target, met again on a second program.** One requires the synthesis be counted as transport rather than an anchor and the other admits a verified compilation step with no clause about where the transport stops, and this loop is again the exit with the C and without the theorem. Reported and not closed, and whether it is one gap or two is a reading.
    * **The intermediate language the register, the specification and the plan all name for this route is emitted by neither exit, met again on a second program.** The reaching exit emits C source text and the verified exit encoded machine words, and neither emits the named intermediate. Reported and not closed.
    * **The proof gate's switch no longer carries its prover and nothing else, and two tracked sites still say it does.** An inventory of that switch today lists four Rocq libraries beside the prover, installed by other work, where the page recording the lowering stack's terms states the emptiness as a deliberate property. The instance inside this landing's own rewritten line is repaired here; the instance in the page's QuickChick paragraph and the one restating it in the QuickChick command's own source sit outside this landing's edit and the repair is owed at each site.
  * Landed: Tier A.

#### Q4a · Measure host-side inference demand and state the target traffic budget

  * **The demand side of R-12-085's ceiling is measured, stated in the register's own terms, and the verdict is bandwidth.** [The report](inference-demand.md) carries the static demand, the host baseline, the quality comparator and the target traffic budget of one dense model at every quantization its publisher ships, and [inference-demand/](inference-demand/) carries the machine-readable output every figure in it is read from. At R-18-004a(vii)'s five tokens per second over its 8,192-token context the measured four-bit configuration demands **12.46 GB/s** of sustained second-class read on its weight stream alone and **15.67 GB/s** with a `q8_0` cache added, against R-18-004b's **8 GB/s** M-class grant floor; the projection to the floor's own three-billion-parameter model demands **9.29 GB/s** on the weight stream alone at the density a published four-bit format actually costs, and **12.50** and **15.33 GB/s** with the two cache formats. Storage is the term that fits: that projection is **2.83 GB** resident under a `q8_0` cache, inside R-18-004b's **3.2 GB** usable second-class payload floor at 88% of it, and **3.39 GB** under an f16 one, 106% of it. Compute demands **64.4 GFLOP/s** and is undecidable, no artifact measuring an M-class array throughput to compare it to. The slot grant is undecidable until M6.8 and R-15-108.
  * **The register's supply figures are floors and maxima on quantities a composition declares, not values, and every comparison here is taken that way.** R-18-004b says *at least* 8 GB/s granted to the M-class island, *at least* 20 GB/s aggregate, *at least* 4 GB of second-class payload and a declared exclusivity fraction of *at most* 20%, so the 3.2 GB usable payload is a floor exactly as the two bandwidth figures are, and a demand above any of them is met by a composition above the floor and fails only against one at it. What the report therefore states twice per row is the multiple of the grant floor the grant would have to be raised to, and the share of the aggregate that raised grant would take from a floor R-18-004b also requires to cover scanout, the compositor's per-frame surface traffic, the decode frame pool and the camera burst ring **concurrently** with it. Every projection to the floor's own model size takes more than half that aggregate, 54% at the most favourable combination constructible and 62% at the measured four-bit density with the cheaper cache. The unit's base is the report's own convention and not the register's, which fixes none; against a binary payload every capacity multiple falls by 1.0737, and the report says what that does to the two figures that sit near the line.
  * **Which class the KV cache sits on is decided by no artifact, so the budget states the demand with it and without it rather than assuming.** R-18-004a(vii) places the *model* wholly resident on the second class and fixes nothing about the cache; R-15-247s's second list names bulk by volume and model weights and names no cache, and its criterion is latency-criticality, which a cache read once per generated token can be argued either side of. The placement is the whole-program memory plan's (R-08-012a), exactly as the compute and output buffers are, so every figure folding the cache in is an upper bound conditional on that placement, and [budget.json](inference-demand/budget.json) carries both readings per row. **The register tension this item reports does not turn on the placement**: the floor's own three-billion-parameter model exceeds the 8 GB/s grant floor on its weight stream alone.
  * **Nothing here grades its own homework.** The comparator is mean KL divergence and top-1 agreement against the widest weight the publisher ships, and the threshold that makes a configuration quality-acceptable is the inference-quality `PG-` predicate of `docs/product-gate-contract.md`, which Q1 authors. **Quality-acceptable is Q1's to decide**, the report says so at its summary and again at its comparator, and no configuration is declared acceptable. That contract is cited by name rather than by hyperlink because a lane cannot link a file another lane creates without K-12 refusing it; the link is the integrator's once both land.
  * **The host does not establish that decoding is bandwidth-bound, and the report declines to borrow the conclusion it was expected to supply.** The eight per-file benchmark rows were taken in eight windows over nearly three hours beside nine sibling lanes and across a power change; they do not order by bytes per token, one figure's standard deviation reaches 80% of it, four of the eight rows carry a figure whose standard deviation exceeds a third of it, and the **widest** file is fastest in all four throughput columns. So the cross-file comparison was taken instead as one invocation over every file, twice: once spanning a power transition whose bias runs the same way as its result, and once whole inside a single state with a sampler reporting `Discharging` on all **131** samples, every one of them tracked. Neither pass shows a flat byte rate: the achieved rate varies by 1.35x across the first pass and 1.84x across the second pass's repacked files. **What the host establishes is that throughput falls with bytes per token at a rate short of proportional, and no more**; the bandwidth-bound reading of the target stays [the estimates](performance-estimates.md)' and R-15-247p's, and the budget is derived from bytes and the register's own rate rather than from any measured throughput.
  * **What each of the two passes resolves is decided by the standard deviations printed beside it, and the ordering among the three widest files is not one of them.** The end-to-end fall does resolve and is large: the four-bit file generates at 2.449 plus or minus 0.103 against the widest file's 0.811 plus or minus 0.269 in the deciding pass, 5.7 combined standard deviations apart, a file reading 72% more per token generating at 33% of the rate. Among `Q5_K_M`, `Q6_K` and `Q8_0` the adjacent differences are 0.91 and 0.69 of their combined standard deviations at three repetitions under nine-lane contention, and the first pass resolves one of its two. So the claim the report makes is that generation falls with bytes per token across the range of the four repacked files, and not a ranking of the three widest against each other.
  * **What the host allocates is not what a target holds, and the difference is exact rather than estimated.** This backend maps the file and then repacks the block-quantized matrices it has an interleaved kernel for into a second buffer, so the process holds those weights twice. The repacked set is identifiable to the byte: for the four repacked files it is every tensor less the **784,384** bytes of `f32` normalizations, matching the loader's reported 2,375.16, 2,749.23, 3,146.67 and 4,075.68 MiB exactly, and for `Q5_0`, whose block type has no repacked kernel, it is the `q6_K` embedding alone at **304.28 MiB** against that tensor's 319,065,600 bytes. The maximum resident set sits between one copy plus the buffers and two copies plus the buffers for the four, and just above one copy plus the buffers for `Q5_0`, which is the same mechanism read from the other side. The budget scores one copy and records the second rather than charging it.
  * **Bits per parameter is not a sufficient statistic for quality, and the pair that proves it is two five-bit formats.** `Q5_0` at 5.604 bits and `Q5_K_M` at 5.735 differ by **0.131 bits per parameter**, and the k-quant divides the mean KL divergence by **5.6** (0.088814 plus or minus 0.002093 to 0.015998 plus or minus 0.000364) and lifts top-1 agreement by **8.3 points** (84.848 plus or minus 0.501 % to 93.177 plus or minus 0.353 %), both differences tens of combined standard errors wide. The pin's own `ggml/src/ggml-common.h` says why: `block_q5_0` is 32 elements under one `ggml_half` scale with no minimum, where `block_q5_K` and `block_q4_K` are 256 elements under a super-block scale and minimum plus a six-bit scale and six-bit minimum for each of eight sub-blocks. **What `Q5_0` costs against the four-bit k-quant is 13.1% more bytes per token for no quality this comparator resolves**: top-1 0.31 of a combined standard error apart, the perplexity ratio 0.70 and inside the instrument's own self-check residual, delta-p RMS 0.09, and mean KL divergence 1.97 apart in `Q5_0`'s favour. It is also the file the backend does not repack, so it is slowest on the host. A reader ordering formats by nominal width pays for width and misses the format that buys quality at the same width.
  * **Two entries of the same floor are in tension, and doing this item's arithmetic is what shows it.** R-18-004a(vii) names a three-billion-parameter *four-bit* dense model, and no published four-bit format of a model this size costs four bits: the file measured costs **4.955** bits per parameter once its block scales and its `q6_K` embedding and down-projections are counted, and the loader's own `4.95 BPW`, now recorded in each end-to-end file, agrees. So the weight stream alone of the floor's own member demands 9.29 GB/s at the floor's own rate, above R-18-004b's 8 GB/s grant floor before a single cache byte is read and whichever class that cache is placed on, and clears it only at a pure four bits with no scale, which no format has. That is a register act and it is reported rather than taken.
  * Net change: 3,941 lines added and none removed across 32 files, 31 of them new, at 87ce1e3. The whole change is [the report](inference-demand.md), the 30 files of machine-readable output beneath it (29 `.json` and one `.py`) and one row plus two paragraphs in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) covering the instrument and the weights. **No line of the register, the spec, the co-read ledger, the checklist, this log or the findings register moved, and no figure in [the estimates](performance-estimates.md) was touched.** The clone, the 13.5 GB of weights, the 1.55 GB of base logits and every raw tool log stay in a build lane outside the checkout; the pin is not a gitlink and nothing under it is tracked. `.json` is a kind [marks.py](../tools/vos/checks/marks.py) refuses a licence mark of, and the one `.py` carries the `Apache-2.0` mark its kind wants; the shell scripts and the collector that drove the passes are not tracked, and the report names in section 9 which tracked file each tool produced and which are transcriptions the collector took, each carrying the predicate a reader checks it against.
  * Exit evidence, every figure measured at 87ce1e3 or at the pin it names. **The instrument.** llama.cpp at commit `427291b5b34cd914a31b3fd3b61a68f6184f4b9f`, which `git tag --points-at` answers **`b10816`** and nothing else; the `v0.4.0` tag is `5266f24da75dc449bd56cbed7addb9c8e4a6a73e` and the pin is **seven** commits after it, which is why the binaries report `0.4.0-dev (build 10816, commit 427291b5b)`, why every tracked benchmark file carries `build_commit` `427291b5b` and `build_number` 10816, and why [manifest.json](inference-demand/manifest.json) records the build tag, the release tag's own commit and the distance rather than a release name. Configured `cmake -B build -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release -DGGML_NATIVE=ON -DLLAMA_CURL=OFF -DLLAMA_BUILD_TESTS=OFF`, the configure detecting `-mcpu=oryon-1+rng+crc+dotprod+i8mm+nosve+nosme`, under cmake 4.2.3 and GNU 15.2.0, producing exactly **five** executables, `llama-bench`, `llama-completion`, `llama-perplexity`, `llama-cli` and `llama-gguf`, with no `llama-server` binary and no web front end; the shared-library layout does build a `libllama-server-impl.so` beside them, which nothing here invokes, and both the report and THIRD-PARTY.md say so. Licences read at the pin's own files on 2026-09-05: `MIT` at the root under "Copyright (c) 2023-2026 The ggml authors", `ggml/` carrying no licence file of its own, `vendor/` holding **six** subtrees beside its own `CMakeLists.txt`, and **thirteen** vendored components each read at its own file, none carrying a reciprocal or a non-commercial term. No build wall time is quoted as evidence, `ccache` being on. **The weight.** `Qwen/Qwen3-4B-GGUF` at revision `bc640142c66e1fdd12af0bd68f40445458f3869b`, `LICENSE` there being the stock Apache-2.0 text with its appendix filled in as "Copyright 2025 Alibaba Cloud" at 11,544 bytes and SHA-256 `5de36594c10839788a8c589443a8ef9d8b8d17c65a1b5807206ae037fc36c6bd`, with README front matter `license: apache-2.0`, read 2026-09-05. **Five** GGUF files at that revision and no others, no f16 and no `q4_0` among them; each downloaded by revision and each `sha256sum` equal to the digest the repository's own tree listing states, at 2,497,280,256, 2,823,710,976, 2,889,513,184, 3,306,260,704 and 4,280,404,704 bytes. The three-billion-parameter candidates were read at the publisher's own metadata on 2026-09-06 and each refused on terms or on form: `HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM3-3B` is Apache-2.0 and publishes **no GGUF at all**, `Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct-GGUF` declares `license: other` with `license_name: qwen-research`, and `meta-llama/Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct` is `gated: manual`. **The machine.** Snapdragon X Elite X1E78100 (Oryon, aarch64, twelve cores, `asimddp`, `i8mm`, `bf16`, no SVE) under WSL 2 kernel 6.18.33.2 with 15 GB and no GPU, CPU-only at twelve threads, beside nine sibling agent lanes throughout; the host was on battery to 02:07:03Z, on AC from 02:53:43Z, and on battery again from 05:10:53Z, each stage stamping its state, the two transitions bracketed by consecutive stamps rather than timed, and every timed table carrying the state per row. **Static demand.** **398** tensors in each of the five files; tensor bytes 2,491,323,904, 2,817,754,624, 2,883,556,864, 3,300,304,384 and 4,274,448,384, which over 4,022,468,096 parameters is **4.955**, **5.604**, **5.735**, **6.564** and **8.501** bits per parameter, the loader's own `4.95`, `5.60`, `5.73`, `6.56` and `8.50 BPW` agreeing and now recorded per file. The head is tied, no file carrying an `output.weight`, so bytes read per generated token are every tensor plus one embedding row of 2,100 or 2,720 bytes. KV per token is 8 by 128 elements of key and of value over 36 layers, **147,456** bytes at f16 and **78,336** at `q8_0`, hence **1,207,959,552** and **641,728,512** bytes at 8,192 tokens, which the loader's own `KV buffer size` line reports as 1,152.00 and 612.00 MiB for all five files. **Resident bytes.** Per cache format and identical across all five files: KV 1,152.00 against 612.00 MiB, compute 306.75 against 311.75 MiB, output 0.58 MiB (151,936 logits by 4 bytes), graph nodes **1,266** against **1,698**, all four now recorded in each `e2e-<quant>.json`. Maximum resident sets 6,047.62, 4,294.01, 6,726.07, 5,727.16 and 7,218.31 MiB. **The host baseline.** Eight per-file `llama-bench` rows at `-p 512 -n 128 -d 0,8192 -t 12` in both cache formats, the first at five repetitions and the rest at three, and two whole-ladder passes at `-p 256 -n 64 -d 0 -t 12 -r 3`; the deciding pass's tg64 figures are 2.449, 0.945, 1.260, 1.005 and 0.811 tokens per second, which times bytes per token is 6.10, 2.66, 3.63, 3.32 and 3.46 GB/s, and its power record is **131** samples, all `Discharging`, stamped 05:26:50Z to 05:57:56Z against the pass's own 05:26:55Z to 05:53:39Z, at a mean interval of 14.35 s and a worst of 73 s under contention. Five end-to-end `llama-completion` runs at `-c 8192 -n 128 --temp 0.7` over a 556-token prompt generating 127 tokens, sampling costing 25.37 to 30.04 ms against totals of 29.8 to 120.9 s, under a tenth of one percent in every row, and the tool's own unaccounted term 10.18 to 14.61 ms on the four AC rows, now recorded per file, so the two terms `llama-bench` excludes by its own documentation at the pin do not move a token rate here. **The comparator.** `llama-perplexity --kl-divergence` at `-c 2048 --chunks 5` over 10,240 tokens of this repository's own spec and register concatenated (2,050,087 bytes, SHA-256 `740ba746a70121052138205f07e192ce9fa8881b9398d8d5ddad3de6b90e97e1`), against Q8_0 base logits: mean KL divergence 0.095627 plus or minus 0.002759, 0.088814 plus or minus 0.002093, 0.015998 plus or minus 0.000364 and 0.008798 plus or minus 0.000544 with top-1 agreement 85.064 plus or minus 0.498 %, 84.848 plus or minus 0.501 %, 93.177 plus or minus 0.353 % and 95.073 plus or minus 0.303 % for Q4_K_M, Q5_0, Q5_K_M and Q6_K. The base scored against itself returns **0.000000** mean KL divergence, **100.000%** top-1 agreement and a delta-p RMS of 0.001%, which is the instrument's self-check on two of its three columns; its `ln(PPL/PPL base)` is **0.00398 plus or minus 0.00161** rather than zero, 2.5 standard errors out, which bounds what a perplexity comparison here resolves. **The budget.** Ten measured configurations demanding 15.67 to 27.41 GB/s at five tokens per second with the cache and 12.46 to 21.37 GB/s on the weight stream alone, 1.96 to 3.43 and 1.56 to 2.67 of the 8 GB/s grant floor, 78% to 137% and 62% to 107% of the 20 GB/s aggregate, with resident bytes 1.08 to 1.81 times the 3.2 GB usable payload floor; and a three-billion-parameter projection at 9.29 GB/s on weights alone, 12.50 and 15.33 GB/s with the two caches at 4.955 bits, and 10.71 and 13.54 at four. **Not measured, each with its reason**: the target kernels and the fixed bandwidth grant (Q4b's, M6.6 and M6.8 sitting after the M8a gate and R-15-108 having no cell); energy, no instrument reaching WSL on this host; dequantization cost and attention cost at depth as terms of their own, the only rows carrying depth and both cache formats being the per-file table the report disowns; the KV cache's class, which no artifact places; R-18-004a's other seven members, which share the aggregate and the two capacities; concurrency above one session; and a mixture-of-experts model, so R-12-085's expert-count and top-k terms stand unmeasured. **The gates**, all at 87ce1e3, on battery and discharging beside nine sibling lanes, so no wall-clock figure is quoted as evidence. `python tools/run.py` at exit 0 with all three host gates green: `tools/check.py` green over all **94** registered rules with no arithmetic drift, K-61 reading **1,394** pairs and none moved, K-82 reading **562** findings across **90** items unchanged, K-52 at **210** markable files, K-53 at **1,029** tracked files against the lane base's 998, the 31 added being this item's, and K-81's 22 pins unmoved, no gitlink having been added; the selftest killing all **114** live mutants with all 94 rules covered and the repair path holding, a `--fix` on the pristine sandbox rewriting nothing, twelve seeded figures repaired and a second `--fix` moving no byte; and both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5. `python tools/run.py test` at exit 0 over **41** modules and **394** cases. `git ls-files --eol` reads `i/lf w/lf` on every one of the 32 paths this item touches, and re-running the tracked extractor over the five tensor listings reproduces all five `static-<quant>.json` with no difference.
  * Twelve findings.
    * **The floor's inference member and the floor's bandwidth line are jointly satisfiable only at a grant well above the stated minimum, and the aggregate is where the room has to come from.** At R-18-004a(vii)'s own rate and context, a three-billion-parameter model at the density a real four-bit format costs demands 9.29 GB/s of weight stream before any cache is read, above R-18-004b's 8 GB/s M-class grant floor, and 12.50 GB/s with the cheaper cache, which is 62% of the 20 GB/s aggregate that same entry requires to carry scanout, the compositor, the decode pool and the camera ring concurrently with it. Neither figure is a violation on its own, the floors being minima on supply, and together they fix a grant the composition must find inside an aggregate floor that is not obviously large enough to hold it.
    * **The floor says *four-bit* and no artifact says what four-bit costs in bytes.** Every published four-bit format of a model this size costs more than four bits per parameter once its block scales and its wider embedding and down-projection tensors are counted: 4.955 on the file measured. The `q4_0` figure of 4.5 bits is that block type's own density and not a whole-file cost, a real conversion of this model keeping a wider embedding as the measured file does, so it is a lower bound. The bandwidth comparison R-18-004b makes needs bytes, and the entry states a parameter count and a nominal bit width, which are not the same quantity and differ by 24% here.
    * **A pin taken from a release page is not a pin, and a correction that stops at the prose is not a correction.** The commit this lane and its brief recorded as a release carries the build tag `b10816` and nothing else, sits seven commits after the `v0.4.0` tag, and its own binaries report `0.4.0-dev`. Nothing downstream depended on the release, the licence reading being at the pin's own files in any case, but the identity was wrong in a licence table for three commits and stood in the tracked machine-readable record the prose cites for three more after the prose was corrected. The check that catches the first is one command at the pin; the check that catches the second is reading the artifact the corrected sentence points at.
    * **A throughput comparison carries only what its own standard deviations resolve.** Eight per-file rows taken over nearly three hours beside nine lanes and across a power change put the widest file fastest in all four columns, the reverse of what bytes predict. The same five files in one invocation, twice, resolve the four-bit file above every wider one at 5.7 combined standard deviations and do not separate the three widest from each other at three repetitions under contention. So the unit of comparison is one process in one window with the power state sampled beside it, and the claim the run supports is the one its printed errors resolve and no more.
    * **This backend holds the repacked weights a second time, and the repacked set is identifiable to the byte.** For the four files whose block type it repacks, the second buffer is every tensor less the 784,384 bytes of `f32` normalizations, matching the loader's four reported figures exactly; for the one file it does not repack, it is the `q6_K` embedding alone. The maximum resident set brackets accordingly. So a host resident-set figure over-states a target's by close to the whole weight set, and the budget's resident figure must be built from the tensor bytes rather than read off the process.
    * **Bits per parameter is not a sufficient statistic for quality, and the pair that proves it is two five-bit formats.** `Q5_0` and `Q5_K_M` differ by 0.131 bits per parameter, and the k-quant divides mean KL divergence by 5.6 and lifts top-1 agreement by 8.3 points, both tens of combined standard errors wide, because one carries a single scale over 32 elements and the other a super-block scale and minimum plus a per-sub-block scale and minimum over 256. Against the four-bit k-quant that same legacy format costs 13.1% more bytes per token for a quality result that resolves on no column of the comparator. Ordering candidate formats by nominal width therefore pays for width and misses the format that buys quality at the same width.
    * **The realized narrowing factor is bounded well below the nominal width ratio, because the head and the block scales do not narrow with the block type.** [The estimates](performance-estimates.md) read narrower weights as "a factor of two to four directly on bandwidth" against a nominal width ratio. Measured across this publisher's own ladder the factor from the widest file to the four-bit one is 1.72 rather than 2: of the 0.955 bits by which the four-bit file exceeds four, the `q6_K` embedding and down-projections cost 0.56 and the `q4_K` block scales 0.39, the `f32` norms rounding it out. The comparator differs from the estimates' (block types rather than FP8 or MX against bf16) so this neither confirms nor refutes that figure; what it adds is that the achievable ratio is a property of the whole file and never of the block width.
    * **The comparator's base is itself a quantization, and one of the instrument's three columns fails its own self-check.** The only permissively licensed dense GGUF at this size publishes no unquantized weight at its pinned revision, so the widest weight and the base every divergence is measured against is an eight-bit file. The base scored against itself returns zero mean KL divergence, 100.000% top-1 agreement and a delta-p RMS of 0.001%, so two columns are where they should be; its ln perplexity ratio to itself is 0.00398 plus or minus 0.00161 rather than zero, which that agreement cannot honestly produce, and the residual bounds what a perplexity comparison over this corpus and chunking resolves.
    * **The two terms `llama-bench` excludes and the plan's item names do not move a token rate on this host.** Sampling costs 25.37 to 30.04 ms against totals of 29.8 to 120.9 seconds across five end-to-end runs, under a tenth of one percent in every one, and the tool's own unaccounted term is 10.18 to 14.61 ms. What the end-to-end run is actually worth is the first-token latency and the maximum resident set, neither of which `llama-bench` reports at all.
    * **The second-class capacity comparison has no per-consumer split and its figure is a floor, so a single-consumer measurement cannot be closed at that entry.** The 3.2 GB usable payload is R-18-004b's *at least* 4 GB less an exclusivity fraction of *at most* 20%, summed over R-15-247s's second list for the whole roster, over which the inference server is one member; the floor's own projected model takes 88% of it under the cheaper cache before any other consumer is placed. A multiple below one is therefore not yet a fit, a multiple above one fails only against a composition declaring the minimum, and nothing in the corpus declares what share inference may have.
    * **A truncated download passes every size-free check and only the publisher's own digest refuses it.** Two of the five weights ended at `curl` exit 56 at 57% and 32% of their length; both loaded far enough to look like files, and the tree listing's SHA-256 is what caught them before either was measured. A weight verified by existence, or by a self-computed digest, would have produced figures for a truncated model.
    * **The KV cache's memory class is a placement nobody has taken, and the budget's second-largest term rides on it.** R-18-004a(vii) places the model wholly on the second class and fixes nothing about the cache; R-15-247s's second list names bulk by volume and model weights and names no cache, and its criterion is latency-criticality, which a cache read once per generated token can be argued either side of. At the declared context that cache is 1.21 GB under an f16 format and 0.64 GB under `q8_0`, a third to a half of the weight stream, so the class it sits on moves the measured four-bit demand between 12.46 and 18.50 GB/s. The report states both readings and decides neither.
  * Landed: Tier A.

#### Q5a · Export the placement problem and search it over the standing plan

  * **The problem is read out of the proof file and typed in nowhere.** [vos/memplan.py](../tools/vos/memplan.py) reads [MemoryPlan.v](../proofs/MemoryPlan.v)'s `Definition demo_* : list` chains, the literal fields of `build_plan`, every `: Plan := build_plan` application, `placed_by_name`'s arms, `criterion_class` and the `RegionKind` constructors, and emits [tools/generated/memory-plan.json](../tools/generated/memory-plan.json) with the file's md5 in its header. It is the third host row of the generated table, so **K-88** decides its byte identity at every gate and `run.py check --fix` regenerates it; `run.py placement export` and `check` are the same emitter by hand. A list absent or malformed, a kind no constructor spells, a literal missing, a `placed_by_name` deciding fewer kinds than the inductive carries or a standing plan no longer built is a `PlanError` the generated group reports as its finding, never an empty roster, on the fail-closed ground that group states.
  * **Four fields the item's export list names are declared absent by name rather than invented.** Owner, bank, reserved size and the slot's timing bound are no field of the plan: the charge is read over region indices with no line item, the bank map is R-15-228a's input read only as an island map, a region's reservation is exactly its length, and the slot bound is [CyclicExecutive.v](../proofs/CyclicExecutive.v)'s `Frame`. Each is carried in the artifact with its ground, and whether the plan gains an owner or a `bank_of` field is a register act reported below and not taken.
  * **The check is a port of the file's own definitions and it admits nothing.** One Python function per Gallina definition over Python integers, `containment_ok`, `colouring_ok`, `slot_bases_quantized`, `slot_lengths_quantized`, `plan_ok`, `places_ok`, `slot_indices_held`, `pool_fits`, `placement_delta` and R-15-007c's `representable_granule`, each docstring naming what it ports. [The tests](../tools/tests/test_memplan.py) hold the port to the file's own verdict on the demo plan and on every variant plan it builds, hold the granule port to the figures the file states at the threshold and to every declared granule count of the demo plan, and show the literal reading of R-08-014 refusing the shared-slot plan the file admits, which is the inversion M1.9 reported and the reading this port deliberately does not take. As the item's own text puts it, the validator is development hygiene and the proof status stays with the `.v`.
  * **The search is per island, complete over a declared set, and never the admitter.** The candidate set is stated once in the tool and printed at the head of every report: every assignment of slot bases to an island's regions at multiples of the region's step, the step being the least common multiple of its R-15-007c granule and the island's quantum, the quantum being the gcd of the standing plan's bases in that island from the island's base, with the other islands held and islands, classes, kinds, lengths, live ranges, fetch counts and charged slots fixed. A prefix is pruned only where the file's own pairwise colouring test refuses it, every leaf is the whole plan handed to the port, and the ranking reads scores of what the port admitted. The finer grid at every multiple of each region's granule alone is counted and printed beside the enumerated set and is enumerated nowhere. R-08-012a's first term, the footprint, is a function of live ranges and lengths and no base moves it, so the report prints it unmoved on both sides and ranks on span used and then padding.
  * **[The search contract](placement-search.md)** states the export's shape, the candidate-set predicate, the objectives in the register's order with what a base can and cannot move, the eight checks and the entry each decides for, the report's shape and eight refusals PS-1 to PS-8; it restates no figure the tool computes, on M0.17's precedent.
  * **No solver is added, and the ground is the item's own order.** The enumeration completes over its declared set without one, so nothing couples a placement to a scheduling search; the brief's wheel-availability ground was measured and found false, as the findings below record.
  * Net change: 2,113 lines added and 7 removed across nine files, five of them new, at `2957352` by `git diff --numstat cb28fa5..HEAD`.
  * Exit evidence, at `2957352`: the export reads **8 regions**, **34 typed lists**, **4 literal fields** and **16 plans** from [MemoryPlan.v](../proofs/MemoryPlan.v) at md5 `79516919a1598797065a565db8e35fa2` (predicate: every `Definition <name> : list nat|bool|RegionKind` whose body is a `cons` chain, every literal field of `build_plan`, every `: Plan := build_plan` application; the one `app`-defined list is named unread) and declares **4** fields absent by name; **K-88** decides **3** host rows outright of its **4** artifacts, one of them new, and the artifact re-emits byte-identical (**8,737 bytes**) in a second clone of the branch at the same hash; the three host gates green with every one of **115** mutants killed over the **94** rules the registry carries, the new K-88 case among them, the repair path holding and the registry covered, taken with `python tools/run.py` plain and no `--fix`; `run.py test` at **401 cases over 42 files**, 7 of them the new module's; ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5 clean; the enumeration complete over **776,736** candidates in island 0 (the product of 31, 32, 29 and 27 bases per region at quantum 32) and **58,426,368** in island 1 (104, 114, 88 and 56 at quantum 16), of which **255,024** and **120** leaves reach the port and every one of them is admitted, **521,712** and **58,426,248** grid points being decided at a refused pair, the standing plan among the candidates in both; island 0 standing versus best on span used, unused reservation and padding **416 / 32 / 0** against **384 / 0 / 0** with the footprint **384** on both sides, island 1 **1,904 / 16 / 16** against **1,888 / 0 / 0** with the footprint **1,888** on both sides, and the file's own `shared_slot_plan` is the best found in neither island (it moves region 4 onto region 2's slot and leaves regions 6 and 7 where the demo plan puts them); worst-case timing the sum of placement deltas at **5** on both sides, `class_of` being the register's placement; status line *complete enumeration over the declared set; not an optimality claim outside that set; admitted workload: n/a, no composed roster (R-18-004d unauthored, Q1 open)*; the search at exit 0 in 69 s and the wave at exit 0 in 86 s, both with `Win32_Battery.BatteryStatus` reading 2, on AC, at the time taken; `pypi.org/pypi/ortools/json` read 2026-09-05 giving ortools 9.15.6755 with 34 wheels over the tags cp39, cp310, cp311, cp312, cp313 and cp314, both lanes at Python 3.14 (host 3.14.7, guest 3.14.4). No admitted-workload comparison is taken, the plan's figures being witness values carrying no composition claim and no composed roster existing. No register, coverage-matrix, absence-contract, crown-jewel, spec or critique edit; the co-read ledger unmoved; no `run.py evidence` sweep owed, the model being neither read nor written; no gitlink populated and no build lane opened.
  * Eight findings.
    * **The plan carries no owner and no bank, so two of the register's own terms cannot be exported.** R-08-012a's third locality term is bank spreading over banks the plan does not model, R-15-228a making the map an input the plan reads only as `island_of`, `island_base` and `island_span`, and R-08-045's per-line-item charge is read over `placed` as region indices with no line item named. The export declares both absent with their grounds. Whether R-08-011's plan gains a `bank_of` and an owner field is owed at R-08-011 and R-08-012a and is not taken here.
    * **The footprint is not a function of bases, so a base search cannot reach R-08-012a's first objective.** The proven simultaneous peak is decided by live ranges and lengths alone; what bases move is span used, unused reservation with it one for one, and padding. The report prints the footprint unmoved on both sides and the contract refuses a footprint gain attributed to a base search (PS-7).
    * **The improvement found is over witness values and credits nothing.** The demo plan's bases were chosen to exhibit refutations, region 6 at 224 being the odd-base witness's neighbour, so a packing 32 bytes tighter in island 0 and 16 in island 1 proves the machinery reaches an admitted assignment the standing one is bettered by and says nothing about the product; the checklist's gains table is not realized by it.
    * **The brief's ground for refusing OR-Tools was false the day it was written, and the refusal stands on the item's own order instead.** The scout recorded ortools 9.15.6755 as shipping cp310 to cp313 wheels only; the same PyPI record read 2026-09-05 lists 34 wheels over cp39 through cp314. The item admits CP-SAT only where a coupled placement or scheduling search justifies it, and the enumeration completes over its declared set with nothing coupled, so no dependency is selected and no licence is read.
    * **The candidate set is chosen by the plan it compares against.** The quantum is the gcd of the standing plan's own bases from the island's base, which is what makes the standing plan a member and the set small enough to walk, and it means a standing plan at coarser offsets yields a coarser grid; the finer granule grid (356,944,861,377 and 7,321,746,600 points at `2957352`) is counted and printed beside the enumerated one and never walked. Completeness is over the declared set and the contract refuses reading it past that (PS-3).
    * **The contract restates two facts the tool owns and no rule holds the restatement.** §2 quotes `PREDICATE` verbatim and §4's table restates `CONSTRAINTS`'s check-to-entry pairs; a change to either in [vos/memplan.py](../tools/vos/memplan.py) leaves the document stale with no finding. K-101 is not allocated and no new rule is added here; a K-68 co-stated pin or a rule of K-58's shape is the holder, and this is why the landing is Tier A rather than Tier B under K-88.
    * **`docs/critique.md:154` becomes a claim to re-read on landing.** The paragraph says nothing in the corpus prices packings; this landing prices a packing on span used, unused reservation and padding over a witness plan, and the sentence's surrounding paragraph (critique.md:144-159), which names no item, is not answered by a witness-plan search and stays.
    * **The comparison on admitted workload is Q5b's and owed behind Q1 and a composed roster.** No composed roster exists to compare on: the demo figures are witness values, R-18-004d's demonstration set is unauthored, R-18-004b's per-class resident bytes are summed by a whole-program plan that does not yet exist, and the absolute limits are Q1's to state. The report's status line says so and the cost inputs print as symbols, none read and none rounded, the two fetch constants being witness values and the bank-count contract's coefficients pending.
  * Landed: Tier A.

## M0 · Hardware reference

### M0.1 · Pin upstream models

  * `sail-riscv` pinned at `beaf4499`; `sail-cheri-riscv` pinned at `bb07488d`. The vendoring baseline is `8f91355e` (M0.6a); the pin tracks the edition the curation is reconciled against, so advancing it is the act of carrying an upstream change across rather than a record of where the tree came from.
  * Finding: the CHERI tree embeds older `sail-riscv` commit `b748a82`; reconciliation therefore requires a semantic transplant, not configuration of one shared base.
  * Upstream carriage is a **disposition per commit** rather than a merge: the curated tree has deleted most of what upstream touches, so a change is applied, or refused with the deletion that makes it inapplicable, or refused because carrying it would be a register act. Against `8f91355e..beaf4499`, five apply and nineteen do not.
    * The `Zvknh` predicate grouping (#1881) **applies**, a missing parenthesis that let `zvknhab_check_encdec` guard only the `Zvknhb` disjunct, landing in `zvknhab_insts.sail`.
    * The `config_file` build dependency (#1885) **applies** to the two prover-backend rules that survive, SMT and Rocq, the three `rmem` rules it also fixed having gone with the dormant targets at c1.
    * The widening-accumulate source-overlap checks (#1884) **apply** whole, `vd` being read as well as written by `vwmacc*`, `vfwmacc*` and `vfwmaccbf16` while the overlap chain guarded only the destination: eight `valid_src_overlap` guards across five forms in three instruction files.
    * The WFI external-interrupt wake (#1868) is **inapplicable**: its bug is that `MEIP`/`SEIP` are not held in `mip`, and M0.6d deleted those fields, their sources, and `read_mip` itself, leaving `mip` carrying `MTI` alone and the bare read total.
    * The Hypervisor extension (#1769) is **inapplicable in every hunk**, R-15-003 and R-15-006 excluding the hypervisor, each hunk being conditioned on virtualization this tree does not have. That covers the two fragments that read as general: the `memory_exception` signature change belongs to an `ExceptionContext` restructuring whose three added fields have no reader here, and the `currentlyEnabled_measure` correction names three extensions the measure table lost with the scalar F/D and Zc\* deletions.
    * The `mhpmevent` VSINH/VUINH relaxation (#1889) is **inapplicable** twice over, conditioned on H and landing in a `Zihpm/zihpm.sail` that went with R-15-077's deletion of the counters.
    * The ELF-load bounds checks (#1877) **apply** whole: a loaded segment must fit one defined memory region, and no two loads may overlap. `ModelImpl::memory_regions()` joins `main_memory_regions()` as the unfiltered reading of `pma_regions`, `elf_info` gains a `loaded_regions` vector that the device-tree write and the ELF loader both record into, and `write_dtb_to_rom` takes the filename and that record rather than the bytes, so the device tree is checked against the segments already placed and names itself in the diagnostic.
    * The gdbserver fixes (#1888) **apply in part**. The `save-restore` and `group` attributes on the integer registers and the PC, the register map cached in `protocol_handler` and reached through it by the three register responses, and the `m_insns_per_tick` type correction all land. The hunks lifting the `D` check off the `F` check have no site, c4 having deleted the floating-point annex the target XML would emit (R-15-039), and the `regnum` spacing repair sits inside that same deleted annex.
    * Ten changes are **inapplicable on one ground**, that the privilege mode each extends is not implemented. Svbare (#1894) and Sstvecd (#1903) are named in [extensions.sail](../model/model/core/extensions.sail) among the supervisor extensions deleted with the mode (R-15-003) and the translation modes deleted with the MMU (R-15-002, R-15-038), so there is no `satp` to legalize and no `stvec` to configure. Shgatpa (#1893), Shcounterenw (#1898), Shvstvecd (#1902), Shvstvala (#1908), Shtvala (#1909) and Shvsatpa (#1906) each register an `Ext_Sh*` and legalize an `hgatp`, `hcounteren`, `vstvec`, `htval` or `vsatp` this tree carries none of, R-15-003 and R-15-006 excluding the hypervisor; #1898 is inapplicable twice over, `hcounteren` also naming the counters R-15-077 deletes. Bare translation with no S-mode (#1863) lands in a `sys/vmem.sail` c3 deleted when `translateAddr` collapsed to the identity, and the TLB trace log (#1905) splits `print_tlb` on a `supports_hypervisor()` predicate over a TLB the same batch removed.
    * The `pmlen_of_mode` restoration (#1907) is **inapplicable**: c2 deleted pointer masking whole, and its one hunk lands in `extensions/pointer_masking/pm_utils.sail`, which went with it.
    * The misaligned LR/SC relaxation (#1900) is **inapplicable in every hunk**: `Zalrsc` went at c3 (R-15-025), so neither `GlobalMisalignedExceptions` nor `PMAMisalignedExceptions` carries the `lrsc` row it reshapes, the configuration carries no such key, the `LoadReserved` and `StoreConditional` access kinds its new predicate matches on do not exist, and `sys/vmem_utils.sail`, where its largest hunk lands, is the file c3 renamed `sys/mem_access.sail` as its surviving content moved.
    * The Xvisor boot target (#1892) is **inapplicable**: it restructures `os-boot/`, which went at S3 with `doc/` beside it.
    * The config schema in the release artifacts (#1919) is **inapplicable** on the same deletion, its one hunk landing in `.github/workflows/release.yml`.
    * The XUANTIE hypervisor test suite (#1890) is **inapplicable** three ways over: the suite is the hypervisor's, the workflows it also edits went with `.github/`, and its release bump would move `TEST_DOWNLOAD_VERSION` off `2026-06-10`, the edition every landed refusal figure is measured at. Upstream's `2026-08-20` is therefore a divergence this tree holds on purpose, and no rule in [tools/check.py](../tools/check.py) reads it.
    * `Zilx` (#1858) is **refused on a ground that is not a deletion**: the profile's extension set is frozen and normative, so admitting a nineteen-instruction experimental extension is a register act rerunning the review gate (R-05-150, R-18-034) rather than a carriage. Its first-party coverage also lands in a `test/first_party/` M0.18 retired. The refusal is also not close: all but three of its forms are the base-ISA width ladder [cheri_custom.sail](../model/model/extensions/CHERI/cheri_custom.sail) refuses by name; of the three doubleword forms, `lxsd` and `lxd` are what `cld`'s scale field already spells as its scale-3 and scale-0 cases and the third, the zero-extended-index `lxsuwd`, has no consumer named anywhere in this tree; and it has no store form where the profile's indexed row is a load and store pair. It opens no authority path outside capabilities either, the merged register file resolving its base to a capability that `cap_mode_auth` authorises like any other load's, so it fails no admission test and is not worth its opcode points. Two things it is not: a re-pin target for the capability indexed load, its base being an integer register, so it puts no capability instruction on an upstream track and leaves [the profile](isa-profile.md)'s `partial` re-pin cell with nothing to exchange; and evidence on R-15-007g's question of whether the scale immediate earns its bits, implicit scaling being a different design rather than a measurement of this profile's emitted mix. What reopens it is a named new fact, a store form, an explicit scale immediate or a ratification milestone.

### M0.2 · Stand up the Sail toolchain

  * WSL Ubuntu 26.04, opam 2.5.0, OCaml 5.4.0, Sail 0.20.2, and z3 5.1.0.
  * Sail 0.20.2 is fidelity-pinned and installed as `opam install sail.0.20.2`, the bare `opam install sail` resolving to whatever is current and dropping the pin silently; the OCaml the switch is built on stays the distribution's, fidelity being a property of the emitter.
  * The solver is pinned on a weaker ground worth stating: soundness is not a property of which sound solver answered, so no version is *owed*. The pin buys a solver this tree names rather than one the archive happens to carry, checked before the move by typechecking the model under 4.13.3, 4.16.0 and 5.1.0 for the same verdict; it costs the distribution's patching, which becomes this tree's to carry.

### M0.3 · Build stock `sail-riscv` baseline

  * `sail_riscv_sim` built out of tree; 664/664 tests pass.
  * Build scripts raise the OCaml stack limit with `ulimit -s 131072`.

### M0.4 · Build stock `sail-cheri-riscv` baseline

  * `cheri_riscv_sim_RV64` built against embedded commit `b748a82`; 229/229 bundled RV64 tests pass under the repository’s C-emulator protocol.
  * This build remains the capability-semantics oracle; no upstream CHERI test corpus is bundled.

### M0.5 · Reconcile the baselines

  * Use current `sail-riscv` as the base and transplant the CTSRD capability layer.
  * Rationale: the current base contains required modern extensions and tooling, while excluded CHERI features substantially reduce the transplant surface.
  * AIA exists on neither base and is fresh work under M0.6g.

### M0.6a · Stand up the curated tree

  * Vendored `model/` byte-identically from `8f91355e` with LF normalization fixed.
  * `tools/run.py model build` builds out of tree with the larger OCaml stack.
  * Exit evidence: build green; 664/664 tests pass.

### M0.6b · Measure configuration-level curation

  * `model/config/verifiedos.json` captures all profile rows supported by the config schema.
  * Profile run: 132/134 adopted-family physical tests pass; both failures are expected profile refusals.
  * Source-level residue: scalar F/D coupling, CSR narrowing and trap totality, `vstart`, and AIA.
  * Decision: configuration identifies the deletion worklist but does not substitute for source removal.

### M0.6c · Remove excluded source surface

  * For every removed extension, update the project file, extension directory, extension registry, config schema, test lists, and stray config readers.
  * Require `tools/run.py model typecheck`, full build, schema validation, and profile-subset tests after each batch.

#### c1 · Leaf extensions

  * Removed unentangled excluded modules, obsolete reservation-size declarations, entropy hooks, dormant targets, and the virtual-memory test half made unusable by removing `FENCE.I`.
  * Net change: 3,079 lines removed and 77 added across 50 files.
  * Exit evidence: build green; 352/352 tests pass; generated and profile config keys match exactly.
  * Expected profile refusals: 26 of 199 physical-variant tests.
  * Reservation plumbing remained for c3, which took it.

#### c2 · Entangled extensions

  * Removed `C` (`Zca`, `Zcb`, `Zcf`, `Zcd`), `Zicbom`, `Zicbop`, pointer masking, `Stateen`, CFI/`Zicfilp`, `Smcntrpmf`, and `Zicntr` with their core and system hooks: the `cacheop` union narrowed to `CB_zero` alone and its arms dropped from the PMA, PMP, page-table, and fault-type matches; `mseccfg` deleted outright; `MPELP`/`SPELP`/`LPE`/`PMM`/`CBIE`/`CBCFE` gone from `mstatus`, `sstatus`, and the `envcfg` pair; `misa[C]` hardwired to zero with the `ext_veto_disable_C` hook and its file; the landing-pad checks out of the step function; and `transform_effective_address` reduced to the identity.
  * Net change: 2,494 lines removed and 69 added across 47 files, 19 of them deleted.
  * Exit evidence: build green; 346/346 tests pass; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema with key sets consistent.
  * Expected profile refusals: 27 of 199 physical-variant tests, every one explained: the three c1 cuts, the standing M0.6b pair, nine supervisor and PMP tests c3 takes, ten `Zfh` tests the profile excludes, plus `zicntr` with `instret_overflow` and `uc-p-rvc` from this batch. The sweep now caps each run at ten seconds, which books `rv64si-p-dirty`, whose handler never terminates with S off, as a refusal rather than a hang.
  * Three findings.
    * Fixed-width fetch is a *deletion of gating* **and** of the 16-bit path, this batch taking the first half and M0.20 the second: ILEN=32 makes a word whose low two bits are not `11` an unallocated 32-bit encoding rather than a shorter instruction, so the parcel check goes with `C_ILLEGAL` and the compressed decode mapping it was the only user of, and nothing is left load-bearing with no clause behind it.
    * The alignment relaxation lives in four places rather than one, `fetch`, `rvfi_fetch`, `jump_to`, and the `xepc` legalization pair, and only the first two are visible from the extension registry.
    * The Lean emulator's handwritten register initializer was already stale from c1, writing `stimecmp` which went with `Sstc`; the typechecker cannot see it because that target is dormant. It and the two `SAIL_MODULES` lists naming deleted modules are corrected here.

#### c3 · Privilege and translation batch

  * Removed S and U modes with delegation and the whole S-mode CSR bank, `satp` with the `Sv*` walkers, TLB and `sfence.vma`, PMP, the reservation plumbing, and the performance counters. The privilege *type* goes with the modes: `Privilege`, `cur_privilege`, and the privilege parameter threaded through `is_CSR_accessible`, `mem_read`, `mem_write_*` and `pmaCheck` all had exactly one reachable value. `mstatus` narrows to the extension-context gate, `mret` restores the interrupt-enable stack and nothing else, `sret` is gone, `mip`/`mie` keep only their machine bits, `translateAddr` collapses into the identity `physical_address`, and `menvcfg`/`senvcfg` go with the mode their every bit gated.
  * Net change: 5,006 lines removed and 742 added across 68 files, seven deleted outright and three renamed as their surviving content moved (`vmem_types.sail` → `mem_access_types.sail`, `vmem_utils.sail` → `mem_access.sail` with `vmem_read`/`vmem_write` → `data_read`/`data_write`, and the `mstatus` SXL/UXL unit test replaced by one over the properties this batch establishes).
  * Exit evidence: build green; 329/329 tests pass; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema with key sets consistent.
  * Expected profile refusals: 28 of 199 physical-variant tests, every one explained: ten `Zfh` tests the profile excludes, the three c1 cuts, the three c2 cuts, the standing M0.6b pair, and this batch's ten, being the seven supervisor tests, `pmpaddr`, `ssvnapot-p-napot`, and the RV64 machine-CSR test. Under the max configuration the same cut excludes 17 tests, the RV32 and RV64 halves of the supervisor family and `pmpaddr` plus the two RV64-only rows; the RV32 machine-CSR test still passes and is not excluded.
  * Four findings.
    * `minstret` had a consumer that is not architectural, RVFI's `order` field, so deleting the counter would have deleted the differential rig's instruction ordering with it; a model-internal `retire_count` register with no CSR address replaces it.
    * The reservation flag on the memory API was never only that: `res`/`con` selected the reserved-read and conditional-write kinds of the concurrency interface, and after `Zalrsc` its one caller is the AMO, whose two halves are exactly those. So the parameter is *derived* from `is_amo_access(access)` rather than deleted, and stops being passable independently of the access it describes.
    * Two tests hang rather than fail and so cannot be left to a ctest timeout: `rv32si-p-dirty` and `rv64si-p-dirty`, whose handlers wait on a page-table A/D update that never comes with S off, the behaviour c2 booked as a profile refusal now appearing where it blocks.
    * `pagesize_bits` survives the walker: PMA regions must still be 4 KiB-aligned so that no PMA property changes inside one access, so the constant is renamed `pma_granule_bits` rather than deleted.

#### c4 · Vector fork

  * Removed `vstart` and, with scalar floating point, the whole coupling it was named beside: the `f0`–`f31` register file, `fcsr`/`frm`/`fflags`, `mstatus.FS`, the `F`/`D`/`Zfh`/`Zfhmin`/`Zfa`/`Zfinx`/`Zdinx`/`Zhinx`/`Zhinxmin` extensions with their instruction files, and the scalar half of the bf16 converts. The two forks the profile books (R-15-040, R-15-040a) are now the model's shape rather than a residue in its configuration: `Zve32f` no longer requires `F` and `Zve64d` no longer requires `D`, and the validator rule that demanded them is gone.
  * The vector unit keeps every floating-point form it had here, and M0.19 has since reclaimed two of the three encodings the next bullet books as the freeze's. The scalar operand of a `.vf` instruction is the low SEW bits of an **integer** register, which is where a soft-float-register calling convention already puts a floating-point scalar (R-15-039, R-15-040, [spec.md](spec.md) §15); `vfmv.f.s` writes its element zero-extended into one rather than NaN-boxed, there being no wider register to box into; and rounding is the static RNE of R-15-083, since RVV encodes no rounding-mode field and the conversions that name round-toward-zero pass it themselves.
  * Net change: 5,448 lines removed and 430 added across 56 files, nine deleted outright. The `c_emulator` harness is in that count: the GDB stub's floating-point annex, the `--trace-fpr` option, and the `freg`/`fcsr` accessors and write callback all named registers that no longer exist.
  * Exit evidence: build green; 261/261 tests pass; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema and its key set agrees with the generated max configuration at 129 keys ([tools/run.py model config-keys](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)), 29 fewer than M0.6d's 158.
  * Expected profile refusals: 53 of 199 physical-variant tests, 52 refusals and the standing `rv64si-p-dirty` hang. This batch adds 24 to M0.6d's 29: eleven `rv64uf`, twelve `rv64ud`, and one more `rv64uzfh`, the test that had passed on F/D register moves while the residue stood. The ctest suite loses the same three families in both XLENs, 66 tests, which is the whole of 327 → 261.
  * Four findings.
    * **The `.vf` forms had to be re-homed rather than deleted**: ten instruction families name an `f` register upstream, and deleting the register file leaves the encodings needing a source, so the operand moves to the integer file and no encoding changes. The cost is that `vfmv.v.f` and `vfmv.s.f` become bit-for-bit duplicates of `vmv.v.x` and `vmv.s.x` and `vfmv.f.s` differs from `vmv.x.s` only in zero- versus sign-extension, which is three encodings the freeze can reclaim rather than a curation batch's call.
    * **`fflags` has no destination and two comparisons still read it**: 44 accrual sites go, and the softfloat interface's flag half is discarded except at `fp_gt`/`fp_ge`, where the invalid-operation flag *is* the result, a quiet comparison answering false on a NaN. The software-visible consequence is that no sticky IEEE flag is readable, so a program needing to detect an invalid or inexact result tests the result.
    * **The scalar files were carrying the vector unit's classification predicates**, 33 `f_is_*_{H,S,D}` plus `negate_{H,S,D}`, and the float library's width-generic `float_is_*` are the same tests, so each three-way dispatch collapses to one line and the negate to a sign-bit flip; only `canonical_NaN` and three flag constructors moved, to [float_classify.sail](../model/model/core/float_classify.sail).
    * **The `vstart` cut deleted an implementation-compatibility hack**: the four scalar-move instructions computed an LMUL-based element count only to match the bound other implementations chose for the `vstart` check, so the count, the LMUL read and the question go together; the whole-register load and store lose their partial-register prologue and the vector-crypto element-group start collapses to zero.
  * One residue, booked rather than closed. R-15-039a states that a vector operation is all-or-nothing while the model writes elements as it goes, so a fault at element *i* leaves the earlier elements written with nothing recording where. On this machine that is a fail-stop program error rather than a resumable trap; whether the register-group write should be buffered to make the claim literal belongs to the capability-fault path M0.6e authors and is settled there.

### M0.6d · Close the CSR bank

  * Narrowed `mie`/`mip` to the machine-timer bits, hardwired the implementation identifiers to zero, made `misa` read-only, and deleted `Sdtrig`'s `tselect`. `MEIE`/`MEIP` and `MSIE`/`MSIP` are gone as *fields*: the `Minterrupts` bitfield carries `MTI` alone, so the unnamed bits of the underlying `xlenbits` are the hardwiring and no legalization can set them. `mip` becomes read-only outright, its one writer the timer comparator.
  * Net change: 455 lines removed and 135 added across 19 files, three deleted outright. Two stragglers of c3's delegation cut go with them, both dead rather than wrong: a CMake variable holding the delegatable-bits mask nothing has read since `mideleg` left, and the `WFI` comment stating an exemption over a register that no longer exists.
  * Exit evidence: build green; 327/327 tests pass; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema and its key set agrees with the generated max configuration at 158 keys ([tools/run.py model config-keys](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)).
  * Expected profile refusals: 29 of 199 physical-variant tests, every one explained: ten `Zfh` tests the profile excludes, the three c1 cuts, the three c2 cuts, the standing M0.6b pair, c3's ten, and this batch's one, `mi-p-breakpoint`, which opens by writing `tselect` to count the triggers it may use. The sweep is now a repository instrument rather than a per-batch shell loop ([tools/run.py model sweep](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)), which is also what classifies a hang as a refusal that blocks rather than leaving it to a ctest timeout.
  * Four findings.
    * The trap on an unallocated CSR address was already total and needed no work: `is_CSR_accessible` defaults to false in `csr_end.sail`, so `check_CSR` rejects every address no clause claims, and c3's exclusion of `mi-p-pmpaddr` for trapping rather than reading zero is the standing evidence. This batch adds four more addresses, `tselect` and `tdata1-3`, whose name-map rows went with the register.
    * Deleting the two interrupt *fields* deletes their two sources with them: the Simple Interrupt Generator device existed only to drive `MEIP`/`MSIP` and goes entirely, and the CLINT's `msip` door with it, leaving the CLINT as the machine timer and nothing else.
    * The external-interrupt bit was the sole reason `mip` read one value into `rd` and another into its own read-modify-write, so its deletion collapses `doCSR`'s two-value split into one and takes the platform-interrupt OR, the `XipReadType` enum, and `read_mip` with it.
    * Two enumerations follow the one-reachable-value rule c3 applied to `Privilege`: `InterruptType` keeps `I_M_Timer` alone, and `breakpoint_cause` goes entirely, `ebreak` being the only breakpoint source once the trigger module is deleted, which also removes a non-injective mapping whose two arms both encoded cause 3.

### M0.6e · Transplant CHERI capability semantics

  * Port capability types and compression, merged registers, tag memory, PCC/sentries, machine trap capabilities, load/store checks, and ISAv9 trap causes.
  * Differentially compare RVFI-style traces with the M0.4 oracle.
  * The transplant carries ISAv9's **128-bit** parameterization rather than the frozen one, and that is the batch's shape rather than a delay: the M0.4 oracle is the differential reference, it is a reference only while the two models agree on the format, and what this batch owes M0.6f is an algebra whose field widths are the only thing that has to move (R-15-007a).
  * The extension hooks the transplant hangs off (`ext_data_get_addr`, `ext_control_check_pc`, `ext_fetch_check_pc`, `ext_check_CSR`) have been in their final, privilege-free shape since c3.

#### e1 · Fix XLEN at 64

  * `xlen` stops being a configuration key and becomes the constant 64, and the RV32 configuration goes with it: the generated `rv32*` configurations, the `--rv32` default and its embedded JSON, the RV32 test lanes in all three corpora, and the RV32 prover-backend target. The base is RV64 (R-15-001), no plain-RV32 target exists anywhere on the platform (R-18-002), and there is one model of one machine (R-15-005).
  * Net change: 385 lines removed and 254 added across 40 files. Deleted with the base: `mstatush`, which is the RV32 high half of `mstatus` and whose address is now unallocated and therefore trapping (R-15-014); the `Architecture` enumeration with its two-bit encoding and the `architecture()` and `in32BitMode()` queries over it, `misa.MXL` becoming the constant `0b10`; `Zbkb`'s RV32-only `zip` and `unzip`; the RV32 encodings of `zext.h` and `rev8`; the RV32 arms of the `Zbs` mask computation; and `asidlen`, left over from address translation. Thirty-seven vacuous `xlen == 64` guards collapse with them.
  * Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema and its key set agrees with the generated max configuration at 128 keys ([tools/run.py model config-keys](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)), one fewer than c4's 129, the key being `base.xlen`. The RV64 profile sweep is unchanged at 53 of 199, 52 refusals and the standing `rv64si-p-dirty` hang, which is the point of running it: the freeze removes no RV64 surface, and 261 ctest cases become 150 because the corpus carried an `rv32*` half for a machine this model can no longer be asked for.
  * Four findings.
    * **The transplant forces this cut rather than merely profiting from it.** A register is a capability whose address field is the register width (R-15-007i), and the 128-bit encoding e2 ports has no 32-bit instantiation in this tree, so an RV32 configuration would be a machine with no capability format to run under.
    * **Fixing XLEN changes the generated C++ interface and not only the model.** A configuration-dependent `bits(xlen)` is emitted as the length-carrying `sbits` and a static one as a native `uint64_t`, so sixteen callback signatures in the emulator harness follow it, and the physical-address ones follow too because `physaddrbits_len` was conditional on XLEN as well.
    * **`in32BitMode` was already unreachable and the enumeration under it nearly so**, the one-reachable-value rule c3 applied to `Privilege` arriving here from the configuration rather than from a deleted mode.
    * **The RV32 half of the corpus was never evidence for this profile**, exercising a base the profile does not have, so its removal costs no coverage and every excluded RV64 row is still excluded for the reason it was.

#### e2 · Port the capability format and algebra

  * [cap_format.sail](../model/model/core/cap_format.sail) carries the field widths and the packed encoding; [cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) carries CHERI Concentrate over them: decode and encode, the null-capability memory transform, bounds, permissions, sealing, and the monotone derivations with their representability checks. 638 lines of Sail across three new files, the third being the unit test below, with the upstream licence vendored as `model/LICENCE.cheri` because the port derives from a BSD-2-Clause artifact this repository otherwise holds only as a submodule.
  * The capability layer lands in `core/` rather than under `extensions/`: the machine is purecap-only (R-15-001, R-18-002), so the capability *is* the register type, there is no hybrid mode to gate, and an extension gate would be a configuration key with one admitted value. The extension directory takes the instruction surface in e5.
  * Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass, and the eight properties in [test_capability.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_capability.sail) pass in the model's own `$[test]` harness: the all-zeroes granule decodes as untagged NULL and NULL stores back as all zeroes (R-15-182, R-15-060), encode and decode round-trip, the reset capability spans the address space, an aligned narrowing is exact and reads back, a five-byte narrowing is byte-exact (R-15-007c), an unrepresentable address move yields an untagged result rather than a trap (R-15-007h), sealing changes only the object type, and permissions round-trip.
  * Two findings.
    * **The relocation guards are dead at the source and are not carried across.** `have_cheri_relocation` is a constant `false` upstream and both the PCC and DDC relocation paths hang off it, so the port folds them out rather than transplanting unreachable branches into a model with no DDC to relocate.
