  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at 36 properties; the corpus green at 11 of 11 with every trace digest matching; and the checker green on **all 52 rules** with every mutant killed, the two new ones being that the manifest and the document name the same members and that every program assembles with the check count it records.
  * Two findings, and the first is what a whole-program corpus is for.
    * **A fetch of an instruction wholly inside PCC's bounds raised a length violation**, which `cap-control`'s bounded sentry entry found on its first run. The profile excludes `C` and fixes ILEN at 32, so `min_instruction_bytes` is four; it does not adopt `Ziccif`, so a fetch still splits into two 16-bit granules; and the per-granule bounds check asked for a whole instruction from the *second* granule, two bytes past the instruction. A capability bounded exactly to the end of its last instruction was therefore unusable as an entry point, which is the shape R-15-071's bounded domain entry takes. The check now takes the width its caller reads, and two properties in [test_capability.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_capability.sail) hold both halves.
    * **The store-side root arrives in the link register**: reset hands `c1` the root data capability and `c1` is `ra`, so a program that calls overwrites its own authority. Every corpus member therefore opens by moving it to `c8`, and §3's composed initial distribution is where that stops being the program's problem.
  * Absences are recorded rather than left to be discovered, each closing with the milestone that supplies it, and [the corpus document's §7](differential-corpus.md#7-what-the-corpus-cannot-exercise-yet-and-why) owns the list rather than this note: two of the three recorded here were `cseal`'s success case and an access-system-registers violation, which stand; the third was the M0.6g and M0.6h instructions, and both items have since landed and put them in the model, so that absence is closed and §7 no longer carries it.

### M0.13 · Two-geometry CBO block-size constraint

  * The instrument is a sixth derived view, [the welded block-size constraint](block-geometry-constraint.md), governed by R-15-014a (vii): the candidate space, a closed table of twelve constraints each citing the entry that owns it and each labelled `derivable` or `owed`, the two geometries and why one parameter answers to both, the output matrix, and five refusals in the freeze contract's own idiom. **Six rows are derivable today and six are owed**, to R4 and R5 for the macro architecture and its measurement, to R5 alone for the single-access sense width, to R-17-041's unmeasured latency magnitudes and the M1 schedule for the per-block latencies and the sweep's preemption granularity, and to R5 and the freeze's second act for the bank count.
  * **K-57** in [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py) holds the number itself. It reads the four artifacts that write it, recomputes the candidate set from the granule R-15-203 fixes and the payload R-15-181a fixes rather than reading the set the document states, and requires every site to agree and to lie inside it, the set's ceiling held against R-15-007q's own statement of the bound as well as against the arithmetic that gives it. The sites and the entry are reported and never repaired: all four sites are under a `-text` tree, R-15-007q's ceiling is the normative statement rather than a copy of one, and which value inside the set is taken is R-15-014a's second act rather than arithmetic. The view's own four figures are none of those things, so `--fix` rewrites them from the arithmetic, and the two that state their figure a second way in the same sentence are rewritten as whole sentences.
  * Net change: 325 lines added and 13 removed across nine files, two of them new.
  * Exit evidence: the checker green on **all 58 rules against 57**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the six views the register obliges all present and the new one carrying all five of its bearing requirements; the floors group's uncounted enumerations at **fourteen against thirteen**; `check.py --fix` recomputing the derived-views figure in the README and the critique from the table rather than by hand; and both type checkers clean. No WSL run is in this item's evidence: `model/` is read but not written.
  * Five findings.
    * **The item cannot evaluate its own output, and saying so is the result rather than a shortfall.** The second-class axis needs a deck row width, a page size, and a single-access sense width, and none of the three exists anywhere in this repository, the device evidence in [the alternatives register](architectural-alternatives.md) being cell-level and process-level. R-15-247m forbids supplying one meanwhile, and the macro architecture that would state a row is R4's to author and R5's to measure. So the matrix ships with both axes empty and the document is the instrument that will fill it, which is what M0.11 did for the freeze report before a backend existed.
    * **The first-class half is computable now, and it turns on a ceiling the destination register sets.** `cloadtags` and `creclaim` both end by zero-extending `bits(caps_per_block)` into an integer destination, so a group wider than XLEN has nowhere to be returned and the model refuses it at typecheck. At the 8-byte granule that is **512 bytes**, and with one ECC codeword as the floor the derivable set is 32, 64, 128, 256, or 512 bytes.
    * **The declared 64 bytes is interior to that set, which is what makes the owed rows the interesting ones.** Neither derivable end holds the value where it is, so a search that scored the SRAM macro and stopped would return an interval that looked decided, which is the silent foreclosure R-15-014a (vii) is written against and why C7 and C8 are two rows rather than one.
    * **Holding the number meant letting one rule read the model, which the selftest's sandbox had made impossible.** `build_template` stands every `model/` path up as an empty file, and its own prose said no rule reads model content. The carve-out is a named list, `MODEL_FACTS`, read by the sandbox and the rule alike, so a path the rule reads and the list omits fails every sandbox's baseline loudly rather than passing on the host and going red later.
    * **Two of the four sites had no assertion behind them, which is what the rule is worth.** The model's declaration and the composition's are checked against each other at each of the four instructions. The generated test-matrix template and the harness's `caps_per_block` literal are transcriptions with nothing holding them, and the template is not JSON in any dialect, so it is read as the template it is rather than parsed.

### M0.14 · Two latency classes in the Sail model

  * The two classes land as composition-time declarations and nothing else: [core/memory_class.sail](../model/model/core/memory_class.sail) carries the enumeration and the per-class record, the placement is a PMA beside every other property the composition fixes about an address range ([sys/pma.sail](../model/model/sys/pma.sail)), the constants enter the attested devicetree R-15-247m puts them in ([postlude/device_tree.sail](../model/model/postlude/device_tree.sail)), and the shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried ([postlude/validate_config.sail](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail)). **No instruction, no access type, and nothing on the decode surface** (R-15-247h, [absence-contract.md](absence-contract.md) A-17): no access path calls the class query at all, a machine consulting the class at run time being the hierarchy R-15-247 refuses.
  * The record is eight fields per class, `qualified` beside seven numbers: usable density, bank count, the retention corner as a floor and a ceiling, and the four latency constants. Both configurations declare a second-class region beside the first-class one, at 8 GiB above the existing RAM, so both classes are live at distinct constants in the profile's own configuration and in the generated max configuration alike.
  * Net change: 907 lines added and 18 removed across 13 files, two of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **48 properties against 46**, two of them new; the differential corpus green at **18 of 18** with one member and 8 checks new and the seventeen existing digests unmoved; the generated devicetree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at 2,224 bytes, inside the 4 KiB ROM region it is written to; each of the six new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on all 54 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * Five findings.
    * **The record is symmetric and the machine is not, and the validator is where the difference is held.** R-15-247m names the same four groups per class, so the record is one shape read twice; R-15-247n makes the first class latched and the second charge-storing with no restoring feedback, so three fields have no meaning on the first class. They are declared zero there under the model's zero-is-absent idiom and the validator refuses anything else, rather than leaving a composition free to declare a refresh deadline over a latched array.
    * **The retention corner is two fields because only one of them is ever read.** R-15-247m states it as a measured interval rather than a point, and R-15-247c consumes retention by the refresh deadline alone, so a mean with a tolerance would invite the ceiling into a guarantee. A floor and a ceiling with one consumer named in one function is that requirement realized: a second consumer shows up as a new call site rather than as new arithmetic.
    * **There is no separate fetch constant to declare.** R-15-247j prices second-class code against "that class's fetch constant", and with R-15-164 deleting every cache there is no instruction cache and nothing to amortize, so a fetch is a read of whichever class the code resides on. The delta the placement rule makes admission-visible is the difference of two numbers the record already carries, computed by the static memory plan (M1.9).
    * **A placeholder nothing distinguishes from a measurement is how it becomes an input, so the qualification state is attested.** R-15-247m admits no density figure as an architectural input ahead of the measurement, and bring-up needs numbers before R5 has any. `qualified` is carried in the record and emitted into the devicetree, false in every configuration here, so a tree says on its face which it holds. No property asserts its value: a test fixing it would be a test about the calendar.
    * **The corpus member is a set of claims that nothing is observable, which is what makes it worth running.** R-15-247s puts no trust gradient on the boundary, so [platform-memclass.s](../corpus/platform-memclass.s) checks that a capability round trip, the tag a data store clears, `cloadtags`, `cbo.zero`, `cbo.scrub`, an atomic and a bounds violation all answer the same on the second class as on the first, and that the revocation filter is keyed by the loaded capability's base rather than by the class of the slot holding it. The real difference is a latency constant, invisible from inside the machine and exactly the quantity the memory plan charges placement against.

### M0.15 · Capacity, placement, and bank checks in register tooling

  * Three rules land, one per group the item names, each costing the three edits [tools/README.md](../tools/README.md) states. **K-54** in [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py) derives every stated tag-plane figure from the two register fields it is arithmetic over and repairs it under `--fix`: twelve claim sites across four documents, five spellings (`1.5625%`, `1.56%`, its halved comparator `0.78%`, `15.6` MB per GB, and four tag bits per codeword), all of them the 64-bit granule R-15-203 fixes and the 256-bit payload R-15-181a fixes. **K-55** in [floors.py](../tools/vos/checks/floors.py) holds five per-class capacity figures against the entries that own them and the per-class bank count against the three artifacts that book it open. **K-56** in [compounds.py](../tools/vos/checks/compounds.py) partitions twenty region classes between the two latency classes, three of them held at the entry that decides them rather than only in the criterion that collects them, and holds that partition total over the ten physical byte classes R-08-045 charges.
  * Net change: 387 lines added and 12 removed across eight files.
  * Exit evidence: the checker green on **all 57 rules against 54**, three of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered in both directions; the floors group's uncounted enumerations at **thirteen against ten**, the three new readings among them, so none of the three rules can pass over an empty table; `check.py --fix` rewriting nothing on the landed tree; and both type checkers clean against the pins. No WSL command is in this item's evidence: nothing here reads `model/`, which the checker's corpus excludes and the selftest's sandbox stands up empty.
  * Five findings.
    * **The first rule found a real disagreement on its first run, which is the argument for writing it.** [performance-estimates.md](performance-estimates.md) stated the ECC codeword at 128 data bits carrying two tag bits, where R-15-181a fixes the payload at 256 bits with four and names 128 the *fallback* for a macro that will not sense the wider unit. Tag bits per codeword is payload over granule, so the sentence was arithmetic over two register fields and `--fix` rewrote both halves. What it argues is untouched, that no data-side check-bit area moves when the granule halves; it was arguing it at the wrong codeword.
    * **The clause the item asks for has no operands, so the bank-count half ships as a booking rather than a comparison.** *A stated island bandwidth ceiling its bank grant cannot deliver fails the pass* needs two figures and only the count is stated, and that is a placeholder on its own face while `qualified` is false on both classes. The ceiling is not a figure at all: R-15-247p makes it fixed by the TDM schedule and the bank binding, so it is an output of the count under search times a per-bank width M0.8 owes and a slot share M1 owes, and R-15-228a forbids the memory plan from producing it. A prohibition over the corpus was tried and is the wrong instrument, firing on a wired link rate and on scanout's framebuffer draw. So K-55 holds the three bookings and the comparison arm opens when M0.8 and M1 land the operands.
    * **The tag plane is one field read five ways, which is why it is one rule rather than five claims.** A granule that moved would leave every spelling individually plausible and jointly wrong, the state no reader detects. The DECTED band beside them is the opposite case, *about one check bit per codeword* at no fixed width, so its figures are nobody's arithmetic and are held by inequality against the bare plane they include; the one figure that *is* a product of the others, the 40% a sidecar would consume of the largest first class, is computed and compared while its 100% companion is left alone as a deliberate cap on a 120% product.
    * **`compounds` stopped being about one document, and only the group's name survived the widening.** It was written entirely about the estimate archetype band; the two-class placement is the same defect at a different granularity. Making room for it meant splitting the group's `run`, the band half returning early on three paths where the placement must be decided on all of them.
    * **The placement is held total over the charge rather than over its own words.** R-08-045 enumerates every physical byte the composition charges and R-15-247s answers all ten terms: nine are named across the two lists, the arenas under R-08-045's wider word so that a Wasm guest's is charged and placed on the ground a browser's is; *application payloads* are the tenth and are answered by criterion, a payload's cycle-critical part being the first list's scalar working set and its bulk the second list's bulk by volume. K-56 therefore takes the charge as its enumeration, and a byte the composition pays for that neither list nor that clause accounts for is a finding rather than a silence.

### M0.16 · Refresh and discharge sequencer in the Sail platform model

  * The sequencer lands as §12 matter and nothing else: [sys/memory_sequencer.sail](../model/model/sys/memory_sequencer.sail) carries the composition-fixed cadence table, the bank-staggered phases, the mode-exit discharge path (R-15-247q), and the fail-stop read taken once after a fixed dwell (R-15-247f); the register slave's window enters the address map in [sys/platform.sail](../model/model/sys/platform.sail) and **answers no requester, at any width, in either direction**; the cadence enters `platform_config` and the attested devicetree beside the class constants M0.14 put there; and the shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried. **No instruction, no access type, and nothing on the decode surface** (R-15-247h, [absence-contract.md](absence-contract.md) A-17), and nothing in the sequencer reads `mtime`, a counter, an occupancy figure, an access record, or a temperature, which is R-15-247r held by what these functions take rather than by a rule about them.
  * Net change: 963 lines added and 14 removed across 12 files, two of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at 166 keys, seven of them new; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **52 properties against 48**, four of them new; the differential corpus green at **19 of 19** with one member and 8 checks new and the eighteen existing digests unmoved; the generated devicetree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at 2,733 bytes, inside the 4 KiB ROM region it is written to; each of the twelve new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on all 54 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * Six findings.
    * **The discharge needed no mechanism of its own, which is R-15-247e read as a model.** The requirement realizes the drain through the cells' *existing* write devices, and this model's ordinary write path is those devices, a write through it already clearing the validity tag of every granule it covers (R-15-007r). So the exit-path discharge is a **schedule over the write path**, adding no instruction, no access type and no second way to reach the array, which keeps A-17 an auditor's search of the decode surface. It lands where R-15-182 and R-15-060 already put an all-zeroes granule, which is why R-15-189j's OFF→ON regeneration pass is obviated rather than scaled to capacity.
    * **A window that refuses and a window that is absent are different facts, and only the first can be stated by claiming the address.** An IO address no device claims falls through to the RAM path and reads as zero, so a slave left unmapped is silent rather than refusing and *no requester-visible behaviour outside the mode transition* would have nothing behind it. Claiming the window and answering every access with a fail-stop is what a program can run into, and [platform-memseq.s](../corpus/platform-memseq.s) runs into it one page from the contrast.
    * **Refresh and discharge are modeled differently on purpose, only one of them having architectural content to execute.** A refresh restores a charge this model does not represent and disturbs nothing observable, so what it can get wrong is the cadence, and the model carries the deadline arithmetic and a refusal. The crossing direction is not taste: the retention floor is multiplied up into cycles rather than the sweep divided down into microseconds, which needs no runtime divisor and rounds nothing, where the other direction could round a cadence into a compliance it does not have.
    * **The unit of work is the bank because the walk over the banks is R-15-198's**, power and reset sequencing being one composition-time sequence table executed by the RoT firmware as the only sequencer. That also keeps the exit path a property rather than a measurement: a composed bulk domain is gigabytes, and a whole-domain pass is a run of the emulator rather than something a `$[test]` asserts.
    * **The fail-stop's negative reading has no source in a valid composition, and that is stated rather than papered over.** The completion indication is the pass's own answer, negative when a bank's write is refused or a phase reaches no bank, and this model has no charge to leave behind, so a valid configuration always confirms. What the model holds is the **shape**, read once after a fixed worst-corner dwell with no poll and no retry, over a real architectural latch rather than a fault-injection register. A machine that can leave charge behind joins that read as a case rather than as a mechanism.
    * **One validator arm was dead the moment it was written, and running the seeded refusals is what found that.** `config_is_valid` short-circuits and the class record's own check refuses a zero bank count first, so a second message saying the same thing is a branch no configuration reaches; that arm has to be the arithmetic's precondition, an early return rather than a refusal. Generally: a validator group placed after another inherits what that one already refuses, so a message it repeats is unreachable rather than redundant.

### M0.17 · Per-class bank-count DSE with droop as a hard constraint

  * The instrument is a seventh derived view, [the bank-count exploration contract](bank-count-dse-contract.md), governed by R-15-247p: the candidate set with its ground, the pruning predicate and the two objectives stated as expressions over named symbols, one row per symbol with what owes it, the report's shape, and six refusals. **Seven symbols, of which six are pending and one is stated**, the stated one being the tag plane's array-efficiency share R-15-247a fixes.
  * Beside it [tools/quarantine/bank-dse.py](../tools/quarantine/bank-dse.py) scores every candidate on the arithmetic that exists without a coefficient: bank bytes, the refresh sweep against the deadline the retention floor sets, the mode-exit discharge dwell, and whether the region divides the count exactly. Every coefficient-dependent column prints the symbol it waits on rather than a number or a blank, and the verdict line states that **no candidate is admitted** and why. **K-58** in a new [banks](../tools/quarantine/checks/banks.py) group holds the contract's declared point against the composition's, holds it inside the candidate set, holds every symbol the report names against the contract's table, and refuses a pruning coefficient stated while the class's `qualified` flag is false.
  * Net change: 576 lines added and 44 removed across fifteen files, four of them new.
  * Exit evidence: the checker green on **all 59 rules against 58**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the seven views the register obliges all present and the new one carrying all four of the register entries its five body patterns select; the floors group's uncounted enumerations at **fifteen against fourteen**; `bank-dse.py` exiting 0 over **11 candidates, 8 of which clear the shape constraints and none of which is admitted**; `check.py --fix` recomputing the derived-views figure rather than by hand; and both type checkers clean. No WSL run is in this item's evidence: `model/config/verifiedos.json` is read and not written.
  * Six findings.
    * **The search cannot be run, and what it is missing is every coefficient that would make it a search.** Of the three quantities R-15-247p names, none exists as a number: the droop envelope wants a peak bank current and a delivery-network ceiling, both read off power vectors nobody has produced; the read energy per bit wants a bitline capacitance R-15-247m puts on a repaired macro; and the island bandwidth ceiling is an *output* of the bank count times a per-bank width and a TDM schedule M0.8 and M1 have not produced, R-15-228a forbidding the memory plan from producing it. So the contract's table is six pending rows and one stated, and the instrument admits nothing.
    * **The declared count is one doubling from the only shape constraint that can fail.** The refresh sweep at 4096 banks is 29,696,000,000 cycles against a 60,000,000,000-cycle deadline, 49.5% of it; at 8192 it is 99.0%; at 16384 it fails outright. Nothing said so, the figure being a product of a bank count, a cadence and a retention floor declared in three different places. **This is the item's one substantive result and it points the wrong way from the objectives**: both objectives reward finer banking and R-15-247g's envelope rewards it too, and the deadline is the one term that punishes it, two candidates above the declared point.
    * **The hard constraint's asymmetry is what the rule protects, and it fails silently rather than loudly.** A pruning predicate with no operands admits everything or nothing depending on how it is written, and what changes that is a coefficient quietly acquiring a value. So K-58 reads the coefficient table's status column against the composition's qualification flag in the direction that bites: while the class is unqualified the two pruning symbols must be pending, a droop envelope with operands meaning a macro had been measured.
    * **The estimate document disqualifies its own figures as inputs here, which is load-bearing rather than modest.** [performance-estimates.md](performance-estimates.md) carries a read energy of 0.1 to 0.5 pJ/bit and closes that row by saying every figure in it is an order-of-magnitude estimate over a retention corner spanning a factor of sixty and is not a freeze input. Filling `C_bl_per_row` from them would make a placeholder an architectural input, which R-15-247m forbids, so the contract's refusal list names it.
    * **Holding the number meant one more model file inside the checker's reach, and the carve-out moved to where it can be seen.** M0.13 opened it for the welded block size; the bank grant is the second such fact, so the named list moved to [vos/corpus.py](../tools/vos/corpus.py) beside the `model/` exclusion it carves out of. Sandbox and parses read the one list, so a path a rule reads and the list omits fails every sandbox's baseline rather than passing on the host.
    * **R-15-108's parameter list was short the per-class bank count, and the question that left open is now answered.** R-15-247p and R-15-014a (viii) both put the count in the frozen set while R-15-108's criterion named the bank/macro/tier-to-island assignment and no count, leaving open whether the assignment was that parameter under another name. It is not: an assignment distributes objects over the banks a map already grants, presupposing a set of banks rather than fixing how many exist, which R-15-228a states from the other side by making the map an input to the §8 plan. R-15-108 now names both, and the composition's 64 banks on the first class and 4,096 on the second are what a one-entry reading would have collapsed.

### M0.18 · Retire the first-party test harness

  * `model/test/first_party/` goes entire, with its `FIRST_PARTY_TESTS` option, the three rows that named its directory in a tree listing, a test-suite index and a codespell skip, and the two licence rows that rested on it. **The ground is M0.6f's, read one directory over**, and it is one ground rather than the list of stale macros three items booked: every program in the set is an ordinary RV64 program addressing memory through integer base registers, which a purecap machine reads as untagged capabilities and faults on, so the twelve tests collapse into one disposition exactly as `riscv-tests`' eleven extension-by-extension filters did (R-15-001, R-15-001c, R-15-007h).
  * **The harness is not stale in one place, it is unrunnable in four, and each was measured rather than argued.** Compiled at its own `-march=rv64gcv_zfbfmin -mabi=lp64d`, the second instruction it retires is `0x4081`, a 16-bit parcel, illegal at ILEN 32 once `C` left at c2 (R-15-036). Drop `c` and it reaches `fmv.w.x` in `crt0.S`, illegal because the `f0`-`f31` file left at c4 (R-15-039). Drop that and it reaches `csrw pmpaddr0`, `mcause` 2 against an unallocated CSR address (R-15-075, R-15-014). Underneath all three the recovery path is already gone: `la t0, 1f` assembles to `auipcc` plus `addi`, so the first instruction yields a PCC-derived capability and the second **untags it**, `csrw mtvec` installs an untagged `MTCC`, and the first trap through it faults `mcause` 28 `mtval` 0x402. Every run ends `possible trap loop detected`, the set's own escape hatch for an unsupported PMP being itself unreachable here.
  * **So no `-march` string and no curated subset revives it, and that decides the arm.** Keeping the three vector tests would keep programs that cannot reach their own trap handler, stack, or HTIF exit door: `reset_handler` opens by writing integer zero over all thirty-one registers, `ra` first, which is `c1` and the store-side root reset hands it (R-15-007p), so the prologue disarms the program before `main`. What the set needs is M1.4's purecap toolchain, whose `la` derives a capability off PCC rather than materializing an integer, as the corpus assembler already does. **This item closes the staleness and does not claim the dormancy**: reviving the lane is gated on M1.2 and M1.4.
  * **`encoding.h` is deleted rather than curated or generated.** A surgical edit inside a 5,380-line vendored header carrying 3,481 defines closes nothing and costs the permanent vendored divergence [I4](#build-loop-instruments)'s second clause and [I8](#build-loop-instruments) both refuse. Generating it from the model's decode surface is worse, being a second copy of what [vos/dialect.py](../tools/vos/dialect.py) already transcribes from the `encdec` clauses, generated for a consumer that reads **three** of the 3,481 defines. Deleting the file leaves nothing to reconcile at the next pin and nothing for the absence contract's auditor to hit.
  * **Two third-party components leave what this repository redistributes**, the RISC-V encoding header and nanoprintf 0.8.0, and the paragraph resolving their position outside [model/LICENCE](../model/LICENCE)'s `dependencies` carve-out goes with them: no component now states its terms in a source-file header instead of a licence file beside it.
  * **K-80** in the [links](../tools/vos/checks/links.py) group is what the deletion owed. [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) discharges its notice obligations by naming the file each component's terms arrive in, stated as a backticked path wherever the file is not one a sentence sends a reader to; K-12 reads links and a path in backticks is text, so the vendored table was unheld and deleting a licence file would leave a page claiming to redistribute terms the tree no longer carries with every rule green. Membership is the **index** rather than the filesystem, which separates the claim from K-12's, and the reading is fail-closed four ways.
  * Net change: 9,227 lines removed and 170 added across 33 files. Twenty-three are deleted outright and carry 9,196 of the removals; the other ten are the files that named the harness and the three that hold the new rule.
  * Exit evidence: build green; **ctest unmoved at 2 of 2**, which is the measurement that says what the option cost, `FIRST_PARTY_TESTS` being off so the tree contributed no registered test to remove; the model's own `$[test]` harness unmoved at **84 properties**; the profile sweep unchanged at **199 refusals of 199**; the differential corpus green at **25 of 25** with 234 checks over 8,489 records, manifest version 8, every trace digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` unmoved at 4,890 bytes; `model.py reference` green; the checker green on **all 77 rules against 76**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the floors group's uncounted enumerations at **33 against 32**; and both type checkers clean.
  * Three findings.
    * **The RV32 half was broken at build rather than dormant, and no gate could see it because the option is off.** The retired `first_party/CMakeLists.txt` registered every test twice and asked cmake for `config/rv32d_v256_e32.json`, while [model/config/CMakeLists.txt](../model/config/CMakeLists.txt) generates the `rv64d_v${vlen}_e${elen}` stem alone. Half the harness therefore depended on a file no rule produces, so `-DFIRST_PARTY_TESTS=ON` failed at `configure` rather than at a test, and the XLEN loop was removed here rather than upstream.
    * **What the deletion costs the checker is a sixth of what it reads, and its subject does not move at all.** `corpus.is_model_citation_path` admits `.h`, `.c`, `.json` and `CMakeLists.txt`, so K-63 read those twelve files and every sandbox copied them: the window falls from **214 files and 1,873,537 bytes to 202 and 1,562,634**, `encoding.h` alone being 201,590 of the 310,903 removed, while K-63's own figure is **unchanged at 1,271 citations across 88 files**, none of the twelve carrying a requirement id. A rule whose cost falls by 17% while its subject is untouched is the shape of a window admitting by kind rather than by name.
    * **`model/.github/` stayed byte-identical to its pin here and I8's enumeration grew by one, and S3 has since answered it: the tree is deleted.** Six of `ci.yml`'s eight matrix rows passed `-DFIRST_PARTY_TESTS=true` to a build that declares no such option, joining `generated_smt_rv32d` as a second reason those workflows could not run if they ever ran. Editing the workflow would have been the permanent vendored divergence I4 and I8 both refuse, which is why this batch moved that item's prose rather than the file; deleting the tree whole is the act neither refuses, and it is S3's.

### M0.19 · Land the vector-FP fork's decided residue and name `Zvfbfmin` in the model

  * **Two model acts the profile amendment created, and neither adds an instruction.** R-15-040d strikes `vfmv.v.f` and `vfmv.s.f` from the decode surface: with the scalar operand re-homed to an integer register they are bit-for-bit duplicates of `vmv.v.x` and `vmv.s.x`, which is the dead Sail surface R-15-048 excludes two suites for, so the fork had minted two instances of the thing the profile refuses on principle and carried them as *candidates*. `vfmv.f.s` stays and keeps its `Zkt`-list entry, differing from `vmv.x.s` in zero- rather than sign-extension, which is the fork's own semantics (R-15-040c) and decides something. And R-15-067i adopts `Zvfbfmin` as `Zvfbfwma`'s own standards-track dependency: [extensions/bfloat16/zvfbfmin_insts.sail](../model/model/extensions/bfloat16/zvfbfmin_insts.sail) is already in the curated tree with its configuration key reading false in every shipped file, so the act is the profile and the model agreeing rather than a transplant.
  * **The strike is deliberately not stated as a §6 exclusion row**, which is what makes this item's own evidence load-bearing. K-66 holds §6's rows against the model's assembly clauses, so a row naming these two mnemonics would be the instrument; but they are the fork's residue rather than an extension declined, and putting them in a table of *excluded extensions* would misfile a decision about this profile's own encodings. What holds them instead is the corpus and this item's exit evidence, which is the weaker instrument and is why the item exists rather than riding a rule.
  * **One neighbouring clause is booked with it rather than left to be rediscovered, and the call is that the file stays whole.** `Zbc` is excluded by name (R-15-048a) and `clmulr`'s clause in [extensions/B/zbc_insts.sail](../model/model/extensions/B/zbc_insts.sail) is gated on it alone, so it is reachable by no shipped configuration while `clmul` and `clmulh` stay reachable through the adopted `Zbkc`. M0.18's ground decides it the other way here than there: a whole-file deletion leaves the next re-pin nothing to reconcile, but this file is not one an excluded extension owns, two of its three clauses being `Zbkc`'s, so the only deletion available is the surgical edit inside a vendored file that [I4](#build-loop-instruments) and [I8](#build-loop-instruments) both refuse. It would buy nothing the key does not: the clause decodes for no composition and its encoding point already reaches the illegal-instruction path by the route every unallocated point does (R-15-014). What the file records is the distinction a curator would otherwise have to derive, that **this exclusion is a supported flag reading false where `Zacas`'s is a property of the model**, and what separates them is which deletion the file admits rather than how firmly either is excluded.
  * `Wasm-parallel` does not apply and no milestone gates on it: it is a decode-surface act in the same lane as M0.6c, ungated, and it is the whole of what the vector-lane and floating-point amendment owes the model.
  * Net change: 37 lines added and 87 removed across four files, none new and none deleted. Three are the model's and the fourth is the profile's one-clause repair.
  * Exit evidence: build green, ctest 2 of 2; the model's own `$[test]` harness unmoved at **96 properties**; the profile sweep unmoved at **199 refusals of 199**; the differential corpus green at **26 of 26** with 245 checks over 8,830 records at manifest version 9, every trace digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` unmoved at 5,717 bytes; `model.py reference` green; the proof gate closing 104 constants; the checker green with every mutant killed and the registry covered, **K-63 at 1,626 citations across 93 model files against 1,614 across 91** and **K-66 reading 136 assembly clauses against 138 and 608 decoded names against 610**; the co-read ledger unmoved, no register entry or spec span having been touched; and both type checkers clean.
  * Six findings.
    * **The corpus assembler never carried a vector-FP row, so the follow-through the deletion was expected to owe did not exist.** [vos/dialect.py](../tools/vos/dialect.py) spells `vmv.v.i`, `vmv.v.x`, `vmv.s.x` and `vmv.x.s` and no `vf`-prefixed mnemonic at all, so no corpus member could assemble either struck word before the strike either; K-66's encoder-row half is unmoved at 353 and only its assembly-clause half moves.
    * **K-66 measures the strike it cannot catch.** Its two model-side readings each fall by exactly two while its verdict is unchanged, §6 spelling these forms in prose where the rule reads rows, which is what makes those two figures this item's evidence rather than a rule's.
    * **The profile contradicted itself about the fork inside one section, and the amendment created it.** §6's account of what the first fork costs at the instruction level said the profile keeps every one of those encodings and re-homes the operand rather than deleting the forms, which the paragraph four below it, added by the same amendment, had already falsified. Repaired in R-15-040d's own vocabulary: the re-homing holds wherever the re-homed form still decides something, and what it leaves deciding nothing is struck below. No rule reads that pair and none can, [co-read](../tools/vos/cli/coread.py)'s prose document being [spec.md](spec.md) alone, so the profile has no K-61 partner.
    * **M0.6c's c4 carried the same claim and its own bullet already refuted it**, *the vector unit keeps every floating-point form it had* standing beside *three encodings the freeze can reclaim rather than a curation batch's call*; this item is that reclamation for two of the three, and c4's sentence is repaired where it stands.
    * **`Zicclsm` is `Zbc`'s unrecorded sibling.** R-15-048a excludes it by name, `Ext_Zicclsm` sits in the isa-string roster with its key false in all three shipped files, so its exclusion is likewise a key rather than a shape of the model, and it has no instruction file of its own to record the disposition in.
    * **Nothing left with the two clauses, each candidate checked against its callers by name.** `illegal_fp_vd_unmasked` keeps three callers, `get_scalar_fp` nine, and `fp_static_rm`, `init_masked_result`, `get_vtype_vta`, `AGNOSTIC` and `valid_reg_group` are every vector form's; `vfmerge.vfm` is unreached by either struck point, its own clause fixing `vm` at zero, and `vmv.v.x` and `vmv.s.x` sit live at the two encodings the strike named the duplication against.
  * Landed: Tier A.

### M0.20 · Close the fetch path's instruction-length decision and the harness's restore idiom

  * **Two defects the plan reports against the curated model, and the first is answered against the model rather than against the rig.** F-000d asks which of the two emitters is right about a word whose low two bits are not `11`, and the answer is the packet: what was wrong is the fetch. `C` is excluded and ILEN is fixed at 32 (R-15-036, R-15-014), [fetch.sail](../model/model/postlude/fetch.sail) asks PCC for four bytes and refuses a PC that is not four-byte aligned, and beneath those two checks it took a *length* out of the first granule's low two bits, which is the instruction-length encoding of the extension this profile does not have. **The deletion discharges R-15-036's own acceptance criterion rather than merely tidying the model**: that criterion asks for no variable-length fetch, mid-instruction reinterpretation or alignment-fragment machinery *anywhere in the front end*, and a length taken out of the first granule is the first of those three by name. The length decision goes and the granule split stays: both granules are read on every fetch, and the split is the precise-fault-address mechanism M0.12's per-granule check was corrected for rather than a way to discover a shorter instruction.
  * **The trap value is what the defect was observable as, and it is what a corpus check can decide.** `instbits` is `bits(32)` because `mtval` carries the faulting instruction, and the compressed branch put a zero-extended sixteen-bit halfword there, so a handler could not see the word that trapped. [`cap-trap`](../corpus/cap-trap.s)'s ninth check decides it rather than asserting it: `0x00100290` is `addi t0, zero, 1` with the two instruction-length bits cleared, so the value the handler compares is one the low halfword on its own could not produce, and the check passes only where the whole word reaches `mtval`. It sits beside that member's unallocated CSR address, whose own comment already said the two traps are one sentence read from two sides.
  * **The 16-bit decode surface goes with the length decision that fed it**, having no other user: `F_RVC`, `isRVC`, `encdec_compressed`, `C_ILLEGAL`, `ext_decode_compressed`, the `half` type, and the Isla entry point's 16-bit arm. What carries an unallocated word instead is `ILLEGAL`, the wildcard `encdec` clause every unallocated point already reaches, so such a word traps by the route a reserved `fm` and an unallocated CSR address take (R-15-014) and `mtval` is the whole of it.
  * **The second defect does not reproduce, and the two properties written to catch it are what say so.** F-081 holds that a `$[test]` property saving a list register and restoring it by assigning the saved binding does not restore it. [test_matrix_scratchpad.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_matrix_scratchpad.sail) now ends in one property per restore shape, each observing its own restore inside its own body, and both pass; the emitted code says why the assigning one does: a Sail list is reference-counted, `COPY` increments before it kills, so a saved binding *aliases* the register rather than being a copy of it and the restore is exact. **Both shapes restore, and the claim is in the tree rather than in this note**, which is what keeps it a measurement a later reader can re-take. The two scratchpad sites move to the shape [test_fec.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_fec.sail) and [test_rot.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_rot.sail) already take, on the ground that survives the measurement: it reads what it restores from the register itself, so it is correct whatever the list representation is, where the other is correct because of what the backend happens to do. The two sentences those files carry claiming otherwise are repaired where they stand.
  * **What the harness cannot reach decided where the evidence had to go.** `fetch` is private to the `postlude` module and the `$[test]` harness lives in `unit_tests`, so no property can call it; and the harness runs under the generated max configuration, where `Ziccif` selects the other one of the two fetch sequences (M0.6g). Both halves push this item's deciding evidence into the corpus, which is F-080 met from the other direction: the harness reaches the restore idiom and cannot reach the fetch, and the corpus reaches the fetch and cannot reach a register the composition does not declare.
  * Net change: 191 lines added and 87 removed across 16 files, none new and none deleted, plus the regenerated model bundle.
  * Exit evidence, re-measured at the repair pass rather than carried from the first landing: the whole exit-evidence sweep green on all six members; build green, ctest 2 of 2; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **98 properties against 96**, two of them new and one written on each restore shape; the profile sweep unmoved at **199 refusals of 199**; the differential corpus green at **26 of 26** with **246 checks against 245** over **8,860 records against 8,830** at manifest version 10, `cap-trap` at 9 checks and 321 records the only row that moves and every other digest unmoved; the attested devicetree compiling with no warning at 6,235 bytes, inside the region it is written to; the proof gate closing 4,135 constants over 179 witnesses; the regenerated bundle byte-identical at 4,566,517 bytes to what the emitter writes from this checkout, held by **K-88** over 158 owner digests, with the generated dialect table byte-identical because no `encdec` clause moved; **K-72** reading all 6 hand-written words on a ground the encoder answers to; **K-63** at 1,661 requirement citations across 97 model files; **K-51** at 26 members with 246 checks; **K-00** reading the checker's registry covered at **91 rules** in both directions; the co-read ledger unmoved at 1,394 pairs, no register entry or specification span having been touched; the tools' own suite green at 38 modules; and both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5.
  * **What this item's gate could not decide, stated rather than filled in from a tree it did not land.** `check.py` exits 1 here and every one of its **17** findings is derived arithmetic this landing's own hours and findings moved: K-36 twice, K-37 nine times, K-96 four times and K-82 twice. The repair is `--fix` and it is the integrator's, this item being agent-parallel, so the selftest reports `the unmutated sandbox does not pass, so no case can decide anything`, runs no mutant, and **this item quotes no mutation figure at all**. `model.py reference` names revision `1462112-dirty`, which is this item's model change committed and nothing else in the tree: the sweep was re-taken over that commit so that the revision beside these figures is one a reader can check out, where the first landing recorded the branch's base instead.
  * Ten findings.
    * **The fetch contradicted the two checks above it inside one function.** It asked PCC for `min_instruction_bytes`, which is four because ILEN is 32, and refused a PC that is not four-byte aligned on the ground that instructions are 32 bits wide, and then derived a second, shorter length from the word it had just authorised four bytes for. A model that states a fixed width twice and re-derives a variable one below it is not carrying an option, it is disagreeing with itself.
    * **The packet was right and the trace was wrong, which is the direction S11's finding could not take.** `rvfi_insn` was set to the whole injected word before the compressed branch and the commit trace's `fetch_callback` was handed the halfword after it, so the disagreement was not two conventions but one emitter reporting a word the machine did not have. `mtval` carried the halfword too, which is the same defect where a program rather than a rig can see it.
    * **What decides the boundary of the deletion is which act killed the construct, not that it is dead.** Six lose their last user here and go with the length decision that fed them, three of them the ones c2's own first finding recorded as left standing, which is what this deletion closes; `cregidx`, `creg2reg_idx`, `encdec_creg` and `creg_name` have had none since c2 took the compressed instructions, so their ground is that batch's and removing them here would be a second act riding on this one. They are booked rather than swept, and nothing on the fetch or decode path reaches them. `ILLEGAL` is untouched and is what R-15-014's own route already wanted.
    * **F-081's claim is false and only running it could say so.** The restore by assignment is exact, the emitted list being reference-counted with `COPY` incrementing before it kills, so the saved binding and the register are the same chain and the restore cannot lose it. What is true of the idiom is weaker and is what the repaired sentences now say: it is correct because of a property of the backend rather than of the property that relies on it.
    * **The emitted `cons` overwrites its destination without releasing what it overwrote.** Where the tail is not the destination's current value it writes a fresh node over the old pointer with no decrement, so the old list is never freed; where it is, which is what every cons into a register in this tree is, the refcount transfers to the tail and nothing leaks. No site here is the leaking shape, and a property that consed some *other* list into a register would be.
    * **`cap-trap`'s own trap count was stale in two artifacts and in two directions before this item touched it.** The source header said the path is taken seven times and [the corpus document](differential-corpus.md) said eight traps of which five are capability violations, where the program raised eight traps of which six were. No rule reads either figure, K-51 holding the `gp` check count and not the prose beside it. Both are repaired and both now read nine and six.
    * **The `$[test]` harness cannot reach the fetch at all.** `fetch` is `private` in a module the harness only requires, so the profile's whole fetch path is decidable from the corpus alone. That is a sharper version of M0.6g's split than the configured-policy case it was stated for: there the harness reaches the code and answers about the wrong machine, and here it does not reach the code.
    * **M1.4-prime's corpus-image criterion cannot tell a member that grew from an encoding that moved**, its subject being the encoder and its input being the corpus, so the first edit to any member reds it and its own instruction is never to repair a red run by rerecording. What admits a rerecord is measuring the old figure back: assembling the corpus with the changed member's pre-change source under the *current* encoder reproduces the transcribed 55,992 bytes and digest exactly, so the delta is this member's alone and no encoding moved. **The delta is also not the instruction count it looks like**, and the criterion's figure is an image total rather than a text total: the four added statements assemble to five words and twenty bytes of `.text`, of which the image shows sixteen once the padding to the next section's alignment absorbs the rest. That measurement is now the recorded procedure beside the figure rather than a judgement the next lane has to invent.
    * **A lane that may not run `--fix` cannot run the mutation selftest at all.** Every landing moves the hours and the findings totals, so `check.py` is red until the arithmetic is rewritten, and the selftest's baseline *is* `check.py`: it reports that the unmutated sandbox does not pass and decides nothing with any mutant. An agent-parallel lane leaving `--fix` to its integrator therefore has no mutation figure to quote, and the two arms are the integrator re-taking it after the merge or the lane quoting one from a tree it did not land. This item takes the first, and the gap is a property of the fan-out shape rather than of this landing.
    * **The revision stamp's dirty marker decides nothing on this lane, and the two sides of it disagree.** The sweep was re-taken over a committed tree so that its revision would name something checkable, and the guest still stamped `1462112-dirty` where the host reads the same commit clean, `git status --porcelain` answering empty and `git describe --always --dirty` answering `1462112`. The build trees are all outside the checkout (`run.py model lane`), so nothing the run writes is in it. Which side is right about the tree is not determined here and no cause is asserted; what is measured is that the marker beside a revision figure is not evidence the tree was edited, which is the half of M0.10's dirty-checkout finding a reader would otherwise take it for.
  * Landed: Tier A. It changes what the machine does with a word, it deletes decode surface, and it moves two findings landed items recorded, one falsified by a measurement taken here and one closed by the deletion, so it is read whole rather than spot-read.

## M1 · Toolchain spine (incl. the CHERI-CompCert prerequisite)

### M1.1 · Locate and pin SECOMP2CHERI

  * `upstream/SECOMP` pinned at `5c20b839` (`ccs-main` head): the PriSC'23 abstract's `secure-compilation/CompCert` link redirects here (GitHub rename), so SECOMP2CHERI and the CCS 2024 SECOMP artifact are one repository, a CompCert fork on the 64-bit RISC-V backend carrying CompCert **3.17** at this pin (`VERSION`, `Changelog.md`'s head release of 2026-02-13, and the banner `ccomp` itself emits) where the README still names the 3.12 it forked from, permanently archived at DOI `10.5281/zenodo.11007678`; license is CompCert's (INRIA non-commercial for the core), a provenance fact the fork inherits.
  * Condition: alive, not abandoned (pushed June 2026; blame merged March 2026). Compartment-aware CompCert holds through every pass with the correctness proof adapted (admits confined to `Stackingproof.v` pending recent changes); recomposition and blame proofs complete; back-translation complete on `ccs-backtranslation`; the top-level RSCC theorem is formalized in `security/RSC.v` but not yet integrated with those three. States Rocq 9.1 / OCaml 5.2.1 / Menhir as its requirement, which is a requirement rather than a measurement: what the pin builds under on this toolchain is M1.2's.
  * Carried features, the CHERI half: the capability backend lives in-tree under `cheririscV/` (33 files: `CapAsm.v`, `CapAsmgen.v`, `CapAsmgenproof*.v`, capability memory model `CapMemory.v`, Georges-et-al calling convention with uninitialized/directed capabilities), emitted via `-dcapasm` with an admittedly incomplete pretty-printer; per the abstract it is "for the most part also implemented in Coq, but not yet fully integrated with CompCert and not verified". Branch `fix-cap-backend` (`1881ed0f`, June 2024) is 15 commits of unmerged backend work ahead; the 2022 `cheri-backend` branch is superseded.
  * Implication for M1.2, where the usability claim is measured rather than inferred and the contingency is answered: the re-homing's real gap is the unverified, unintegrated capability backend and its ISAv8-lineage capability model versus the §4.1 64+1-bit purecap dialect, exactly the residual [inspirations.md](inspirations.md) states.

### M1.1a · Decide the compiler-provenance fork

  * **Contained.** The CHERI-CompCert backend is a build-time producer that this repository never redistributes, so its non-commercial term reaches nothing this repository offers and [COPYRIGHT.md](../COPYRIGHT.md)'s map is preserved whole. **Both other arms stay open rather than being foreclosed**, and that is part of the call: containment is reversible in a way the other two are not, so it is what a proof-of-concept spends while the first release's floor is recorded and its instruments are not (R-18-004a, R-18-004c).
  * What containment forfeits, stated rather than elided: the toolchain is undistributable, so a third party cannot rebuild the image from source, and the reproducible-bootstrap story and R-05-023a's validation instrument both lean on exactly that. The forfeit is recorded here so that whoever later reaches for the bootstrap claim finds the reason it is not available rather than discovering the term.
  * **The arm is re-weighed against what M1.2 measured and it holds, with the trigger for re-weighing it again named here rather than left to a reading.** This call was priced against a *verified* backend, and what M1.2's reconnaissance found is a capability backend whose step relation is vacuous under 45 admits, so the thing being contained is worth less than the forfeit assumed. Two things stop that from flipping the arm. M1.1b's arm authors over stock CompCert, which shrinks the contained material to CompCert's own verified passes and so leaves the term reaching a smaller and better-understood tree, and it makes *obtain terms* a single negotiation rather than two, AbsInt having no standing to grant anything over SECOMP's own contribution. And containment stays the reversible arm, which is the property that selected it. **Re-weigh at M1.2's midpoint**, and re-weigh on the bootstrap claim rather than on the licence: the question that decides it is whether a third party rebuilding the image from source has become load-bearing, not whether the term has become more irritating.
  * The arms not taken. **Obtain terms** is the only arm that keeps both the verified backend and an unencumbered platform, at a cost nobody here has yet asked for, and it is priced as two negotiations rather than one: the AbsInt Software Usage Agreement conveys rights in CompCert while `cheririscV/` is SECOMP's own contribution, in which AbsInt has no standing to grant anything. **Route around it** reaches purecap lowering without the CompCert backend, which is a redesign of the compilation lane rather than a substitution inside it: it forfeits the secure-compilation criterion the whole post-M10 hardening program is stated against, and it is the one arm on which VST's own CompCert trees can reintroduce the same term through the concurrency path; acquiring VST through the opam distribution is what does not convey it.
  * **This is one of three questions on the plan that can invalidate work rather than delay it, and it is a decision rather than an edit** (M4.1a and M6.0a are the others). The pin's own `LICENSE` decomposes the term: the CompCert verified compiler is under the INRIA Non-Commercial License Agreement, a **revocable** grant running "solely for educational, research, or evaluation purposes", which is not an open-source license and is incompatible with every license this repository's content is offered under ([COPYRIGHT.md](../COPYRIGHT.md)). M1.2 is the act that spends it, so the call is taken here.
  * **The dual-licensed subset does not rescue this, and the decomposition is the finding.** `lib/`, `common/`, twelve `cfrontend/` files, `backend/Cminor.v`, `cparser/`, `export/`, four per-architecture files and the build files are dual-licensed LGPL-2.1-or-later; `flocq/` and `MenhirLib/` are LGPL-3.0-or-later; `runtime/` is BSD-3-Clause. **The verified backend passes, the RISC-V backend, and SECOMP's own `cheririscV/` are on none of those lists.** The RISC-V backend is precisely what M1.2 takes, `cheririscV/` being read rather than carried forward after M1.1b, so the gate holds for the same reason over a smaller tree.
  * **The same term is reachable through the proof stack, and that is the second place to hold it.** VST's `LICENSE` places the opam distribution under BSD-2-Clause and carries two CompCert trees beside it: `compcert/` is dual-licensed and contains the Clight a sequential refinement requires, while `compcert_new/` "contains some files that are not dual-licensed" and VST's own direction is not to use it without satisfying the CompCert licence. That attaches to VST's *concurrency* path, which §7's L2 linearizability and §5's per-core kernel could reach for. It is a clause of this call rather than a call of its own, adding nothing under **obtain terms** or **contain it** and becoming a second route back to the same licence only under **route around it**. Acquire VST through the opam distribution, which does not convey it.
  * Three further non-permissive upstreams sit on this plan and do not resolve alike: M6.0a is the same kind of fork from the same licensor and needs its own arm, Inria granting Vélus separately; M4.1a is the same kind for a different reason, a translated kernel specification reaching the shipped image; and M2.1 is not, the emulator fork reaching no image, so it carries a containment rule instead.
  * Exit evidence: the arm is named, the arms not taken are recorded with what each forfeits, and the instrument is quoted rather than inferred from lineage in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md), which decomposes the grant at the pin and identifies the material this milestone actually takes as sitting outside the dual-licensed subset. `upstream/SECOMP` is a gitlink and its contents are not tracked: `git ls-files -s upstream` returns one `160000` gitlink per submodule and no file beneath any of them, so a working tree populated at the pin holds that artifact under the upstream's own grant and this repository conveys none of it.

### M1.1b · Decide the backend's start-from

  * **Authored over stock CompCert's RISC-V backend.** M1.2 does not re-home SECOMP's `cheririscV/`; it authors the purecap backend against the frozen profile over the stock RISC-V one, and `upstream/SECOMP` stays a pin read for its compartment layer rather than a tree carried forward. The decision prices M1.2 rather than sitting inside it, which is why it is taken here.
  * **What decides it is that the pin is a design sketch rather than a translation whose parameters move.** `executeAllowedCap` is the constant `false` and gates all five step rules, so the target language's transition relation is vacuous and no `step` is derivable; there is no `semantics` record, so it does not plug into CompCert's simulation framework at all; 35 `Admitted.` and 10 `admit.` stand in the fourteen files that build, with five capability instructions declared with their semantics commented out and one of those emitted by `CapAsmgen` on the generated return path; and of eight re-homing axes four are replacements rather than re-parameterizations, one adds an obligation with no upstream analogue, and the eighth is excluded from the estimate outright.
  * **Against that the frozen profile is nearer stock RV64 than SECOMP is**, which is the positive half of the same argument. SECOMP assumes run-time `Mem.alloc` where this machine allocates at composition into a flat dense 36-bit space; a `URW*` frontier discipline across 26 `PCU*` forms where this frame is laid out at composition (R-08-011); computed bounds where these are statically known and every narrowing must be exactly representable (R-15-007k); and a per-compartment heap through DDC where there is neither. The purecap delta concentrates in `Asmgen` and the calling convention, and axis 1's landed measurement is the evidence: the definitions move mechanically and the proofs do not.
  * **No licence position moves and that is what keeps the arm cheap.** The verified backend passes carry the INRIA non-commercial term from either tree, so M1.1a's containment arm carries over verbatim and the arm is a technical call rather than a fourth provenance fork.
  * **What the arm forfeits, recorded at the decision.** The deferred robust-preservation theorem's vehicle becomes SECOMP's compartment-aware front and middle end over a backend this project authored. It stays reachable rather than being given up: recomposition, blame and back-translation are stated over the compartment layer rather than over `cheririscV/`, so what changes is that the final integration is against an authored backend. Axis 1's landed work in the contained tree is not discarded either, its measurement being what selected this arm.
  * Exit evidence: the arm is named, the arm not taken is recorded with what it forfeits, and M1.2 is re-priced against it below. No register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit.
  * Landed: Tier A.

#### M1.2a · Re-baseline the delta against stock CompCert's RISC-V backend

  * **The start-from the decision names is not what the pin carries, and that is the first result.** `riscV/` at `5c20b839` is SECOMP's compartment-aware modification of CompCert's RISC-V backend rather than CompCert's own: **12** of its 28 files carry the compartment layer, on the **757** lines matching `comp_of`, `compartment`, `fn_comp` or a standalone `cp` case-sensitively, which is a token reading over the pin and not a diff against a stock release, no such release being pinned here and the gitlink being a depth-1 clone with no history to diff against. `Asm.v:442`'s function record holds an `fn_comp` and `Asm.v:652`'s `exec_instr` takes a compartment beside the register set, and two lemmas are admitted in `Asm.v`, both in the compartment layer's own material: `call_arguments_equiv` (`:1197`, `Admitted.` at `:1201`), which relates the relational call arguments to the executable `get_call_arguments` the cross-compartment trace reads, and `take_step_correct` (`:2249`, thirteen live `admit.` and `Admitted.` at `:2312`), whose live script names `cp_eq_dec`, `allowed_call`, `get_call_trace` and `flowsto_dec`. Every figure below is measured against that tree, because it is the one the gitlink carries and the one the build lane configures. **Whether the authoring starts from it or from the upstream release nothing here pins is a start-from question this item reports rather than takes**, M1.1b owning that call.
  * **The start-from's size, and the five axes with no counterpart in it.** 28 files and **17,965** lines against `cheririscV/`'s 33 and **30,906**. Across all 28 files the tokens `cap`, `capability`, `cheri`, `seal`, `otype`, `sentry`, `locality`, `uninit` and `tag` occur **zero** times. `perm` is not in that list and does occur, so the census of what carries it is given rather than a count: the `Mem.perm` and `Mem.set_perm` families with their lemma names, `Val.add_permut` and `Val.addl_permut`, which are permutation lemmas and not permissions at all, and hypothesis names bound in proof scripts. That is CompCert's memory-access permission and a naming collision, not a capability field. So axes 2, 3, 4, 5 and the capability half of 8 have nothing to re-parameterize and nothing to delete: what the start-from removed from them is the whole of the measurement `cheririscV/` supported, and what it left is authoring from the model.
  * **Axis 1, the capability's storage width. Removed: the axis.** There is no capability type to weld to a pointer width, so `CapAST.v:57`, `:58` and `:171`'s reading of a capability's width off `Archi.ptr64` has no analogue, and the landed 35-site repoint over six files with its fourteen lines of tactic repair is a measurement of a tree this item does not take. `Archi.ptr64` here is a bare `Parameter` (`Archi.v:23`) extracted from `Configuration.model` (`extractionMachdep.v:23`) and already means the pointer width alone, which is the state the landed axis had to manufacture. **Left: the capability type itself**, at [cap_format.sail](../model/model/core/cap_format.sail)'s `cap_size`, with the chunk, the typed slot and the in-memory validity tag that go with it, none of which any `riscV/` file declares. The axis inverts: it was the one axis with landed work and it is now the one with the least start-from under it.
  * **Axis 2, the permission field. Removed: the replacement, both sides of it.** `OCapValues.v:39-51`'s twelve-element enumeration, its hand-written `permFlowsTo` and its three-valued locality have no counterpart, so the 167 lines of the fourteen compiled files that read the two are not lines this start-from has, and neither is the deletion of `locality` as a field. **Left: the whole lattice**, `perms_expand` and `perms_narrow` transcribed from [cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) lines 88-184, which is M1.2c's cell unchanged. This axis and axis 6 are the two whose authoring half costs the same against either tree.
  * **Axis 3, uninitialized capabilities. Removed: the frontier discipline and the design act of undoing it.** The start-from has no `URW*` permission, no `UNINIT` access kind, none of the 26 `PCU*` forms and no `store_zeros_routine`, so nothing has to be un-built. What it has instead is the ordinary RISC-V frame, and that is what M1.2d authors over: `Asmgen.v:859` and `:861`'s `Mgetstack` and `Msetstack` arms through `loadind` (`:733`) and `storeind` (`:745`), `transl_load` (`:780`) and `transl_store` (`:811`) over `indexed_memory_access` (`:712`), `make_epilogue` (`:838`), and the layout `Stacklayout.v:43`'s `make_env` fixes. **Left: the carve, and the carve is not in Gallina.** `Pallocframe` and `Pfreeframe` are pseudo-instructions expanded in OCaml at `Asmexpand.ml:690` and `:710`, and the carve is an integer `expand_addptrofs X2 X2 (-sz)` at `:697` and `:705` with the `+sz` restore at `:717`. Under the profile that add becomes a capability increment, and every subobject bound taken from it is a narrowing R-15-007k requires exactly representable, at a site outside the verified pipeline in both trees.
  * **Axes 4 and 5, sealing and sentries. Removed: both divergences whole.** `OCapValues.v:24`'s `seal := ptrofs`, the separate seal-capability object at `:263`, `seal_capability` and `unseal_capability` at `:1480-1496`, the per-call-site otype `CapAsmgen.v:1374-1376` mints, the `E`-permission sentry that upgrades while preserving the cursor at `OCapValues.v:1472-1476` and the second mechanism at `CapGlobalenvs.v:180-184` are all statements about a tree this item does not take, and the start-from names none of them. **Left: both spaces, authored.** The four-bit otype with its three reserved classes ([cap_format.sail](../model/model/core/cap_format.sail) line 58, [cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) lines 49-65) and the two reserved sentry otypes with `cjalr` admitting each only in its own role (R-15-008, R-15-071), which is M1.2c's cell unchanged.
  * **Axis 6, bounds. Removed: nothing, and nothing changes.** The axis is stated wholly over the profile, and the start-from confirms it from the other side: no `riscV/` file computes a bound, an alignment for one, or a representable length, because the backend has no bounds at all. R-15-007k's side condition still has no upstream analogue in either tree, its input is still M1.9's slot plan, and M1.2e stands as written.
  * **Axis 7, the register file, is where the start-from change pays and where the remaining deletion is larger than the axis was priced at.** Removed: all three of the pin's own defects. `X32` and `DDC` occur **zero** times in `riscV/`, so the second-reserved-scratch entanglement at `CapAsm.v:1670` with `CapAsmgen.v:919`, `:921` and `:928`, and the per-compartment heap capability at `CapAsmgen.v:1352` and `:1392`, both go with the tree; and the broken stack convention goes with them, `Asm.v:102-103` notating `SP` as `X2` and `RA` as `X1`, the RISC-V ABI's own assignment, against `CapAsm.v:133`'s `SPC := X3` sealed with itself at `CapAsmgen.v:1318` and `:135`'s unused `SPPC := X2`. The integer file is `X1` through `X31` (`Asm.v:44-51`) with `X0` modelled separately (`:53-54`), which is 32 and encodable in five bits. **Left: a deletion twice the size the axis carried, and a merge with no starting point.** `Asm.v:60-68` declares `F0` through `F31`, **64 of the 169** instruction constructors the file declares (`:148` to `:362`) name a `freg`, `Machregs.v:47-54` makes **32 of the 58** machine registers float, and `Conventions1.v:59`, `:70`, `:232` and `:241` carry four float register lists; against `cheririscV/`'s 30 constructors, R-15-039's deletion is the larger half of this axis rather than its mechanical part. And the one thing the pin already agreed with, `CapAsm.v:43-44`'s merged integer-and-capability file, is absent: `preg` is `IR`, `FR` and `PC` (`Asm.v:81-84`), so the merged file [reg_type.sail](../model/model/core/reg_type.sail) carries is authored rather than inherited.
  * **Axis 8, the address model, is ruled a re-homing, and the ruling is taken off the pin's own allocation sites rather than off the paragraph.** Three facts decide it. **CompCert assigns no address to anything, at any stage including its last**: `block` is `positive` (`common/Values.v:22`), a pointer is `Vptr block ptrofs` (`:40`), `riscV/Asm.v:456`'s `regset` is `Pregmap.t val` and `:975` makes the stack pointer a `Vptr stk Ptrofs.zero`, so the final target semantics is still block-addressed and the flattening into R-15-002a's 36-bit space is the composer's act, [vos/image.py](../tools/vos/image.py)'s, after the backend. A flat dense space is therefore not a thing the compiler has to be given, and R-15-002a is not in competition with the block model. **Composition-time allocation already exists here and is already the majority of it**: `Genv.alloc_global` (`common/Globalenvs.v:856`) allocates one block per global definition, size zero for a function (`:860`) and the declared size for a variable (`:866`), and `init_mem` folds it before the program runs, which is R-08-011's shape for everything the plan is static about. **And the run-time `Mem.alloc` the axis names is three constructs, not one, and no one of them is the memory model.** The **frame**, one block per activation, at each language's internal-function rule (`Cminor.v:569`, `CminorSel.v:467`, `RTL.v:315`, `LTL.v:391`, `Linear.v:298`, `Mach.v:671`), reaching the target at `riscV/Asm.v:974`'s `Pallocframe` and freed at `:986`, with two zero-size dummy blocks at the cross-compartment call beside it (`Mach.v:569-570`, `riscV/Asm.v:1267-1268`); R-08-011 lays it at composition, which is axis 3 and M1.2d's cell. The **front end's per-variable blocks**, `alloc_variables` at `Clight.v:277`, `Csem.v:132` and `Csharpminor.v:289`, one `Mem.alloc` per declared local rather than one per activation, which `Cminorgen.v:250-251`'s `build_compilenv` folds into that single frame block before any backend language sees one; nothing has to remove them. And the **heap**, `extcall_malloc_sem` (`common/Events.v:1264-1269`) with `extcall_free_sem` (`:1397-1405`), reached through `external_call`'s `EF_malloc` and `EF_free` arms (`:2049-2057`), which is the one predicate every language's semantics uses, `riscV/Asm.v:1584` and `:1605` included; R-08-010 deletes it, and that deletion is two arms of an external-function table rather than a change to `mem`. **So the ruling is a re-homing on a narrower ground than an allocation count**: the block model survives the profile intact and what the profile removes is a frame placement and a builtin pair. **Two things the ruling does not reach.** The value carrier, axes 1 and 2: a capability `val` and a tagged `memval` are authored whatever the address model does. And the heap deletion's own site, which is in `common/` rather than in `riscV/`, so it lands on the seam below with the memory model and not in any of the five children.
  * **The re-baseline finds a seam none of the six children carries, and it is not in the backend.** A capability `val` and a tagged `memval` live in `common/`, where `Values.v`, `Memory.v`, `Memdata.v`, `Memtype.v`, `AST.v` and `Globalenvs.v` are, with `Locations.v` in `backend/`. The upstream's own measurement of that work is the seven files it forked into `cheririscV/`, `OCapValues.v`, `CapMemory.v`, `CapMemdata.v`, `CapMemtype.v`, `CapAST.v`, `CapGlobalenvs.v` and `CapLocations.v`, **10,999 lines against the 18,621 of the seven originals**, both counted as this cell counts every other line figure, and taken while keeping the block model, which is the cheaper of the two positions available. M1.2b names the capability type and the register file, M1.2c the lattice, M1.2d the frame and M1.2e the narrowing, and none of them names the memory model, so the split is short a child. **The id for that child is not allocated here** and the omission is reported rather than filled.
  * **The containment gate is re-derived against this start-from rather than inherited, and the licence line falls between the two halves of the delta.** The pin's `LICENSE` names the dual-licensed subset at `:20-23` and puts on it `lib/` whole (`:25`), `common/` whole (`:27`), twelve `cfrontend/` files (`:29-40`), `backend/Cminor.v` with `backend/PrintCminor.ml` (`:42-43`), `cparser/` and `export/` (`:45`, `:47`), `extraction/extraction.v` (`:52`), the build files (`:54-57`), and per architecture exactly `Archi.v`, `Builtins1.v`, `CBuiltins.ml` and `extractionMachdep.v` (`:49-50`). Applied to the start-from that is **4 of `riscV/`'s 28 files and 202 of its 17,965 lines**, leaving 24 files and 17,763 lines under the INRIA Non-Commercial License Agreement alone, exactly as `cheririscV/` sits there, so M1.1a's arm carries over on a decomposition measured here rather than on a sentence inherited. **Two things about it are new.** The seam the paragraph above finds is on the *other* side of the split, a derivative of `common/` being available under `LGPL-2.1-or-later` at the licensee's option (`:59-61`), so the memory-model half of the delta is the half that could be conveyed if the arm ever moved and the backend half is not. And the commercial arm is one grant rather than two under this start-from, `LICENSE:1-10` putting the whole distribution under INRIA's agreement or an AbsInt Software Usage Agreement and the material taken being CompCert's own rather than SECOMP's contribution in which AbsInt has no standing, which is what M1.1b's arm claimed and what the file confirms; **the compartment layer measured above qualifies it**, the specific bytes at the pin being partly SECOMP's, so a start-from taken from the gitlink rather than from the release keeps a second contributor in the picture.
  * **The re-price, and why this measurement does not support one.** M1.2's 38 h is a redistribution of a figure its own cell says nothing measured, and the two quantities that would have balanced were both properties of `cheririscV/`. What this item measures does not replace them.
