It removes work rather than adding it on seven of the eight axes, so it is not evidence for a larger figure; it names one seam of measured upstream size, 10,999 lines, that no child carries, so it is not evidence for a smaller one; and nothing here converts upstream Gallina lines into attended hours, the class-X authoring ratio being fitted over two completed items and stated of estimates rather than of line counts. **So the six midpoints and the six ranges stand and no range moves, and what changes is the figure's standing**: it was a floor with axis 8 named and excluded, and it is a floor with axis 8's frame half ruled in and priced inside M1.2d, and the seam above, the capability memory model with axis 8's heap half beside it, named and excluded in its place. **M1.1a's re-weigh trigger is still not reached**, that trigger being M1.2's midpoint and the bootstrap claim: 3.1 h stands against a 38 h cell, and nothing here moves a third-party rebuild onto the critical path.
  * Exit evidence: the eight axes re-measured against `riscV/` at `5c20b839` with every claim carried to a file and a line, axis 8 ruled, the containment gate re-derived at the pin's own `LICENSE`, and the re-price declined with its ground stated. No compiler code is authored and no file is copied out of `upstream/SECOMP`, which stays a gitlink whose contents are not tracked, `git ls-files -s upstream` returning one `160000` line per submodule and no file beneath any of them. No register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit, and no co-read pair moves. Net change: this cell and eight entries in [the findings register](findings-register.md).
  * Eight findings.
    * **The pin carries no stock RISC-V backend to start from.** Its `riscV/` is the compartment-aware modification of one, the layer's four tokens reaching 757 lines over 12 of 28 files, and it carries two admitted lemmas in `Asm.v`, both in that layer's own material.
    * **Five of the eight axes have no counterpart in the start-from at all**, nine capability tokens occurring zero times across its 28 files, so the cost those axes carried was a property of the tree being left rather than of the work.
    * **The register-file axis inverts.** The pin's three register defects, the unencodable thirty-third register, the `DDC` and the stack capability sealed with itself, all go with the tree, while the deletion the profile requires doubles: 64 of 169 instruction constructors name a float register and 32 of 58 machine registers are float.
    * **The address model is a re-homing, and the ground is not an allocation count.** No stage of the compiler assigns an address to a block, so the flat dense space is the composer's and not the compiler's; composition-time allocation already exists as `Genv.alloc_global`; and the run-time allocation the profile removes is a frame placement and a `malloc`/`free` builtin pair rather than the `mem` type. The heap half's site is in `common/` and lands on the seam below rather than in any of the five children.
    * **The capability value carrier and its tagged memory are a fork of `common/` rather than of the backend**, measured at the upstream's own such fork at 10,999 lines over seven files, and no child of the split names it.
    * **The licence split runs between the two halves of the delta.** The backend the start-from supplies is outside the dual-licensed subset, 24 of 28 files and 17,763 of 17,965 lines, and the `common/` tree the memory model forks is inside it.
    * **The frame carve is an integer add on the stack pointer expanded in the compiler's OCaml**, outside the verified pipeline in both trees, and it is the site every frame-derived narrowing has to become exactly representable at.
    * **A delta measured against a start-from is not a property of the work.** The half of it that is the start-from's own defects evaporates when the start-from moves, and the authoring half it leaves was never measured, which is why the figure's standing changes here and its midpoint does not.
  * Landed: Tier A.

### M1.4′ · Extend the in-tree assembler and composer to the emitted mix

  * **The bring-up toolchain is already in the tree, and the inversion is the item.** M0.12 shipped [vos/dialect.py](../tools/vos/dialect.py) transcribed from the model's own `encdec` clauses, [vos/asm.py](../tools/vos/asm.py) with the parser, the fixed-point layout and the purecap pseudo-instructions, and [vos/image.py](../tools/vos/image.py) writing the position-fixed ELF the emulator loads. M0.12's own gate recorded 351 mnemonics over 6,658 records and its cell still states them; what **this** item starts from is the tree since, the table at 353 rows and the corpus at 26 members over 8,830 commit-trace records, and the two are not the same measurement. General dynamic linking is excluded by M1.4's own first line, so *the linker* here is an image composer, which `image.py` is.
  * The document calls that trio the acceptance corpus M1.4 is checked against rather than work M1.4 repeats. **Inverted: it is the bring-up toolchain and M1.4 is the hardening replacement.** Both are untrusted evidence-producing machinery (§0), so nothing about the trust story changes.
  * **The table is generated now, and that is the item's whole argument.** `dialect.py` held one row per mnemonic transcribed from the model, which is the older discipline this plan names: a hand-written restatement held equal by a rule can only tell you the two disagree after they already do. [vos/dialectgen.py](../tools/vos/dialectgen.py) writes the table out of the bundle S15 landed, [tools/generated/dialect-table.json](../tools/generated/dialect-table.json) is what it wrote, and `dialect.py` loads it. The generated table carries **931** admitted mnemonics. The transcription carried 353 rows and the generation reproduces 352 of them, `fence` being the one it does not, so **579** forms the model decodes had no row and nothing said so.
  * **The join is two clauses and one non-obvious step, and the step is where a reader loses.** `encdec` gives the bits and no name, `assembly` gives the name and no bits, and both match one constructor **positionally**, so position is the variable's identity across the two. Skipping that binding leaves **49** mnemonics carrying two or three candidate encodings, every one of them a row the transcription already had, which is a defect invisible in the count and lethal in the bytes. The same reading carries three facts for free: a variable that is a selector on one side and a plain field on the other has its bits fixed by the mnemonic (`sh1add.uw` against `sh2add.uw` is two values of one field), a left pattern of the shape `imm @ 0b0` states the branch and jump shift structurally, and a mnemonic mapping and an encoding mapping over one position are quantified jointly, which is what makes `amoswap.d.aqrl` a row of a cross product rather than a spelling somebody wrote out.
  * **The over-approximation is real and is decided rather than papered over.** An enumeration admits combinations the `encdec` clause forbids: the model spells a load `"l" ^ width_mnemonic(width) ^ maybe_u(is_unsigned)` and rules out the unsigned doubleword in the guard, so the product contains `ldu`. For a census that is fail-safe and for an assembler it is not, because a mnemonic refused is a program that does not assemble at the line that wrote it and a mnemonic wrongly admitted is an image that loads and traps elsewhere. So every guard is **parsed** by [vos/sailexpr.py](../tools/vos/sailexpr.py) and evaluated against the model's own definitions, three-valued: extension presence resolves down to `config extensions.X.supported`, machine state answers `UNKNOWN` and is admitted because `currentlyEnabled` is `hartSupports & misa[X] == 0b1` and an assembler can only decide presence, and a joint constraint over the encoding's own fields decides outright with every field the row fixes bound into the environment first. A guard the evaluator does not reach **refuses**, on K-67's and K-75's ground.
  * **The policy reproduces three hand rules without being told about them, which is the evidence that it is reading the model rather than agreeing with the transcription by construction.** `clmulr` is refused and `clmul` and `clmulh` are admitted, off `Zbc` being unsupported and `Zbkc` supported; `lc` into the null register is refused because the clause says `when cd != zreg`; and `vkeccak.vi` takes 12 or 24 rounds because `keccak_valid_rounds` admits those two. The last two were one-off rules in the encoder quoting a clause and are now that clause's own guard, carried to the site as a per-operand requirement.
  * **Two of §4's three inputs now have a producer.** [vos/compose.py](../tools/vos/compose.py) packs an assembled image's sites into bundles and writes the link map from S5 and the per-site entry-and-escape table beside the encoded image, and `run.py model freeze-emit` drives it over the corpus. [vos/freezeschema.py](../tools/vos/freezeschema.py) owns §4's three field tuples and its four declared paths, and the analyzer reads them from there: the schema had one end until now, and a producer that declared it again would be a transcription held together by nothing. The direction is the one K-83 permits.
  * **The sidecar stream is not written here and its absence is the point.** Its `operand_class` and `producer` columns are the emitter's intent, which is why §4 has the backend label them before assembly rather than have an analyzer infer them after; a composer inventing them would be a label inferred over a measurement. It is M1.2's. What that leaves at the analyzer is stated against the run rather than against the checkout, because the two answer differently: [freeze-report.py](../tools/quarantine/freeze-report.py) reads the three streams out of the ignored build tree, so on a fresh clone it reports **3 of 3** absent exactly as it did before this item, and on a tree where `run.py model freeze-emit` has been run it reports **1 of 3**. The un-quarantine condition [the quarantine's README](../tools/quarantine/README.md) states is decided by that run and not by the checkout, and the README now says so on its own row.
  * Net change: 4,219 lines added and 882 removed across 28 files over the item's four commits, seven of them new, beside a generated artifact of 952 lines that no person edits.
  * Exit evidence: the differential corpus at **26 of 26** with **245** checks over **8,830** records at manifest version 9 and trace schema 1, and every member's image **byte-identical** to what the transcription produced, 55,992 bytes over 26 images at sha256 `03f88f69731aabf5` **and the same digest and size recomputed from `8189229` exported to a tree outside every checkout**, so the figure is the transcribed encoder's rather than this one's; the three host gates green with all **86** registered rules covered, all **98** live mutants killed and both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; the tools' own suite green over **36** modules, two of them this item's; the quarantined gate green over its two rules, four mutants and **35** freeze cases, one of them new and joining what this composer writes to the analyzer that waits on it with zero residue; the generated table at **931** admitted mnemonics over **2** unread clauses and **12** refusals, each named with the guard that refused it; and the composer emitting **3,185** sites over 26 members into 1,062 bundles, on which the freeze report reads **3 of 3** inputs absent before the emission and **1 of 3** after it. No wall-clock figure for the wave is quoted, for the reason the last finding measures.
  * Seventeen findings.
    * **A mapping applied twice in one clause needs its arguments in the key, and the failure is silent and large.** `AMO` encodes acquire and release with two applications of `bool_bit`, so a selector looked up by the mapping's name alone gives both bits the first one's value: every `.aq` form is mislabelled `.aqrl` and every `.rl` unordered, **72** of the 144 atomic rows wrong, with the row count and every unordered form unchanged. Caught by holding the generated constants against the transcription's own encoder over the 352 rows both carry, which is the one comparison a generation act gets to make exactly once.
    * **An operand's admitted range is in the `execute` clause and in neither of the other two places a reader would look.** The `encdec` clause states the field's width and every pattern of that width decodes, so the encoding says nothing about the range; the `assembly` printer says how a disassembler *displays* it, and on this model that answers wrongly twice, `lui` printed `hex_bits_signed_20` and materialized above the signed half and `cincoffsetimm` printed unsigned and semantically an offset increment. The execute clause says which reading is meant in one word, `sign_extend` or `zero_extend`, and that is what the generator reads. `_materialize` and `la` now hand their upper immediate over signed, which moves no byte: `lui` masks its operand into twenty bits either way.
    * **A `signed(` scan is a substring of `unsigned(` and a name is a prefix of longer names.** Bounded on one side only, the reading above refuses every vector register above fifteen, `vmv.x.s`'s `unsigned(vs2)` reading as a signed field. Bounded on both sides now, and the reading is asked only of an immediate, a register number being an index into something rather than a value with a sign.
    * **`fence` is the one row still authored, and the reason is a property of the emitter.** Its `assembly` clause is a `forwards ... when` whose body Sail leaves as unstructured text, so the bundle carries its mnemonic as a skeleton and its operand run not at all. Its `encdec` clause resolves like every other; what cannot be generated is the spelling, and it is the one row of this table the repository still writes by hand.
    * **The table now carries the vector arithmetic, which reverses an authored scoping decision, and the reversal is reported rather than argued.** M0.8b scoped the vector rows to the memory surface and what feeds it, on the ground that a row nothing in the corpus writes is a row nothing checks. That ground was the cost of transcribing them, and generation removes it. Nothing in the register, the profile or the plan decides whether the encoder table should carry the whole admitted surface or a declared scope, so the whole surface is carried and the question is put to the author stage rather than settled here.
    * **Which configuration admission is taken at is fixed by no artifact.** The dialect is the frozen profile's and is one ISA across all five core classes, while `currentlyEnabled` is per-composition and the three shipped configurations differ on six vector-crypto keys. The union is taken, over the three files `dialectgen.CONFIGURATIONS` names, on the ground that an assembler serving every class wants it; no entry says so, and this is reported rather than closed.
    * **Twelve forms are refused, and the twelve are four kinds rather than one.** Eight carry a guard over a vector register operand the evaluator cannot bind, five spelling it `zvk_valid_reg_overlap(vs2, vd, get_lmul_pow())` and three `zvknhab_check_encdec(vs2, vs1, vd)`; two bfloat16 forms print a shape this assembler's operand vocabulary does not write; one is `ldu`, whose joint width-and-sign constraint lives in the `encdec` guard and is the over-approximation above refusing itself; and one is `clmulr`, which is simply `Zbc` off at the shipped configurations and is the only refusal of the twelve that an enabled extension would remove. Each is refused with its own guard quoted rather than admitted on the strength of a reading nobody took, and the artifact carries the twelve strings the run wrote.
    * **The composer mints the site ids and no artifact says whether they are the ids the backend will carry.** §4 requires that every site join, and the join needs the sidecar's `site_id` to be the link map's. M1.2's backend does not exist, so what is minted is the unit's name and the site's ordinal among the instructions that unit emits, which is stable across runs and under any edit below it. Reported, and not a naming convention this item invents and calls the contract's.
    * **A `bundle` and `slot` column filled at the reference instantiation is not FD-2's verdict, and the run says so on its own line.** §8 declares the candidate geometries and §6's FD-2 states no default arm at all, which M1.8a recorded as an owed act. The geometry is a parameter of the run, the emitted figures name it, and a sweep passes the other two.
    * **The dictionary is empty and the hit rate is zero, and that is the honest report rather than a degenerate one.** FD-1 states it has no default arm because the dictionary is the encoding, so a composer that selected one would be an instrument grading its own homework. Under an empty dictionary every site is a two-slot verbatim escape, which is exactly what the canonical 32-bit stream this composer lays down already is.
    * **`build/` was not ignored, and an untracked file under it is the trap [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) names.** `check.py` reads the git index and the selftest's sandbox reads untracked files, so emitted output under the checkout leaves the checker green and the baseline red. Ignored in the same edit, on `/out/`'s exact precedent and anchored the same way.
    * **The freeze report's own producer clause was prose true when it was written.** It named all three producers in one breath because all three inputs were absent; two landing here made it a claim about a state the run was no longer in. It is derived from §4's input table now, over the absent streams alone, and the same reading is carried to the four prose sites that stated it by hand: the report's docstring and its fixture line, the analyzer's two pending symbols, and the quarantine's README.
    * **A test pinned a sentence that depends on untracked output.** The live-run case held the report's verdict verbatim, and that verdict now reads *one* of three absent on a tree that has emitted and three on one that has not, both correct. The case forces the absent state instead, and the end-to-end join it could not make is a case of its own.
    * **The host lane's cost is the selftest's, multiplied, and it is stated as the multiplication rather than as a wall clock.** A case runs the checker whole and every host row's generator runs with it, so the generator's own median of **0.131 s** over five runs is paid once per case and once per baseline: **98** mutant cases over **9** sandboxes is 107 runs of it before the repair path, which is the price of K-88 deciding byte identity outright rather than against the last commit, and the figure a second host row would pay again. **No wall-clock figure for the wave is quoted, and the reason is a measurement rather than a policy.** Five warm runs of `run.py` at this gate, nothing else reading the checkout, took **71.1, 81.2, 83.3, 108.9 and 138.5 s**: a spread of nearly two, on a machine reporting `PowerOnline: False` and clocking to it. A median over that is not a figure a later reader can hold anything against, which is the ground [the tools' README](../tools/README.md) already states for quoting none. The multiplication is a property of this landing; the wall clock is a property of the host it was taken on.
    * **An un-quarantine condition became a property of the working directory the moment one of its inputs got a producer.** [The quarantine's README](../tools/quarantine/README.md) states the freeze analyzer's release condition as `freeze-report.py` reporting fewer than three of §4's three inputs absent, "stated so the reverse act is mechanical". The three streams are looked for under a build tree [.gitignore](../.gitignore) excludes, so with a producer landed the same revision answers **3 of 3** on a fresh clone and **1 of 3** on a tree where `run.py model freeze-emit` has run. The condition now names that run, the report says on its own face that absence is read off ignored output, and the verdict names the schema's producers rather than who *owes* a stream. This is [F-128m](findings-register.md)'s defect one level up: that one was a test pinning such a sentence, this one is the trigger the test's subject feeds.
    * **This landing falsified a document it did not touch, and no rule reads that section.** [The corpus document](differential-corpus.md)'s §3 stated `dialect.py` as transcribed from the model's `encdec` clauses and stated that the vector arithmetic is not encoded, "because a row nothing in the corpus writes is a row nothing checks". Generation makes the first false and the vector widening makes the second false, and the cell above announced the second reversal while the document stating the decision went on stating it. Both are repaired at the document. No rule is added: what would have to be held is a paragraph's claim about a Python module, which is the general standing case rather than a countable fact.
    * **The item arguing that a fact has one owner restated its generated table's header by hand.** The admitted count stood in two sentences of this cell and one docstring with nothing computing it, so it was right the day it was written and would have gone wrong, silently, the first time the model gained a form; K-88 holds the artifact's bytes against its generator and reads nothing a document says about them. Both document sites are registered claims now, recomputed from the header under `--fix`, and the docstring states no count at all, a docstring not being a document `check.py` reads. The refusal count stays prose: registering it would put `twelve` into K-26's alternation, where it collides with twenty-three unrelated sentences, which is the same collision the provisioner's rows declined to pay for.
  * Landed: Tier B under **K-88**, which holds the generated table byte-for-byte against the generator that writes it, running that generator at the gate rather than trusting the index. The rule id this item was priced against stays unspent and is not named here, on S14's own ground: [check-rules.md](../tools/check-rules.md) registers a rule when a check carries it, so an id written in a document and registered nowhere is a citation no reader can resolve. Extending the generated table with a `host` lane is one edit where a rule of its own would have been three and a mutant, and the rule that already exists is the one whose subject this extends.

### M1.5 · Run the CertiCoq-to-Wasm oracle

  * The loop closes end to end ([tools/wasm-oracle/](../tools/wasm-oracle/)): `demo.v`'s `Nat.eqb (fib 20) 6765` compiled by `CertiRocq Compile Wasm` and run on stock Node prints `true`, the §0 inner loop with no cross-toolchain, no image, and no machine model involved.
  * Release rather than pin: the oracle installs **CertiRocq 0.9.1 for Rocq 9.1** (`rocq-certirocq.0.9.1+9.1`, MIT), which carries the merged CPP 2025 CertiCoq-Wasm backend (`theories/CodegenWasm`, proven against WasmCert-Coq) that the 0.9 opam release predates, and which depends on `rocq-metarocq-erasure-plugin` and `rocq-metarocq-safechecker-plugin` at `>= 1.5.1`, released MetaRocq rather than the unreleased 9.1 branch. Neither a source commit nor a compatibility patch is carried here. Rocq is 9.1.1 by the release's own `rocq-core {>= "9.1" & < "9.2~"}` constraint, not staleness, and it is this oracle's alone: **M1.2 does not share it** and builds against the distribution's Coq 8.20.1, for the two reasons M1.2 measures.
  * **The retirement the release made available is taken.** The pinned commit `4f53ca97` and its two-site patch are gone, and the patch is not merely unnecessary but inapplicable: 0.9.1 already carries `inlining` inside `make_unsafe_passes`, no longer holds the `erasure_config` field the patch moved it from, and has moved both edited plugin sources. The prover does not move, so nothing this oracle stands on moves. Two caveats keep this a refresh: a release that adds a backend is not a release that has exercised it here, so the retirement is green **only against the same `demo.v` round trip**; and CertiRocq's `< 9.2~` constraint still fixes the prover, so a later Rocq bump is a separate act with the proof gate on the other end. It does not reach the claim that the same version serves M1.2, which rests on SECOMP's constraint rather than CertiRocq's.
  * **The round trip runs in an opam switch rather than in the container, because the container's base image has no arm64 build.** `rocq/rocq-prover` publishes `linux/amd64` alone and this box is arm64, so a container here is emulated. The release is installed into a switch of its own beside the proof gate's `rocq-9.1.1`. The evidence therefore covers the package and the dependency closure opam resolves for it; the [Dockerfile](../tools/wasm-oracle/Dockerfile) installs the same package by name and version, and its base image and repository configuration are the half this evidence does not reach.
  * Refresh evidence: `rocq-certirocq.0.9.1+9.1` installed from `rocq-released` on `ocaml-base-compiler.4.14.2`, resolving `rocq-core` 9.1.1, both MetaRocq plugins at 1.5.1+9.1, `coq-wasm` 2.2.0 and `coq-compcert` 3.17; `rocq c demo.v` emitting a 32,415-byte `demo.oracle_demo.wasm`; and `node --stack-size=10000000 run_demo.mjs` on Node 22.22.1 printing `true` at exit 0, `run_demo.mjs` needing no change because 0.9.1 exports the same names.
  * Environment findings, booked for later lanes. The container recipe exists for the prover rather than for privilege: CertiRocq pins Rocq 9.1.1 and [tools/run.py proofs](../tools/vos/cli/proofs.py) compiles the shipped proofs under the same version, in a switch of its own because `rocq-core` caps dune below what the Sail packages need, which is why [tools/vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) resolves the prover rather than assuming it. Neither privilege nor toolchain argues for a container, the distribution carrying gcc 15.2.0 and running as root, and no container runtime being installed in guest or host. The WSL VM idles out between commands and takes containers with it, which is the stock WSL2 `vmIdleTimeout` rather than a runtime problem; the switch that disables it lives only in `%USERPROFILE%\.wslconfig`, global and permanent, so the fix is scoped into the repository instead: every loop in [tools/run.py model](../tools/vos/cli/model.py) starts a detached, idempotent, self-expiring keepalive (8 h default, `VOS_KEEPALIVE_HOURS=0` opts out, `model.py keepalive --stop` ends one early). Cisco Umbrella re-signs `release-assets.githubusercontent.com`, so the Windows-exported proxy root CA must be installed into any Linux trust store fetching GitHub release assets.
  * WSL Containers (`wslc.exe`) declined, revisit at GA: it is Docker-shaped, unlicensed, and returns its session VM's memory to the host, but it needs WSL ≥ 2.9.3 from the pre-release channel (this box is 2.7.11), is public preview with GA targeted for fall 2026, and moves the wrong finding. On the proxy it regresses: [microsoft/WSL#40945](https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/issues/40945) reports the backend session VM receives *no* proxy environment and Windows root CAs still do not propagate, so the registry pull happens where this lane cannot reach it with the Umbrella CA. Preview also lacks Compose and `build --platform`. The live reason to revisit is idle memory rather than container lifecycle.

### M1.6 · Stand up GC-free lowering routes

  * **Re-scoped on a measurement that forks the route, and the fork is the result.** The toolchain stands up whole in a switch of its own and lowers a supervision-tree-shaped predicate end to end, so the route is live rather than declined; what the run measures is that it has two exits and neither carries both properties this cell claims for it. The **verified** exit, `coq-bedrock2-compiler` over `coq-riscv`, carries proof transport all the way to encoded machine code and emits **39 instructions over twelve distinct mnemonics**, every one base integer and the stack pointer an integer register, with no capability instruction anywhere: that is the plain-RISC-V compilation target R-18-002 forbids. **The width it emits at is 32 and not 64**, `Words32Naive` and `RV32I` being what the shipped recipe instantiates and `Sw`, `Lw` and `Jal` being what comes out, which sharpens the gap rather than softening it, the entry forbidding a non-capability target at either width. The **reaching** exit, `ToCString` to C and then M1.2's contained purecap `ccomp`, is the only one aimed at a backend R-18-002 admits, and it runs the Gallina-to-C step through a pretty-printer carrying no theorem, neither `ToCString.v` holding a `Theorem`, a `Lemma`, a `Proof` or a `Qed`, bedrock2's at 324 lines and Rupicola's own at 217 and both `Definition`s and `Fixpoint`s alone. So this cell's *its target exists* is true of the exit with no proof on it and false of the exit that carries the proof, which is S12's reading confirmed from the artifact rather than cited. **Neither exit produced an R-18-002-admissible artifact here, and the second half of that is a measurement rather than a construction**: the verified exit lands on the target the entry forbids by name, and the reaching exit's backend emitted plain RV64 on this program, its capability printer stubbed at exactly the arms the program uses, so that exit's admissibility is contingent on M1.2 completing the backend rather than shown here.
  * **The subset measurement refutes both halves of S12's forecast and names the blocker that is neither.** That forecast is the reading of the same artifact taken from the upstream's own description of its subset, that M6.1a's Gallina state machine fits it almost exactly and what falls outside is inductive datatypes and general recursion at a hand-written compilation lemma per constructor; S12's cell is repaired here to point at these figures rather than to go on asserting it. Taken construct by construct over [SupervisionTree.v](../proofs/SupervisionTree.v)'s 1,905 lines rather than from the paper: **general recursion is at zero sites**, all thirteen `Fixpoint`s structural over a list or a `nat`, with no `Program Fixpoint`, no `Function`, no `Equations`, no well-founded order and no fuel parameter anywhere, which is the discipline that file's own header states it was written to and M6.1a's note already records as kept rather than claimed. The three inductives are real, 23 constructors across them, but every one is a closed nullary enumeration with no recursion and no parameter, which is the cheap case rather than the expensive one. **What blocks is that the artifact is a higher-order specification and the target language has no function values at all**: bedrock2's `expr` has seven forms and none is a closure, and its `cmd`'s `call` names its callee by a string literal, so there is no indirect call either. Against that, seven of `Machine`'s sixteen fields are function-valued and are projected 45 times, eleven of the file's type-valued definitions are function types with four of them second-order over `Signals`, and four of the thirteen fixpoints take a function argument, applied to a lambda at 22 sites and to a record field at four more. Of the file's **100 definitional commands, 73 do not cross as written**; nine more cross only through the inductives; and the residue of **18** is word-and-list code that still needs `nat` to `word` and `bool` to `word`, the file carrying **118** lowercase `nat` tokens and **40** `Nat.*` comparisons beside them, 20 `Nat.ltb`, 13 `Nat.leb` and 7 `Nat.eqb`, against one `word` in a comment and no `Z` at all.
  * **A successful lowering here would lower §8's oracle rather than anything that ships.** §8's own Toolchain line puts the init system's control plane in Lustre compiled by Vélus and keeps the Gallina model host-side as the differential-testing oracle, and M6.1b is the item that lowers what ships. So the artifact this cell's route was aimed at is not a shipped component, and no measurement of the route against it decides anything about the image.
  * **The route is re-pointed inside this checklist at the consumer §0's own route list already gives it.** §0 lists four GC-free routes and assigns the *arena and extraction* route to the static init tree, naming the Fiat/Bedrock synthesis separately and pointing it "where explicit-memory code is wanted directly; already the platform's method for wire parsers (Narcissus, §5) and field arithmetic (Fiat-Crypto, §4)". A re-price that substitutes a live route for a dead one carries the dead route's consumer across with it unless the substitution is read against that list, and that is the mis-assignment the measurement finds here; R-05-043 names the same consumer in the register and [the spec](spec.md) names this route by name beside it. Wire parsers are word-and-buffer code and squarely inside the measured subset, so that is where the route goes, with the target fork above carried forward as the open question rather than closed here. **The CompCert-C/VST route stays the default**, which the measurement leaves standing.
  * **It still partially repairs a recorded forfeit, and the terms are read at each package's own file rather than inferred from lineage.** M1.1a's containment arm forfeits a third party being able to rebuild the image from source, and a component lowered this way is third-party-rebuildable. Four of the five packages are `MIT` at their own `LICENSE`; **`coq-riscv` is `BSD-3-Clause`** at its own `LICENSE.txt`, "Copyright (c) 2017-2018, MIT" for the institution, carrying a name-endorsement clause the other four do not and neither a reciprocal nor a non-commercial term. That does not recover the kernel; it recovers exactly the allocation-light components §0 assigned to the dead route.
  * **The toolchain stands up in a switch of its own and the first attempt fails on the saving it was meant to make.** `rupicola-9.1.1` is additive beside the four switches that exist and no package in any of them moves. Creating it over `ocaml-system`, to reuse the distribution's compiler as the Sail switch does rather than build one, fails: `rocq-runtime.9.1.1`'s configure exits 1 at 39 s reporting *Zarith library installed but no development files found*, and the cause is that `ocaml-system` leaves `/usr/lib/aarch64-linux-gnu/ocaml/5.4.0` ahead of the switch's own directory on findlib's path, so `ocamlfind query zarith` answers with the distribution's runtime-only copy while the complete `zarith.cmxa` the switch has just installed sits unread. Over `ocaml-base-compiler.5.4.0`, which is what the proof gate's `rocq-9.1.1` carries and why that switch does not have the problem, the whole stack installs at exit 0.
  * **Two of the routes have a packaged start-from, and the absence this item carries is on the target rather than on the extraction.** The MetaCoq-to-Rust half is released rather than a research artifact: `AU-COBRA/coq-rust-extraction` is a Rocq plugin built on ConCert's verified MetaCoq erasure procedure and distributed on opam, erasure being the step that carries a computational-soundness proof. **MCQC** is where §0 puts it, `mit-pdos/mcqc`, Gallina to C++17 with no runtime and no collector. Between them they are the precedent §0 claims and not more than it.
  * Three caveats separate that precedent from a route. `coq-rust-extraction`'s verified half is the *erasure* and not the Rust emission, so what it buys is a proof about the step above the backend rather than a certified backend; its demonstrated targets are smart contracts rather than systems code; and the arena discipline is the caller's to impose rather than something the plugin supplies. MCQC is a Haskell tool over Coq's JSON extraction, which makes it a standing existence proof rather than a maintained tool this lane rides. And the terms of both are unread here, as every start-from's are until the milestone that would incorporate one reads them.
  * **The genuine absence is the target, and it is the priority-zero item §0 already books.** No Rust toolchain emits RV64 purecap. The CHERIoT fork builds `compiler-builtins`, `core` and `alloc` for a **32-bit** CHERIoT target, with compartments, MMIO and the platform calling conventions still unimplemented; CyberHive's `rust-cheri` targets **Morello** rather than RISC-V. So the arena route's extraction half is nearly free and its compiler half is unbuilt by anyone, which is the reverse of the way the pairing reads.
  * One 2026 artifact is recorded because it presents as a further route and is not one on this plan's own rule: a Rocq-to-microcontroller pipeline exists that extracts to Scheme and runs it on a bare-metal continuation-passing virtual machine. A VM in the image is the managed runtime §0 bans, in the same sentence that bans the collector and for the same two reasons, so it is inadmissible here on the discipline rather than on quality. Naming it costs less than re-deriving that when somebody proposes it.
  * Net change: 83 lines added and 14 removed across three documents, no file added anywhere and none removed. Nothing is added under `tools/`, `model/`, `rtl/` or `proofs/`: the whole change is this cell, four sites elsewhere in this document that the measurement moved (S12's cell, whose subset forecast it refutes; the risk row that stood over the re-scope in the future tense; §12's completed enumeration; and the arithmetic `--fix` recomputes), three rows and a paragraph in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) covering the five packages installed, and eleven entries in [the findings register](findings-register.md), ten this item's and one indexing S12's own target reading, which nothing had indexed. The probe sources and every emitted artifact stay outside the checkout, the pin is left unpopulated as it was found, and no byte of M1.2's contained tree is copied here. **That departs from M1.5's precedent and the ground is the difference between the two items rather than a saving.** M1.5 carries [tools/wasm-oracle/](../tools/wasm-oracle/) because its artifact is a *loop a later reader re-runs*, one file against one released package, green as the answer. This item's artifact is a *fork*, and the outcome is that neither exit reaches; carrying the probe would carry a lowering of a program §8 does not ship, against a five-package switch this tree does not create, whose figures no gate re-derives and whose only re-runner would be an item that has just been told not to take this route. What a later reader needs is the constructs and the counts, and those are in this cell and re-derivable from [SupervisionTree.v](../proofs/SupervisionTree.v), which is tracked. If the fork is ever closed at the target end, the probe is cheap to author again from the transcription list this cell states and is worth carrying then.
  * Exit evidence, every figure below measured again at the reading rather than carried, except the six one-shot timings named at the end, which are the run's own and are not re-spent. **The corpus.** The pin answers `git ls-files -s upstream` at `b5894db6`, and the checkout's `upstream/rupicola` is empty, as this item leaves it. In the switch: `Rupicola/Lib/ToCString.v` at **217** lines and `bedrock2/ToCString.v` at **324**, each carrying **zero** `Theorem`, `Lemma`, `Proof` and `Qed` tokens; `riscv-coq`'s `InstructionSet` at **sixteen** constructors; the installed `Rupicola`, `bedrock2`, `bedrock2Examples`, `compiler`, `coqutil` and `riscv` trees answering a case-insensitive `CHERI|purecap|capability|otype` scan with **four** hits, every one the `otype` inside `notypeclasses` or `prototype`; and **21 of 43** Rupicola example files carrying no `Derive`, `Examples/Tree/Tree.v` among them at **226** lines and none. **The reaching exit, end to end.** `fnv1a` **1,633**, `update` **1,300**, `scramble` **1,293** and `no_stranger` **1,624** bytes of C, each a module of its own, `no_stranger`'s `Derive` closing at `Qed` again at exit 0; then `fnv1a`, `scramble` and `no_stranger` as one module at **2,463** bytes over **70** lines, which is the **4,550** of those three separately, less two copies of the **1,108**-byte load-and-store preamble a module carries once however many functions follow it, plus the **129** bytes of forward declaration and `static` qualification a multi-function module adds. That module through M1.2's contained `ccomp` 3.17 at exit 0 to **1,947** bytes of assembly over **115** lines, **zero** capability instructions and **23** distinct base-integer mnemonics; and `-dcapasm` at exit 0 to an **18,213**-byte Gallina term over **327** lines carrying **36** `TODO` tokens across **19** distinct instruction arms. **The verified exit, re-run whole**: **39** instructions and **39** encoded words over **twelve** distinct mnemonics, all RV32I. **The gates.** All three host gates green at exit 0, every one of **97** live mutants killed, all **86** registered rules carrying a case, the repair path holding with a second `--fix` moving no byte, ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5 clean, the tools' own suite green at **33** modules and **295** cases, `run.py proofs` closing **3,327 constants** under the global context, and `run.py model typecheck` at exit 0. K-61 reads **1,384** pairs and none moved, no register entry and no prose one cites being touched here; K-82 reads **291** findings across **64** items where it read 289 across 63, the two added being this item's tenth and S12's own target reading, which the register had not indexed. **The one-shot timings**, from the run that took them: the recursive populate in **18.1 s**, `rupicola-9.1.1` created over `ocaml-base-compiler.5.4.0` in **110 s**, `rocq-released` added in **3 s**, **19 packages** installed in **752 s** and **865 s** in all at exit 0, after the `ocaml-system` attempt exited **31** at 39 s.
  * Ten findings.
    * **The route forks and neither exit carries both properties the plan asks of it.** The verified exit reaches encoded machine code with the proof intact and lands on base-integer RV32I, which R-18-002 forbids anywhere; the exit that reaches a purecap backend leaves the prover through a printer with no theorem on it. There is no third exit: the whole installed stack carries **zero** `CHERI`, `purecap` and `capability` tokens, its only four hits on that alternation being the `otype` inside `notypeclasses` and `prototype` twice each, and `riscv-coq`'s `InstructionSet` enumerates its **sixteen** constructors as the two widths crossed with the four subsets of M, A and F, so the extension axis is closed by construction and there is no arm a capability set could be added at without editing the type.
    * **The subset measurement refutes S12's two predictions and finds the blocker in a third place.** General recursion is at zero sites and the three inductives are nullary enumerations, the cheap case; what does not cross is higher-order code, at 73 of 100 definitional commands, because the target language has neither a function value nor an indirect call.
    * **The artifact §8 would hand this route is §8's own differential-testing oracle and not the shipped control plane**, which §8 assigns to Vélus and M6.1b lowers, so the lowering that was being priced would have lowered the oracle.
    * **A re-price that substitutes a live route for a dead one carries the dead route's consumer across with it**, unless the substitution is read against the route list, and §0's own list already gives the Fiat/Bedrock route a different consumer. Re-pointing this item is a checklist act and is taken here; re-pointing what §0 assigns is not.
    * **R-05-043's criterion and R-05-021's admission each assume a single path with both properties.** R-05-043 requires the synthesis be counted as proof transport rather than an anchor, which is true of the arm that does not reach this machine and false of the arm that does; R-05-021 admits a verified compilation step freely with no clause about where the transport stops. Reported open as a register act rather than closed, and whether the two are one gap or two is a reading.
    * **One consumer in §0's route list is stale, and it is narrower than a disagreement between §0 and §8.** §0's arena-and-extraction bullet names the static init tree as its consumer where §8 assigns that control plane to Vélus and keeps the Gallina host-side. The rest of §0 already reads it the other way twice, naming the Lustre control plane of §8 among the components that genuinely differ from the Gallina contract and calling §8 *the one exception* to that contract, so what is left is the one bullet rather than two sections at odds. Reported rather than repaired: §0 is what the plan's own opening reserves as the authority on what each milestone means, where §12 is where execution state is tracked, so a lane edits its cell and not the route list.
    * **`ocaml-system` puts the distribution's findlib directory ahead of the switch's own, permanently, in the switch's own `findlib.conf`.** A package the switch has just installed complete is then resolved to the distribution's incomplete copy of the same name, and the build fails reporting the package absent rather than shadowed. This is S13a's environment-leak finding one level down, in the compiler package rather than in an inherited environment, and it is why a switch of its own must also be a compiler of its own.
    * **Rupicola ships an inductive-datatype example that it does not lower.** `Examples/Tree/Tree.v` declares a recursive parameterized `tree` over 226 lines and carries no `Derive` and no compilation lemma, and **21 of the 43 example files carry no `Derive` at all**, among them every data-structure example. So the shipped evidence for the datatype half of the subset is modelling rather than lowering, which is what makes the per-constructor price a forecast rather than a measurement even where it is right.
    * **The chain reaches the non-capability backend from a Gallina source, which is M1.2's finding met from the other end.** All **nineteen** stubbed arms are ones a derived loop needs, and they are the whole stub set for this program rather than a selection from it: `Paddl`, `Psubl`, `Pxorl`, `Pmull` and `Psltul` for its body, `Psllil`, `Psrlil`, `Pcvtw2l`, `Pluil` and `Ploadli` for the widening and the constants under it, `Pbeql`, `Pbnel` and `Pbgeul` for its exit test, and `PCUlbu`, `PCUlhu`, `PCUlw`, `PCUsb`, `PCUsh` and `PCUsw` for every load and store through a capability. Five arms account for **18** of the 36 tokens, `Psubl`, `Pluil` and `Pcvtw2l` at four each and `Psllil` and `Psrlil` at three.
    * **The intermediate all three artifacts name for this route is absent from both of its exits.** R-05-043 reads *the Coq decoder reaches machine code by Fiat/Bedrock correct-by-construction synthesis to imperative Clight*, [the spec](spec.md) names the Rupicola/Bedrock2 route as the instance of that synthesis in the same sentence, and §0's third bullet says the same. Neither exit emits Clight: the verified one is `coq-bedrock2-compiler` over `coq-riscv` straight to encoded RISC-V words, and the reaching one is `ToCString` to C *source text* and then a C front end. Clight is CompCert's own internal language and reaching it is what would put this route inside the CompCert-C/VST discipline the other three §0 routes share, so its absence is what makes the reaching exit a second front end rather than a shared back half. Reported and not closed, being a register act, and it is the checkable half of the entry gap above.
  * Landed: Tier A.

#### M1.8a · The instrument, its two renderings, its gate, and the checker rule

  * [tools/quarantine/freeze.py](../tools/quarantine/freeze.py) is the instrument the contract specifies and [tools/quarantine/freeze-report.py](../tools/quarantine/freeze-report.py) runs it: the six-member corpus manifest with each member's producer, its pins and the decisions §2 sends it to, the seven-step recipe with what each step records, the five operand classes, the five region classes with their enumerated refusal reasons, the nine decisions each carrying question, corpus, unit, procedure, threshold, default, variants and columns, the declared parameters read out of §8 rather than copied beside it, the report's two renderings generated from one record, and §9's twelve CI predicates as predicates over that record. It is [bank-dse.py](../tools/quarantine/bank-dse.py)'s idiom from M0.17: every column whose operand does not exist prints **the symbol it waits on** rather than a number or a blank, and the verdict line states what is not decidable and why.
  * **A cell has four kinds and the gate has three outcomes**, which is what lets the report be read before its corpus exists. A `value` is a measurement, `n/a` is an axis that does not apply, `pending` names the symbol the cell waits on, and `fixture` is a number the wiring produced from a synthetic input; no predicate reads a fixture cell as a value, so the fixture exercises the §4 join end to end without ever clearing the gate. A predicate accordingly passes, rejects, or defers on a named symbol, and over today's record four decide, eight defer and none rejects.
  * **The ordered act runs in the order §1 fixes and `G-6` checks**: FD-3 first, then FD-1 with FD-2 jointly, then FD-4 jointly with FD-3, then the five order-4 rows, then the closing pass at the admitted configuration whose S6 output is the realized dictionary the freeze records. Each decision carries a row per variant §6's Procedure names rather than one per knob, so FD-1's row set **is** the curve §6 requires be reported and not only its chosen point: 36 variants across the nine, of which FD-1's fourteen are the declared candidate sizes against the two site-varying policies and FD-2's three are the declared geometries. The nil row is carried against FD-3 keyed by the requirement that struck it, with every byte and cycle column `n/a` (R-15-036n), and a measured row against it is what `G-5` rejects. FD-8 is carried with the emitted adjacency histogram the instrument owes it and its admission in `residuals` rather than as a verdict, that decision being the deferred §15 exploration's. FD-9 is carried in this report rather than a second one, on the cycle axis over `FM-6` with its byte columns `n/a` throughout.
  * **K-77** in a new [freeze](../tools/quarantine/checks/freeze.py) group holds the instrument's own enumerations against the contract's in both directions, and holds two further pairs that are relations rather than memberships: §2's Feeds column against the corpus each decision declares, quantified over the **union** of the two rosters so that a decision naming a member neither side declares is reached rather than walked past; and each declared parameter against the decision that spends it, four of §8's keys naming their decision outright and the two backticked floors bound by the decision's own §6 section, so FD-4 spending the 0.5% opcode floor where the contract puts the 0.1% one is a finding. Report-only, because adding a member to the instrument is a producer, a column set or a predicate body and removing one from the contract is a specification act of the R-18-003b class, and neither is arithmetic to recompute.
  * Net change: 3,750 lines added and 19 removed across twelve files, four of them new.
  * Exit evidence: `freeze-report.py` exiting 0 with **3 of 3** of §4's inputs absent, four of §9's twelve predicates deciding, eight deferring on a named symbol and none rejecting over a record of **36 variants and 612 required columns**; the checker green on **all 77 rules against 76**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the floors group's uncounted enumerations at **43 against 32**, eleven of them new, nine memberships and two relation sizes; the tools' own suite green with a new module of **34 cases**, one seeded defect per CI predicate and each rejected by name, plus one more case in the entry-point bracket; and both type checkers clean. No WSL run is in this item's evidence and nothing under `model/` is read or written.
  * Eleven findings.
    * **The item's landed half was gated on neither milestone §12 books it behind.** What an instrument needs from a backend is a transport rather than a design: §4 fixes the sidecar record's eleven fields, §2 the corpus, §6 every column and §8 every threshold, so the whole instrument is derivable from the contract alone. That is the M0.11 authoring decision paying out as booked, an instrument arriving with its own acceptance criteria having graded its own homework where one arriving against criteria authored before any backend existed cannot.
    * **§0's ordered act and the contract's §1 read as two different orders.** §0 lists the dictionary before outlining and §1 puts FD-3 first, on the ground that its transform changes the corpus every later row is measured against (R-15-036o, R-15-036p), which is what `G-6` mechanizes. They reconcile at different granularities: §0's clause is the **recipe's** order, where a build composes and merges before selecting a dictionary at S6, and §1's is the **decisions'**. The instrument runs both and the report prints both under their own headings; what would settle it is a word in §0 saying which its list is.
    * **The encoded image cannot be decoded per site before FD-2 is taken, so §4's third input arrives on two paths.** Recovering a site's dictionary index means decoding the bundle format, whose header width and slot count are what FD-2 decides, so an analyzer that decoded the image would need the answer FD-1 and FD-2 are jointly measuring. The composer knows the geometry it encoded at, so the image is delivered as its bytes and a per-site entry-and-escape table. The three inputs and the join are unchanged; what moves is which side produces that column.
    * **Two of the three declared bundle geometries are already decided at the break-even hit rate, and that is the one substantive result the instrument produces today.** R-15-036h's slot model and R-15-036j's packing term are arithmetic over the geometry and the hit rate, so each declared `(h, k)` is scorable against the derived 22.4-bit bar at the *p* = 0.804 break-even before anything is measured. The 64-bit bundle fails for every admissible packing term, its 16-bit header amortizing over three slots at 21.33 bits per slot, and would need *p* at least 0.95 with no packing loss at all; the 256-bit bundle clears it for every admissible term; the reference 128-bit bundle clears it only while the realized packing term is at most **0.029** against its own bound of 0.199, asking the encoder for about a seventh of its worst-case padding. All three are readings of the model rather than the acceptance test, which is on observed output; what they decide is where the sweep's attention goes.
    * **A gate with two outcomes would print the same twelve lines whether or not any predicate had a body.** Eight of the twelve defer over today's record, so nothing separates one deferring on a pending operand from one that would defer whatever it was handed. Each is therefore seeded a defect in [test_freeze.py](../tools/quarantine/tests/test_freeze.py) and required to reject it by name, and two needed their reading widened to reject at all: `G-7`'s rebuild is the CI gate's act, so what it decides here is a hash recorded with no pins beside it; and `G-4`'s delta-item half is K-70's, so what it decides is the record's own shape.
    * **§6's column paragraphs are not a membership a rule can read, which is a property of the document rather than a gap in K-77.** Each states its columns as backticked tokens, and the same idiom in one sentence carries an operand-class id, a corpus member and a formula, so a scan over them reads three vocabularies as one and would report a column named `OC-5`. The column sets are the instrument's schema, held by its own tests, and `G-4` decides over the record they produce.
    * **FD-2 is the one decision §6 states no default arm for.** FD-1 states that it has none and says why; FD-2 is in the same position, its structural tie-break selecting among configurations that clear the bar, and states nothing. The instrument carries `n/a` with that ground rather than inventing the reference instantiation as a default, which would be a decision taken by an analyzer.
    * **A membership held in both directions is not a relation held in both directions.** Two id sets held equal catch a member renamed on either side. A relation walked from one side catches only what that side names, so a decision measured over a corpus member neither roster declares is never reached at all. Both of K-77's relations are quantified over the union of the two rosters, and the same reading turns the declared-parameter pair from a set comparison into a per-decision binding.
    * **A floor under the document does not floor the rule.** The floors group catches a *document* whose row shape moved; it cannot catch a comparison deleted from the *rule*, K-77's feeds loop being removable with the checker green and its mutant still killed. So the two relations carry a floor over their own size, and one rule gets three selftest cases rather than one, a case seeded at one pair proving nothing about the other nine.
    * **A `vos` parse that reads a path off disk is outside the checker's corpus, and the checker's corpus is the git index.** K-77 reads the contract through `Context.text` where the tool reads it through `read(root)`, which is the one place a rule and the tool sharing a parse have to differ: a document deleted from the index and left in the working tree is one this repository does not have, and a rule reading it off disk reports green over it.
    * **§3 says §9 makes an omission a rejection and no predicate of §9 does.** The rule is that S6 runs inside every variant, which §3 calls its most consequential methodological rule and the easiest to skip. None of `G-1` to `G-12` is that predicate. The instrument records the rule per variant so a report says on its face whether the dictionary was re-selected, and the missing predicate is the contract's to add rather than this instrument's to invent.

### M1.9 · Two-class static memory plan and placement WCET delta

  * **It runs ahead of M1.2 rather than after it**, which repairs an ordering M1.2's own axis-6 paragraph flags: R-15-007k requires every narrowing the backend emits to be exactly representable and discharges that against the slot plan, so the rule is otherwise written against a plan that lands two items later. The plan is R-08-011's and needs no backend to produce.
  * Per-region class assignment in the whole-program plan, the R-15-247j placement rule, and the admission-visible WCET delta for second-class regions computed against that class's fetch constant, as an **input to §11 admission rather than a report about it**. Includes the R-14-015 interpreter-arena placement: the arenas are second-class regions and the interpreter body is first-class, which raises the origin-pool ceiling *P* for the same first-class budget.
  * One statement artifact in [ApexTheorem.v](../proofs/ApexTheorem.v)'s idiom and nothing else. [MemoryPlan.v](../proofs/MemoryPlan.v) carries the plan, the placement rule and the delta, and its one `Require` is a dependency rather than a citation: R-15-247j's acceptance clause makes the delta an input to §11 admission, §11 admission here is [CyclicExecutive.v](../proofs/CyclicExecutive.v)'s R-11-006 interval arithmetic with R-11-009's switch duty already inside it, so the delta is charged into the declared bound of the slot the region's code runs in and at the composed constants one unit more of it turns an admitted frame into a refused one.
  * **The gate's green line means compiled, axiom-free, non-vacuous and enumerated, and it does not mean verified.** Nothing here is compiled, lowered or run on either emulator; the computed checks are decided inside the kernel by conversion in the silent `Example ... := eq_refl` form.
  * **The three repairs the second read demanded, and what makes each checkable.** R-15-007c's regime above the threshold is stated as the **bound the entry states** rather than as an equality, split into a no-coarser clause, a coarsest-within-that-bound clause and a power-of-two clause; the last two exist because a pure upper bound is satisfied by a one-byte granule everywhere, which would leave the plan-wide refutation with nothing to exclude. That refutation takes an arbitrary granule, derives one byte from byte-exactness and contradicts at a length past the threshold, so it draws its falsity from the bound rather than from an equality, and its twin shows the same granule keeping the regime it does not break. The four obligations that were refuted only of a decision procedure now have **completeness lemmas**, which repairs every existing refusal rather than adding four one-off negations. The admission join's bypass, where a plan declaring a slot index no frame carries absorbed its whole delta and passed every check, is closed by a range check that is sound and complete and conjoined into the plan's well-formedness, with the range taken from the frame rather than from the plan.
  * **The seeded population is what this item stayed open for, and it left nothing to close.** All 461 mutants over ten operators are refused, none survives and none is stillborn, which is a verdict about this file's own boundary discipline rather than a foregone result: every comparison the checks make is read at the value where it decides, every generated family is stated as a bounded quantifier over its own index as well as computed, and each of the fifteen variant plans moves exactly one declared quantity, so a seeded weakening has a conversion sitting on it wherever it lands. Where a clause pins a figure by inequality alone, something else reaches it: `the_charged_rung_moves_two_declared_bounds_and_nothing_else` holds the charged rung's two moved bounds only above the bounds they moved from, so either could be raised again and keep that clause, and what refuses raising either is the three delta ladders read at the three slots three regions declare.
  * Net change: no line of [MemoryPlan.v](../proofs/MemoryPlan.v) moved, the population having exposed no absent statement to add; what this item's closing act changes is this cell and [the findings register](findings-register.md).
  * Eleven findings, seven of them register gaps reported rather than closed.
    * **What closes the region-kind enumeration is unstated.** R-15-247s's two lists name twenty kinds and R-14-015 a twenty-first, R-08-045's charge names application payloads which R-15-247s answers by criterion rather than by name, and no entry says these are all the kinds a composition may place; that entry's own closure criterion is stated over the charge's ten terms rather than over the kinds. The inductive is the register's own names plus the by-criterion arm and carries no count. Owed at R-15-247s.
    * **Whether the delta is charged per region or per task is unstated.** R-15-247j prices second-class *code placement* and R-11-009 charges a *task*'s real cost, so a task whose code spans two regions is addressed by neither. This file charges per region and sums nothing. Owed at R-15-247j.
    * **Whether a hard task may hold any code on the second class at all is unstated.** R-15-247j places all hard-task code on the first class and says nothing about a hard task calling a cold second-class routine, which is the case the delta would price if it arose. The placement rule is stated over region kinds and no reachability relation is stated. Owed at R-15-247j.
    * **Which regions an origin-pool member owns is unstated.** R-14-015 books the ceiling raise as a consequence and R-14-009 fixes the pool at *P* identical compartments with one manifest and one static memory plan, and no entry enumerates the member's own regions. The roster is a field and the ceiling is stated over an arbitrary roster and an arbitrary population bound. Owed at R-14-015 or R-14-009.
    * **What R-08-011's live range is measured in is unstated.** That entry fixes each object's live range at compile time and R-08-014 makes the side condition an interference test over it, and no entry states the ordering the range is an interval of. Two fields carry an interval over nat and no unit is claimed. Owed at R-08-011.
    * **R-08-014's own quantifier refuses the mechanism it is the side condition for.** The entry states slot disjointness over *disjoint* live ranges, and read literally that demands separate slots of exactly the pair R-08-012's collapse onto the proven simultaneous peak exists to colour together. The two readings are opposite checks rather than a stronger and a weaker one, which is machine-checked here: the literal one refuses the shared-slot plan and admits the overlapping-live one, and the reading taken answers the other way on both. Owed at R-08-014.
    * **Every composition magnitude is a field**: the roster, the kinds, the cycle-criticality judgment, the assignment, the bases, the lengths, the granule counts, the live ranges, the islands and their extents, both class constants, the fetch counts, the charged slots, the placement list, the origin roster, the fixed first-class charge and the first-class budget. The demo plan instantiates them with arbitrary witness values that carry no composition claim.
    * **Every one of the 461 kills is the prover's, and the generated input side reaches none of this file.** [Vectors.v](../tools/quickchick/Vectors.v) computes over [PartitionContext.v](../proofs/PartitionContext.v), [CyclicExecutive.v](../proofs/CyclicExecutive.v) and [EndpointIPC.v](../proofs/EndpointIPC.v), so no mutation of this file can move a vector and the 1,503-vector baseline is a constant across the population: the second oracle contributed no kill and could not have. That is a measurement of the instrument's reach rather than of this artifact, and it is M3.2's finding met one file over.
    * **A mutant of this file cost a recompile of every shipped proof, where only one of them could move.** The Coq lane compiled the whole staged `proofs/` directory per mutant, and nothing in the tree `Require`s this file, so twelve of the thirteen sources then shipped decided nothing about any of its 461 mutants. Measured on this checkout, this file compiles in 61.9 s and the other twelve in 13.7 s together, so about a sixth of every mutant was spent where the mutation cannot reach. Reported rather than closed here; the tools have since answered it, the loop compiling the mutated source and its `Require` closure alone and the whole directory once at the baseline.
    * **A whole-population run of this loop does not survive a fan-out, and the loop takes none of the lease every model loop takes.** The un-narrowed run was attempted five times and completed none: twice the WSL distribution was torn down under it mid-run, at fifty minutes and at twenty, with three and four lanes running `seed coq` beside it, and three times it could not start at all, the distribution having wedged into refusing every launch until it was terminated and restarted. Two things make a teardown cost the whole run rather than its residue: `seed.py` never calls the keepalive [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) exposes and [run.py model](../tools/vos/cli/model.py) takes on every subcommand, and the report is printed once at the end, so a run that dies at mutant 460 says nothing about the 459 it decided. What decided this population instead is two runs partitioned by operator whose union is all 461 mutants and nothing else, launched detached inside the guest so that no host-side lifetime reaches them either. Reported rather than closed: the lease and the incremental report are both tools acts.
    * **No rule computes a proof artifact's line count, and the figure this gate re-measured had drifted.** The file is 5,273 lines by `wc -l` and is unmoved since the revision the earlier figure was taken at, so that figure was a miscount rather than a stale reading of an edited file. Every figure below is re-measured for the same reason.
  * Exit evidence, at `81892291`: the proof gate at **3,327 constants**, **518 of them this file's**, every one closed under the global context, with one `Require` naming the sibling the paragraph above justifies, no `Compute` and no top-level axiom; the **whole** seeded-weakening population at **461 mutants over ten operators, 461 killed, none surviving and none stillborn**, stillborn reported apart and being zero, taken as two runs partitioned by operator whose union is the population, both against the prover-then-vector oracle under Rocq 9.1.1 in `certirocq-0.9.1`, and **0** kills attributable to the vector half in either run; the file at **5,273 lines** with no em-dash, no `Admitted`, no `Axiom`, no top-level `Parameter` and no top-level `Compute`; every requirement id it cites resolved against an entry of the register rather than against a longer id containing it, which no rule holds for a `.v` file; the three host gates green with every one of **97** mutants killed over the **86** rules the registry carries, the repair path holding and the registry covered; and both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5. No `run.py evidence` sweep is owed: it measures the model build, the property harness, the profile sweep, the corpus, the devicetree and the reference, and this item touches none of them. No register, coverage-matrix, absence-contract or crown-jewel edit, so the review gate is not re-entered and the co-read ledger is unmoved.
  * **The actual sums the whole item rather than its last gate**: the authoring, two three-lens adversarial reads, the two repairs between them, and this gate, whose own cost is machine time under four sibling lanes holding the same WSL guest and under the five un-narrowed runs that guest would not carry.
  * Landed: Tier A.

## M2 · Fast emulator, struck

### M2.1 · Fork CHERI-QEMU and narrow compressed capabilities

  * **State: the library is narrowed and cross-checked, and the fork is cut as far as anything can compile before M2.2.** The two upstreams are gitlinks, `CTSRD-CHERI/cheri-compressed-cap` at `78a34ba5` and `CTSRD-CHERI/qemu` at `d0bb921c`, which is the tip of that project's own default branch `qemu-cheri` and carries `VERSION` 7.0.0. The 64+1-bit instantiation agrees with the Sail model over **21,546 vectors the model itself emitted, with no disagreement and no parameter mismatch**, beside the model's fifteen format properties ported and holding. The base is cloned at that commit on branch `verifiedos/m21` in the separate repository the containment rule puts it in, the format is in the vendored library's header chain and its target-selection macros, and **the library builds inside the fork's tree with its own suite at 590 of 590 passing**, so the five formats it already carried are unregressed. The curation is **four files, 820 insertions and 2 deletions**, which is the size a re-parameterization should be. Every figure in this cell is evidence taken in that separate repository, which is published nowhere, and none of it is reproducible from this tree, which carries the two gitlinks and no file of the fork or the narrowed library.
  * **The fork made one assumption the frozen profile breaks, and finding it is most of what cutting the fork bought.** `cheri_defs.h` derived the capability width from the target word size: 32-bit target to a 64-bit capability, 64-bit target to a 128-bit capability, `#error` otherwise. A capability is twice a register on every target that fork carries, and the frozen profile is the combination the chain has no arm for, **a 64-bit capability on a 64-bit target**. The width is now stated by the target with the old inference kept as the default, and the RV32 arm is refused outright rather than given a plausible format (R-15-005). That is where *the capability is the register* becomes a fact the emulator is built on, and anything downstream assuming `CHERI_CAP_SIZE == 2 * TARGET_LONG_SIZE` is now wrong where a compiler can find it.
  * **Where the build forces a choice the refusal is the choice.** The instantiation supplies no `flags` accessor, no execution-mode enum, no user permissions, no reserved-field accessor, no level bits, and none of `is_representable_cap_exact`, `get_alignment_mask` or `checked_setbounds`. A zero-width definition or a plausible zero would let a port read a field this format does not have and be wrong silently; left absent, each is a compile error at the line that wanted it, and that error list is M2.2's worklist rather than a defect here.
  * **What does not compile, stated exactly.** `qemu-system-riscv64cheri` does not link and cannot until M2.2, for two reasons: the 79 symbols below, and the fact that this machine carries no QEMU configure stack, no meson and no `glib-2.0` or `pixman-1` development packages, installing which on a shared VM to watch a build fail for reasons already enumerated is not a trade this item takes. The format-selection edit is therefore checked by a test quoting the macro chain verbatim and holding that it pastes to `cc64v_`, the half of that edit capable of producing a wrong-but-valid identifier.
  * **The narrowing is a new parameterization rather than a selection among the library's existing ones, which is what the estimate is against.** Upstream's 64-bit instantiation is Concentrate over a **32-bit** address in a 64-bit capability where this profile is a 64-bit capability over a 36-bit address, so *already carrying 64- and 128-bit instantiations* names a neighbour rather than a starting point. Three things move that are not widths and none is reachable by `#define`. The exponent is an explicit five-bit field carrying its own case flag, where upstream sets an internal-exponent bit and steals three bits from **each** mantissa, so the frozen format is *more* precise in the normalized case and upstream's split-exponent macros have no reading here. The permission field names one of 32 enumerated sets where upstream's is one bit per permission, so every operation over it is a lattice operation. And the address is narrower than its container, where upstream's fills a `uint32_t` exactly, which makes its cursor sign-extension a no-op there and would move every bounds decode in the top half of the space here. What **is** inherited unchanged is the genuinely Concentrate part: the three-bit region correction, the representable limit, the derivation of the top mantissa's high two bits, the wrap correction, the shape of setbounds, and the field-macro layer, included from the pin rather than copied.
  * **The oracle is the model and the vectors are its output rather than a transcription.** The rig compiles the model's own `prelude`, `core/xlen.sail`, `core/cap_format.sail` and `core/cap_common.sail` with a generator that calls those functions and prints what they return, then requires the C to reproduce every line, the vectors crossing as text so no adapter sits between the two implementations. That is upstream's own method. It reaches decode over every exponent field, `setCapBounds` across the byte-exact threshold and at every power of two, address movement and both offset forms, the representability check, sealing over all sixteen object types, the permission lattice over all 32 codepoints with every single-bit mask, and the bounds check.
  * **Finding, about the algebra rather than either implementation: `setCapBounds`'s containment property holds over a domain that nothing states.** The domain is a requested top at or below 2^36; above it the decoded top wraps below its own base, so the derived region does not contain the requested one. The model does exactly this and the cross-check agrees, so it is not a divergence, and it is unreachable with a tag, `CSetBounds` clearing the tag on precisely the test that fails there. What is owed is that **R-15-007a's representation-correctness proof state the condition** rather than carrying containment unconditionally, and that the format's `$[test]` harness reach that edge. Reported rather than closed: no register entry governs this item.
  * **The Rust device-model option this item was to price is measured and refused at this base.** [The alternatives entry](architectural-alternatives.md) books it contingent on the base having rebased far enough forward to carry QEMU's Rust build; the pinned tree is QEMU 7.0.0 with no `rust/` and system emulation still under `softmmu/`, five major releases short. Taking the option means carrying the CHERI capability layer across that rebase, which is larger than the fork it would serve, so it is refused at this pin and reopens at a later re-pin.
  * **Where M2.1 stops is where the library's consumers begin, and that boundary is measured rather than asserted.** `target/cheri-common/` binds the library through one macro pair and reaches it at **124 call sites naming 79 distinct symbols**, four families of which name state the frozen format does not carry: the capability-mode flag, the reserved field, user permissions, and the level bits. Six further permission bits and two object types have no reading in an enumerated lattice or a two-edge sentry split, and the rounding surface the fork calls is the one R-15-007k deletes. The instantiation implements none of them deliberately, an absent field given a zero-width definition still reading as a field a port will try to use. Deleting those readers is M2.2's decode-surface work, which is why the re-price stays on one cell.
  * **The terms are read at the pin rather than asserted from the project's reputation, and both of them are.** QEMU's `LICENSE` releases the emulator as a whole under **GPL version 2**, makes an unmarked file version 2 or later at the recipient's option, restricts four paths to version 2 only (`bsd-user/`, `linux-user/`, `hw/vfio/`, `hw/xen/xen_pt*`), and leaves the Tiny Code Generator mostly BSD or MIT. The fork is copyleft on every arm, which is all the containment rule needs, and the version-2-only term reaches it only through user-mode trees one bare-metal machine type does not want. **The library this item narrows is a separate and permissive work**: `CTSRD-CHERI/cheri-compressed-cap` is **BSD-2-Clause**, so an instantiation at the frozen widths is not a derivative work of QEMU and is not bound to the fork's repository. It sits beside the fork anyway, the library and the decode surface being read and changed together, and its copy of upstream is taken from the library's own repository rather than the fork's vendored subtree. [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries both reads at the pins, plus one `test/` file under `Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception` in the fuzz harness the narrowing does not take.
  * The containment rule that follows is a rule rather than a gate: the fast emulator is a development instrument that no shipped image links against or embeds, so it lives in its own repository under those terms and nothing it touches propagates to the platform. What the rule forbids is the shortcut, vendoring any part of it into this tree, which would put a copyleft term on a repository that currently carries none. The differential rig reaches it as a separate executor over the trace schema, which is the interface that keeps the two apart.
