## M3 · Boot chain

### M3.1 · Add RoT configuration and peripherals to the curated Sail tree

  * The four peripherals land as memory-mapped register slaves and nothing else: [sys/rot.sail](../model/model/sys/rot.sail) carries the lifecycle enumeration and its acyclic relation (R-09-032), the one-way fuse bank (R-09-003, R-09-033), the two attributed noise sources into one conditioner behind separate health tests and a single latched fail-stop (R-15-241b, R-15-241c, R-15-241e), the four counters R-10-013's closed enumeration names, and the windowed watchdog whose pet is a challenge-response over a nonce the device issued (R-15-240); the four windows enter the address map in [sys/platform.sail](../model/model/sys/platform.sail) and answer the RoT alone; the composition-fixed parameters enter `platform_config` and the attested devicetree beside the sequencer's cadence M0.16 put there; and the shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried. **No instruction, no access type, no CSR, and nothing on the decode surface** ([absence-contract.md](absence-contract.md) A-17): `Zkr`'s entropy-source CSR is excluded outright (R-15-037), the lifecycle state is read through a window because R-09-032 makes it a hardware input to the reset path rather than a software-writable register, and no extension-registry row and no opcode moves.
  * **The third configuration is a third *file* and not a third tree** (R-15-005), which is M0.8b's finding carried to the class R-15-005's own criterion names: *no second capability encoding forks the model, the RoT's scalar core included*. [config/verifiedos-rot.json](../model/config/verifiedos-rot.json) composes hart 15 and the same binary runs it, with the same roster, the same class table, and the same address map.
  * **This entry is itself the act of entering M3, and it is a plan call rather than a technical one.** Nothing technical gated it: the item needs the WSL Sail lane and nothing from M1 or M2, every mechanism it wants had a landed precedent, and M0.16's memory sequencer is already an RoT-driven register slave whose own file said *until the RoT core arrives (M3.1)*. What the ordering buys is that R-09-032's *no state outside the enumeration and no edge outside the relation exists in RTL or Sail*, the one place the register names Sail directly for the RoT, stops being a claim with no artifact behind it.
  * **The peripherals are the die's rather than the composition's**, the memory sequencer being the precedent read from the other side: its window is declared in a C-class composition and refuses every requester, the die having one management processor whichever of its cores an emulator composes (R-15-194, R-15-113). So the four windows are declared identically in all three shipped files and what decides whether a door opens is the composed hart's class, read off the roster rather than declared beside it (R-15-052b). Three things follow: no key says which class may open them, a second declaration of one fact being the defect this repository is built to catch; the divergence set K-65 holds carries none of the four; and **a corpus member is deliverable after all**, the C-class emulator reaching these windows from the outside, so [platform-rot.s](../corpus/platform-rot.s) is eleven checks of which ten are refusals.
  * **K-65 generalizes rather than being joined**, its claim unchanged and its subject widened: it holds every shipped configuration against the primary in the keys **that file's own row** declares, and grows a direction no pair against the primary can see, that no two of them compose one core. The divergence set had to become per file: the RoT configuration diverges in twelve keys where the V configuration diverges in two, eleven of the twelve forced by a validator clause, so a single tuple would either license those keys on the V pair, where each would be a second machine, or refuse them on the RoT pair, where each is the only value the model admits.
  * **K-78** in [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py) is the rule K-65 structurally cannot be: it holds a shipped configuration against the *model* rather than against another configuration, and it exists because K-65 is **satisfied** by the defect it catches. A vectorless composition that never moved its vector length agrees with the primary rather than diverging from it, so K-65 says nothing while the attested devicetree names four minimum-vector-length extensions the core does not implement. Both readings are parsed out of the extension registry rather than transcribed ([vos/coreclass.py](../tools/vos/coreclass.py)), so a rung re-based or a flag added joins the rule; the ladder is a member of the floors group's uncounted enumerations, a ladder that stopped matching reporting every geometry as below no rungs at all.
  * Net change: 3,080 lines added and 58 removed across 22 files, four of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **96 properties against 84**, twelve of them new; all three shipped configurations validating against the regenerated schema with their key sets agreeing with the generated max configuration at **312 keys against 290**; the differential corpus green at **26 of 26** with **245 checks against 234**, one member and 11 checks new, the manifest at version 9, and every other member's digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green, the generated tree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at **5,717 bytes against 4,890** and accepted by the emulator's own bound against the region it is written into, with the RoT composition's own tree compiling at 5,517 bytes and accepted by the same bound and naming no seed; each of the **fourteen** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration, the fifteenth reaching schema conformance instead, and all three unmutated compositions accepted; a corpus program running to a HTIF verdict under `verifiedos-rot.json`, so the third configuration is runnable end to end and not only loadable; the checker green on **all 77 rules against 76**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite green; the proof gate closing 19 constants; the co-read ledger unmoved, no register entry or prose span having been touched; and both type checkers clean.
  * Eight findings.
    * **`vlen_exp` cannot say *vectorless*, and what it can say was measured rather than argued.** The knob is Sail **type**-level and constrained `3 <= vlen_exp <= 16`, so zero is not available to mean *no vector register file*; and every `Zvl` rung is gated on `sizeof(vlen_exp)` **alone** with no vector-support conjunct, where the eight vector-crypto flags are each refused by name at load. A vectorless composition left at the C class's exponent therefore validates, runs, and emits `zvl32b_zvl64b_zvl128b_zvl256b` into the **attested** `riscv,isa` string of a core with no vector register file, fired against the landed emulator before a line of the repair was written. What the file declares instead is 3, the floor of the type's constraint. **The cost is stated rather than absorbed**: the generated test-matrix configurations cover 6 through 9 only and the SMT and Rocq lanes bake `config/rv64d_v256_e64.json` at emission, so 3 is realizable on the C++ lane alone. That costs this item nothing, those lanes composing hart 0 on the C class, and it is the standing limit on what a *proof* about the RoT composition could be stated against.
    * **The seeded PRNG evades the deterministic-replay obligation rather than discharging it, and saying which is part of the deliverable.** R-15-241 requires every draw accounted for in the replay nondeterminism record, R-16-015 puts every draw from the entropy root in that record's secret class, and R-16-017 requires a secret draw be recorded as a sealed commitment and **never as a value**. A seed in a configuration file replaces the nondeterminism with determinism rather than recording it. What the model carries is everything architectural about the root: two attributed sources, a verdict per source, one conditioner, one latch with no path out of it, and the two orderings R-09-006a and R-12-017 state. What it does not carry is any replay record, commitment or sealing, and [rot.sail](../model/model/sys/rot.sail) says so. The seed is an emulation fact and is kept out of the attested devicetree, checked at every measurement.
    * **One validator arm was dead the moment it was written, and firing the seeded refusals is what found that.** The noise-source *ceiling* is the type's, `plat_rot_noise_sources` being read at `range(0, rot_max_noise_sources)` which the emission writes into the schema as a maximum, so an over-wide declaration is refused at schema conformance before `config_is_valid` runs. The *floor* is the opposite case: a minimum of two is R-15-241e's and no type expresses it. That is M0.16's dead-arm finding one artifact over, and the reason the refusal list is fourteen rather than fifteen.
    * **The watchdog carries no ratio to the spine, and that is a requirement rather than an omission.** R-15-196 makes the RoT's independent slow clock one of exactly three genuinely asynchronous boundaries, *none modeled as fixed-latency*. A `slow_clock_divisor` beside the two bounds is the natural wrong move, being exactly that crossing and attested besides. So the bounds are stated in the watchdog's own ticks, its devicetree node carries no frequency, and the advance is a function **nothing in the model calls**: advancing it from this model's instruction tick would make the timeout a function of retirement, the coupling an independent clock exists to remove.
    * **The two fail-closed seams compose, and the composition is a consequence rather than a third rule.** The pet's challenge comes from the entropy root, a predictable challenge being one a wedged core can compute ahead of time (R-15-240), so a latched health-test failure leaves no challenge to answer and the watchdog bites. It is asserted as a property rather than left to be discovered, the alternative reading, that a stopped root leaves the watchdog quietly disarmed, being available and exactly the degraded path R-15-241b refuses.
    * **The corpus member's ground differs from M0.8c's, which is why the answer differs.** M0.8c installed its region inside the property because no shipped configuration had the structure; here every configuration has the four windows, so a C-class program reaches them from the outside and every check is a refusal. What M0.8c's disposition still governs is what the **RoT** sees: two properties prepend a roster entry for the composed identity and drop the head again, the harness running under the max configuration, which composes hart 0 on the C class.
    * **The attested tree's device windows ride R-15-002b rather than R-09-007**, and the memory sequencer's node was already in that position without saying so. R-09-007 enumerates core classes, islands, the NoC schedule, OPP tables, and radio calibration and limit values, and a device window is none of the five; R-15-002b makes every MMIO aperture's placement *a stated constraint on the attested devicetree*, which is the sentence a `reg` answers to.
    * **What the model stops at is the gate, and R-15-078 is what says so.** *The Sail model carries the gate rather than a model of the debugger* is that entry's criterion, so what is here is the predicate its RTL refinement obligation quantifies over, `no DM transaction reaches the fabric in the production state`. The same line is where this item stops for every firmware-side duty: measured boot, seal and unseal, the quote and the anti-rollback logic are M3.2's, the primitives M3.4's, and R-15-241d's DRBG the crypto core's.
  * **Four register questions are raised here and each now has an answer.** The two the reconnaissance named resolve differently from each other: the watchdog's window bounds **do** have an owner, R-11-017's admission artifact, which emits the operating-point assignment, the TDM NoC schedule and the watchdog windows together and is itself a crown-jewel specification, so their absence from R-15-108's frozen set is a correct sorting and not a gap; the TRNG's start-up sample budget is owed to *the source's stochastic model* (R-15-241b, R-15-241c), an artifact this repository does not hold and which **S5** authors after the M8a gate. Beside them: the §16 deterministic-replay nondeterminism record that R-15-241 and R-16-015 through R-16-021 specify is **S6**'s, on the same ground; and **R-09-034 carries the Debug Module as a carve-out whose own criterion states the reconciliation**, the Module closed permanently in production, live in development and RMA alone behind R-15-079's authenticated entry, and closed in every other lifecycle state on the five surfaces' terms, which is the amendment that costs one entry rather than two and which M3.2 states as a table. The model carries R-15-078's production gate, which is the one all four entries agree on and the one the register puts in Sail.
  * **What remains, and it is neither this item's nor a deferral.** Nothing in `tools/` runs `verifiedos-rot.json`: [env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) hardwires the profile configuration and `run.py model devicetree`, `corpus`, `sweep` and `trace-diff` all take it, so this item's RoT-side measurements were taken by driving the same emulator through the same three steps by hand. Widening those loops with a `--config` path is a tools act rather than a model one and belongs to whoever owns [run.py model](../tools/vos/cli/model.py); until it lands, K-78 is what reads the file no loop reads.

### M3.2 · Implement RoT firmware in Gallina

  * One statement artifact in [ApexTheorem.v](../proofs/ApexTheorem.v)'s idiom and nothing else. [RotFirmware.v](../proofs/RotFirmware.v) states R-09-002's measured chain with no stage executing before its measurement is recorded; R-09-006's one path, which is the file's load-bearing theorem, carrying a chain checked on cold boot to the deep-sleep wake and the recovery generation and through nothing else; R-09-037's lifecycle extension first; R-09-006a's entropy verdict with no key derived, nothing unsealed and no quote completed on a failed root; R-09-025's vector with R-09-026's reference dual over it, appraised by R-12-015's relying party; R-12-014's seal and unseal as four gates and a fifth obligation on what an unseal hands back; R-09-028 and R-09-030's floor, boot counting and automatic revert; R-10-013's four counters against six events; R-12-017's charge before the comparison; R-09-036's diversified roots; R-05-058c's split by verifier; and **R-09-034's lifecycle table for the Debug Module**, with R-15-078's production gate and R-15-079's authenticated entry stated inside it.
  * **Every obligation is stated of an arbitrary parameter, proved of the specification, refuted of an alternative construction, and every refuting construction shown to keep the obligations it does not break.** Four generators produce 45 weakenings from the specification's own lists: sixteen over R-09-002's stage order, all refused; twelve over the input prologue, eleven refused and the twelfth reported as a gap and computed rather than described; seventeen over R-09-025's vector, all refused, beside four transpositions that are admitted because the entry's word is *set*. Beside them the authored constructions no index generates, nine demo machines differing one field at a time, and a blob that opens here and at none of the four presentations that differ by one gate.
  * **The Debug Module's table is closed by R-09-034 and stated as closed, and the clauses that close it are counted honestly.** The five values are literals and the clauses are stated over an arbitrary machine's own liveness field. They are **two independent clauses and a consequence**, not three: the closure of every state outside development and RMA *carries* the production gate, which is proved, and the converse fails, so `the_two_independent_clauses_fix_the_table` fixes the table from the two and never uses the third. The production gate is stated anyway because R-15-078 owns it by name and its RTL refinement obligation and the absence contract's auditor both quantify over it. Three machines break a clause apiece; the one live in production breaks two at once, necessarily rather than incidentally, and says so.
  * **No decision the register owes is taken, and one magnitude is neither a closed literal nor a field.** The ROM seed, the extension, the measurement encoding, the fuse-held state, the entropy verdict, the expected debug-entry response, the accepted roots, the ROM-verified stage set, the floor, the boot bound, the counter values and the witness and reference values are fields. The literals are the enumerations the register closes outright. **R-09-025 closes four of the quote vector's five terms**; the fifth, the chain measurement, is this file's stated reading carrying its own gap, written as a literal rather than made a field because a field would make that entry's *exactly this set* vacuous.
  * **The gate's green line means compiled, axiom-free, non-vacuous and enumerated, and it does not mean verified.** Nothing here executes on either emulator; the computed checks are decided by conversion in the silent `Example ... := eq_refl` form.
  * Net change: 4,149 lines added and none removed in one file, which is new.
  * Five findings, every one a register gap reported rather than closed.
    * **Where the entropy verdict is extended against R-09-029's boot-target latch is unfixed.** R-09-037 fixes the lifecycle extension first and R-09-006a the verdict before any measured stage draws; neither orders the verdict against the latch, which R-09-029 measures *like every other input* without saying where. The two orders reach different digests, which is computed; whether that difference reaches a relying party depends on the fifth finding below. Owed at R-09-006a or R-09-029.
    * **Whether the chain measures each stage before running it or the payload up front is unfixed.** R-09-002 fixes only the pairwise precedence and R-09-006 has the ROM place the verified M-mode image before releasing the boot core, which is the front-loaded shape for one stage and silent about the rest. Both shapes pass and both reach the same digest, so the gap is about what ran between the extensions rather than about what was measured. Owed at R-09-002 or R-09-006.
    * **What a successful boot does to the boot-attempt count is unstated.** R-09-028 states the counting with automatic revert and R-09-006a takes the entropy halt out of it as a §16 fault class consuming no attempt; no entry says whether a success clears the count, so the outcome enumeration here is that entry's two failure classes and nothing models a success. Owed at R-09-028.
    * **What the reference integrity manifest covers where it differs from the quote is unstated.** R-09-026 makes it the dual *covering the same vector*, so the appraisal is stated over whatever vector the quote covers and no manifest-side enumeration is invented. Owed at R-09-026.
    * **Whether R-09-025's *this set* is four terms or five is unfixed, and this file takes a side rather than finding one.** The MUST has attestation cover the chain and the discipline both; the criterion says *the quote's vector*, which is not the quote, and *this set*, which can name the four terms the colon introduces. R-09-026 carries the referent over without fixing it and R-09-037 constrains the lifecycle state rather than the chain. What is argued is the consequence: on the narrow reading a relying party appraises clean on a forked chain, which is computed. That prices the narrow reading and does not refute it, and the coverage check cannot be cited against it because that check is written against the wide one. Owed at R-09-025.
  * **Two things this item does not author are deferrals and not gaps.** The TRNG's start-up sample budget is owed to the source's stochastic model, which **S5** authors; the §16 deterministic-replay nondeterminism record is specified by R-15-241 and R-16-015 through R-16-021, and **S6** authors it. Both sit after the M8a gate. The plan schedules both, so what this file does not author is deferred rather than unowned.

Beside them eleven authored constructions no generator produces, each with a **twin** showing the named defect and not the construction's shape is what refuses it: an installer adding an unplanned edge beside one adding a planned edge that still refines, an installer dropping a planned edge beside one dropping an absent edge that still refines, a root wider than its partition bound, an installer leaving a resident handler, a decode assigning the Store-and-Execute combination, a promotion primitive, a gate reading the mode, a region table that refuses, a hidden singleton, and a lazily initialized static. #### M3.4a · The cryptography licence reads, and SHA-3 in Gallina

  * **This is the milestone the four calls above are read at**, on the rule that a licence is read at the act that would incorporate it. Fiat-Crypto and the two behavioural oracles are pinned, each read at the file its own project states its terms in, and [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries every reading with the file, the commit and the date it was taken at. Beside them [proofs/Keccak.v](../proofs/Keccak.v) is the SHA-3 half authored in Gallina, which is the primitive the rest of the chain cannot be assembled without: R-05-058c puts SLH-DSA at every ROM-verified object and R-05-058a freezes SLH-DSA-SHAKE-256s, so the boot chain's signature is hash-based and rides this item's `shake256`.

So the Apache-2.0 election stands on the instrument instead of on this repository's own prose about it, which is what F-187 asked for. **Two of the other three reads correct the cell above rather than confirming it**, and the third finds that the Jasmin tree pinned here does state terms, so the cell's unlicensed-artifact disposition is about a tree this reading did not identify and is not inherited by the row.
  * **The VST call is falsified in two places, and the parent's prose is left for its author.** Read at `f384d2db`: `coq-vst.opam` declares `"coq-compcert" {>= "3.15" & < "3.17~"}`, so the opam route installs a CompCert into its switch exactly as the CertiRocq oracle's package does, and what the route avoids is *tracking* `compcert/` and `compcert_new/` rather than conveying the term. That upper bound also forbids sharing the oracle switch, which carries 3.17. And the `Makefile`'s `vst` target is `msl veric floyd simpleconc` with `install` copying what `util/calc_install_files` computes over `veric floyd` and the `progs` directory, so `sha/`, `hmacdrbg/` and `hmacfcf/` are test-suite targets that the named acquisition route does not deliver at all. Re-taking that call is a decision at the milestone that owns it and not this item's, so it is reported and the cell above is unedited.
  * **The vectors carry three notice conditions and no licence file.** `usnistgov/ACVP-Server` at `975de31e` has no `LICENSE`, `LICENCE`, `COPYING` or `COPYRIGHT` anywhere at its root; the terms are in the README, and they are notice retention, a change notice on a modified work, and explicit acknowledgement of NIST as the source, with no usage restriction of any kind. No row is added to the fetched table, because K-80 wants that table's License-text column to locate a tracked file and this component has none to locate; the fetched shape is owed at the milestone whose build performs the fetch. What the tree carries today is the FIPS 202 half of the same oracle, read at `XKCP/XKCP`'s `tests/TestVectors/` and quoted as literals inside the statements that decide against it, under that tree's own arm and not these terms.
  * **The oracles are pinned in a pair of lineages rather than a pair of instances.** `hacl-star` is Apache-2.0 at the stock text and its proofs are F\* and Low\*; `libjade` is `CC0-1.0 OR Apache-2.0` in a forty-seven-byte `LICENSE` with both arm texts under `LICENSES/`, and its proofs are Jasmin and EasyCrypt. Neither is copied, vendored or extracted from. **The standing HACL\* takes here is the comparator's and not the interim anchor's, and the entry that books it has to be named** because the register puts that name on the other side of the sentence: R-05-061 makes libcrux/HACL\* an explicitly interim F\*/Z3 widening with a named Coq-native destination, and R-05-022's acceptance lists it first among the three interims carrying a destination and a consumer list rather than beside aiT and Binsec/Rel, which are the two that entry exempts. What R-05-061 books is the **import of a post-quantum primitive** whose correctness would then ride F\* and Z3, which is M3.4b's act; what is pinned here is a second implementation read for its answers, with nothing above it depending on it, so it is not an anchor by R-05-022's own test that an anchor is what something rides as its ground and it has no consumer set for R-05-022a to find growing. `libjade` carries no such entry at all. No entry is edited to say any of this and none is owed one: the distinction is R-05-061's own, between a widening and a comparison. **XKCP is read and declined** on the ground the bring-up SoC's row already carries: its `LICENSE` is a decomposition of a CC0 default across six named exceptions, the AVX2 and AVX512 Keccak assembly CRYPTOGAMS under BSD-or-GPL among them, so *XKCP is CC0* is false of part of what it ships. **What was quoted out of it is read at its own arm all the same**, `tests/TestVectors/` being named in none of the six exceptions and the NIST one of them covering the genKAT sources rather than the files they emit, so the ACVP terms one upstream over do not reach these literals and saying they did would be terms inferred from a lineage.
  * **The Gallina file is the functional layer and nothing else, and it says so.** [proofs/Keccak.v](../proofs/Keccak.v) carries theta, rho, pi, chi and iota by the standard's own names, in the standard's own order and unfused; both round counts under one round-index list; the sponge, pad10\*1, `sha3_224`, `sha3_256`, `sha3_384`, `sha3_512`, `shake128` and `shake256`. **No constant-time property is claimed and none is implied**: R-05-062 and R-05-067 put that obligation on a binary as a 2-safety hyperproperty, a Gallina reference is not one, and the file's being branchless on its inputs is a remark about its shape rather than evidence. No reduction and no distributional claim is made either, R-05-077a's reduction being stated over uniformly drawn keys that a functional reference knows nothing about. So what is shipped is one of R-05-059's three layers, and this is not a shipped primitive under that entry.
  * **The two transcriptions are a differential pair because one derives what the other writes down.** [keccak_p1600.sail](../model/model/extensions/keccak/keccak_p1600.sail) transcribes the twenty-five rho offsets and the twenty-four round constants as tables, on the ground that a reader checks a written row against the published one. This file derives both, the offsets from FIPS 202's own (t+1)(t+2)/2 recurrence over the (x, y) walk and the constants from Algorithm 5's shift register, and then computes that what it derived equals what the standard publishes. **That is the pair that catches both of M0.8d's own defects from the other side**: a mod-5 arm written one row early, exhibited here as a construction and refuted, and a table read in the wrong direction, refuted here by computing that the constant table is not its own reverse. It also states the two forms of pi against each other, the source-indexed one this file uses and the destination-indexed one the Sail file uses, so either is the other's inverse or one of them is wrong.
  * **R-15-056a's round-count clause is a theorem here and a vector besides.** Keccak-*p*[1600, n] is the **last** n rounds, so running the rounds that come *before* the last n and then the short form is the whole permutation, stated over an arbitrary state and an arbitrary round count and proved from the fold's own append law; that is what a known answer cannot show, holding at one input where this holds at every state and every count. The entry also wants both round counts covered by R-15-058's differential oracle, and the short form now carries a value too. **The answer it is checked against is neither transcription's.** The Keccak team publishes the 24-round all-zero state and not the 12-round one, so what stands in for it is the constant the Sail unit's own harness carries at `keccak_kat_zero_12`, which that file's header records as produced by an independent FIPS 202 implementation cross-checked against a standard library's SHA-3: a third route, which is exactly what a known-answer check buys, and not a copy of the Sail transcription, which is what declining it earlier was afraid of.
  * **How much of the word "permutation" is discharged, and whose obligation it is.** The word is in R-15-056's **IS** line and not in its acceptance, which reads whole "it clears all five admission tests; its correctness is a Sail invariant (fixed theta/rho/pi/chi/iota rounds, no tables, no secret-dependent control flow) riding RTL ⊑ Sail" and whose subject is the Sail unit, by the same test that keeps R-15-058 off this file. So showing it of the Gallina transcription is an obligation **this file states of itself**, and four of the five steps carry it: pi is a bijection on the twenty-five lanes and its inverse recovers a state of twenty-five arbitrary words; every rotation the rho table uses is undone by its complement on a lane of sixty-four arbitrary bits; iota is an involution over an arbitrary constant, an arbitrary lane zero and an arbitrary remainder; and chi is a bijection on a row of five bits, checked over all thirty-two rows and refuted of the same clause with its own term dropped. **Theta is not shown invertible**, being a linear map on the 320-bit column-parity space whose inverse is dense, and the composite claim is an obligation the file states of itself and does not close.
  * **Theta's index structure is stated and refuted even so, which is the half a round count and an offset table cannot reach.** The column parity is shown to fold the five lanes of its own column and no lane outside it, over twenty-five arbitrary words with the lanes named on the right so a transposed flattening fails the statement; and D[x] is shown to take the column below unrotated and the column above rotated by one, over five arbitrary parities. The near alternative that clause exists to exclude is the exchange of the two neighbours, which is a theta in every other respect, moves no lane, and agrees with the standard's wherever the two neighbouring parities coincide, so what separates them is the asymmetry alone; it is defined here, held to that single difference, and refuted at the whole permutation against the published state. Without it theta would be the one step map decided by nothing but two whole-permutation known answers.
  * **No decision the register owes is taken.** The word width and bit order, the lane flattening, the byte order of a squeezed output and whether a Gallina reference is inside any acceptance at all are readings of the file, stated as readings. The only literals are the standard's: the 5 x 5 x 64 geometry, twenty-four and twelve rounds, the four output lengths and two security strengths, c = 2n, the two domain separators, and Algorithm 5's eight-bit register and Algorithm 6's seven bit positions. Every rate is computed from the capacity rule rather than written down.
  * **The pair now has a holder, and it is one rule rather than a discipline.** Both gates are green on their own copy of the shared answers, so a constant edited on one side alone leaves the Coq file compiling, its `Example` proved, the Sail harness passing, and the one property the pair was built for gone with nothing saying so. **K-91** reads the three all-zero states both files quote, decodes the Sail literal's twenty-five lane words and the Gallina `Example`'s two hundred least-significant-first bytes to the same twenty-five integers, and holds them equal lane by lane, so a presentation either file rewrites without moving the state is not a finding and a byte that moves is. It reaches `proofs/` off the repository root with no corpus-window change, which is the precedent K-42 already sets by reading the apex statement the same way, and it is report-only on K-89's ground: either side may be the one that is right and the fact worth having is which file moved.
  * **The gate's green line means compiled, axiom-free, non-vacuous and enumerated, and it does not mean verified.** Nothing here executes on either emulator; the light checks are decided by conversion in the silent `Example ... := eq_refl` form and the ones that run a permutation by the bytecode machine, which contributes nothing to the assumption enumeration.

  * Landed: Tier A.

#### M3.4d · The AEAD in Gallina

  * **The one cipher R-10-024 freezes, authored against FIPS 197 and NIST SP 800-38D.** [proofs/AesGcm.v](../proofs/AesGcm.v) carries AES at all three of FIPS 197's key lengths, the cipher and the inverse cipher each composed once from the standard's own unfused step maps; the key expansion with the second substitution branch only a key of more than six words reaches; the GF(2^128) multiplication in SP 800-38D's own reflected convention; GHASH, the counter mode with its own thirty-two-bit increment, both initial-counter constructions, authenticated encryption, and authenticated decryption with its failure arm. **It is M3.4c's residue split out at entry**, which is what M3.4a's landing did to M3.4c at exit, and it is a cell of its own because the AEAD is the one of that item's four deliverables whose content is two standards rather than an acquisition route and a licence consequence.
  * **AES-GCM and not a choice, and the disagreement above it is reported rather than resolved.** R-10-024 freezes the cipher to AES-GCM, one cipher and not a menu, with ChaCha20/Poly1305 the frozen-out alternative; R-10-022 makes the per-extent AEAD one pass over confidentiality and integrity with the tag serving as the stored checksum, and R-10-022a holds the nonce and the tag in the index node rather than beside the ciphertext they authenticate. The parent cell's *both AEADs* disagrees with R-10-024, which is F-205d, and it stays open here for M3.4c's own reason: an entry is the review gate's to move and this item edits none.
  * **The tables are derived and then computed equal to the published ones**, which is the shape M3.4a set at FIPS 202 and the half of a differential pair a second transcription cannot supply. The S-box is the multiplicative inverse in GF(2^8) composed with the affine transform, the inverse taken as *a*^254 because the multiplicative group has order 255 and because *a*^254 is zero at zero, which is FIPS 197's own convention for the element that has none; the round constants are the doubling recurrence; 0x1B is computed from the modulus written as its own exponent list and 0xE1 from R's; and the MixColumns matrix is computed from the polynomial the standard prints beside it. Each is then held against the table FIPS 197 publishes, so a transcription defect is a disagreement with a published table and not a disagreement between two things this repository wrote.
  * **The pair with the curated model is a pair for M3.4a's own reason, and nothing holds it.** [types_kext.sail](../model/model/extensions/K/types_kext.sail) writes the forward and inverse S-box out as 256-entry vectors and decodes the round constants by a case table, so one file derives exactly what the other transcribes. **No rule in the checker reads the two together**, which is the shape K-91 already closed one primitive over, and it is reported rather than closed here: the rule costs the usual three edits and a mutant, and it needs a checker id this lane was not allotted.
  * **The GHASH bit order is the standard's, and the model's form is stated against it.** SP 800-38D reads a block's leftmost bit as the constant term, so its Algorithm 1 multiplies by the generator with a **right** shift and folds R = 11100001 || 0^120 in at the left end, which is the opposite of the convention an implementer reaches for. [zvkg_insts.sail](../model/model/extensions/vector_crypto/zvkg_insts.sail) computes the same product the other way round, reversing the bits inside every byte with `brev8` and then shifting **left** with a reduction by 0x87 at the low byte. Both forms are written out here and computed equal over 36 pairs, so either is the other's image or one of them is wrong. **The same loop without the reflection is the near alternative and it is the interesting one**, because it is the standard's own multiplication under a byte-wise bit reversal: a field multiplication and commutative like it, and refused because it names the wrong bit strings. Its unit is where that is smallest, and it is computed rather than argued from the isomorphism: the identity of the unreflected form is 0x01 00..00, the standard's own 0x80 00..00 reflected, and the standard's own is refused as its identity over the same probe family.
  * **Seven near alternatives, each held to the single difference it exists to exhibit and each shown to keep the obligations it does not break.** An affine transform with the constant one below the standard's, which is still a bijection and differs in the low bit at every one of the 256 inputs and nowhere else; a ShiftRows written against the transposed flattening of the state, which is still a permutation of the sixteen positions, still leaves four of them fixed, and agrees with the standard's at exactly one; the forward and inverse row shifts exchanged, which agree on half the state because a shift by two is its own inverse; a key expansion whose second-substitution guard is one higher; the unreflected GF(2^128) product above; a GHASH input with the associated data and the ciphertext run together unpadded; and a counter mode started at J0 rather than at inc32(J0). **Three of the seven are invisible to a whole family of inputs and the file says which**: the unpadded input agrees with the standard's at every case whose associated data and ciphertext are both block-aligned, which is three of the five published cases; the counter started at J0 agrees at every empty plaintext, which is the first of them; and the raised guard agrees at every key length FIPS 197 admits, parting only at a key of seven words. A vector set with no unaligned member and no non-empty plaintext decides two of them, and no vector at any admissible key length decides the third.
  * **How much of the word "invertible" is discharged, and whose obligation that is.** No entry calls AES a permutation, so it is a claim this file makes of itself, and three of the four step maps carry it: SubBytes is a bijection on a byte over all 256 and is recovered by the inverse substitution over all 256; ShiftRows is a permutation of the sixteen positions whose inverse recovers a state of sixteen **arbitrary** bytes; and AddRoundKey is an involution in the round key over arbitrary bits of equal shape, proved rather than computed. **MixColumns is not shown a bijection.** What stands in its place is the algebra underneath it, that the standard's a(x) and its published inverse multiply to {01} modulo x^4 + 1, together with the round trip on the thirty-two single-bit columns that span the column space over GF(2); the step from a spanning set to the whole map is GF(2)-linearity, which this file does not prove. Beside them the inverse cipher recovers the plaintext at each of the three key lengths, which is a value and not a proof.
  * **No decision the register owes is taken.** The key length, the tag length, the nonce and the associated data are fields of a parameter record rather than literals, with FIPS 197's three key lengths and both of SP 800-38D's initial-counter constructions carrying published answers; and the invocation bound is a fifth field the theorem beside it shows nothing here computes from, which is the shape of that gap rather than a bound taken. The only literals are the two standards' own and every one of them is named in the file's header.
  * **What a reused nonce costs is computed rather than argued.** Two messages sealed under one key and one nonce carry the exclusive-or of their plaintexts in the exclusive-or of their ciphertexts, which is why R-05-126b gives a nonce-typed value the linear grade and why R-10-023 keeps the per-extent nonce random. Neither of those is a property of this text, and the file says so where it computes it.
  * **Nothing about constant time is claimed and nothing is implied.** R-05-062 makes it a 2-safety hyperproperty on the binary and R-05-067 names AES among the primitives written branchless and *then verified on the artifact*; that the S-box here is computed from the field inverse rather than read out of a table is a remark about the shape of the text. The tag comparison in the open function is ordinary structural equality and the file states that a constant-time comparison is the binary's obligation. No masking claim under R-05-004a and no reduction or distributional claim under R-05-077a, so what is shipped is one of R-05-059's three layers and this is not a shipped primitive under that entry.
  * **The published answers reached the file by two routes.** The AES answers are FIPS 197's own, read at Appendices A.1, B and C.1 through C.3 and at the sections that print the S-box, the round constants and both MixColumns matrices; the five AES-GCM cases are the ones published with the GCM specification, and that is the whole of their provenance, nothing in this tree pinning what NIST's own validation sets carry. **Every literal was recomputed before it was written down**, by a second and independent transcription of the two standards held against OpenSSL 3.5.5 through its Python binding, which is the whole of what a known-answer check buys: the constant and the transcription came from different routes. Neither instrument is pinned, neither enters any trust base, and no ACVP file is quoted, so the three notice conditions M3.4a read at that corpus do not reach this file.
  * **The gate's green line means compiled, axiom-free, non-vacuous and enumerated, and it does not mean verified.** Nothing here executes on either emulator; the light checks are decided by conversion in the silent `Example ... := eq_refl` form and the ones that run a cipher by the bytecode machine, which contributes nothing to the assumption enumeration.
  * Net change: 2,045 lines added and none removed in one new file under [proofs/](../proofs/AesGcm.v); the two re-priced lines of M3.4c's cell above, which sheds the AEAD to this item and keeps the disagreement; and one line of M3.4a's own findings, which priced that shed scope at the sibling this item has since split again and is repaired where it stands.
  * Exit evidence: the proof gate at **242 constants in this file**, every one closed under the global context with no `Admitted`, no `Axiom`, no top-level `Parameter` and **no `Require` at all**, so nothing beyond the prelude is reachable and `map`, `nth`, `firstn`, `skipn`, `seq`, `rev`, `repeat`, `fold_left`, `filter` and `concat` are authored for that reason; **5 witnesses over the file's 1 quantified record**, the first of them the first published GCM case whole; **40 seeded weakenings run, 40 killed, none surviving and none stillborn**, which is a sample of 40 over a population of 173 and is named as a sample rather than reported as a score, against a baseline of 1,503 vectors from the Gallina front under Rocq 9.1.1, the prover being the entire oracle in that run because the vector half computes nothing this file defines; the three host gates green with all 107 checker mutants killed, the repair path holding and the registry covered; both type checkers clean; and the co-read ledger unmoved at 1,394 pairs read as they stand. No register, coverage-matrix, absence-contract, profile or crown-jewel edit, so the review gate is not re-entered.
  * Twelve findings, eight of them register acts reported rather than closed here.
    * **No entry fixes the symmetric key length.** R-10-024 names the cipher and stops. R-05-058a freezes the suite by parameter set at Category 5 throughout and names ML-KEM-1024, ML-DSA-87 and SLH-DSA-SHAKE-256s, all three asymmetric or hash-based, and Category 5 is itself defined by reference to the difficulty of an AES-256 key search, so reading a symmetric key length out of it is reading the yardstick as the measurement. The length is a parameter here and all three of FIPS 197's own key lengths carry a published answer. Owed at R-10-024 or R-05-058a.
    * **No entry fixes the tag length.** SP 800-38D admits 128, 120, 112, 104 and 96 bits generally and 64 and 32 for named applications only; R-10-022 makes the tag the stored checksum and fixes no t. Both of the standard's lists are written here as what it admits and never as what the platform takes. Owed at R-10-022.
    * **No entry fixes the nonce length or which initial-counter construction is taken.** SP 800-38D splits the two at a 96-bit nonce, the short arm a concatenation and the long one a GHASH of the nonce and its length. R-10-022 wants a per-extent nonce and R-10-023 wants it random, and neither is a length. Both arms are authored and both carry a published answer. Owed at R-10-022 or R-10-023.
    * **No entry carries the per-key invocation bound.** SP 800-38D bounds the invocations of the authenticated encryption function under one key, and no entry in this register states a bound of any kind; R-05-126b's linear nonce grade forces freshness and not a count. It is a record field here that nothing computes from, which is the shape of the gap. Owed at R-10-023 or R-15-241d.
    * **What a per-extent AEAD authenticates besides the extent is unstated.** R-10-022 and R-10-022a say where the nonce and the tag live and say nothing about the associated data, where an extent sealed with none and one sealed with its own address open differently against the block services R-10-021 puts below the integrity line and leaves free to move a block. Owed at R-10-022a.
    * **One entry names two tag constructions and another freezes one of them out.** R-10-022 writes *the Poly1305/GHASH tag serving as the stored checksum* where R-10-024 freezes the cipher to AES-GCM and names ChaCha20/Poly1305 the frozen-out alternative. Only GHASH is authored here. Owed at R-10-022.
    * **The constant-time entry's enumeration names a primitive the frozen suite does not carry.** R-05-067 lists Keccak, AES, ChaCha and the lattice NTT and samplers as the control-flow-heavy primitives hardened and then verified on the artifact, and ChaCha is what R-10-024 freezes out; those two entries are the only two in the register that name it. So one entry states an artifact-level obligation over a primitive no artifact will exist for. Owed at R-05-067.
    * **No entry fixes the oracle pair for an authored AEAD**, exactly as none fixes it for an authored hash: R-15-058 names FIPS 202 and the ACVP vectors for the model's Keccak unit and nothing names anything for this one, while M3.4's own cell wants two oracles of independent verification lineage and decides neither. What this file used is one implementation, named in its header and pinned nowhere. Owed at R-10-024 or in the plan.
    * **The differential pair this file claims is held by no rule.** One file derives the tables the curated model transcribes and each gate decides its own side, so a table edited on one side alone leaves both gates green and the pair silently reduced to two transcriptions. That is exactly what K-91 closes for the FIPS 202 pair, and the rule closing it here reads `aes_sbox_fwd_table` against this file's own published-table `Example`. It is not written, because it needs a checker id this lane was not allotted.
    * **MixColumns is the one step map not shown a bijection**, so the composite claim that AES is one is an obligation this file states of itself and leaves open. The other three carry it over arbitrary or exhaustive inputs; this one carries the algebraic unit and a spanning-set round trip, and the step between them is a linearity argument no prelude-only proof was found for inside this item.
    * **A published literal is recomputed before it is written down, or it is a transcription and not a known answer.** Every constant this file quotes was produced twice, once by reading the standard and once by an independent implementation run against OpenSSL, and the two were held equal before either reached the file. That is a rule about how work here is done rather than a fact about this item: a single-source literal makes the check that quotes it a check against the transcriber's memory.
    * **Three seeded weakenings survived across two samples, and every one was a site nothing above it decided.** The key expansion's *Nk greater than six* guard is the first: `6 < Nk` and `7 < Nk` agree at 4, at 6 and at 8, so no key length FIPS 197 admits can part them and every published vector accepts the six one higher. It is a parameter now, with the raised guard built beside it and the two parted at a key of seven words, which is a statement about the expansion rather than about the cipher. The second is the restricted tag-length list, which no computation visited at all; both lists are held to the standard's own shape **and to its own count**, the admissible ones descending one byte at a time from the full tag and five of them, each restricted one whole bytes below the shortest admissible and two of them. The count is what the shape alone leaves free, and it decides: a sixth admissible 88 descends one byte at a time like the five above it and a third restricted 24 is whole bytes below 96 like the two beside it, so the shape without the count passes lists SP 800-38D s5.2.1.2 does not admit. The statement that reads the two lists together holds each length to being a whole number of bytes the tag actually has, the truncation equality beside it being the weakest of its three clauses and true of every natural number. The third is the sharpest: **RotWord returning a longer word survives every published answer**, because the exclusive-or that consumes it truncates to the shorter of its operands and cuts the extra byte off again, so the rotation is now stated over four arbitrary bytes in both of its clauses, one place moved and four applications the identity. Each mutant is refused after the addition, which is how a survivor closes.
  * Landed: Tier A.

#### M3.6a · State and refute the ordered sequence

  * One statement artifact in [ApexTheorem.v](../proofs/ApexTheorem.v)'s idiom and nothing else. [DischargeSequence.v](../proofs/DischargeSequence.v) states R-15-247d's order, requesters in reset, tag-plane discharge, a fixed worst-corner dwell, a single completion read, fail-stop on a negative reading, the domain addressable, and separately admitted after that the data sanitization before measured execution; R-15-247f's single read after the dwell with no poll and no retry; the fail-stop arm **as a pair**, so that a machine latching on every reading is not a fail-stop; and R-15-247d's acceptance clause that no path admits a partially sanitized bank, stated over R-15-247e's per-bank drain and R-15-247b's granule-atomic commit. **The order is one order across two paths**, the first four phases sitting on the exit path R-15-247q relocates the discharge to and the last three on the entry path, which is R-15-189j's *what a bulk domain owes on the way in is the confirmation R-15-247d orders* read from the other side.
  * **Every obligation is stated of an arbitrary sequencer, reader, dwell length, reset hold or release schedule, and refuted of an alternative construction**, and where a family of weakenings can be generated rather than authored it is. Four list generators over the specification's own sequence produce the families mechanically: an adjacent transposition, a deletion, and a re-entry at a proper suffix, which is R-15-198's own phrase for what a wake and a standby exit are, each yielding one weakening per position, and a second completion read yielding one per position per phase. **Fifty-one generated members, every one refused, decided as one conversion**, each family fact also stated as a bounded quantifier over the index rather than an enumeration, and beside them an early-release family quantified over the step rather than over an index at all, which computes that exactly seven release schedules are early. Beside them **twelve constructions no index generates**: a read taken before the dwell that still reads once, a poll that is still ordered and still after the dwell, a retry that is ordered on first occurrences and caught only by the count, a sequencer admitting on a negative reading that passes every other obligation in the file, a discharge confirmed per domain off the bank its phase began at, a reader confirming a bank reached rather than drained, a dwell read off the sweep, a dwell differing between the arms, a reset exempting one of R-15-247h's three classes, and a step treating the tag confirmation as the data confirmation.
  * **No decision the register owes is taken.** The dwell length, the bank set, a bank's chunk decomposition and the requester roster are record fields, R-15-247m holding the first to a repaired megabit-class macro and R-15-247p putting the bank count in R-15-014a's frozen set. The only literals taken from the design are R-15-247d's phase order, R-15-247f's one read and R-15-247f's zero retries. **R-15-247f's second acceptance clause is not stated here and the file says so and names M3.6b**: it quantifies over a discharge speed no artifact in this repository carries, and nothing here introduces a speed, a rate, or any quantity derived from leakage, which R-15-247c forbids a containment guarantee from resting on in any case.
  * **The gate's green line means compiled, axiom-free, non-vacuous and enumerated, and it does not mean verified.** Nothing here executes on either emulator; the computed checks are decided by conversion in the silent `Example ... := eq_refl` form.
  * Five findings, every one a register gap reported rather than closed, and the register has since taken all five.
    * **R-15-247d's data-plane disjunction had no arm selected, and the §11 budget read differently on each.** The entry has the data plane deterministically cleared *or* its discharge confirmed, R-15-247b commits both planes atomically at the granule so one pass would satisfy it, and R-17-024a insists the two are two boundaries and not one. Whether the residue sanitization carried its own dwell and its own single read was stated nowhere, and the mode-transition budget carried one dwell on one reading and two on the other. Taken at R-15-247d: the disjunction resolves to one pass over both planes.
    * **The completion indication's arity against the bank staggering was unstated, and the silence was observable rather than harmless.** R-15-247f states a single read and R-15-247g stages the discharge in phases over banks; one read per transition, one per phase and one per bank are three §11 terms and three degrees of partiality visibility. The consequence is machine-checked: on a sweep that reached one bank and not the other, a per-domain reader confirms exactly where the per-bank reader refuses. Taken at R-15-247f: the read is per phase.
    * **R-15-247d's acceptance clause is a necessary condition and not its criterion, and it was found by writing the proof.** *No path admits a partially sanitized bank* is stated of a bank the pass reached and did not finish, so a bank nothing reached satisfies it, and the reader that **is** that clause is shown to admit an undrained domain. What excludes that admission is the completion read's own domain, so the clause and the read are two obligations and the clause alone does not carry the second. Taken at R-15-247d: two obligations and not one, each catching what the other cannot.
    * **R-15-247h's reset hold had no scope, and R-15-190 was where the two readings parted.** The entry held every application core, DMA engine and capability-bearing fabric initiator in reset while the sequencer ran; R-15-190 keeps exactly one island live across standby with R-15-192 running paging reception on it as an admitted hard task, and R-15-190a admits a bulk domain taken OFF at that transition. A die-wide hold and that hard task could not both hold as written. The requester roster here is a field for that reason, so the obligation is stated over whatever the register decides it names. Taken at R-15-247h: the hold is scoped to the requesters that can address the domain being discharged, which is what leaves R-15-190's admitted hard task standing.
    * **Where the fail-stop latch lives was unstated.** R-15-247f and R-15-189n both latch on a negative reading and neither said in which power domain the latch sits, which matters because R-15-247q puts the discharge on the path that collapses the rail. The sequencer here carries the arm and not the latch's location. Taken at R-15-247f: the latch sits in the always-on root-of-trust power domain.
  * Net change: 1,242 lines added and none removed across one file, which is new.
  * Exit evidence: the proof gate at **180 constants against 104**, 76 of them new, every one closed under the global context with no `Admitted`, no `Axiom`, no top-level `Parameter` and **no `Require` at all**, so nothing beyond the prelude is reachable, `Classical` and `FunctionalExtensionality` included and every state equality stated pointwise for that reason; the file compiling with nothing on stdout but those lines; **sixty shipped obligations of which twenty-seven carry a refutation**, over forty-four alternative constructions, thirty-two generated by five generators and twelve authored; **twenty-six seeded weakenings fired and none surviving**, in two equal batches, the first breaking a definition the theorems are stated over and the second collapsing each refuted construction onto the specification, every one of the second batch dying **at exactly its own refutation**, which shows a pair is a pair rather than two statements about the same names; every one of the twenty-seven cited requirement ids resolving against the register by hand, which no rule holds for a `.v` file; the checker green with every mutant killed and the registry covered; both type checkers clean; and no register, coverage-matrix, absence-contract or crown-jewel edit, so the review gate is not re-entered and the co-read ledger is unmoved.
  * Landed: Tier A.

## M4 · Kernel

### M4.1 · Decide revocation sweep quanta

  * **Dropped.** R-08-007's incremental, preemptible, per-core background-slot form stands unamended, and the [quiescent-point declination](architectural-alternatives.md) carries the evaluation, the arms not taken, and the one result that would reopen it. No register edit, which is the arm's whole shape: the call was whether to amend R-08-007, and the answer is that the proposal's payoff has no referent here.
  * **The two grounds that decide it.** There is no root set to make a composition-time artifact: R-08-004 deleted the capability derivation tree and the traversal went with it, so R-08-005a's bitmap **is** the mark, one architectural bit per granule written by the kernel at teardown, and R-08-007b's `creclaim` is a linear pass over granule groups rather than a reachability trace. And the mutator-overlap argument the rule exists to delete is one this design never has to make, because R-08-005 puts *freed ⇒ unreachable* at **access** time and R-08-005b makes the revoked case a defined result of the load instruction: it holds whether or not a quantum runs beside a compartment holding live capabilities. Oberon needed the quiescent point because it had no load barrier to buy instead.
  * **What the arms not taken forfeit.** Accepting would have bought a deleted proof obligation, which is the currency §17 counts, at the price of amending R-08-007, R-08-007a, R-08-007b, R-08-008, R-11-008, R-11-020, R-17-023 and the four §15 entries that argue from the sweep, plus resolving three collisions (R-15-220 fixes partition-switch cost at three terms and a boundary-bound quantum is a fourth; R-07-036 and R-08-007's own *never in another partition's time* forbid borrowing the swept domain's slot; R-11-008's standing reservation is either kept, buying nothing, or retired, changing two more entries) and authoring the §11 stack-depth and callee-graph root-set analysis the proposal assumes and no entry specifies. Deferring was the third arm and is declining by another route: the payoff is purchasable only before M4.2 writes the incremental form, so a deferral past it costs the same thing without recording why.
  * Net change: 75 lines added and 31 removed across six files, shared with the three provenance decisions this commit also closes.
  * Exit evidence: the arm is named and the arms not taken are recorded with what each forfeits; R-08-007 is unamended, so no coverage cell, absence-contract row, or crown-jewel row moves and the review gate is not re-entered; the two documents that described the tension as open no longer do, and the critique's work-list item 11 is closed rather than left standing; the checker green on all 64 rules; and the co-read ledger unmoved, no register entry or prose span having been touched.

### M4.1a · Decide the kernel-specification provenance fork

  * **Authored instead.** M4.2 derives its Gallina objects from the design this repository already specifies rather than from seL4's `spec/` and `proof/` artifacts, so nothing GPL-2.0-only is translated and the kernel carries the same licence as everything else here. What seL4 supplies stays what it always supplied and carries nothing: the endpoint and non-interference *design*, which is an idea rather than an artifact.
  * **The second decision that can invalidate work rather than delay it, and it was found by reading tags rather than by reasoning from lineage.** The seL4 proof repository tags every file and the tags split on directory: `spec/` and `proof/` are **GPL-2.0-only** where `lib/` and `tools/` are BSD-2-Clause, and the executable-spec objects M4.2 would take are on the GPL-2.0-only side, sampled across the Haskell executable spec, the generated design spec, the abstract spec and the refinement proofs.
  * **This item takes code rather than lineage, and the two carry different terms.** What the design takes from seL4 is two ideas and no code, which carries nothing. What M4.2 would take is artifacts, and a translation is a derivative work of its source whatever the target language, so the kernel would inherit the term. Apache states its own licence incompatible with GPL version 2, which makes this a fork rather than an attribution chore, and seL4's syscall note does not reach it, exempting user-level code that calls the kernel rather than code derived from its specification.
  * **This is the one of the three where taking the term is not reversible**, which is why it resolves differently from M1.1a and M6.0a: those contain an instrument that never reaches the shipped image where a translated kernel specification *is* the image, and no later decision undoes a derivation once it is written.
  * The arms not taken. **Take the term** carries the kernel as GPL-2.0-only, splitting this repository's licensing and foreclosing combination with anything Apache-2.0, M1.1a's contained toolchain included. **Narrow the roster**, taking only what the BSD-2-Clause halves supply and hand-authoring the rest, is the middle arm and the only one needing a per-object read before it can be sized; it is declined for buying a fraction of the saving at the cost of a per-file provenance argument on every object M4.2 touches. What the taken arm forfeits is this cell's translation saving and the scrutiny riding a mechanical `hs-to-coq` carriage of an artifact many people have read.
  * Exit evidence: the arm is named, the arms not taken are recorded with what each forfeits, and every source object M4.2 would touch is listed with its repository and its tag in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md), read from the file rather than from the project's reputation.

### M4.1b · Decide the kernel-C provenance fork

  * **Authored instead.** The on-device kernel C is written against the Gallina specification M4.2 authors rather than started from the CHERI-Alliance **CHERI-seL4 / CHERI-Microkit** release, so nothing GPL-2.0-only is taken and the kernel's specification and its C carry one licence. §5's step 3 named that release as the start-from and is repaired at its own site rather than restated here.
  * **The read this rests on is S7's, quoted at the file rather than inferred from the project.** The tree is multi-licence by SPDX tag and the kernel half is GPL-2.0-only: 733 of 1,045 tracked files carry that tag, `src/` at 313 of 324 and `include/` at 297 of 311. The CHERI work is contributed **under** that licence rather than beside it, the RISC-V boot, trap and vspace paths each carrying a Capabilities Limited copyright line above a GPL-2.0-only tag, so there is no CHERI-only subset to take. seL4's syscall note exempts user-level code that *calls* the kernel and not code derived *from* it, and §6 of the GPL text is what refuses the composition with this repository's Apache-2.0 content, Apache-2.0's patent-termination and notice provisions being further restrictions in that section's sense. What is compatible is `libsel4` and the BSD-3-Clause files beside the kernel, which is not what §5's step 3 named.
  * **This is the one of the four provenance forks where taking the term is not reversible**, which is why it resolves as M4.1a resolved and not as M1.1a and M6.0a did: those contain an instrument that never reaches the shipped image, where a kernel C taken under the term *is* the image. It would also leave the specification authored and the C derived under two licences that cannot be combined, which is worse than either consistently.
  * **What the arms not taken forfeit.** *Take the term* carries the kernel as GPL-2.0-only, splits this repository's licensing, and forecloses combination with every Apache-2.0 artifact here, the M1.1a-contained toolchain included. *Obtain terms* is a fourth negotiation on this plan and blocks M4.4's scheduling until it returns. What the taken arm forfeits is the maintained-code start-from and the scrutiny riding an artifact many people have read, and the forfeit is priced rather than absorbed: M4.4, M6.3a and M6.5a lose their start-from and move from class I to class X in this same edit.
  * Exit evidence: the arm is named, the arms not taken are recorded with what each forfeits, and the refused tree is carried with its repository and its tag in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md), read at the file. No register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit, so the review gate is not re-entered.
  * Landed: Tier A.

#### M4.2a · The partition context, the switch, and the cyclic-executive schedule

  * Two statement artifacts in [ApexTheorem.v](../proofs/ApexTheorem.v)'s idiom and nothing else. [PartitionContext.v](../proofs/PartitionContext.v) states R-07-015's total restore over the one merged file of 32 registers of 64+1 bits R-15-007i fixes and over every CSR a partition can name, restored or zeroized per the profile's own disposition; the absence of a vector or matrix component by construction (R-07-014a); R-07-044's pending-bit obligation on both arms of its disjunction; R-15-220's three-term cost with the drain counted once; and R-07-037b's intra-slot rotation, which performs strictly fewer of R-15-220's constants, inherits the register restore whole, and inherits the CSR restore only over the class the omitted pass does not clear. [CyclicExecutive.v](../proofs/CyclicExecutive.v) states R-11-006's interval arithmetic with R-11-009's switch duty inside it, R-11-020's reserved band discharged once across a ladder, R-11-014a and R-11-014d's one phase offset and one frame length, R-11-023's invariance under every retenanting, R-11-024's table swap, R-11-022's focus shape, R-11-025's rung index, R-11-026's hard ceiling under any victim choice, and R-07-033 and R-07-036's time-to-slot map that reads no occupant. The dependency between the two files is R-11-009's, and it is load-bearing rather than a citation: `switch_cost` enters the interval arithmetic as the per-boundary constant, and a demo rung sits exactly at the margin, so one unit more of it turns an admitted frame into a refused one.
  * **Every obligation is stated of an arbitrary relation, cost function or predicate, and refuted of an alternative construction.** That is what R-05-166 asks for and it is what keeps a positive theorem from being a change detector over the file's own definitions: the property is written once, proved of the specification, and shown false of a construction the register's own sentence excludes. Twenty-one of the seventy-six shipped statements carry a refutation, every one proved outright and none admitted, among them a restore truncated to the low registers, a restore that carries the 64 data bits and drops the validity tag, a restore that exempts one nameable CSR, a switch that carries one predecessor CSR across the boundary beside the residue-free twin that shows the carried value rather than the shape is what refutes it, a step that performs what the switch performs held against the containment claim, a rotation that pays `fence.t`, the four-term cost reading R-15-220 refuses, an admission check that reads a tenant, a ladder whose rungs disagree on the reserved band, a focus shape that reads a tenant, a rung change costed one switch per slot, a population step that grants a thinner slot instead of refusing, and a work-conserving time-to-slot map.
  * **No decision the register owes is taken.** Every quantity §7 and §11 leave to composition is a record field rather than a literal, so the CSR bank, the pending type and its static partition, the four switch cost terms, the harmonicity predicate, the focus majority, the rung ladder, the top rung's capacity, the table-load constant, the per-slot bounds and the victim choice are all parameters. The only literals taken from the design are R-15-007i's 32 registers, its single validity-tag bit, and R-15-220's three-term arity. Nothing about endpoints, notifications, badges, message shape, a closed kernel-object inventory, grant-slot transitions, revocation-sweep bookkeeping, or otype assignment is authored, and no register, coverage-matrix, absence-contract or crown-jewel row moves.
  * **The gate's green line means compiled, axiom-free, non-vacuous and enumerated, and it does not mean verified.** Neither file executes anything on either emulator, and both headers say so; what is machine-checked is that the statements typecheck, rest on no axiom, are satisfiable, and exclude something.
  * Eight findings.
    * **R-07-031a's acceptance criterion audits an artifact that does not exist.** The criterion reads *the invocation list is enumerated and closed*, and no artifact here carries that enumeration: not the register, the spec, `proofs/`, the model or `corpus/`. It is the one criterion of that shape whose artifact is missing, R-07-015's and R-15-001b's both landing in the profile's CSR table. A Gallina ABI authored now would become the list rather than implement it, acquiring that standing under a proof gate that never asked the question the review gate asks. Closed by R-07-031b (S1), which numbers the list closed at five; carried back at S25.
    * **R-07-029 and R-07-037a state incompatible things about the same act, and R-12-096 adds a third voice.** R-07-029 makes IPC synchronous endpoints; R-07-037a makes a compartment run to completion *with no blocking call*; R-12-096 has a ring consumer *sleep*, a word the register defines nowhere. What a partition does when an endpoint has no ready peer is unfixed three ways, and choosing an arm in Gallina would resolve it by fiat. Closed by R-07-029a (S1): synchronous is rendezvous or refusal, and R-12-096's sleep is the poll-site yield, nowhere a block; carried back at S25.
    * **Whether the R-07-037b rotation swaps R-07-044's pending bits is unstated, and the silence is observable rather than harmless.** R-07-037b has the intra-slot step *swap register and partition context* and R-07-044 states its discipline at the switch; neither says what the rotation does with the interrupt-file pending bits. `rotation_pending_arm_is_observable` machine-checks the consequence: on the swapping arm the rotation carries nothing across in that component, and on the other two rotations from one successor context disagree on it. So it is a decision the register owes rather than a detail composition may settle, and it was found by writing the proof. Closed by R-07-037c (S1), which swaps the interrupt-file pending component on R-07-044's arm exactly as the switch does and cites this file's observability theorem; carried back at S25.
    * **A same-label group member begins its reaction on state the rotation does not clear, and no entry says what it may assume about it.** [The profile](isa-profile.md) makes the vector CSRs zeroize rather than save, R-07-014c makes `vmclear` the one instruction that clears them, and R-07-037b's intra-slot step omits that pass; its ground for the omission decides confidentiality rather than this, and R-07-014b's sink-before-yield is scoped to a computation that spans slots. `rotation_omits_the_zeroize_at_state_level` machine-checks the consequence: a post-state carrying the previous member's zeroized-class value is admitted by the rotation and refused by the switch. Closed by R-07-037d (S1): a same-label group shares one confidentiality domain, so a member may begin on the zeroize-class state a sibling left; carried back at S25.
    * **R-11-023's criterion enumerates the admission check's inputs more narrowly than R-11-006 and R-11-009 state the check.** The criterion says the interval arithmetic quantifies over *widths and offsets and never occupants*, while R-11-006 states it over slot WCETs and each task's period and R-11-009 requires the switch-duty ratio counted explicitly. The invariance here is proved over all four declared quantities, the reading that keeps *never occupants* load-bearing, and the narrower enumeration is reported rather than repaired. Closed by R-11-023's amended Accept (S1), which enumerates the four and states why *never occupants* makes the list four rather than two; carried back at S25.
    * **R-15-220 fixes partition-switch cost at three terms and names neither the total restore nor R-07-044's pending swap among them.** Its terms are `fence.t`, one `vmclear` pass and OPP relock, and R-18-009 treats those as the whole of it. Yet R-07-044 puts the pending bits *in the switch budget* and R-07-015's total restore is work the switch performs, so either the budget is not exactly three terms or those two sit inside one of the three, and no entry says which. `switch_cost` here is R-15-220's three terms exactly and claims nothing more. Closed by R-15-220a (S1): R-15-220's three are the platform terms and the restore and the pending swap sit beside them in one context term, with R-18-009 corrected to match; carried back at S25.
    * **The surviving object count is five in the prose and three or four in the register, and nothing enumerates the five.** [The spec](spec.md) and [the inspirations](inspirations.md) each speak of five surviving classes without naming them and disagree on what happens to them; R-07-027 enumerates three, R-07-031a names four ABI groups one of which is not an object, and R-08-004d puts the grant table outside the restored classes, while R-07-020's criterion quantifies over an *object inventory* nothing enumerates. Authoring a closed inductive would resolve a three-way disagreement by fiat and then machine-check the resolution, so none is authored. Closed by R-07-027a (S1), which resolves the count as a conflation of two kinds rather than by picking a number, three object classes beside two tables that are not objects, with R-07-020's criterion now naming the inventory it closes; carried back at S25.
    * **The rule registry has a gap at K-64 that nothing records and no rule can see.** [The registry](../tools/check-rules.md) runs K-63 straight to K-65 with no struck row and no retirement note, and the id occurs nowhere else in the tree. K-00 holds registered rows against the checks in both directions and says nothing about a hole in the numbering, so whether K-64 was retired or never allocated is unanswerable. Nothing here depends on the answer. Closed by S1's struck registry row, which records K-64 as never allocated and not reused, so it is not a free id and K-00 holds the live rows alone; carried back at S25.
  * Net change: 2,083 lines added and 7 removed across three files, two of them new.
  * Exit evidence: the proof gate at **104 constants against 19**, 85 of them new, every one closed under the global context with no `Admitted`, no `Axiom`, no top-level `Parameter` and no `Require` but the one naming the sibling artifact, so nothing beyond the prelude is reachable, `Classical` and `FunctionalExtensionality` included, and every state equality is stated pointwise for that reason; both files compiling with nothing on stdout but those lines, the computed checks written as `Example ... := eq_refl` rather than as a `Compute` the gate reads as chatter; every cited requirement id resolving against the register, which no rule holds for a `.v` file; the load-bearing properties fired red against seeded weakenings, and the retenanting theorem's own refutation dying when the retenanting is made the identity, which shows a pair is a pair rather than two statements about the same names; the checker green on **all 76 rules**, all 80 mutants killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite green and both type checkers clean; and no register, coverage-matrix, absence-contract or crown-jewel edit, so the review gate is not re-entered and the co-read ledger is unmoved.

#### M4.2b · Endpoint and notification IPC, and the surviving object inventory

  * One statement artifact in [ApexTheorem.v](../proofs/ApexTheorem.v)'s idiom, [EndpointIPC.v](../proofs/EndpointIPC.v), and nothing else. It states R-07-031b's invocation enumeration closed at five under R-07-031a's four groups and inside R-07-031's *under a dozen*; R-07-027a's inventory closed at three object classes beside two tables that are not objects, with no reply object surviving and the return path a badge; R-07-029's synchronous endpoints and explicit capability transfer under R-07-029a's rendezvous-or-refusal, with its typed refusal, its bounded refusal cost and its re-offer discipline; R-07-030's refusal of a submission-queue opcode surface; R-04-008's no-mint-at-runtime over the transfer; R-08-032's store and R-07-039's loads, from which the emptiness of the notification group is **derived rather than asserted**; R-12-096's coalescible hint with its defined reset and its recheck; R-07-037b's composition-fixed rotation; and R-07-037c's pending-bit obligation over both of its conjuncts and on both arms of R-07-044's disjunction.
  * **Every obligation is stated of an arbitrary carrier, proved of the specification, and refuted of an alternative construction the register's own sentence excludes**, each refuting construction shown to satisfy the clauses it does not break.
