  * **The scope is derived and the derivation is three steps a reviewer checks one at a time**, the cell citing no requirement id. *Step one* reads the three families off M6.5's own lead: R-12-005 and R-12-008a are the SPSC, R-12-096 the notification, R-12-095 with R-12-098 the capacity. *Step two* takes what those spell out, which is a reading: R-12-005 cites R-07-029, R-12-095's acceptance clause cites R-08-047, R-05-097, R-07-036 and R-17-030u, R-12-098 cites R-12-009, and R-12-096 names no id. *Step three* is the judgment and it reaches the rest by section 6's own lead, that a service which cannot state its finite capacities, lifecycle semantics, cleanup bounds and per-operation WCET is not admitted: R-12-094, R-12-097, R-12-101 and R-12-091. Two more are reached by a noun an entry uses and does not define: R-12-096's *sleep* reaches R-07-029a, which says a sleep here is the poll-site yield and nowhere a block, and R-12-101's *slot budget* reaches R-11-006.
  * **Five closed enumerations and no sixth**, the criterion at each being that one sentence of one entry names its members: R-12-008a's four ownership phases, its acceptance clause's three rejected paths, its three concurrently shared cells stated against R-12-091's four header words rather than counted apart, R-12-096's four consumer acts in that entry's own order, and its producer's three. Two more are the *contract's* and are used at RingContract's spelling: the eight statuses and the six lifecycle states, whose length is checked against the contract's own terminal rank rather than written as a figure.
  * **SPSC is stated over an arbitrary interleaving and that is the file's one genuine quantifier over executions.** A schedule is a list over a two-member alphabet, because R-12-008a's three atomics are what let each agent write one index and read the other, and the invariant survives every list by induction. Four constructions are refuted and each keeps the half it does not break: the dual producer breaks the capacity bound and keeps the ordering, the dual consumer breaks the ordering and keeps the capacity, and the untested producer and consumer break one each at the boundary the other sits on. The index algebra is R-12-091's criterion read as it is written: a sequence number with two reductions, and it is computed that no two sequence numbers inside one capacity-wide window share a wire index and that a slot is reused exactly a capacity later, which is what *never overwrites an unconsumed entry* means arithmetically.
  * **The notification half is stated at the interleaving and not at the decision rule**, which is the half RingContract's own header says it does not carry. The producer acts at one step of the consumer's chain, reads the armed word at that instant, and signals only if it is armed, so the lost wakeup is a schedule rather than a predicate: no publication step leaves the consumer asleep with work pending and no signal sent. The consumer that arms after rechecking is refuted at exactly the step between its read and its arm, and it keeps what it does not break. Two further disciplines are refuted: a producer that never signals, which is what makes the signal act of the producer chain live rather than decorative, and a consumer that sleeps on a count of notifications, which is R-12-096's *no notification counter exists* met at a coalesced burst rather than as an absence.
  * **Twenty-six weakenings are generated over the two act chains rather than authored**, three transpositions, four deletions, four proper suffixes and four duplications over R-12-096's consumer order, and two, three, three and three over its producer order, every one refused as one conversion, again per family, and again as a bounded quantifier over the index whose bound one wider reaches the specification and fails. Each transposition and each duplication breaks **exactly one** conjunct and the specification breaks none; every conjunct is broken by some member, which is the other half of the same check. The duplicated stage is the one worth naming: staging a payload twice is reading the client's buffer twice, so R-05-124's copy-once is what refuses it and not a redundancy rule.
  * **Capacity is stated as equalities against declared slack, in the discipline M6.4 established, and the three inequalities that item left open stay its own.** This service declares its accepted ceiling against the completion capacity and against the ring's, its batch against the declared maximum, its segments against the declared segment count, its staging buffer against the declared payload, its cleanup against the declared cleanup bound and its accounted latency against its declared progress bound, each as an equality with a declared margin, so a weakening moves a figure rather than eating headroom. Closing R-12-095's completion-capacity bound, R-12-098's batch bound and R-12-097's commit-point bound is an act at [the ring declaration](../interfaces/ring-reference.json) and therefore M6.4's, and it is not taken here.
  * **Section 6's *no service is grandfathered* is thirteen conjuncts over a declaration, decided from both sides.** `spoiled_at` moves one declared field to the value on conjunct k's own boundary and `declared_without` drops conjunct k from the filter, so each is decided once from the declaration and once from the filter: every spoiling breaks exactly one, no spoiled service is admitted, each dropped conjunct admits exactly the service it stopped checking and no other. The rule itself is stated of an **arbitrary** service rather than of the witness, admission being exactly the absence of a broken conjunct.
  * **The copy is the half a DMA service does not have, and it is where R-12-012a bites.** A delegated buffer is the client's own memory and the client is the Byzantine peer R-12-008a's posture already assumes, so the buffer has two images. The copy-once service reads it once and everything downstream reads the staged copy; the revalidating service validates the length it first read and copies the length it reads second, and it is refuted at the validated extent, at the read count and at the second image while still refusing an overlong length. Beside it the cost is charged at the declared maximum rather than at what arrived, which is what gives R-11-006's admission a figure to read, refuted by an arrival-charged accounting that still bounds a run inside its own extent.
  * **The batch is an amortization unit and the difference from a transaction is machine-checked rather than argued.** On a ring with room for three of five, the specification enqueues three and refuses two while the transactional batch rolls back all five, and the two agree exactly where nothing is refused.
  * **The gate's green line means compiled, axiom-free, non-vacuous and enumerated, and it does not mean verified.** Nothing here is compiled, lowered or run on either emulator. No ring exists and no service rides one: no page is mapped, no descriptor is encoded or parsed, no byte is copied, no notification word is written, no partition is scheduled and no device is touched. R-18-037's canonical SPSC proof is a proof about an implementation under Ztso and this is not one; what is stated is a discipline over an index algebra and an ordered protocol, which is what such an implementation would be refined to. M6.5b owns the DMA service and with it every capability clause of R-12-100, the quiescence rule and the teardown; RingContract already states R-12-097's four cancellation answers and this file states only the cleanup accounting beside them; and M6.2's admission checker exists on neither side, which is IDL-068's own second limit.
  * **The item is not split.** Its own sentence names three families and section 6's lead names four obligations of any service, and all seven are carried. What is outside it was outside it at entry: the systems-language client and server bindings wait on M1.2, and the DMA half is M6.5b's.
  * **Twelve survivors across three passes, every one real, and each closed by adding the statement its absence exposed.** The first pass left nine and the second three, and the second's three are the first's shape met again: a witness field no obligation read, and a strict comparison the generated family cannot reach. Nine were fields the file wrote and nothing looked at, the initial world's six cells, the greedy consumer's own budget, each spoiler's moved figure, the second service's cadence and identity, the two slot witnesses' identifiers, the staged run's datum and the reference descriptor's second buffer reference. Two were conjuncts the family exercised at one boundary and not at both: a batch tested against zero rather than against one, and a cost conjunct whose three costs were never moved apart. The last is the one worth the finding, and it is below. Narrowing the population would have hidden all twelve.
  * **A refuter's keeps-theorem is read at the instant the defect is about, and the first one here was not.** The transposed consumer's keeps-theorem said it sleeps only on an armed word, read at the end of the activation, and a publication landing after the sleep clears that word under the specification as well, so a theorem written to isolate the defect was false of a construction that keeps the property it names. The repair is one theorem and no new construction: state what the refuter never does at all, which here is consume past what the producer published.
  * Net change: 3,450 lines added and none removed in one file, which is new, plus 56 lines in [findings-register.md](findings-register.md) and one line in [vos/checks/estimates.py](../tools/vos/checks/estimates.py), which is the critical chain losing this item, the act every landing on that chain makes.
  * Fourteen findings, eleven of them register gaps reported rather than closed and the other three methods the next artifact of this kind pays for or avoids.
    * **No entry says which agent resets the notification word or at which point.** R-12-096 requires a binary armed state with a *defined* reset and defines none, and IDL-059 restates the requirement. Both arms exclude the lost wakeup and they differ on coalescing: a reset at the signal sends one signal for one arming and a reset at the drain sends two, so what decides between them here is R-12-101's declared maximum notifications and not the entry. That figure's own scope, per activation or per arming, is not stated either.
    * **R-12-100's bounded-segment discipline is stated of zero-copy DMA and reaches a copying service only through R-12-101's per-variant record.** That entry's sentence is about the session-table capability, the validated extent and the fabric throughout; what obliges a service that copies to bound its segments is a record field every operation variant must carry.
    * **A copy-based service has no device contract to import a device-service bound from.** R-12-101 requires exactly that import, and the largest term of this service's per-operation WCET is therefore a figure with no named owner. The service reproduces the declaration's own and claims nothing about its provenance.
    * **Which of R-12-094's six states make a request identifier live is unstated.** R-12-092 requires the identifier unique among the session's live requests and R-12-094 rejects reuse of a live one; Submitted and Accepted are forced and Free and Reclaimed are excluded on pain of no identifier ever being reusable, and Writing and Terminal are the declaration's. Both arms are admitted and the difference is machine-checked on an identifier held by a slot being written.
    * **Whether the accounted latency includes the queueing delay of the requests ahead of a submission is undecided.** R-12-101 names capacity, batch size, polling cadence, slot budget and device latency and no relation among them, so the accounting is a declared field, both arms are admitted, and the difference is exactly the queueing term at every operation.
    * **The polling cadence is declared nowhere.** It is one of the five terms of R-12-101's joint bound, it is not among the constants R-12-091 enumerates, and it is not among the fourteen the ring declaration fixes, so the term the arithmetic turns on has no owner. It is a field here and it is read by the conjunct holding the declared latency to the declared accounting.
    * **Nothing maps R-12-008a's four ownership phases onto R-12-094's six lifecycle states.** R-12-094's acceptance clause says the lifecycle refines those transitions without weakening them and gives no map, and four and six do not pair by counting. Both are carried and neither is stated as a function of the other.
    * **R-12-096 gives the producer a predicate it cannot evaluate.** *Signals only when the consumer may sleep* names the consumer's own recheck, and the only cell R-12-091's header gives the producer is the notification word, so either the armed word is that predicate or the clause names something no header cell carries. The armed word is read as the predicate and the reading is stated.
    * **What a copy-based service copies into has no owner.** R-05-124 has a copy-once parser write its fixed destination buffer whole and no entry gives a server a staging buffer, a size for it, or a lifetime.
    * **No entry excludes a server re-reading a delegated buffer after validating it.** R-12-092 puts the validation before eligibility and says nothing about a second read, and R-12-100's *never reinterpreted after* is stated of the DMA extent alone, so the time-of-check defect a copying service is exposed to is excluded by no entry and is refuted here by construction.
    * **Every composition magnitude of this service is a field.** The accepted ceiling and its two slacks, the batch and its slack, and per operation the segments, the staging buffer, the cleanup cost, the released-reference count, the three reproduced costs, the accounted latency, the progress slack and the progress bound; and with them the live state set, the reset owner, the counter flag, the cadence, the accounting arm, the charging rule and the identity. The demo declaration instantiates each with a witness carrying no composition claim and the ledger pins every one.
    * **A strict comparison between two positions in a list is weakened with nothing to notice.** The two orderings agree everywhere except where both operands are absent, which a generated family of reorderings never reaches: every member it produces still carries both acts. So an ordering conjunct needs a witness outside the family whose two acts are both missing, and the population found the same shape twice, once per chain. Three such chains are added, each carrying a broken count that moves when the comparison does.
    * **An obligation stated as arithmetic over a contract's ranks licenses every construction the arithmetic admits and not the ones the contract's relation names.** Lawfulness here read *one rank more or two ranks more*, which is `lifecycle_malformed_ok`'s own arithmetic; the contract's `lifecycle_malformed` is a partial relation answering at Submitted alone, so the arithmetic licensed a second skip at all six states. The two readings agree on the specification and on the three other refuters and differ on the fourth alone, so the difference is invisible until a construction sits on it.
    * **A header's roster of hand-authored refutations is prose no gate reads and it drifts.** This one named a reordering publisher, a partial enqueue and a skipping advancer the file did not carry, and omitted the greedy consumer, the staging-only producer and the arrival-charged accounting it did. The roster is checked against the file by hand, name by name, because nothing else does.
  * **The artifact was re-read adversarially after the exit evidence below was taken, and the ten defects that reading found are repaired rather than reported.** Five are substantive. R-12-094's lawfulness conjunct read the rank arithmetic rather than the contract's two relations, so the skipping advancer the header's own roster already named was admitted by all three obligations; it is refuted at that conjunct alone now, with the superseded reading kept beside it as a construction and the difference machine-checked. R-12-096's notification conjunct read one operation's declared maximum where the field is per-variant, and it reads all five now. R-12-095's *no partial enqueue* had no refuting construction, and the untested producer is shown to break it and the dual producer to keep it. R-12-096's admitted budget was written as a literal where the contract's `ring_max_batch_size` owns it, against this file's own no-transcription rule. And the two algebras were joined in a comment: this file computes occupancy by subtracting sequence numbers where R-12-091's wire carries indices modulo the span, so `work_pending` and `sleeps` are each met by a bridge theorem now, over every base the span carries and every occupancy the capacity admits. **Reading 1 rested on that identity and nothing stated it.** Five are hygiene: the never-signalling publisher gained the keeps-theorem its three siblings had; three keeps-theorems bounded at an index no theorem decided anything at are stated of an arbitrary publication step; the six service events were counted against the six lifecycle states, which is a coincidence of two counts, and are counted against the contract's own steps now; two ledger lines were bounds admitting every value below them and are sums that move when any of the five records does; and the roster is corrected, having named three constructions the file did not carry and omitted three it did. **The exit-evidence figures below are that gate's own and stand as it recorded them.** Over the repaired artifact the proof gate reports **230 constants against the same 4,135**, a global **4,365**, **16 witnesses over 4 quantified records** unchanged, and a re-run seeded sample of **40 of 379 mutants, 40 killed, none surviving and none stillborn**, with minus-to-plus and orb-to-andb the two operators that sample did not reach.
  * **A mutant costs the Require closure and not the directory, and this file is the first to pay a closure larger than itself.** [RingContract.v](../proofs/RingContract.v) is 36 constants and compiles once per staged tree at the baseline, and nothing under [proofs/](../proofs/) Requires this file, so a mutant is one compile of one source exactly as M6.3a measured. What the Require buys is that every constant this file is stated over has one owner; what it costs is a baseline compile, not a per-mutant one.
  * Exit evidence: the proof gate at **4,352 constants against 4,135**, **217 of them this file's**, every one closed under the global context, with one `Require` naming the sibling the header states, no `Compute` and no top-level axiom, and **16 witnesses over 4 quantified records**; a seeded sample of **40 of 376 mutants over nine operators, 40 killed, none surviving and none stillborn**, stillborn reported apart and being zero, the run stating its own scope as a sample rather than as a population, with the two earlier passes quoted beside it at **31 killed, 9 survived** over a population of 359 and at **37 killed, 3 survived** over 376, every survivor real and each closed above; three operators the sample did not reach at all, minus-to-plus, negb-dropped and orb-to-andb, which is a scope statement and not a result; every cited requirement id resolved against the register by hand, which no rule holds for a `.v` file; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; the tools' own tests green at **356 cases over 38 modules**, which this item owes because it changed [vos/checks/estimates.py](../tools/vos/checks/estimates.py); and **K-00** reporting the registry's **91** rules and the checks in agreement. **Both verdicts are the prover's alone**: [Vectors.v](../tools/quickchick/Vectors.v) computes over the partition, executive, probe and endpoint sources and never this file, so the generated input side reaches none of its 376 sites and every prover-accepted mutant survives the second oracle by construction, which is a measurement rather than a complaint. **The whole population is owed and is not run**, one sample of 40 taking about fifteen minutes of wall clock against a guest this lane shares, and the population being nine and a half times it. No `run.py evidence` sweep is owed: it measures the model build, the property harness, the profile sweep, the corpus, the devicetree and the reference, and this item touches none of them; the two guest runs that bear on it are quoted. No register, ISA-profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit, so the review gate is not re-entered and the co-read ledger is unmoved.
  * **The three host gates are not green on this branch and the reason is arithmetic no lane may repair.** `check.py` reports four figure families the landing moved and `--fix` owns: **K-36**'s four item and subtotal cells, **K-37**'s thirteen restated totals, **K-96**'s thirteen chain and calibration figures, and **K-82**'s two findings-register totals. Every other rule is green, and the selftest's baseline is red only because it runs the checker whole. The one **K-96** finding that is *not* arithmetic is repaired here rather than left: the critical chain named this item and the document carries no open cell under it any more, so the chain loses it in [estimates.py](../tools/vos/checks/estimates.py) and at the two prose sites that spell it out.
  * Landed: Tier A. **Nothing holds what this artifact created**, which is why the tier is not B: the proof gate is not a rule of [check-rules.md](../tools/check-rules.md), no rule reads a `.v` file's statements, and the readings the header takes of R-12-096, R-12-100 and R-12-101 are register readings a reviewer checks by hand. The rules the landing does touch, **K-36**, **K-37**, **K-82** and **K-96**, hold the plan's arithmetic and the findings index rather than the artifact.

## RTL track

#### R1a · The capability format's algebra, and the cross-check that decides it

  * **The format package is whole.** [rtl/vos_cheri_pkg.sv](../rtl/vos_cheri_pkg.sv) carries the bounds construction `csetbounds` performs, the representable-limit comparison and the fast representability check, the address setter and both offset forms with their checked variants, the length as the **wrapping** quantity the model states rather than the saturating one the imported source carries and calls short of being correct, the malformed predicate, and the accessors over the decoded bounds. Each is authored against `cap_common.sail` rather than edited from the imported `set_cap_reg_bounds`, which is what [the delta's §2.1](rtl-reparameterization-delta.md) requires of that row and the reason it prices it as the largest single item there. **No instruction, no datapath and no register file**: this is the format's algebra alone, and `CRAM`, `CRRL` and `CSetBoundsExact` are excluded at the architecture rather than owed (R-15-007k, R-08-011), so no representable-alignment mask is computed and none is returned.
  * **The instrument is the model's own output, and the method is M2.1's rather than a new one.** [`tools/run.py rtl crosscheck`](../tools/vos/cli/rtl.py) compiles the curated model's `prelude`, `core/xlen.sail`, `core/cap_format.sail` and `core/cap_common.sail` together with a generator that calls those functions and prints what they return, then requires a Verilator-driven testbench over the authored package to reproduce every line. The vectors cross as **text**, so no adapter sits between the two implementations, neither is compiled against the other's types, and a disagreement names a line a person reads on both sides. The two halves live in [tools/cheri-equiv/](../tools/cheri-equiv/) because a file-reading testbench is a checking harness and not something this repository would synthesize.
  * **Exit evidence.** The cross-check is green at **658,659 vectors with no disagreement**, line for line over 658,662 lines and over thirteen kinds: decode 540,672, `setCapBounds` 27,347, the address setter 16,000, each offset form 16,000, the representability check 16,000, the bounds check 16,000, the permission lattice 4,128, sealing 512, the null transform across the memory interface 2,000, an integer read as a capability 2,000, and the accessors over the decoded bounds 2,000. Two of those sweeps are **exhaustive over their own domain** rather than sampled: decode over the whole 19-bit bounds encoding, every exponent field against every base mantissa against every stored top mantissa and each at its own address, which is the totality R-15-007a states decode over; and `perms_narrow` over every one of the 4,096 masks the expanded bitmap can take, which is the whole of what `candperm` can hand it. The authored package lints clean under every warning a package can answer; `rtl.py provenance` parses 18 absence rows and 8 removal rows with no finding; the elaboration figures above are unmoved and are **not re-taken**, `elaborate` composing the imported core's own manifest with the configuration package and reading no file this child touches, and the gitlinks it needs being initialized in no checkout of this repository today, which it now refuses by name rather than with a traceback; the checker is green on **all 77 rules against 76**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite is green at 27 modules; and both type checkers are clean.
  * **The vectors are shown to bite rather than assumed to.** Twelve defects were seeded one at a time into a copy of the package and the replay re-run against each: the three-bit region correction, the increment branch's read order, the top mantissa's rounding step, the normalized case flag, the wrap correction's guard, the representable limit, the range test the offset setter takes, one bit of one permission-expansion row, two accessors reading each other's position, one of the two sentry edges, the null transform on the load side, and the reserved-object-type boundary. All twelve are killed, on between 4 and 61,579 lines.
  * **K-79** in [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py), over the new [vos/capformat.py](../tools/vos/capformat.py), holds the parameters themselves. The format is decided across three Sail files and restated across eight other artifacts, and none of the restatements was held against anything: the rule reads the ten parameters out of the model and holds 50 sites against them, requires both packings and all three written-out budget sentences to spend the capability's 64 bits over six abutting fields, and recomputes the figures they derive. Its reader is digits, so the count-word restatements of the same parameters are named as outside it rather than quietly reached. Report-only, and the ground is not K-57's: two sites are under the `-text` tree, R-15-007 is the normative statement rather than a copy of one, and a width in the authored package is spent at six slices, at the reset bounds and inside every shift of the bounds algebra, none of which the rule reads, so a repair would turn a loud finding into a package that elaborates and computes a different format.
  * Six findings.
    * **`setCapBounds`'s containment property holds over a domain nothing states, and this is the third implementation to measure it.** Over the 22,152 vectors with `base` at or below `top` at or below 2^36 the derived region contains the requested one without exception; over the 3,296 with a requested top above it, 48 do not, the decoded top having wrapped below its own base. Both implementations agree on every one, so it is the algebra's rather than either implementation's, and it is unreachable with a tag, `CSetBounds` clearing the tag on precisely the test that fails there. What is owed is that **R-15-007a's representation-correctness proof state the condition**. Reported and not closed: the same gap M2.1 booked from the emulator side, measured again from the RTL side.
    * **The same domain falsifies a sentence the model states about itself, which is that gap seen from the encode side.** `cap_common.sail`'s header says the effective exponent runs to `cap_max_E` = 30 "so *k* + 1 runs to 31 and the five bits are spent exactly". In the wrap domain `setCapBounds` returns an internal exponent of **31** on 1,437 of the 27,347 narrowing vectors, and `capToEncCap` writes 31 + 1 into five bits, truncating to zero and encoding the *denormal* case. Decode's own `min(cap_max_E, ...)` clamp catches it on the way back and the two implementations agree line for line, so nothing is wrong with either; the sentence describes the stated domain and reads as describing the format.
    * **A vector population off entropy alone cannot tell one clause of `setCapBounds` from its absence.** Inside the exponent-increment branch the base's lost-significance flag reads bit 0 of the mantissa as it stood *before* the recompute; reading the recomputed one instead reproduced all 647,179 vectors on the first population. The branch fires with neither mantissa having lost significance only where base and top are both aligned to a large power of two with the top below the base, and there that clause alone decides the exactness verdict. The sweep that reaches it is 840 lines, which turned a surviving mutant into one killed on 33 lines.
    * **The provenance record owes no row for this work, on the record's own grammar.** A binding is a list of settings on [the configuration package](../rtl/vos_c_class_config_pkg.sv) or `n/a` with a stated ground, both readings being about a structure the build does *not* contain. This child adds authored logic rather than disabling a parameter, so there is no absence to bind and a row would be the claim the record exists to refuse.
    * **A hand-maintained count in that record was wrong as written.** Its §4 said four of the rows above it carry `n/a` on the second of the three grounds §1 separates; two do, A-09 and A-10. Repaired with the reading stated, the count being over the items the section lists rather than over the rows above it. Holding it by rule would need a marker on each `n/a` row naming its ground, a document change and a decision rather than a repair.
    * **The checker stated a width of its own, in the module whose preamble says it states none.** K-57's ceiling was `granule * 64` with a comment saying the 64 was XLEN, in a file that reads every other figure out of the artifact that owns it. It now reads `xlen` from [core/xlen.sail](../model/model/core/xlen.sail) through the same parse K-79 uses. In the same act K-57 gained its **fifth** site, the authored package's `Log2CapBlockSize`, the one transcription of the welded block size outside the model and one no rule reached.

#### R1c-i · Write the MMIO windows into the composition

  * **Three of the four are declared and the fourth's premise did not survive the reading.** [The frozen profile's composition](../model/config/verifiedos.json) carries `platform.boot_rom`, `platform.uart` and `platform.blkdev` beside the eight windows it already had, identically in all three shipped files and in [the generated matrix's template](../model/config/config.json.in); [platform_config.sail](../model/model/core/platform_config.sail) gives each one's base and size a name the model can say; [the emitter](../model/model/postlude/device_tree.sail) states every declared aperture's window in the attested tree under R-15-002b; and [the validator](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail) refuses one placed outside IO memory, one placed over another, and a composition that writes the attested tree into the boot ROM. **The RoT window is a premise this item removed rather than a window it declared**: M3.1 landed the OTP fuse bank, the entropy root, the counter file and the watchdog in all three shipped files, and no entry of the register, no row of the profile and no device of R1c-ii's own list names a fifth.
  * **These are placements and the doors are R1c-ii's, which is the decision every other one here follows from.** No register entry gives a serial port or a block device a register interface, so a transmit register or a descriptor ring would be an obligation authored at the composition rather than realized there, and the devices arrive as route-(c) references with the SoC top written over this map. So no slave of the model's sits behind any of the three, and what each one owes is the `reg` R-15-002b makes a stated constraint on the attested tree. **What that costs is stated rather than absorbed, and the enclosing region's PMA decides how much of it there is**: an IO address no device claims falls through to the RAM path (sys/mem.sail), but `pmaCheck` runs ahead of that fall-through, so what is silent is only what the region already permits. The serial port and the block device sit in a readable and writable IO region, so a load and a store inside either are the RAM path's until the top lands. The boot ROM's region is readable and neither writable nor executable, so a load there reads zero while **a store or an instruction fetch is an access fault today**, and R1c-ii has since answered what that costs: nothing on this die fetches from that ROM, so the refusal states the machine rather than a silence.
  * **The arm not taken is M0.16's shape verbatim, and what separates the two is whether the refusal is permanent.** A claimed window refusing every requester at every width in both directions is the landed precedent, it needs no new architectural surface, and M0.16 and M3.1 both took it. It is refused here because nothing on this die may ever open the sequencer's window or the RoT's from another class, where these three are doors whose device arrives at the next child: a refusal written now is state R1c-ii deletes, and it would put the model on the opposite side of R2's trace diff from an RTL top that answers. What the arm not taken forfeits is exactly the silence above, which the ROM region's own attributes already narrow to a load, and which is F-111's own distinction read back the other way.
  * **The serial port's second arm was a transmit-only door through the existing terminal extern, and it is reported rather than taken.** It would give R1c-ii a console that is not HTIF, HTIF being unsynthesizable, and it costs one offset. It is not taken because the register requires no console at all, so the door's shape would be selected here, and its receive half is nondeterminism the §16 replay record (R-15-241, R-16-015 through R-16-021) does not exist to carry, which is M3.1's own ground for keeping the entropy seed out of the attested tree. The gap is carried at F-229c and stays open.
  * **The boot ROM's extent is inside the region that already claims it rather than a region of its own**, because that region's comment has always said it holds the boot ROM and a second region would be one fact written twice. What was missing was never the region: it was the extent, and `memory.dtb_address` was the region's own base, so an image and the attested tree began at the same byte with nothing refusing it. The region goes from 16 KiB to 32 KiB and carries two stated extents where it carried one stated and one implied, and the pairing is written twice on purpose, at composition where only the tree's address is known and at the write where its size is.
  * **K-94 reads the composition against every reader of it, and the wider subject is what makes it worth a rule.** Narrowing it to the composition-and-validator half would have passed on its first run; read against the emitter as well it reported the interrupt file and the revocation sidecar, two apertures every composition declares that had no node at all, inside the file whose own comment says each node below it carries its window under R-15-002b. Both are emitted in the same act, which is why the attested tree moves by more than the three new nodes. Its third hop is the one whose far side is a list rather than a call, the validator's whole-map rows, and it is there because that is the one place the map is still written out.
  * **No corpus member, and the measurement is what removed it.** M3.1's ground for one is that the C-class emulator reaches these windows from the outside; here what it reaches is a RAM-path zero, which is the absence F-111 says cannot be stated by claiming the address. A member asserting that zero would assert the state of an unfinished map, would be rewritten at R1c-ii, and would put a check into the set R2 diffs against an RTL top that answers it. So the corpus stays at 26 of 26 and the manifest at version 9, and the deliverable a member would have been is the six seeded refusals below.
  * Net change: 795 lines added and 74 removed across 17 files, none of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green and **ctest at 2 of 2**; the model's own `$[test]` harness unmoved at **96 properties** and the profile sweep unmoved at **199 refusals of 199**; the differential corpus green at **26 of 26** with 245 checks over 8,830 records at manifest version 9 and every digest unmoved; the generated devicetree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at **6,235 bytes against 5,717** and accepted by the emulator's own bound, inside the region it is written to and clear of the boot ROM's half of it; all three shipped configurations validating against the regenerated schema with their key sets agreeing with the generated maximum at **324 keys against 312**, twelve of them new; each of the **six** refusals fired against a seeded configuration, five at composition and the sixth at the write because only the blob knows its size, that sixth being one composition `--validate-config` accepts and the write refuses at a 6,235-byte blob, and all three unmutated compositions accepted; K-94's third hop fired by deleting the serial port's row from the whole-map list, which it reports once per shipped configuration; the checker green on **all 87 rules against 86**, one of them new, with **all 98 mutants killed against 97**, the repair path clean on a second `--fix`, and the registry covered; K-94 at **33 apertures over 3 shipped configurations**, K-65 at **248 values against 239** and the floors group's uncounted enumerations at **42 against 41**; K-82 indexing **297 findings across 63 items**; the co-read ledger unmoved at **1,384 pairs**, no register entry or prose span having been touched; `run.py model bundle --check` reporting the tracked artifact byte-identical to what the emitter writes from this checkout at **4,562,196 bytes**; the proof gate closing 3,327 constants; the tools' own suite green over 33 modules and 295 cases; and both type checkers clean under ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5.
  * Seventeen findings.
    * **The fourth window already existed four times over, and reading M3.1 is what removed the premise.** The item names a *RoT window* beside the boot ROM, the serial port and the block device, and M3.1 declared four of them in every shipped composition one milestone earlier. Nothing fixes a fifth referent: R1's own list names the RoT **core** and no window, R1c-ii names three devices and none of them is a RoT aperture, and the two plausible readings, a seal-and-unseal service mailbox and the RoT core's own aperture on the fabric, are named by no register entry, so declaring either would author architecture nothing owns. The item's own title states no count either, nothing in the repository reading the word *four* against it.
    * **What obliges the three devices to exist is the plan and what obliges their placement is the register, and the two are not the same authority.** Grepping the register for `serial port`, `console`, `block device` or `blkdev` returns nothing, and each of its ten `serial` hits is a serializer, a serialization point, a serial dependence, a serial-bound challenge or a device serial number rather than a port; `boot ROM` returns three, R-09-003, R-09-036 and R-16-008c's acceptance, and each of the three is the RoT's on-die metal-mask ROM on the RoT's island, reached by no requester on the fabric and a different ROM from this one. R-15-002b is a **placement** constraint and it is enough to write a map and not enough to say what a door does, which is why the doors are deferred rather than invented. Reported and not closed: naming the three is a register act.
    * **Two apertures every composition declares had no devicetree node at all.** The interrupt file and the revocation sidecar were each held in IO memory by the validator and each absent from the attested tree, inside the emitter whose own comment says R-15-002b rather than R-09-007 is what puts a device window there and that *each node below therefore carries its window under R-15-002b*. Nothing read the two sides against each other, so the sentence was true of the nodes that existed and said nothing about the windows that had none. Both nodes are emitted and K-94 now holds the pairing.
    * **The model called two figures attested-devicetree constants and neither had ever been in that tree.** `core/platform_config.sail` says the revocation interval map and the window the kernel revocation path updates the bitmap through are attested-devicetree constants; the window had no node, and the interval had no property anywhere. The revocation node carries both, which is what the kernel needs: the plane answers about addresses, and a reader that cannot find the interval cannot tell which.
    * **Nothing held two declared apertures apart, and the shape of `check_mem_layout` is why.** That function reads one window at a time against the regions, so two apertures declared at one base are both inside the same IO memory region and both pass it; a composition could put the interrupt file on top of the CLINT and validate. The whole-map check walks a list written out one row per window, and each row reads that aperture's own `supported` key and its own base, so a window switched off leaves the check by itself while a window the composition gains joins it only when a row is written. That second half is what K-94's third hop holds, an aperture a shipped configuration declares with no row in that list being a finding, so the omission is loud rather than silent with every gate green.
    * **The boot ROM had no extent and the attested tree began at the region's base.** The ROM region's comment has said since M0.8a that it holds the boot ROM and the tree written at `memory.dtb_address`, and that address was the region's own base while the ROM had no base and no size anywhere in the repository. `write_dtb_to_rom` checks the blob lands in *some* configured region and does not collide with a loaded ELF segment, and with nothing else, so an image and the tree at one byte was a composition nothing refused.
    * **A composition-time bound and a write-time bound over one region are different facts, and only the second knows a size.** M0.8a recorded that the emulator's bound sits on the `--device-tree-blob` path while `--validate-config` exits before reaching it; met again here from the other side, the composition knows where the tree starts and never how far it runs, so the boot-ROM pairing is refused at the address in the validator and at the extent where the blob is written. Both halves were fired, and the seeded composition that passes the first and fails the second is what shows they are two clauses rather than one written twice.
    * **Three subcommands the command table declares answerable on either lane were refused on the host.** `vos/cli/__init__.py` put `config-keys`, `validate-config` and `asm` in the model command's `host_ok` set, and `vos/cli/model.py`'s `main` loaded the build environment before it dispatched, which exits on `win32` by design. So all three printed *the model loops run inside WSL* on the lane the table says they answer on, and this item's three key-set comparisons and three schema validations were taken from inside the guest. Reported rather than repaired here; M1.4′ has since answered it, the environment reader taking a host-lane arm and the dispatch asking the entry point's own table which subcommands need the guest.
    * **The plan's own cell for this work stated a composition four apertures out of date.** R1c's bullet said the frozen profile's composition declares `clint`, `imsic`, revocation and memory-sequencer windows and no UART at all, which M3.1 falsified by adding four RoT windows and this item falsifies again by adding three more. It was true when written and no rule reads it: a citation resolves, a count is not asserted, and the sentence is a claim about a file's contents. Repaired at the parent bullet, which now states the map and points at the artifact.
    * **The silence this item priced its whole decision against is narrower than five sites said it was, and the enclosing region's PMA is why.** `pmaCheck` decides an access before `checked_mem_read` and `checked_mem_write` reach the RAM fall-through (sys/mem.sail), so what falls through is only what the region already permits. The serial port and the block device are in an IO region that is readable and writable, so a load and a store inside either are the RAM path's; the boot ROM's region is readable and neither writable nor executable, so a load there reads zero while a store or an instruction fetch is an access fault. The over-broad sentence stood in this cell, in [platform_config.sail](../model/model/core/platform_config.sail) and in all three shipped compositions, and all five are repaired against the regions the composition actually declares.
    * **The boot ROM's extent is declared inside a region that refuses an instruction fetch**, which is the one access an SoC boot ROM exists for. `executable` reads false on that region and has since before this item, the attested devicetree sharing it. Reported and not closed here: turning it on makes the attested tree's own bytes executable, so it is a composition decision to take with the top that fetches from the ROM rather than ahead of it, and it was handed to R1c-ii along with the device. **R1c-ii has since answered it by declining**, R-09-006's chain placing the verified M-mode image in main SRAM and releasing the boot core into it, so nothing on this die fetches from that ROM and *the one access an SoC boot ROM exists for* is a claim about a bring-up flow this design replaces.
    * **An enumeration was presented as a grep result and was not that grep's result.** The finding above said *boot ROM* occurs at R-09-003 and R-09-036; searching the register returns three, the third being R-16-008c's acceptance. The conclusion is unmoved, R-16-008c's being the metal-mask ROM R-09-002 measures, so no entry obliges an SoC boot ROM; what was wrong is that a sentence stated what a search returns and stated a different set. The clause beside it holds as re-measured: zero hits for `serial port`, `console`, `block device` and `blkdev`, and each of the ten `serial` hits something other than a port.
    * **The repair for F-205 replaced a stale enumeration with a hand-maintained count, which is the defect F-205 names.** The parent bullet said the composition declares *eleven apertures*, and nothing computes eleven: K-94's own figure is over three files at once, and [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py)'s claim table owns no aperture count. It is deleted rather than owned, the bullet now naming what this landing put there, which stays true when a twelfth window arrives. The same shape at M0.8a's landed cell, a ROM region size this item moves, is repaired in the same act.
    * **The whole-map check is a list somebody maintains, and two artifacts said it was built.** `declared_apertures` is written out one row per window; each row reads its own aperture's `supported` key and its own base, so a window switched off leaves the check by itself, but a window the composition gains joins only when a row is written. The Sail file's comment said exactly that and this cell and the findings register both dropped the qualifier. Both are repaired, and the half that was hoped rather than held now is: **K-94 gained a third hop** reading that list against what the shipped configurations declare, fired by deleting the serial port's row, which it reports three times, once per shipped file.
    * **The obligation the disjointness refusal fail-stops on is filed nowhere.** R-15-002b places every aperture inside the 36-bit space with none at a wide power-of-two offset and makes the placement a stated constraint on the attested devicetree; it does not say two apertures may not share a byte, and no other entry does. The refusal borrowed that entry's word *dense* to carry a claim the entry does not make. The message and the function's comment now state the ground they have, which is that two devices cannot answer at one address and an attested tree naming one address twice describes a die that does not exist. Reported and not closed: requiring it is a register act, and this is the lens this item applies to itself at the serial port's second arm.
    * **A composition comment narrated its own revision.** The ROM region's paragraph said what R1c-i changed there while the region's opening comment already states where each half is. It carries the durable half alone now, that the two are held apart at composition and again at the write.
    * **The evidence sweep's model revision is a stamp taken at cmake configure, not a reading of the tree it prints from**, which is what a note quoting it has to know. `git describe` runs once at configure and the emulator carries what it said (env.py's `git_dir`, which translates a lane's `.git` pointer through `wslpath` so the guest can resolve it at all), so a lane that configured over an edited tree keeps printing `-dirty` however clean the checkout has since become. Measured both ways rather than assumed: with that same `GIT_DIR` the guest resolves this worktree and names exactly the paths git names on the host, and `run.py model reference` over a checkout `git status --porcelain` reports empty still prints the marker. So the marker is honest about the tree that generated the reference and the misreading available is *when* it was taken, which is why this cell quotes no revision.
  * Landed: Tier B under **K-94**.

### R4 · Second-class macro architecture specification

  * The specification lands as an eighth derived view, [the second-class macro architecture](second-class-macro-architecture.md), governed by R-15-247m: one row per property the item's cell names with the entries owning it and the shape it forces; a five-level organisation (deck, bank, subarray, row, page) with what is fixed and what is measured at each level; what a row holds and the two ports a 2T0C cell exposes; discharge, its per-phase completion indication, refresh, and what the macro does not carry; the controller-facing interface as constants of the composition; a measurand table defining every quantity R5 returns at the macro level with its corner and the item and entry it enters; five dated device readings under R-18-001a; twelve refusals MA-1 to MA-12; and what the document is not. **It decides the shape and states no value**: every density, latency, retention, current, energy, dwell and geometry figure is a symbol with status `pending`, the composition's placeholder point is named as a placeholder and not repeated, and the one organisational choice the five properties leave open, that a bank lies within one deck, is taken with its three register grounds and its forfeit stated beside it.
  * The view is registered in [views.py](../tools/vos/checks/views.py) with seven body patterns, each a sentence the document carries word for word, and listed under the README's derived views; **K-14** holds its seven bearing requirements and **K-48** its citations from that row on. No rule holds the architecture's own facts, and none is derivable, its content being a shape rather than arithmetic over any owner.
  * Net change: 191 lines added and 0 removed across three files, one of them new, by `git diff --numstat cb28fa5..28e1897`.
  * Exit evidence, every figure at 28e1897 unless stated: the checker on **all 94 rules** with K-00 agreeing both directions and exactly 2 findings, both K-24's view count (README.md:348 and docs/critique.md:3 asserting seven where the artifact gives eight), which is the arithmetic the integrator's `--fix` rewrites, so the selftest's baseline refuses on this lane and **no mutation figure is quoted**; K-49 at all 8 views present and K-14 at all 7 bearing requirements carried by the new view; K-61 at all 1,394 pairs read as they stand, no register entry and no spec span having been touched; K-12 and K-13 green over the new document; both type checkers clean under ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; `run.py test` green over 41 modules and 394 cases; the document at 176 lines by `git diff --numstat` and 123 non-blank by `Measure-Object -Line`, citing 50 distinct register ids by `R-\d\d-\d{3}[a-z]?` counted distinct, every one resolving under K-11; the property table at 5 rows and the measurand table at 22 rows, 22 of them `pending` and 0 cells outside the owed-to column carrying a digit that is not an R- id, a C-row id or R5; 5 dated readings, each read on 2026-09-05 from the page the row links, and the 5 further pages the alternatives register cites named with what each returned to an unauthenticated read; `bank-dse.py` at 8 of 11 candidates clearing the shape constraints and none admitted, with 6 coefficients pending and `qualified` false, unchanged from M0.17; K-55 at 3 artifacts still booking the per-class bank count open, and docs/block-geometry-constraint.md, docs/bank-count-dse-contract.md, tools/quarantine and model/ carrying no diff from cb28fa5; `git status --short` empty in the worktree after the last commit. No WSL run is in this item's evidence: `model/` is read and not written, and `git ls-files --eol` shows 0 files outside `upstream/` at anything but `i/lf w/lf`. The full wave at 28e1897 completed in under a minute on battery and returned exit 1 on the K-24 drift alone. Landed agent-parallel at 0.6 h summed session wall-clock, the earlier session's start unrecorded.
  * Six findings.
    * **No register entry states what a macro architecture specification must contain, so the document is written against a measurement obligation and one sentence of the specification.** A search of the register at 28e1897 for `macro architecture`, `deck row`, `page size` and `sense width` reaches R-15-014a, R-15-108's criterion and R-15-181a's criterion and no entry whose head line names the architecture; R-15-247m obliges the measurement only, and the five properties live in the memory subsystem's prose. The view therefore binds nothing, which is what a derived view is, and an entry stating the specification's required content is a register act reported and not taken.
    * **Five of the ten pages the alternatives entry cites did not serve an unauthenticated read, and the entry's own readings carry no date at the sentence.** On 2026-09-05 the two IEEE Xplore pages returned no readable body, the Science Advances and ResearchGate pages returned 403, and nature.com redirected to its sign-in endpoint, so the dual-gate on current, the two-layer 4F² bit-cell, the 8×8 multi-deck array, the 1 Mbit VLSI 2023 macro and the review's retention figure stand on readings this lane could not re-take, and the document carries none of them. R-18-001a puts the re-take at the milestone that spends them, which is R5.
    * **The one shape decision the properties leave open has no owner in the register.** That a bank lies within one deck is taken on R-15-247g's envelope, R-15-247m's repair level and R-15-173's fallback, and it forfeits cross-deck periphery amortisation; each ground is an entry and the conclusion is nobody's. A later reader taking it as decided would be reading a derived view as normative, which its header forbids, and whether it becomes an entry is the register act above.
    * **The eighth view moves a count two documents state, and the drift is left for the one `--fix`.** README.md:348 and docs/critique.md:3 assert seven derived views where K-24 reads eight from the views table, and the selftest's baseline refuses on the same finding, so no mutant decides anything on this lane; in a scratch clone at 29bdfd8 with only that count repaired, the selftest kills 114 of 114 mutants with 0 stillborn over all 94 rules, a figure recorded here as taken and not as this item's evidence, since it is a tree this item did not land.
    * **The first draft carried four claims its sources do not establish, and the adversarial pass is what found them.** The imec roadmap row said the 4.5-hour retention is stated at room temperature, where the page states no temperature beside it; the deck fallback attributed the words *fewer decks* to R-15-173, which bends capacity and names no deck; the island-ceiling sentence cited R-15-050 for a clause that is R-15-247p's Accept; and one of the seven view body patterns was carried with a different initial case. All four are repaired at 28e1897, and the three imec pages were re-read to confirm that none states a temperature or a life beside its retention figure.
    * **Two sequencing sites still place this item after the co-simulation gate.** `tools/vos/checks/estimates.py:93` lists R4 first in `AFTER_M8B` and the checklist's Q6 at line 611 says the topology comparison precedes it, and the ruling that opens R4 now as a document act reconciles both at landing; neither is edited here, so at 28e1897 the item is landed where the tool's partition says it is not yet reachable.
  * Landed: Tier A. The artifact carries claims the register does not own, an architecture's shape, and no rule holds a macro architecture's facts, so Tier B's fourth condition cannot be met; K-14 and K-48 hold the view's citations and not its content.

## Build-loop instruments

### Initial check/emit/FAST tooling

  * No note was recorded at this item's landing.

### I0 · Standardize the tools on one language

  * Every tool in [tools/](../tools/) is Python 3.14, the one interpreter both lanes carry: the host has no `bash` and the WSL guest no `pwsh`, so three languages could not be reduced any other way. The shell prelude's two load-bearing tricks survive the move and improve: the OCaml stack the Sail emission needs is `resource.setrlimit` in the parent rather than `ulimit -s`, and a stage's cost is `os.wait4` on the child that was asked about rather than `/usr/bin/time` reporting the running maximum over every child so far.
  * Shared parses move into `tools/vos/` and the checker's rules into one module per group, so no fact is parsed twice; the entry points collapse from sixteen to five, the four model loops and the trace rig becoming subcommands of [tools/run.py model](../tools/vos/cli/model.py). [tools/README.md](../tools/README.md) states the lane rule, the conventions, and the three edits a new rule costs.
  * Exit evidence: the checker's output is unchanged line for line except where it now names the package rather than a file, and it runs in 0.95 s against 2.2 s; the mutation selftest kills all 50 mutants in 19 s against 129 s; `model.py build` is green at 150/150 with the same `ALL_DONE` marker; `sweep` returns the same 146/52/1 of 199; `trace-diff --corpus` returns the same 54 prefixes at 97 in 2.2 s against 7.1 s; and the proof gate returns the same 19 constants closed.

### I1 · Move sources to WSL ext4 and uncap local parallelism

  * The item's four clauses do not resolve alike and two are closed. `ctest -j$(nproc)` is done, the build setting `test_jobs=cpus`. Moving the checkout to `~/src/VerifiedOS` is refused on the measurement below. The two left standing are Remote-WSL and cache-hostile WSL memory reclamation, and they share one shape: each is a setting on the human's box rather than in this tree, the second reachable only through `[wsl2] autoMemoryReclaim` in `%USERPROFILE%\.wslconfig`, global to every distribution and permanent until a human deletes it, the same boundary [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) draws around the idle timer beside it. The item is open on a posture call rather than a measurement, and no benchmark closes it.
  * **The rejection under the refused half is overturned, and the move that clears the way for does not pay.** The `/mnt/c` rejection [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) carried measured *reading* 1.19 MB of Sail sources across 125 files, where the stage that pays is `configure`, which walks the whole 824-file cmake project and pays per stat rather than per byte. On a quiet toolchain with the arms alternated, a re-configure is **17.0, 17.4 and 20.4 s at 10% to 12% of a core** from `/mnt/c` against **0.9, 1.0 and 1.1 s at 47% to 48%** from a byte-identical ext4 copy, and a fresh configure is **26.7, 29.5 and 34.9 s** against **11.3, 7.3 and 7.6 s**. CPU time is 1.7 to 2.4 s against 0.4 to 0.5 s, so the gap is wait rather than work. The suspect is real and worth about 17 s of every build.
  * **What the same measurement then removes is the move.** The tax is symmetric and the host lane pays it in reverse: `tools/check.py` costs **1.0, 1.1 and 1.6 s** over the NTFS checkout against **11.4, 13.5 and 18.5 s** over the same tree on the `wsl.localhost` share, and that is the loop run after every document edit. Host git refuses such a checkout until `safe.directory` is relaxed. So relocating the checkout alone would buy 17 s of a build and spend more than that on every checker run; what would pay is moving the *working posture* into the guest, which is this entry's Remote-WSL clause and a workflow call rather than a benchmark.

### I2 · Use one shared content-keyed SMT memo cache

  * **Declined, and on the pinned compiler's cache format rather than on a benchmark.** Sail's memo cache is one flat file of fixed records, a 16-byte digest of the SMT query and one byte of verdict, read whole into a map at startup by `load_digests` and rewritten whole from that map at exit by `save_digests` (libsail 0.20.2, `constraint.ml`). There is no lock, no atomic rename, and `open_out_bin` truncates in place. A build tree's cache measures 1,418,412 bytes, which is 83,436 records with no remainder, over verdicts 6,612 unknown, 31,725 sat and 45,099 unsat. Benchmarking is the wrong instrument here: what a shared path does is not slower, it is unsound, and a measurement that failed to trigger it would say nothing.
  * Three consequences, each alone enough. Concurrent runs do not **merge**: each writes back what it loaded plus what it learned, so the later writer's file lacks everything the earlier learned during the overlap, which is worst exactly when the cache is cold and the learning largest, the case sharing was proposed to fix. A reader arriving mid-rewrite sees a prefix, or one byte outside the verdict range and takes Sail's invalid path, which empties both maps and **replaces the shared file with only that run's entries**: measured deterministically, one such byte destroyed **77,260 of 83,436 records**, Sail exiting 0 having printed `SMT cache file 'sail_smt_cache' is invalid` into a build log nothing reads for it. And two writers interleave at whatever offset each buffer flushed; records are 17 bytes and OCaml flushes at 65,536, which is 1 modulo 17, so the sixteenth boundary falls on a verdict byte and leaves one run's digest carrying another run's answer to a different obligation. That last is a false answer to a typechecking obligation in a build whose output is a verification oracle, the trade I4 refuses on `CCACHE_SLOPPINESS`.
  * **What was done instead is copy-on-create, which buys the same cold start with one writer per file.** A lane standing up copies a warm cache from the primary worktree's tree rather than pointing at it, so the ~25 min a cold cache costs is paid once on this machine and not per lane, and a stale copy costs misses and nothing worse. That landed with I7, and the standing boundary is unchanged: content-keying is by obligation and not by prover, so no copy crosses a solver version or a distribution.
  * Finding, closed by I7 rather than by this item: the shared cache I2 proposed already existed for the typecheck loop, `/root/build/verifiedos-typecheck-smt-cache` being one file every checkout wrote, and it was live across four concurrent lanes. It is per lane now.

### I3 · Benchmark a current z3 binary on a cold emission

  * **The cold emission separates the solvers where the typecheck could not, and it separates them in the pin's favor.** Two alternated repeats per arm in a disposable lane, cold each time by deleting the memo cache and dirtying the emission edge: the pinned z3 **5.1.0** emits cold in 216.3 and 212.9 s against the distribution **4.13.3**'s 274.7 and 286.2, about 214.6 against 280.5 at the midpoints, **+31% for the distribution's solver**, every distro arm auditable by the tool's own WARNING line in its captured log. The open cell's reading, that the workload does not separate them and the pin stands on reproducibility rather than speed, was true of the typecheck it measured (cold 46.9/59.3 s pin against 44.5/48.2 distro, warm 5.5/5.8 against 5.7/8.0, spreads wider than gaps) and is false of the emission: the pin now stands on both grounds.
  * **The ~25 min cold figure the tools quoted was roughly 7× stale, and the staleness is the curation's.** A cold emission today is ~3.6 min pinned and ~4.7 distro against a warm 36 to 39 s in the same lane, so the once-measured figure described a model whose surface the batches since deleted. [tools/run.py model](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)'s seeding rationale now carries the measured pair; the seeding keeps paying about three minutes per lane standup, just no longer catastrophically.
  * Three observations ride the numbers. **The two solvers do measurably different work for byte-identical output**: 4.13.3 writes 23,306 memo records where 5.1.0 writes 17,558 and peaks ~117 MB higher, while the emitted C++'s md5 is unchanged across all four cold runs and the warm anchors, so the difference is solver-side effort and never artifact. **Per-arm determinism is exact**, both repeats of each arm writing byte-identical cache sizes. And **one emit regenerates about a fifth of the seeded cache**, 17,558 records against the seeded 84,849, so the cache a lane copies accumulates more than the emission's own obligations and a lane is returned to warm by a restore, never by a re-run.
  * Exit evidence: quiet-box gate empty; four cold runs and three warm anchors all exit 0, logs under /root/logs; the seeded cache saved before the first deletion and restored byte-exactly at 1,442,433 bytes, a 35.7 s warm emit confirming the lane; no cache crossed a solver boundary.

### I4 · Add shared ccache, then emit only when generated output changes

  * The ccache half is the half that is a choice rather than a measurement: the binary is installed and [tools/vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) puts the two compiler launchers on every configure when it finds one, against a `CCACHE_DIR` shared by every build tree, with no `CCACHE_SLOPPINESS` set on purpose, a false hit in a build whose output is a verification oracle not being worth the hit rate.
  * **The second clause is declined, because measuring it removed its premise.** The clause was that a regeneration producing byte-identical C++ still pays for the 150 s single-threaded compile the critical path ends in. It does not, and the ccache half is why: a `touch` on one model source re-emits C++ whose md5 is the one it replaced, and the compile and link that follow cost **4.5 s over 23 edges**, the 10.5 MB unit among them a direct hit. Ninja's floor with nothing touched is 2.9 s of that, so emitting-only-on-change would save **1.6 s** against a 41.2 s emission, under 4% of the cycle and only where a model edit leaves the generated C++ identical, against a permanent divergence in a vendored upstream `CMakeLists.txt` that every reconciliation would carry. The emission's own 41.2 s is not addressable this way at all: what the output will be is not knowable without producing it.
  * **Finding: the shared `CCACHE_DIR` does not reach across build trees, and the claim that it did was wrong.** ccache hashes the command line along with the source, so the fast tree, differing by `-g`, and a second worktree, differing by every `-I`, share no entry with it: a lane's first 367-unit build against a cache holding 939 entries took 367 misses and no hits. `CCACHE_BASEDIR` would make lanes share and is deliberately not pulled, the object a second lane would get back carrying the first lane's directory in its debug info, a wrong artifact by the standard that keeps `CCACHE_SLOPPINESS` unset. [tools/vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) now records what the cache does and does not reach.
  * Exit evidence, taken in one lane of I7's layout: emission after a source `touch` 41.2 s, generated C++ byte-identical at 10,539,981 bytes over 336,909 lines, follow-on compile and link 4.5 s against a 2.9 s null-rebuild floor, 18 ccache direct hits across the two commands. The generated unit compiled alone at `-O2 -g` is 139.1 s wall and 115 s CPU with a 1.02 GB peak, and that wall figure is an upper bound rather than a clean one: a sibling lane was building and the compile held 83% of a core.

### I5 · Benchmark generated-TU flags, clang versus gcc, and mold

  * Measured in a disposable worktree lane on a quiet toolchain: eighteen compiles of the generated unit in round-robin order so drift spreads across the variants rather than down the list, two or three repeats each, medians reported, repeats drifting mildly downward so no thermal penalty hides in the tail, ccache disabled into a scratch directory so no lane cache was invalidated. The unit has grown to 10,835,851 bytes over 344,963 lines, and the clean baseline is **81.8 s wall at a 1.20 GB peak** for gcc 15.2.0 at `-O2 -g`: the 139.1 s on record was the upper bound it was booked as, roughly 40% of it the sibling lane rather than the compiler.
  * **clang 21.1.8 compiles the same unit at the same flags in 38.7 s, 2.1× faster than gcc, accepting the extracted command verbatim** (two `-Wparentheses-equality` warnings against generated comparisons, nothing dropped); with `-g` off the pair is 21.3 s against 61.3 s. The flip is not taken inside a benchmark cell, a compiler change being its own act with its own build, ctest and corpus evidence; what this item hands that act is the 43 s it buys on the largest term of a real-edit build.
  * The `-g` split is located rather than adopted: dropping `-g` saves 20.5 s on gcc and 17.4 s on clang, `-g1` recovers most of that at 63.4 s while keeping line tables, and `-fno-var-tracking-assignments` locates about 14 s of gcc's `-g` cost in var-tracking. Canonical `-O2 -g` RelWithDebInfo stays the exit criterion and the fast tree already drops `-g`.
  * **`-ffile-prefix-map` is compile-time-free and its premise is removed, the third closure of that shape** (I2, I4's second clause, I6 and I8 are the others): 81.5 s against 81.8 is noise, and the cross-lane ccache hit it was in scope to enable does not materialize even with `CCACHE_BASEDIR` set per lane, ccache stripping the map's values from its hash and the defeating input being the checkout's `-I` paths under /mnt/c, which no single `CCACHE_BASEDIR` covers alongside the /root/build tree paths. Diagnosed from ccache's own debug-mode hash input, where every differing line is a worktree path; the two lanes' units were md5-identical, so the miss is the layout's.
  * **mold is declined on its own measurement**: the simulator link is 0.70 s under GNU ld, 0.24 s under mold and 0.27 s under lld, and a 3× win on 0.7 s is half a second against a warm build stage whose floor is 2.9 s. mold 2.40.4 and lld 21.1.8 stay installed and idle.
  * Exit evidence: quiet-box gate empty before the window; 18 of 18 compiles and 9 of 9 links exit 0, logs under /root/logs; per-variant medians as above; [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py)'s recorded baseline updated from the contended figure to the clean pair.

### I6 · Benchmark the same Sail 0.20.2 binary built with flambda

  * **Declined on its own measurement, which came in far under even the re-ranked forecast.** A flambda-built Sail 0.20.2 (OCaml 5.4.0 both sides, `flambda: true` the only differing axis) emits the model's C++ in a median **33.8 s against the stock binary's 34.4**, three alternated repeats per arm with every run inside ±0.7 s of its arm's median: 0.6 s, about 1.7%, where the estimate had been roughly 12 s. Warm typecheck is 11.96 s against 12.00. The emitted C++ is byte-identical across the arms, both hold 98% of a core, and the flambda binary is a third larger on disk. A second Sail switch carried through every future pin bump buys half a second of a 34 s stage, so the switch is deleted, and what the item leaves behind is that the emission's cost lives in Sail's own evaluation rather than anywhere a compiler mode can reach.
  * Two facts the A/B surfaced outlive it, both about the harness. **The emission bakes the sail binary's absolute path at configure time**, eight occurrences in `build.ninja`, so an arm is a configured tree rather than an environment: the flambda arm ran in a second tree under the same lane, its memo cache pre-seeded by copy, its first emission doubling as the proof the seed took (34.7 s rather than the cold case). And **the no-edit route to a second compiler's Sail is a second opam root whose switch is literally named `default`**, [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) applying `opam env --switch=default` and honoring `OPAMROOT`; a future Sail-version experiment takes the same route.
  * Exit evidence: quiet-box gate empty; six timed emissions and seven typechecks all exit 0, logs under /root/logs; both arms' `sail --version` identical at 0.20.2; the flambda root and its second tree removed with the benchmark's lane rather than carried.

### I7 · Run canonical builds in the background and standardize worktree lanes

  * One WSL toolchain serves as many checkouts as there are git worktrees, and they shared one build tree between them. Each checkout now carries a **lane**, derived from the checkout rather than declared: a linked worktree's `.git` is a file naming its administrative directory under the primary checkout's `.git/worktrees/`, so the lane is git's own name for the worktree and is unique within the repository by construction. The primary worktree has no lane and keeps every path it had; a linked one builds under `/root/build/lane-<name>/`, one directory holding that lane's build tree, its iterate tree, and its SMT cache, so a lane is retired by deleting it. The M0.4 oracle's tree is the one every lane still shares, because it is stock upstream at a pinned commit and no curation here reaches it.
  * **Three things collided and only one of them was loud.** Measured while four lanes ran against this toolchain: cmake refuses outright to aim an existing cache at a second source tree, so `/root/logs/model-build.log` carries `CONFIGURE_EXIT=1` and `The source ".../VerifiedOS-m08c/model/CMakeLists.txt" does not match the source ".../VerifiedOS/model/CMakeLists.txt"`; that refused run had already opened the shared log with `w`, truncating the primary lane's from 10,667 bytes to 444, so a build that never started destroyed the record of the last green one. The third is silent and is the one lanes exist for: `sweep`, `corpus`, `trace-diff`, `devicetree` and `reference` all read the simulator back out of the build tree and none of them can tell whose model generated it, so a lane building after another lane reports on the other lane's machine.
  * A build **holds its lane** for as long as it runs, and a second one over a live one is refused by name rather than merged into it. The lock is `flock` and not a pidfile, so a killed build leaves nothing to break and there is no liveness test to get wrong; `build --background` hands the lock to the run it detaches by inheritance rather than dropping it, and `wait` blocks on that lock in the kernel before reading the log for the verdict. Waiting on the lock rather than on `ALL_DONE` is what ends a wait on a build that died without writing one.
  * **What a lane costs is a copy and not a cold build**, which is what makes one worth having and is why [I2](#build-loop-instruments) declines to share instead. A lane standing up copies the warm SMT memo cache and the downloaded riscv-tests from the primary worktree's tree, so the ~25 min a cold cache costs is paid once on this machine rather than once per lane: measured, a lane from nothing is green in 237.7 s. A build tree is 362 MB, so four lanes are about 1.5 GB against 947 GB free, and disk is not the constraint. What a lane genuinely does pay is the compiles, because no ccache entry crosses a worktree: that is I4's measured finding, 367 misses and no hits on a first build.
  * **Defect found by standardizing the lane, and fixed here: `git describe` cannot run in one at all.** A linked worktree made by the host's git writes a *Windows* path into its `.git` file, which inside the guest is not absolute, so git appends it to the worktree and exits 128 with `not a git repository`. cmake's `git describe` is one of those runs, so every lane configured with `Failed to run git describe: 128` and stamped its emulator `unknown commit`, silently restoring the state M0.10 exists to end; `model.py reference` caught it at exit 1. The configure now carries a `GIT_DIR` translated by `wslpath`, scoped to that one child so it cannot follow a later `git` into the oracle's tree; the lane's emulator reports `636aa07-dirty` and `reference` exits 0.
  * Finding, reported rather than closed: the sibling lanes had already worked the collision around by hand with a second build tree named for one worktree. That workaround is per-agent and undeclared, and reaches neither the log nor the typecheck cache, which every lane still shared as one file; the derivation reaches all three.
  * **Finding, closed here: two ways of standing a lane up reached no seeder, and the second was made by the fix above.** `emit` configures a tree that does not exist yet, and it is the loop the memo cache is worth most to, being exactly the emission [I3](#build-loop-instruments) prices cold at ~3.6 min against ~36 s warm; a lane opened with `emit` rather than `build` paid that. And the typecheck cache sits beside the build trees rather than inside one, so the seeder, which copies into a tree, never reached it: giving each lane a file of its own closed the sharing and left the copy owed, I2 declining to share a cache rather than to warm one. Both seed through one `_seed_cache_file` now, which keeps a target that already exists and copies nothing when the donor is the target, so the primary worktree is not a case any caller has to know about.
  * Exit evidence: `model.py lane` on the primary checkout prints the four paths it always used, unchanged. A lane standing up from nothing is green at 375 of 375 edges in 237.7 s, configure 29.6 s, build 172.9 s at 715% CPU, ctest 35.2 s, seeded from the primary tree rather than cold. A second `model.py build` against a live one exits 1 naming the holder's pid and writes nothing. `wait` returns the finished log's verdict at exit 0, and against a stand-in holder it returned 6.0 s after that holder exited, with no interval anywhere in it. In the lane, `corpus` is 21 of 21 with 193 checks over 5,231 records, `devicetree` compiles with no warning at 4,466 bytes inside its region, and `reference` names the model at `636aa07-dirty` over 62 properties. Every later figure in this section was taken in that lane, and the ones marked contended were taken with a sibling lane building.

### I8 · Optionally move quick checks to push CI and canonical tests to nightly CI
