  * **Declined, and the measurement removes the premise by finding no edit loop to take either half off.** Warm on a quiet host, five arms round-robined so drift spreads across them rather than down the list, three repeats each after a discarded warmup, medians: `check.py` **0.92 s**, `typecheck.py` **0.64 s**, `test.py` **8.87 s**, `check-selftest.py` **19.93 s**, `gate.py` **19.78 s**. The whole pre-land host surface is the wave plus the tools' own suite, **28.7 s**, and the loop actually run after a document edit is the 0.92 s one. A push-triggered runner subtracts none of that, because its verdict arrives after the push while this gate is what decides whether the change lands at all; what it adds is a second opinion on a third machine, at the two costs below.
  * **"CI" is already spoken for in this plan, and what it names there is the gate's predicates and their instrument rather than a trigger or a host, so the two subjects do not meet.** Four places could conflict and none does: M7.1 reruns the composed boot under Sail as the golden cross-check, the §10 rig promises one CI runner diffing one trace format across three executors, [the measurement contract](freeze-measurement-contract.md) §9 states twelve rejection predicates over a freeze report, and [the differential corpus](differential-corpus.md) §8 resolves *where*, answering `tools/check.py` on the host and `tools/run.py model corpus` in WSL. None of the four fixes a trigger, a schedule or a machine, which is this item's residue.
  * **Push CI spends the pin discipline only where a rule declines to reach, and the host half is owned.** K-67 holds the ty and ruff pins at every site stating one and K-75 holds the interpreter floor the same way, so the workflow's two `uv tool install` lines and its `python-version` are rows of those rules' own site tables rather than copies nobody reads: extending an enumerated window is one edit and a mutant apiece, not the rule apiece a new window would cost. What stays outside every window is the runner label, the action references the workflow pins, and the bootstrap route, which is the owed act F-198b already carries.
  * **The nightly half cannot ride a stock hosted runner, and the way it fails is silent rather than loud.** This lane is two opam switches, `default` at ocaml-system 5.4.0 carrying Sail 0.20.2 and `rocq-9.1.1` carrying Rocq 9.1.1, over a z3 **5.1.0** prepended to PATH ahead of Ubuntu 26.04's own 4.13.3, on Python 3.14.4; no stock image carries one of the three. [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py)'s `_prepend_z3_path` warns and continues when that prefix is absent, so a runner without it typechecks green against the distribution's solver, and a nightly gate reporting about a machine other than the one it names is worse than none. What would make a nightly canonical build affordable is a restored SMT memo cache, which [I2](#build-loop-instruments) has already measured: no copy crosses a solver version or a distribution, and a restore onto a hosted image is precisely that.
  * **Finding: this tree carried CI workflow files, their being inert was what kept them green, and S3 has since answered it by deleting them.** `model/.github/` held a composite action, a `dependabot.yml` and six workflows, vendored byte-identically with the model tree; GitHub reads workflows from the repository root's `.github/` alone, so not one ever ran. Two of `ci.yml`'s own lines said why that was load-bearing: its ninja line named `generated_smt_rv32d`, a target that does not exist since the prover-backend loop reduced to the RV64 arm, and its cmake line passed `-DFIRST_PARTY_TESTS`, an option no `CMakeLists.txt` declares since M0.18. The shared action pinned Sail 0.20.2 while installing neither the pinned z3 nor Rocq. Making one live would have been the permanent vendored divergence [I4](#build-loop-instruments)'s second clause refuses, which is why this decline moved no file.
  * Exit evidence: five arms at three repeats each all exit 0, on a box sampled at 4.5% to 15% before the window, with one selftest repeat at 31.75 s against its arm's 19.09 and 19.93 kept in the median rather than dropped; `origin` is reachable and its `refs/heads/main` is this work's base, so the decline is on the cost and not on an absent remote; this item adds no workflow file, and `model/.github/` stands byte-identical to its pin.

### I9 · Hold the co-stated facts and derive the reading view

  * The one drift K-61 cannot see by construction, a fact stated in two of its pairs moving in one while the other stands green, was surveyed across both documents against the whole holder inventory and closed where it was found: rule K-68 pins the surveyed facts at every site that states them, nine new K-24 quantities read each restated count from the artifact that owns it so `--fix` rewrites a grown list's own restatements, K-69 holds the figures one entry fixes with the repair always running from the owner, and K-60 answers every free-prose VLEN token against the composed geometries.
  * Where position was the defect the bookmarks moved rather than the machinery: the clock-discipline block leaves the interrupt entry's pair for the entries that state it, nine further `-2` bookmarks fold restating lines into their own entries' pairs, four prose restatements become citations per the register's own cite-don't-restate discipline, and the truncated tag-plane share is corrected to the value its arithmetic gives and pinned as a K-54 site.
  * The reading loop is priced down rather than restructured: `co-read.py --show --all` prints every pending pair in one read and `--where` opens both sides at file:line, and `tools/run.py view` derives the join the two documents never gave a reader, the prose with every entry rendered beneath the bookmark that cites it, written into ignored `out/` and never a source. [spec.md](spec.md) now opens with the precedence banner every other document in the constellation already carried.
  * Exit evidence: check.py green at 69 rules with K-68 holding 18 co-stated facts at 45 sites; the selftest kills every mutant with the K-69 repair lane proving pristine-fixpoint both ways; 31 co-read pairs re-read and blessed the same day their spans moved; the view weaves all entries with none left homeless; test.py and typecheck.py green throughout.

### I10 · Flip the model build's compiler to clang

  * The adoption I5 priced, taken as its own act with its own evidence: [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py)'s `_compiler_args` now passes clang for the model's C and C++ on every configure that finds one, found-or-absent in `_ccache_args`'s shape, so a machine without clang builds under cmake's default and nothing pretends otherwise. The canonical and fast trees were retired by deletion, the canonical's memo cache saved aside and restored into the fresh tree exactly as a lane seed would be, and the fast tree left to reseed from the canonical on its next use.
  * **The fresh-tree build stage nearly halves**: 81.4 s at 259% CPU against the 175 to 181 s the gcc lanes measured the same day, 149 s over the three stages together, and the largest term of a real-edit build is now the 38.7 s clang compile of the generated unit rather than gcc's 81.8 (I5).
  * **What cmake actually does with an existing tree handed a new compiler was worth exactly one probe, and the tree already being retired was the specimen**: neither acceptance nor a refusal by name, but a silent wipe-and-reconfigure that carries only the compiler variables forward, dropping the build type passed on the same command line and dying on the project's own no-build-type guard. An existing tree ends in an error either way, so retirement by deletion is the migration, which is the lane idiom already.
  * **The flip revealed eight latent warnings and introduced none**: clang's `-Wuninitialized-const-pointer` fires eight times in the vendored berkeley-softfloat-3 sources, a `commonNaN` passed uninitialized by const pointer, a diagnostic gcc does not carry. The vendored tree stays byte-identical to its pin, so the warnings are booked rather than repaired; the two `-Wparentheses-equality` warnings in the generated unit stand as I5 found them.
  * Exit evidence: build green with all three stage exits 0 and `CMakeCache` naming /usr/bin/clang and /usr/bin/clang++; the differential corpus at **24 of 24 with every trace digest unmoved**, which is the gate a compiler flip answers to; `devicetree` unchanged at 4,466 bytes with no warning; `reference` green at 77 properties over corpus v6's 224 checks and 6,658 records; the restored memo cache serving at 1,442,433 bytes; test.py and both type gates green.

### I11 · Hold the upstream pins against the gitlinks that own them

  * **The licence record's pin column was a hand-copy nothing owned, and the fact it copies is the one whose defect cannot be repaired downstream.** [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) states a short commit per submodule, and that commit is the edition each upstream's terms were read at, so a gitlink advanced without its row moving left the page asserting a reading nobody had taken while every other rule stayed green over prose that resolves, cites nothing and counts nothing. K-81 holds the two together in both directions, a gitlink with no row and a row naming no gitlink alike, and it reads the commit out of the **git index** rather than out of a `git submodule status`, which is what makes it decide the same thing on the unpopulated checkout most checkouts here are.
  * **Two hops with two owners, because a pin is restated well past the record.** The index owns the record and the record owns every restatement, and there are 29 of those: the plan's completion notes, the RTL delta's provenance table, ten ported model headers carrying the commit each file was transplanted from, and the two places under [tools/](../tools/) naming the capability oracle's build tree after the pin it was built from. A site is found by the shape a restatement takes, an abbreviated object id on a line that has already named the upstream, over the **whole index** rather than a list of places to look, so a site nobody registered is read on the day it is written. The one part of the index left unread is `model/` outside the ported files, vendored upstream at its own revisions, where a recorded commit is somebody else's provenance rather than a pin taken here.
  * **Nothing is repaired, and that was the one call worth taking twice.** A short commit in a normalized document reads like a token substitution and so like `--fix`'s business, and that is wrong about every site this repository has: each states the commit **beside what was done at it**, the record's row beside the terms read there, the delta's table beside the date its tree was read on, a completion note beside a licence reading taken at a milestone, a ported header beside the file it was transplanted from. A rewrite of the commit alone leaves each describing work done at a commit it no longer names, which is the half-a-sentence hazard that keeps K-70 report-only and is worse here, recording a licence reading nobody took under a flag run to repair arithmetic. Two further grounds would each suffice: the ported headers sit under the `-text` tree, and the oracle tree's commit is a directory's name rather than a sentence's figure. **A pin whose own row is a finding has its sites left alone by both hops**, so one gitlink advancing is one finding at the one place a person has to act.
  * **What the rule cannot attribute it does not decide, and the residue is stated rather than closed.** A commit is paired with the last upstream named on its own line, so one standing on a line naming none is read by nothing: the delta's `Read at` sentence and its second table's display-name cell are both such sites. Attribution by nearness across lines is worse in that document's own tables, which put one upstream's row four lines above another's cell; membership against the whole pin set is worse again, this plan recording many commits of other trees.
  * **Four commits are declared rather than filtered out.** Each is written beside an upstream's name and is not that upstream's pin: the commit the curated model was vendored from, the older `sail-riscv` the CHERI oracle's tree embeds, the tag object a submodule's tag points at, and the numeral of a Zenodo DOI. Naming each asks for a decision the next time one is written, where a shape rule tight enough to exclude them would quietly grow to admit the next one. **The vendored model's baseline stays unheld on purpose**: no gitlink owns it, a vendored tree having none, which is the shape K-71 already declines for the corpus manifest's edition.
  * **One edit and not a rule, beside it.** [The differential corpus](differential-corpus.md) states its own size in words in its opening sentence and nothing held it, K-50 holding membership in both directions and saying nothing about how many members there are. It is a computed quantity and a claim row now, rewritten by `--fix` like every other. **The corpus's size as this plan's completion notes state it is deliberately not registered beside it**: those record what an edition measured at its gate, and holding a live manifest against a frozen measurement would be red the day the corpus advances as designed.
  * **Finding, fixed here: the mutation sandbox's index carried no gitlink at all.** It stands each submodule up as a directory holding one stand-in file and indexes the tree with `git add -A`, so what it recorded at each `upstream/` path was that stand-in. That cost nothing while no rule read one; a rule that does would have reported every pin as owned by nothing and failed the baseline, which reports as a red tree rather than as a bad mutant. The template now puts the repository's own gitlinks back into its index after the add, on every build rather than only a fresh one, because a carried index is the older of the two.
  * Exit evidence: `check.py` green with K-81 holding 12 pins and 29 restating sites, the rule's own pass costing a median **0.22 s** over nine repeats on a box a sibling session was gating on, nearly all of it the commit scan across the index; the selftest kills every mutant, K-81's among them, and the repair lane still proves pristine-fixpoint both ways, K-81 carrying no branch in it; `test.py` green with 12 new cases over the pin parse, which answer for the record's four ways of moving, for a fenced table under its heading, and for the scan's own refusals; both type gates clean. No WSL lane and no build was run and none is owed: this act reads the git index and tracked text, and nothing it changes reaches the model, the RTL, or a toolchain.

### I12 · Give the tools one entry point, and the lane to the tool

  * **Seventeen executables under [tools/](../tools/) become one.** [tools/run.py](../tools/run.py) reads the command table in [vos/cli/](../tools/vos/cli/), and each of the old entry points is now a module of that package: it keeps its docstring, its argparse and its `main(argv)`, and loses only its own preamble and `__main__` block. A command is a name rather than a path, `run.py help` lists every one with the lane it runs in, and `run.py <command> --help` is the help that command always printed. I0 collapsed sixteen entry points to five by making the model loops subcommands; this is that act finished, and the residue it removes is the one a caller pays: knowing which file answers which question.
  * **The lane stops being the caller's to remember.** The document gates run on the host and the model, RTL, generator and prover loops run inside WSL, and a guest command asked for on the host is now re-launched there by `run.py` rather than refused, so there is no `wsl -u root -e python3` to spell. The subcommands that read this checkout rather than drive a toolchain answer on either lane and are named in the table: `model config-keys`, `rtl provenance`, `oracle list`, `seed list`, `testrig protocol`.
  * **[tools/check.py](../tools/check.py) is the one path that does not move, and the ground is the register's rather than convenience.** The register, [the coverage matrix](coverage-matrix.md), [the crown jewels](crown-jewels.md), [the field bindings](field-bindings.md) and [the findings register](findings-register.md) all cite that path for what it decides, so moving it would put a path the tree does not carry into five documents, three of them normative, and dirty the co-read pairs of every entry that names it to say nothing new.
  * **`run.py evidence` is the composite the exit-evidence discipline was paying for by hand.** Every landed item quotes the same measurements, and taking them was six commands into the guest and then a transcription of six figures into a note, which is six chances to quote a figure from a run that is not the one being reported. It runs six members in order, the build and its bundled suite, the reference, the profile sweep, the differential corpus, the devicetree and the proof gate, and ends in the block a note quotes, the `$[test]` property harness being one of the block's figures rather than a member of its own, read back out of what each member printed rather than re-derived; a member that did not report a figure says so and nothing invents one. `--no-build` measures the tree as it stands.
  * **The subcommand dispatch is written once.** Four commands carried the same twenty lines, a parser built from the module docstring, one subparser per row of a `COMMANDS` table, that subcommand's own flags, and the handler the table names; that is the two-copies-of-one-parse defect these tools exist to catch, sitting in the tools. It is `cli.dispatch` now, with each module keeping only what differs, and `cli.Table` replaces four identical handler-type aliases.
  * **The quarantine keeps its own gate and is not a command here**, which is what K-83 makes it: a `run.py` row for it would be exactly the coupling that rule holds the landing loop out of.
  * Net change: 1,109 lines added and 906 removed across 54 files, fifteen of them renamed and three new.
  * Exit evidence: the host gate wave green over all four members with `--tests`; the checker green with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite green over 33 modules; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.74 and ruff 0.16.4; `oracle list`, `seed list`, `rtl provenance` and the guest hop each answered through the entry point; and `run.py evidence --no-build` green in the guest over five members at **96 properties, 199 refusals of 199, the corpus at 26 of 26 over 245 checks and 8,830 records, the devicetree at 5,717 bytes and the proof gate closing 180 constants**. No register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit, so the review gate is not re-entered and the co-read ledger is unmoved.
  * Four findings.
    * **One module read `sys.argv` directly and would have raised the moment a dispatcher called it.** `rtl.main` took no argv at all, being the one entry point that parsed the process's own arguments rather than a list handed to it, so it typechecked green under a cast and failed only when called. It takes argv like every other command now, and keeps the marker it writes around the dispatch rather than pushing that into the shared helper.
    * **A selftest case anchored on a construct the refactor deleted, and the case said so rather than passing.** K-83's path mutant seeded the `sys.path` preamble in a tool that is now a package module with no preamble, so the mutation would not apply and the run reported it as unseeded. It is re-seeded at the one preamble the move leaves standing, which is the entry point's own; a case that had been written to match loosely would have seeded something else and killed a mutant about nothing.
    * **The documents cite tool paths 180 times and only one of those paths is cited normatively**, which is the measurement that decided the exception above rather than a taste for keeping `check.py` where it was. The register names `tools/check.py` and no other tool, in six places; every other citation is in the plan, the tools' own README, the differential corpus, the RTL pages or this repository's licence pages, where a path is a pointer rather than a claim a review gate audits.
    * **The tool surface is obligation rather than fat, which is worth recording before the next simplification is proposed.** A census over the tracked Python finds no public function defined in one module and named nowhere else, and no module outside `tests/` that nothing imports: 33,108 lines with no dead code in it. What makes the directory large is the number of cross-artifact facts somebody decided to hold, so the lever that shrinks it is S15's, generating what is transcribed, and not a search for unused code.
  * Landed: Tier A. No rule holds the one-entry-point fact, so the mechanical conditions Tier B is admitted on are not met, and saying so is cheaper than inventing a rule to hold a directory listing.

### I13 · Close the three tool defects the plan reported and did not close

  * **A lane's revision stamp was noise rather than a reading, and one arm separates the two accounts of it.** [I7](#build-loop-instruments) gave the configure child `GIT_DIR` so that cmake's `git describe` can resolve a lane at all, and gave it no work tree, so git takes the child's own working directory as the tree; cmake runs that describe in `model/cmake`, which is `project_version.cmake`'s own `WORKING_DIRECTORY`, so the index was read against that directory, every tracked path in it read as deleted, and `-dirty` was appended whatever the lane's state was. R1c-i met the same suffix from the other side and read it as the configure-time tree's honest state, and both accounts predict exactly the suffix that was seen, so the arm that decides is the environment and not the tree. Taken from that directory, on the host over a throwaway checkout and in the guest over this lane, both of them reporting empty to `git status --porcelain`: the commit with `-dirty` on it under the directory alone, with `git status` naming every tracked path deleted; the commit alone under the directory and the work tree together, and `-dirty` back the moment one tracked file is edited; and, under no environment at all, the commit alone on the host, where git's own discovery finds the tree the child stands in, against *not a git repository* in the guest, which is the condition the directory is handed over for in the first place. Both halves ride now, so an emulator's revision is a reading of the lane it was configured in rather than a constant, which is the only state in which a genuinely edited lane can be told from a clean one.
  * **The parse every rule reads the corpus through could not read a lane at all from inside the guest.** [vos/corpus.py](../tools/vos/corpus.py) asks git for the index, and it asked with no environment, so on a linked worktree created by the host's git a guest-side `python3 tools/check.py` ended in a traceback naming *not a git repository* rather than in a verdict, and no rule decided anything. The translation was already written and owned one module over, so this reaches it rather than repeating it, and the reach covers the staged-blob read beside the listing, which is what the byte-identity rules stand on.
  * **The third defect was repaired before it was reported, and reading its own register entry is what found that out.** F-204 restates F-000o's neighbour F-000p, which M1.4-prime closed by giving the environment reader a host-lane arm and making the dispatch ask [the command table](../tools/vos/cli/__init__.py) which subcommands it applies to. Measured rather than inferred: all nine declared subcommands answer on the host today, `config-keys` and `validate-config` and `asm` among them. What was owed was never the repair but the holding, so the declaration is now exercised rather than asserted, and the two prose lists that had drifted from the table since are repaired with it.
  * **One owner for the overlay, three readers.** `GIT_DIR` was spelled out at the configure and again in a test's own git helper, which is the two-copies-of-one-parse defect [tools/README.md](../tools/README.md) states these tools exist to catch, in the tools; `env.git_env` is the one place it is composed and the corpus parse is its third reader. `VOS_GIT_DIR` names the administrative directory outright, read at call time like every other override that module carries, which is the route left open on a guest with no `wslpath` and the hook a case in a child process stands on. What that reach costs is one line each in the three test modules that copy an enumerated list of tool sources into a sandbox: a shared parse gaining an import fails such a sandbox with an `ImportError` rather than with the assertion the case makes, which is loud and is exactly the enumeration those lists are for. The same discipline is applied one function up while the file is open: `git_dir` had written out the `.git` pointer parse a second time under a docstring that already named it one of `_gitdir_pointer`'s two readers, so it reads through that function now and the docstring is true of both.
  * **`load(toolchain=False)` promised to skip what only means anything where the toolchain is, and skipped it on one lane of the two.** The arm was the win32 refusal's, so inside the guest a `host_ok` subcommand still raised the OCaml stack, shelled out for the opam switch and announced an absent solver prefix before answering a question about a JSON file. It is one guard now, and held where it operates rather than where it compiles: a guest-only case runs both readings in children of their own, and inside WSL `load(toolchain=False)` leaves `PATH` and the stack soft limit exactly as it found them and adds no opam variable, while the full `load()` beside it moves all three.
  * Net change: 729 lines added and 81 removed across 16 files, one of them new.
  * Exit evidence: the host gate wave green over its three members with the tools' own suite beside it; the checker green on all 91 rules with all 107 mutants killed, none of either new, and the registry covered; the tools' own suite green over 39 modules and 364 cases on the host, one module and 8 of those cases new, with a ninth new case guest-only and run in the guest; both type checkers clean under ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5. Each of the three repairs is decided by a case that fails when the line the defect lived on is put back, and each was run both ways: `test_model_host`'s `configure-hands-the-work-tree` drives the configure in a child of its own, with the run stubbed, and reports the one-key overlay the caller used to compose for itself; `test_corpus`'s `reads-a-checkout-git-cannot-find` reports git's own *not a git repository* against the untranslated parse; and `test_lanes`'s `declared-subcommands-answer` reports `run.py model asm` refusing the lane against a dispatch that loads the environment before it reads the subcommand. `test_env`'s `git-env-names-the-work-tree` sits beside the first of those and decides the other half of it, that the pair those two keys compose is the environment real `git` answers truthfully under. The guest was read as well as the host, this act changing the environment of a child that only ever runs there: from `model/cmake` inside WSL on this lane, the `git describe --tags --always --dirty --broken` that `project_version.cmake` runs answers the commit with `-dirty` appended under the directory alone and the commit alone under the pair. The fourth repair is decided there rather than here, `load()` on win32 refusing before it reaches the guard at all, so `test_env`'s guest-only `host-lane-reading-drives-no-toolchain` is what holds it, reporting the opam environment, the moved `PATH` and the raised stack limit against the guard written as the platform arm. No build was run and none is owed: this act changes what a configure hands its child and no line of the model, the RTL or a proof. No register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit, so the review gate is not re-entered and the co-read ledger is unmoved.
  * Seven findings.
    * **The always-on marker is the missing work tree and not the configure-time snapshot, and only an environment arm tells the two apart.** Both readings predict `-dirty` over a clean lane, so the observation R1c-i took cannot decide between them and the account it recorded was the available one rather than the measured one. What decides is running the same describe from the same directory under three environments, on both machines: the pair answers the commit alone on either, where the directory by itself appends the marker on either, and the empty environment separates them, git's own discovery answering on the host and finding no repository at all in the guest.
    * **A finding restating a closed one stays open for as long as nobody re-measures it.** F-204 was raised from prose against a defect M1.4-prime had already closed, and its own neighbour in the register said so; the entry cost a reader the belief that three declared subcommands do not answer. What it leaves is that a restatement is worth a measurement before it is worth an act.
    * **`--help` decides nothing about which lane a command answers on.** argparse exits during parsing, before a module reaches its environment at all, so a subcommand that would refuse the lane still prints its usage and exits 0. The obvious probe is the one that reports green about exactly the defect it is aimed at, so the case carries an argument table that reaches every handler instead.
    * **A case that sets a process-global override sets it for every module the runner is running in the pool beside it.** Pointing the corpus parse at a throwaway checkout through the environment is the obvious fixture, and it failed twenty-five cases across six other modules, each of them reading the real tree through a variable this case had set. The composition is split from the reading so the first case needs no override at all, and the second runs in a child process, which is also the honest shape: a lane's own failure is `python3 tools/check.py` in a process of its own.
    * **The set of subcommands that answer on either lane is prose in three places and a table in one, and two of the three had drifted.** [tools/README.md](../tools/README.md) named seven of nine and [the entry point](../tools/run.py)'s own docstring five; the third is I12's completion note, which records what that act put there and is left as the record it is. Every subcommand the table declares is held against the README page now and the entry point's list stops enumerating; the other direction, a page naming what the table does not declare, is held by nothing and is what keeps the entry open.
    * **A host-lane reading that skips the toolchain preparations skipped them only on the lane that cannot take them.** The guard was written as the win32 arm rather than as the reading's own, so the claim was true where it was untestable and false where it could be measured. What closes it is a case on the machine that can, which is the guest, and a suite that runs on one lane only would have left the arm unread whichever way it was written.
    * **A case at the owner decides nothing about the caller the defect lived at.** The overlay's own case reports a clean checkout stamped `-dirty` under the environment the configure used to hand its child, which is a fact about `env.git_overlay`; the line that was wrong was `_configure`'s, and putting that line back alone left that case, the three host gates and the whole suite green. A repair is held by a case that fails when the *edited line* is restored, which for a caller means driving the caller.
  * Landed: Tier A. Nothing in [the rule registry](../tools/check-rules.md) holds a fact this landing created: what holds them is the tools' own suite, which is not a rule this plan's Tier B admits, and saying so is cheaper than spending three edits and a mutant to turn a behavioural test into one.

### I14 · Make a seeded run survive its own lane, and absorb the loop that kept its own accounting

  * **A mutant recompiles the mutated proof and what requires it, and the entry asking for that narrowing was the thing still open.** The Coq lane compiles the closure of the mutated stem through [vos/proofs.py](../tools/vos/proofs.py)'s dependency order, which is the parse [run.py proofs](../tools/vos/cli/proofs.py) already derives, and the whole directory once at the baseline, where nothing on disk is trusted yet. For the default subject that closure is two sources of sixteen, and the source that dominates a whole-directory pass is required by nothing, so it is paid once rather than once per member. The closure holds the whole failure set rather than a prefix of it, which is what lets a kill go on naming *which* proofs refused a mutant.
  * **A run is now built to outlive its lane, and that is two acts rather than one.** Every oracle takes the keepalive lease [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) exposes, which every model loop already takes and this family alone did not: WSL2 starts its idle timer once the last process in the distribution exits, so a population costing a compiler or a prover per member was the work most likely to lose the machine under it and the only work holding nothing up. And every verdict is written to a journal as it is decided, so a run killed anyway still says what it decided rather than losing the lot at the report it never printed. The head carries the population and the fraction being run, because both are known before the first mutant; the closing line is written only by a run that reached its report, so its **absence** is the record. The report itself is unmoved: accumulated, printed whole, in population order.
  * **The journal is a second copy and never a second accounting.** It is [vos/seeded.py](../tools/vos/seeded.py)'s, beside the verdicts it writes down, and it opens and closes the file around each line rather than holding a handle, which is the flush: nothing waits in a buffer for an exit that may not come, at one `open` against a member that costs a prover. Under `--jobs` the lines land in completion order where the report is in population order, which is the difference between a record of what was decided and a report a completion note quotes.
  * **The fifth mutation loop joins the vocabulary the other four share, and the two verdicts it merged are named apart.** [The quarantine's gate](../tools/quarantine/gate.py) carried its own seeding and its own accounting, and reported a case whose mutation no longer applies and a mutant the rule read and said nothing about in one findings list under one label: both failed the run and neither was named, though the first is repaired in the case and the second in the rule. It reads the shared verdicts now, which K-83 permits by construction, that rule forbidding the landing loop reaching into the quarantine and saying nothing about the reverse. Its population is authored and always whole, so its report closes on a scope saying so, and it produces no stillborn verdict at all, its oracle being a group whose run always runs.
  * **A narrowed run states what it decided rather than what it picked.** The scope was filled where the population is picked, which is before any verdict exists and is right for the two oracles that always finish. The `$[test]` loop is the third and can stop early, refusing to continue past a restore it cannot verify, so its closing line could state a run larger than the verdicts printed under it. It is narrowed at the point the report is made instead, which leaves the other two reporting exactly what they reported before.
  * Net change: 603 lines added and 105 removed across twelve files, one of them new, and no line of it reaches the model, the RTL or a document any review gate audits.
  * **Behaviour on the Gallina lane is measured rather than asserted.** The same sample of two mutants of `proofs/CyclicExecutive.v` is run before and after, in two lanes against one prover, and the two reports are the same report: the baseline at **1,503 vectors** from Rocq 9.1.1 in `certirocq-0.9.1` both times, **2 of 2 killed with nothing survived or stillborn**, the same two verdict lines with the same movements, `1,152 of 1,503` and the prover's own refusal, the same scope of **2 of 138** naming the same seven operators nothing asked about, and the same closing line. The one line the run gains is the journal's path, and the journal beside it carries the head, those two verdicts in the order they were decided, and `complete: 2 verdict(s) decided, exit 0`. The Sail oracle is exercised on the same shape at a sample of one over the frozen Keccak spec, whose baseline is **1,000 vectors** and whose sampled mutant did not compile: the run reports a population with nothing live in it as the finding it is and exits 1, which is the vacuous-pass floor unmoved, and its journal carries that one verdict and its closing line.
  * Exit evidence: the four host gates green in one run; the selftest killing all **107** mutants over **91** registered rules with the repair path holding at twelve seeded figures, both fixpoint proofs moving no byte, and the registry covered both ways; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; the tools' own suite green over **38 modules** and **361 cases**, five of them new over the journal, the finding count and the narrowed scope. The quarantine's own gate exits 0 over the same **2 rules, 12 floors and 4 mutants**, all four killed and now reported as killed by name, over **56 test cases** against 52, the four new ones holding the two verdicts apart. The co-read ledger is unmoved and no register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix line is touched, so the review gate is not re-entered.
  * Four findings.
    * **A disposition read open over an act the tree already carried.** Nothing holds a findings-register disposition against the code it describes, so an entry goes on asking for work that is done with every gate green, and the only thing that catches it is somebody reading both. The entry is re-dispositioned; the class stands, a disposition being a judgment no rule computes.
    * **A run's stated scope counted what it picked rather than what it decided.** For two of the three oracles those are the same number. For the one that writes into the checkout they are not: it stops the moment it cannot verify its own restore, and the closing line, which is the line a completion note copies, would then have stated a run larger than the verdicts printed under it.
    * **Eight relative links inside the tools' own docstrings resolve to nothing, seven of them naming an executable the one-entry-point move retired**, and no rule reads a link written in Python prose. Repaired under `tools/`; the same retired name stands in three shipped proof headers and is left, reported and not closed, a comment edit there costing a guest run of the proof gate this item does not take.
    * **A baseline that would not stand up discarded the prover's account of why it would not.** The emitter appends its failures to a list the caller passed as a throwaway, so a tree that did not compile was reported as a tree that did not run, and locating which proof refused cost a second whole run at exactly the moment a reader has nothing else to go on.
  * Landed: Tier A. No rule holds a verdict vocabulary, a lease or a journal, so Tier B's fourth condition has nothing to name; inventing one to hold a module's report shape would be worse than saying so.

### I15 · Hold the facts the plan reports as held by nothing

  * **Four candidates, three rules and one decline, and what decides each way is a measurement rather than a preference.** The plan and [the findings register](findings-register.md) carry candidate rules the acts that found them declined to write, each recorded as a gap with an id spent nowhere. Three of the four had something to hold and are written; the fourth had its subject closed under it by a later act, so writing it would have been a rule comparing a generator's output against the same generator's accounting of the same run. **A rule with nothing to hold is a rule that decides nothing, which is the defect this checker exists to catch**, and that is S14's precedent applied a second time.
  * **K-97 holds the elaborator's version pin at the three sites that state it.** An upstream pins by version as well as by commit, and [the licence record](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries both kinds on one page; what owns the two is not one artifact, a commit pin being the git index's and a version pin being the constant every `rtl` loop refuses another value of. So the reading runs the other way from K-81's: `VERILATOR_PIN` is the owner and the record's development-tools row, the record's own Verilator paragraph and the plan's RTL-track cell are the restatements. K-81 could not see it, a dotted version being no object id, and K-67 reaches the two checkers `run.py typecheck` installs and no third pin. Reported and never repaired on K-81's own ground, each site stating the version beside what was done at it.
  * **K-99 holds what the ring emitter says about the wire encoding against the profile's own rows.** K-89 decides that the generated artifact agrees with its owners, the declaration and four register entries, and that is the whole of what it decides; the emitter also states the encoding, and where it takes that from is [the typed IDL profile](idl-profile.md)'s §4, which is not an owner it reads. Three readings: IDL-023's ladder is **recomputed** from the rungs that entry states in words rather than compared against a second copy, IDL-053's member-to-row assignment is held against the terms the emitter's descriptor size spends in both directions, and `WF-11`'s members are held against the widths its buffer-reference size spends. That drift had already walked a width rung through, which is what [F-218b](findings-register.md) records.
  * **K-100 holds the lane's fact table to naming things this tree has.** No artifact declared the distribution-package set the model build needs, so `run.py provision` became its owner and what stood in for the entry nobody wrote was a discipline stated once in a completion note: every row names a consumer already in this tree. That is a gate now, over the table itself rather than a transcription of it, so a row written tomorrow is inside the rule that day. **It found one on the day it landed.** What it does not decide is whether the set is complete, which is the half [F-197c](findings-register.md) is actually about and which stays a register act's.
  * **The fourth is declined, and the measurement is the whole of the argument.** S15 reported that 593 of the 946 mnemonics the model spells had no row in the corpus assembler's encoder table and that no rule read the gap. M1.4′ then generated that table out of the same bundle: it stands at **932 rows against 946 enumerated mnemonics**, so the gap is **14** rather than 593, and every one of the 14 is named by the generated artifact itself: twelve in its `refused` list, ten of them with the guard that refused each and two with a printed shape this assembler does not write, and the remaining two, `illegal` and `c.illegal`, carried by the `ILLEGAL` clause of the two its `unread` list names, the other of the two being the `FENCE` clause the table answers with its one authored row. K-88 holds that artifact byte-for-byte against the generator that writes it, at this gate, running it rather than trusting the index. A rule over the residue would therefore hold one run's output against the same run's accounting of itself, which is a pattern encoding its answer. **The id it was priced against stays unspent and unreserved**, on S14's ground: an id named in a document and registered nowhere is a citation no reader can resolve.
  * **What no rule here reaches is stated rather than left.** K-97's window is the three live restatements and not a scan, so the plan's completion notes stating the elaborator's version beside an elaboration run at it are outside it, being measurements recorded at a gate. K-99 reaches three rows of §4.2 and the profile's own holders section now says which, the completion's members being R-12-093's and placed by no row. K-100 holds a row's symbol only where this checker may open the file, so `model/CMakeLists.txt`'s `find_package(GMP)` is held at the path alone.
  * Net change: three rules at three edits apiece and four mutants, plus the profile's holders section, the tools' README, this note and its register entries. Four present-tense claims elsewhere in this plan are repaired where they stand rather than left to be met: S9's two candidate-rule bullets and M6.4's owed binding because this item answered them, and S15's encoder-table bullet because M1.4′ did. The report test's two fixture labels are respelled to ids no registry can carry, so the id this item declined stays unspent in the code as well as in the documents. No register, spec, coverage-matrix, crown-jewel, absence-contract or profile-§6 edit, so the review gate is not re-entered and the co-read ledger is unmoved.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run, exiting 0; the checker green on all **94** registered rules against 91, K-97 reading three restatement sites, K-99 holding six descriptor members and five buffer-reference members, and K-100 reading 19 rows over 22 artifacts, 17 symbols and 13 `run.py` commands; the selftest killing all **111** mutants against 107 with the registry covered in both directions and the repair path unchanged, its twelve seeded figures rewritten and a second `--fix` moving no byte; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; and the tools' own suite green over 38 modules, the whole wave exiting 0 with `--tests`. No WSL run is in this evidence and none is owed: every rule here reads the git index, tracked text and one Python emitter that runs on the host, and nothing it changes reaches the model, the RTL or a toolchain.
  * Seven findings.
    * **The lane's fact table named its second owner by a bare file name**, `quickchick.py` where the index carries `tools/vos/cli/quickchick.py`, so the row pointed at nothing a reader or a tool could open. It is the finding K-100 made on the run that landed it, which is the evidence that the rule bites rather than a claim that it would.
    * **IDL-023 states its ladder twice and the second statement is the one the emitter implements.** The entry fixes the width of a length or count field and then, in the same sentence, the width of a discriminant, and `disc_width` is the discriminant's. A rule reading the first occurrence would have left the second free to move with every gate green, which is this rule's own subject arriving inside it. Both are read and required to agree, and the criterion's restatement of the ceiling is held against the top rung beside them.
    * **The encoder-table gap is closed by generation rather than by a rule, and the figure fell by a factor of 42.** 593 of 946 mnemonics had no row when S15 measured it; the table is generated now and the residue is 14, each named in the artifact with the guard, the unwritable printed shape, or the unread clause that accounts for it. Recorded as the ground of the decline rather than as a defect: what makes a rule unwritable here is that the accounting and the artifact are one act.
    * **This landing falsified a section written to say what nothing holds.** The profile's own holders section reported the binding from §4.2 to the emitter as owed and not written, which K-99 makes false; the repair states which rows are reached and which are not, because *no rule holds §4.2* is a wider claim than any rule replaces it with. It is the stale-claim shape this repository catches everywhere else, arriving in the paragraph that exists to report such gaps.
    * **The elaborator's other version is restated twice and owned by nothing.** The imported core's own flow pins the same tool at a different version through nix, stated in the record's Verilator paragraph and in the RTL lane's comment, and no constant here fixes it because nothing here runs that flow. K-97 does not reach it and would be inventing an owner if it did. Reported and not closed.
    * **A rule declared fail-closed at each reading was fail-closed at one of three.** K-100 refuses per row on the path, and a row is under no obligation to name a symbol or a `run.py` command, so neither of those readings could be refused there and neither carried a floor: an owner column reworded off the `path's SYMBOL` form drops every symbol with each path still resolving and the gate green, which is the vacuous pass K-47 exists for arriving inside the rule that declared it could not happen. Both now carry a floor inside the rule, on the ground K-84 states. It is F-218d's shape one item on, and finding it is what makes the row a price rather than a claim.
    * **A declared residue gave a ground that did not describe the tree.** K-97's window leaves the provisioner and its test out because both compose the version from the constant, which is true of the fact row and the pin loop and not of the two further places those same files spell `5.032` as a literal, `_number`'s docstring and the case pinning what `_number` parses. Those are samples of the elaborator's banner, fixed by the shape a dotted version takes in a greeting and true the day the pin moves, so the exclusion stands and only its stated reason was wrong.
  * Landed: Tier A, and the condition that decides it is the second rather than the fourth. **K-97**, **K-99** and **K-100** hold the three facts this landing created, each at the sites it created them over, and **K-88** holds the artifact the declined rule would have read, so the fourth condition is met in whole. What is not met is *no Tier-A edit*: the binding K-99 supplies falsifies a section of [the typed IDL profile](idl-profile.md) written to report what nothing holds, and a profile is read at a full attention whether the edit is normative or a repair to a claim about the tools. Saying so is cheaper than arguing that a holders section is outside the document carrying it.

### I16 · Put an authored source in the imported build, and partition the elaboration diff

  * **Two mechanisms R1b's first seam wants, and neither existed.** [`run.py rtl`](../tools/vos/cli/rtl.py)'s file list composed the imported manifest with a substituted *configuration package* alone, so an authored source could reach the imported build only by being copied into the imported tree, which is the one thing [rtl/](../rtl/) forbids; and its elaboration diff failed on any module the curated build instantiates and the stock one does not, which is what every authored replacement is. R1b's cell books both as its precondition rather than as its work, and this is that precondition.
  * **A substitution is a declaration, the composition honours it, and nothing is copied.** `SUBSTITUTIONS` names one authored source under `rtl/` standing where the imported manifest names one or more imported ones. The authored file is placed at the first of those lines and the rest are dropped, so one authored file for a tree of imported ones is one entry where the tree was and the manifest's own compile order decides where it sits rather than a second ordering nobody maintains. The imported sources stay behind their gitlink and what is composed is a list of paths across the two, which is the property [the lane's own preamble](../tools/vos/cli/rtl.py) already stated and this keeps.
  * **Several imported sources to one authored source is the shape the real replacements take**, the flat-SRAM subsystem standing where a whole cache tree did, and it is also what makes the diff below able to attribute at all: every module kind a named imported file declares is one the substitution displaced rather than one a parameter removed.
  * **All three ways a declaration can fail to mean anything are refusals rather than warnings.** A declared substitution reaching no line of the manifest leaves the imported source in the build and the authored one out of it, which elaborates cleanly and measures the wrong design; an authored path not in the checkout does the same; and an imported path the manifest names and the checkout does not carry drops its line from the build with nothing able to read the module kinds it declared, so that row's displacements would be reported as the parameters' removals, which is the one mis-attribution the partition exists to prevent. All three are refused per substitution and by name, and the sharpest case is a line the unreached filter already drops: it is available to declare and not available to substitute, and the two ways of losing a line are indistinguishable in the result and opposite in meaning.
  * **The diff is partitioned rather than signed, and that is the whole of what makes an authored replacement legible to it.** A kind the curated arm instantiates and the baseline does not is an **introduction** where an authored source in the curated file list declares that module and a **finding** where none does. A kind the baseline instantiates and the curated arm does not is the disabling parameters' own only where no replaced imported source declared it; where one did, it is reported apart, so [the provenance record](../rtl/synthesis-provenance.md)'s *Elaborated result* column keeps meaning what it says. Two findings the older diff could not state are new: an authored module that reached the file list and never reached the netlist, and a declaration one of whose imported sources missed.
  * **What accounts for a kind is read out of the SystemVerilog rather than declared beside it.** Both attribution maps are the `module` declarations of the authored and the replaced imported sources, comments of both kinds removed first, so a module renamed on either side moves the map instead of leaving a table to go stale. A declared name is reduced to its kind the way a netlist name is, its parameter hash dropped at the first double underscore, because the two name spaces are joined and a module carrying a double underscore of its own would otherwise join to nothing and be reported at once as inert and as unexplained. A `package` declares no module and is owed no appearance in a netlist, which is why the inert half is over declarations and not over rows.
  * **The substitutions reach the curated arm and never the baseline.** The baseline is the imported tree at its own stock configuration, and one carrying authored sources would make the difference a statement about two curations rather than about the parameters, which is the reading the whole record rests on.
  * **`rtl filelist` is the composition without the elaboration**, so a lane learns whether its declaration reached a line before it pays two minutes for an elaborator to say the same thing less legibly, and `--show` prints the composed list itself. A row it takes reports both attribution counts whether or not either is zero, so a declaration that accounts for nothing says so instead of falling silent. It reads the checkout and drives no toolchain, so it is the second `rtl` subcommand that answers on either lane.
  * **`AUTHORED` is deliberately not the substitution table.** It is the set `rtl lint` compiles *alone*, and a source written to stand in the imported datapath compiles against the imported packages, so folding the two together would turn a green loop red on a legitimate authored file. What a substituted source meets instead is the elaboration's own **compile**, where an error refuses the arm and a warning is read by nothing: `_elaborate` passes `-Wno-fatal` and no `-Wall`, and `elaborate` prints the elaborator's output only on a non-zero exit. [rtl/README.md](../rtl/README.md) already measures that gap at the one authored source standing in the imported build today, the configuration package, two of whose own warnings the lint gate does not reach. So the two declarations sit beside each other saying which is which, and what a substituted source has is a compile gate rather than a lint one.
  * Net change: 731 lines added and 47 removed across eight files, one of them new.
  * Exit evidence: a composition run against the imported core's real manifest, whose 223 non-blank lines leave 166 once the unreached filter has run and compose **169 entries** with the three OpenTitan primitive packages prepended and nothing substituted. Against that, one row standing [vos_cheri_pkg.sv](../rtl/vos_cheri_pkg.sv) where the manifest names the five write-through data-cache sources composes **165 entries**, places the authored file at the first line it replaces, attributes the five module kinds those files declare and introduces none, and refuses by name when one of its imported paths is changed to a line the manifest does not carry. **That row is declared nowhere and is not a row anyone would declare**: `SUBSTITUTIONS` is empty until R1b writes its first one, so the figures were taken by calling `_file_list` and `_substitution_modules` with the row directly against the primary checkout, and they are a measurement recorded here rather than one a command reproduces; the capability package is standing for five cache modules, which is why it introduces nothing. The checker is green on all **91 rules** with every one of **107 mutants** killed, the repair path holding and the registry covered; the tools' own suite is green at **39 modules and 373 cases** against 38 and 356; both type checkers are clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; and the co-read ledger is unmoved at **1,394 pairs**, this item touching no register entry and no prose span. **No elaboration is in this evidence and none can be**: `upstream/opentitan` is uninitialized, the elaboration puts its primitive packages at the head of the imported core's file list and refuses by name, and initializing it is a clone this item does not take. Both mechanisms are held by the tools' own tests against a synthetic manifest and by that composition run, and neither has been through Verilator.
  * Six findings.
    * **The two authored-source declarations are not one list, and merging them would break a green loop.** `AUTHORED` is what lints standalone and a substituted source is compiled against the imported packages, so a substitution table grown out of `AUTHORED` would report a legitimate authored file as a lint failure. Closed here by declaring the two apart and stating the difference at both.
    * **A declaration that reaches no line and a file list one source short are indistinguishable in the result and opposite in meaning.** The composed list carries no trace of which happened, and the arm elaborates either way, so the declaration has to be fail-closed rather than best-effort. Closed here, per substitution and per imported source; the case that makes it sharp is a line the unreached filter already dropped, which is available to declare and not available to substitute.
    * **The record's *Elaborated result* column changes what it means at the first substitution that displaces a module kind, and the record is not this lane's to edit.** Today every row of that column is what the parameters removed, and once an authored source stands in the imported build a removal can be the substitution's. The instrument attributes and reports the two apart, so the column stays true of the rows that carry it; what is owed is a sentence in [the record's §5](../rtl/synthesis-provenance.md#5-where-the-enumeration-is-taken) naming the partition, at the first row that displaces a module kind and not before, the documents carrying present-tense state. R1b's own first row displaces none, so that sentence falls due later than the first declaration does. Reported and not closed: R-15-103's binding and K-76's reading are the author's.
    * **The attribution is by direct declaration and is therefore incomplete by construction.** A module a replaced file instantiated that a third imported file declares reads as parameter-removed, because nothing here elaborates a third arm to tell the two apart. The answer is a row naming that file too rather than a cleverer reader, which is why a substitution takes several imported sources to one authored source. Standing, and it is what a curator prices when writing a row.
    * **A row whose two sides are both packages is visible to no member of the partition, and the row R1b's re-pointing would declare is one.** [vos_cheri_pkg.sv](../rtl/vos_cheri_pkg.sv) declares zero modules, so the introduction and inert members say nothing about it; `core/include/cva6_cheri_pkg.sv`, the imported source it would stand at, declares zero modules too, so the displaced member says nothing either. Measured over the primary checkout, both attribution maps come back empty and the composed list is 169 entries with the row and 169 without it. The partition therefore first decides something at a row whose imported side declares modules, which is the flat-SRAM one, and until then it is carried rather than exercised. Standing, and it is why the inert check reads declarations rather than rows.
    * **The end-to-end elaboration evidence is owed and cannot be taken in any checkout of this repository today.** The gitlink the elaboration refuses by name on is `opentitan`, which S3 left uninitialized because nothing open reached it, and reaching it is a clone rather than a command. Reported and not closed: R1b takes that evidence at its own gate, and until then both mechanisms rest on the tools' own tests and on a composition run that reads the manifest without elaborating it.
  * Landed: Tier A. No rule holds either mechanism, and the reason is not that one would be hard to write: what they create is a declaration whose member set is empty today, so a rule over it would report `ok` about nothing until R1b declares a row. The tools' own tests are the gate a behavioral claim answers to here, and saying so is cheaper than a rule that decides nothing.
